APPROVAL  AND  ACCEPTANCE  SHEET  
 The  thesis  entitled  “PsyClick:  Clinical  Decision  Support  System  for  Detecting  
Single-Session
 
Psychomotor
 
Disturbances
 
via
 
EWMA-Based
 
Multivariate
 Analysis  of  Keystroke  and  Cursor  Dynamics”  prepared  and  submitted  by:   Añonuevo,  Jon  Rian  A.  Ballano,  Denise  Nicole  M.  Huertas,  Lana  Denise  R.  Tablate,  Judea  Ann  C.    In  partial  fulfillment  of  the  course  requirements  for  the  Degree  of  Bachelor  of  Science  in  
Computer
 
Science
 
with
 
specialization
 
in

Engineering
 
has
 
been
 
examined
 
and
 
is
 
hereby
 
recommended
 
for

=== PAGE 15 ===
 
ABSTRACT  
 
Mental  health  professionals  in  the  Philippines  operate  under  severe  resource  constraints,  
relying
 
almost
 
exclusively
 
on
 
subjective
 
self-report
 
instruments
 
and
 
verbal
 
interviews
 
that
 
are
 
vulnerable
 
to
 
concealment
 
and
 
recall
 
bias.
 
PsyClick
 
is
 
a
 
prototype
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support
 
system
 
that
 
addresses
 
this
 
gap
 
by
 
passively
 
capturing
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics
 
during
 
a
 
structured
 
10–15
 
minute
 
in-session
 
task
 
sequence
 
and
 
applying
 
a
 
multi-stage
 
statistical
 
pipeline
 
to
 
detect
 
psychomotor
 
agitation
 
and
 
slowing.
 
The
 
system
 
computes
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
multivariate
 
anomaly
 
statistic
 
against
 
a
 
within-session
 
Exponentially
 
Weighted
 
Moving
 
Average
 
(EWMA)
 
baseline
 
built
 
from
 
the
 
participant’s
 
own
 
calibration-phase
 
behavior,
 
decomposed
 
into
 
the
 
Psychomotor
 
Slowing
 
Index
 
(PSI)
 
and
 
Psychomotor
 
Agitation
 
Index
 
(PAI)
 
via
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
Analysis,
 
and
 
classified
 
into
 
Green,
 
Amber,
 
or
 
Red
 
clinical
 
flags
 
through
 
a
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classifier
 
and
 
Heuristic
 
Decision
 
Tree.
 
The
 
system
 
was
 
evaluated
 
on
 
105
 
normative
 
adult
 
participants
 
and
 
15
 
clinical
 
sessions.
 
Domain-segmented
 
T²
 
profiling
 
confirmed
 
threshold-exceedance
 
rates
 
of
 
88.6–90.5%
 
across
 
four
 
stressor
 
domains,
 
with
 
a
 
systematic
 
dose-response
 
trajectory
 
across
 
three
 
task
 
difficulty
 
levels.
 
Cohen’s
 
kappa
 
between
 
system
 
flags
 
and
 
independent
 
clinician
 
observational
 
ratings
 
was
 
κ = 0.466
 
(Moderate
 
Agreement),
 
indicating
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
detects
 
behavioral
 
signals
 
that
 
complement
 
clinician
 
observation.
 
Expert
 
evaluation
 
under
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010
 
yielded
 
an
 
overall
 
mean
 
rating
 
of
 
4.54/5.00
 
(Excellent),
 
confirming
 
technical
 
readiness
 
across
 
Functional
 
Suitability,
 
Performance
 
Efficiency,
 
Usability,
 
Reliability,
 
and
 
Security
 
dimensions.
 
Correlations
 
between
 
psychomotor
 
indices
 
and
 
PHQ-9/GAD-7
 
scores
 
were
 
near-zero
 
(T²–PHQ-9:
 
r
 
=
 
−0.024;
 
PSI–PHQ-9:
 
r
 
=
 
0.058;
 
PAI–GAD-7:
 
r
 
=
 
−0.041;
 
all
 
p
 
>
 
.10),
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
ipsative
 
within-session
 
design
 
of
 
T²,
 
wherein
 
each
 
score
 
measures
 
acute
 
within-session
 
deviation
 
from
 
the
 
patient’s
 
own
 
baseline
 
rather
 
than
 
trait-level
 
symptom
 
severity.
 
PsyClick
 
provides
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals
 
with
 
an
 
objective,
 
privacy-preserving
 
behavioral
 
augmentation
 
tool
 
for
 
in-session
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support,
 
and
 
establishes
 
a
 
replicable
 
methodological
 
foundation
 
for
 
future
 
validation
 
using
 
longitudinally
 
matched
 
designs.
 
 
 
 
 
Keywords:  psychomotor  assessment,  keystroke  dynamics,  clinical  decision  
support,
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²,
 
EWMA,
 
digital
 
phenotyping,
 
mental
 
health
 
informatics,
 
convergent
 
validity
 
 


=== PAGE 16 ===
 
 
Chapter  1  
 
INTRODUCTION  
 
A  sharp  contrast  between  legislative  intent  and  clinical  reality  characterizes  the  
mental
 
health
 
environment
 
in
 
the
 
Philippines.
 
While
 
frameworks
 
like
 
the
 
Philippine
 
Mental
 
Health
 
Act
 
establish
 
a
 
legal
 
mandate
 
for
 
comprehensive
 
care,
 
the
 
actual
 
delivery
 
of
 
services
 
is
 
often
 
compromised
 
by
 
systematic
 
obstacles
 
common
 
in
 
developing
 
nations.
 
These
 
include
 
prohibitive
 
operating
 
costs,
 
a
 
critical
 
shortage
 
of
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals,
 
with
 
ratios
 
as
 
low
 
as
 
2
 
to
 
3
 
workers
 
per
 
100,000
 
population,
 
and
 
the
 
ubiquitous
 
influence
 
of
 
cultural
 
stigma
 
that
 
discourages
 
help-seeking
 
behavior
 
(Alibudbud,
 
2024).
 
Within
 
this
 
constrained
 
landscape,
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals
 
such
 
as
 
licensed
 
psychologists,
 
psychiatrists,
 
and
 
clinical
 
counselors
 
must
 
conduct
 
high-stakes
 
assessments
 
with
 
limited
 
time
 
and
 
even
 
more
 
limited
 
objective
 
data.
 
Current
 
clinical
 
assessment
 
methods
 
remain
 
heavily
 
reliant
 
on
 
subjective
 
self-report
 
instruments
 
and
 
verbal
 
interviews,
 
both
 
of
 
which
 
are
 
vulnerable
 
to
 
concealment,
 
recall
 
bias,
 
and
 
self-stigma.
 
This
 
creates
 
a
 
significant
 
clinical
 
"blind
 
spot":
 
adults
 
can
 
easily
 
mask
 
their
 
distress
 
during
 
in-session
 
interactions,
 
effectively
 
concealing
 
their
 
condition
 
from
 
clinicians
 
who
 
lack
 
access
 
to
 
real-time,
 
objective
 
behavioral
 
data.
 
This
 
gap
 
is
 
critical
 
because,
 
at
 
present,
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals
 
possess
 
no
 
readily
 
available
 
in-session
 
tools
 
to
 
quantify
 
the
 
subtle
 
psychomotor
 
variations.
 
These
 
changes
 
in
 
motor
 
rhythm,
 
typing
 
latency,
 
and
 
cursor
 
behavior
 
often
 
manifest
 
involuntarily
 
even
 
when
 
verbal
 
disclosure
 
is
 
suppressed.
 
1  

=== PAGE 17 ===
 
Psychomotor  dysregulation,  manifesting  as  either  agitation  (erratic,  impulsive  
movements)
 
or
 
retardation
 
(slowing
 
of
 
fine
 
motor
 
activity),
 
serves
 
as
 
a
 
potent
 
behavioral
 
signal
 
of
 
underlying
 
cognitive
 
and
 
emotional
 
disturbance.
 
It
 
is,
 
in
 
essence,
 
the
 
"body
 
language"
 
of
 
internal
 
mental
 
anguish.
 
Studies
 
showed
 
that
 
these
 
motor
 
abnormalities
 
directly
 
impaired
 
an
 
individual's
 
interaction
 
with
 
their
 
environment
 
and
 
often
 
worsened
 
if
 
left
 
undetected
 
(Damme
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024;
 
Wüthrich
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024).
 
Crucially,
 
in
 
the
 
digital
 
epoch,
 
these
 
manifestations
 
transfer
 
into
 
the
 
virtual
 
domain;
 
minute
 
differences
 
in
 
keystroke
 
rhythm,
 
flight
 
time,
 
and
 
cursor
 
trajectory
 
can
 
serve
 
as
 
quantifiable
 
"digital
 
biomarkers"
 
(Acien
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022).
 
This
 
study
 
introduces
 
PsyClick,
 
a
 
clinical
 
decision-support
 
system
 
designed
 
to
 
bridge
 
this
 
gap.
 
Through
 
leveraging
 
high-resolution
 
input
 
logging
 
to
 
detect
 
these
 
"invisible"
 
psychomotor
 
anomalies,
 
PsyClick
 
provides
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals
 
with
 
a
 
quantifiable,
 
data-driven
 
basis
 
for
 
early
 
intervention,
 
moving
 
assessment
 
beyond
 
subjective
 
disclosure
 
toward
 
objective
 
behavioral
 
phenotyping.
 
 
1.1.  Background  of  the  Study  
Mental  health  assessment  in  educational  institutions  presents  significant  challenges,  
especially
 
among
 
adults
 
who
 
frequently
 
experience
 
high
 
levels
 
of
 
psychological
 
distress
 
but
 
often
 
postpone
 
or
 
avoid
 
seeking
 
assistance.
 
Traditional
 
counseling
 
methods
 
depend
 
primarily
 
on
 
verbal
 
self-disclosure
 
and
 
clinician
 
observation,
 
both
 
of
 
which
 
are
 
vulnerable
 
to
 
concealment,
 
recall
 
bias,
 
and
 
self-stigma.
 
Empirical
 
evidence
 
indicates
 
that
 
many
 
adults
 
are
 
either
 
unable
 
or
 
unwilling
 
to
 
accurately
 
communicate
 
their
 
internal
 
mental
 
states,
 
leading
 
to
 
under-detection
 
of
 
clinically
 
relevant
 
distress
 
and
 
missed
 
opportunities
 
for
 
early
 
intervention
 
(Tuason
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022).
 
Consequently,
 
there
 
is
 
an
 
increasing
 
demand
 
for
 
complementary
 
2  

=== PAGE 18 ===
 
assessment  methods  that  utilize  inherently  non-invasive,  passive  data  collection  to  provide  
objective
 
indicators
 
of
 
mental
 
and
 
cognitive
 
states
 
without
 
interrupting
 
the
 
user's
 
natural
 
workflow
 
(Subash
 
et
 
al.,
 
2025).
 
Current  mental  health  assessments  primarily  employ  structured  interviews  and  
standardized
 
self-report
 
instruments,
 
such
 
as
 
the
 
Patient
 
Health
 
Questionnaire-9
 
(PHQ-9)
 
and
 
Generalized
 
Anxiety
 
Disorder-7
 
(GAD-7).
 
Although
 
these
 
tools
 
are
 
psychometrically
 
validated
 
and
 
clinically
 
valuable,
 
they
 
are
 
inherently
 
subjective,
 
capturing
 
only
 
what
 
an
 
adult
 
is
 
able
 
or
 
willing
 
to
 
report
 
at
 
a
 
given
 
time
 
(Negeri
 
et
 
al.,
 
2021).
 
These
 
limitations
 
are
 
exacerbated
 
in
 
resource-constrained
 
environments,
 
including
 
the
 
Philippine
 
context,
 
where
 
a
 
shortage
 
of
 
trained
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals
 
limits
 
the
 
depth
 
and
 
frequency
 
of
 
assessment
 
(Billote
 
&
 
Ponce,
 
2020;
 
Lally
 
et
 
al.,
 
2020).
 
As
 
a
 
result,
 
counselors
 
frequently
 
lack
 
objective
 
behavioral
 
evidence
 
to
 
support,
 
triangulate,
 
or
 
contextualize
 
their
 
clinical
 
judgments.
 
Psychomotor  theory  provides  a  scientifically  grounded  approach  to  addressing  this  
gap.
 
Extensive
 
research
 
demonstrates
 
that
 
mental
 
states
 
such
 
as
 
depression,
 
anxiety,
 
and
 
cognitive
 
overload
 
are
 
reflected
 
in
 
measurable
 
changes
 
in
 
motor
 
behavior.
 
Depression
 
is
 
frequently
 
associated
 
with
 
psychomotor
 
slowing,
 
evidenced
 
by
 
prolonged
 
reaction
 
times
 
and
 
reduced
 
movement
 
velocity.
 
In
 
contrast,
 
anxiety
 
and
 
heightened
 
arousal
 
are
 
linked
 
to
 
psychomotor
 
agitation,
 
which
 
is
 
characterized
 
by
 
increased
 
variability,
 
hesitations,
 
and
 
erratic
 
motion
 
patterns
 
(Elkjaer
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022;
 
Freihaut
 
et
 
al.,
 
2021).
 
Recent
 
advances
 
in
 
Human-Computer
 
Interaction
 
(HCI)
 
research
 
have
 
shown
 
that
 
these
 
psychomotor
 
signatures
 
are
 
also
 
present
 
in
 
fine-grained
 
digital
 
behaviors,
 
such
 
as
 
keystroke
 
dynamics
 
and
 
cursor
 
movements,
 
which
 
serve
 
as
 
reliable
 
digital
 
biomarkers
 
correlated
 
with
 
mental
 
health
 
states
 
(Corcoran
 
et
 
al.,
 
2020;
 
Braund
 
et
 
al.,
 
2023).
 
3  

=== PAGE 19 ===
 
Building  on  this  foundation,  PsyClick  is  informed  by  digital  phenotyping,  an  
emerging
 
approach
 
using
 
devices
 
and
 
sensors
 
to
 
continuously
 
capture
 
behavior
 
and
 
physiological
 
data,
 
which
 
focus
 
on
 
extracting
 
clinically
 
meaningful
 
signals
 
from
 
routine
 
human–computer
 
interactions
 
through
 
computational
 
and
 
statistical
 
methods
 
(Thieme
 
et
 
al.,
 
2020;
 
Bufano
 
et
 
al.,
 
2023).
 
In
 
contrast
 
to
 
systems
 
that
 
depend
 
exclusively
 
on
 
population-level
 
norms,
 
PsyClick
 
incorporates
 
both
 
normative
 
analysis
 
(comparison
 
with
 
a
 
study-derived
 
baseline
 
model)
 
and
 
individualized
 
analysis
 
(within-session
 
and
 
cross-task
 
comparison
 
of
 
an
 
adult’s
 
baseline
 
with
 
performance
 
during
 
cognitively
 
demanding
 
tasks).
 
This
 
dual
 
analytical
 
strategy
 
addresses
 
inter-individual
 
variability
 
in
 
motor
 
behavior,
 
a
 
recognized
 
confound
 
in
 
psychomotor
 
assessment,
 
and
 
enhances
 
the
 
interpretability
 
of
 
results
 
for
 
clinical
 
application.
 
The
 
rationale
 
for
 
this
 
project
 
is
 
to
 
bridge
 
subjective
 
counseling
 
practices
 
and
 
objective
 
behavioral
 
evidence
 
by
 
providing
 
a
 
clinical
 
decision-support
 
system
 
that
 
quantifies
 
psychomotor
 
agitation
 
and
 
slowing
 
during
 
controlled,
 
in-session
 
tasks,
 
without
 
conducting
 
a
 
diagnosis
 
or
 
employing
 
invasive
 
monitoring.
 
Beyond  the  limitations  of  self-report  instruments,  the  specific  psychomotor  
symptoms
 
these
 
tools
 
attempt
 
to
 
measure,
 
namely
 
psychomotor
 
retardation
 
and
 
agitation,
 
also
 
manifest
 
involuntarily
 
through
 
fine
 
motor
 
behavior
 
during
 
computer-based
 
composition
 
tasks.
 
Recent
 
studies
 
in
 
behavioral
 
biometrics
 
and
 
digital
 
phenotyping
 
have
 
demonstrated
 
that
 
open-ended
 
free-text
 
typing
 
tasks,
 
wherein
 
participants
 
compose
 
responses
 
under
 
conditions
 
of
 
varying
 
cognitive
 
and
 
emotional
 
load,
 
generate
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics
 
that
 
reliably
 
reflect
 
psychomotor
 
state
 
(Vizer
 
et
 
al.,
 
2009;
 
Mastoras
 
et
 
al.,
 
2019).
 
Critically,
 
this
 
detection
 
does
 
not
 
require
 
any
 
semantic
 
or
 
linguistic
 
analysis
 
of
 
typed
 
content;
 
the
 
signal
 
resides
 
entirely
 
in
 
the
 
temporal
 
structure
 
of
 
motor
 
behavior.
 
Factors
 
such
 
as
 
the
 
latency
 
4  

=== PAGE 20 ===
 
between  keystrokes,  the  smoothness  of  cursor  trajectories,  and  the  variance  in  typing  rhythm  
serve
 
as
 
involuntary
 
manifestations
 
of
 
the
 
same
 
neural
 
networks
 
underlying
 
psychomotor
 
function
 
(Moussa-Tooks
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024).
 
PsyClick
 
operationalizes
 
this
 
principle
 
by
 
administering
 
graduated
 
microstressor
 
prompts
 
as
 
a
 
stimulus
 
condition
 
for
 
Task
 
3.
 
These
 
prompts
 
consist
 
of
 
ethically
 
validated,
 
low-intensity
 
situational
 
items
 
drawn
 
from
 
the
 
daily
 
hassles
 
and
 
microstressor
 
literature
 
(Kötter
 
et
 
al.,
 
2020).
 
Unlike
 
direct
 
personal
 
questioning,
 
which
 
poses
 
ethical
 
risks
 
of
 
triggering
 
distress
 
responses
 
in
 
vulnerable
 
populations
 
(a
 
concern
 
raised
 
during
 
institutional
 
ethics
 
review),
 
microstressor
 
prompts
 
activate
 
mild
 
cognitive
 
and
 
affective
 
engagement
 
without
 
constituting
 
clinical
 
triggers,
 
while
 
still
 
generating
 
sufficient
 
behavioral
 
signals
 
for
 
multivariate
 
psychomotor
 
analysis.
 
Existing  tools  in  the  digital  phenotyping  and  psychomotor  assessment  literature,  
while
 
scientifically
 
rigorous,
 
exhibit
 
three
 
structural
 
limitations
 
that
 
prevent
 
direct
 
translation
 
to
 
Philippine
 
clinical
 
settings.
 
First,
 
population-level
 
normative
 
databases
 
for
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics
 
have
 
been
 
constructed
 
almost
 
exclusively
 
from
 
Western
 
undergraduate
 
samples
 
(Mastoras
 
et
 
al.,
 
2019;
 
Braund
 
et
 
al.,
 
2023;
 
Prasetyo
 
&
 
Fitri,
 
2025),
 
and
 
no
 
locally
 
validated
 
Filipino
 
psychomotor
 
norms
 
currently
 
exist,
 
rendering
 
cross-population
 
normative
 
comparisons
 
clinically
 
unjustifiable
 
in
 
this
 
context.
 
Second,
 
keystroke-dynamics
 
systems
 
optimized
 
for
 
continuous
 
passive
 
smartphone
 
monitoring
 
(Liu
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024;
 
Fadul
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024)
 
presuppose
 
device
 
ownership,
 
internet
 
connectivity,
 
and
 
weeks
 
of
 
longitudinal
 
data
 
collection,
 
none
 
of
 
which
 
are
 
reliably
 
available
 
in
 
single-session
 
Philippine
 
counseling
 
contexts.
 
Third,
 
prior
 
digital
 
phenotyping
 
research
 
has
 
largely
 
treated
 
clinical
 
classification
 
as
 
a
 
machine-learning
 
problem
 
(Heckler
 
et
 
al.,
 
2023),
 
producing
 
black-box
 
outputs
 
that
 
cannot
 
satisfy
 
the
 
transparency
 
requirements
 
of
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support.
 
PsyClick
 
addresses
 
5  

=== PAGE 21 ===
 
all  three  gaps  by  adopting  an  ipsative  within-session  baseline  that  requires  no  population  
norms,
 
a
 
structured
 
10-15
 
minute
 
in-session
 
protocol
 
compatible
 
with
 
existing
 
clinical
 
workflows,
 
and
 
a
 
fully
 
auditable
 
White
 
Box
 
pipeline
 
from
 
raw
 
input
 
to
 
clinical
 
flag.
 
1.2.   Theoretical  Framework  
Psychomotor-Cognitive  Link  
Fritze  et  al.  (2025)  describe  the  Psychomotor-Cognitive  Link  using  a  trans-network  
analysis
 
of
 
mental
 
health
 
symptoms,
 
sensorimotor
 
functioning,
 
and
 
cognitive
 
performance.
 
The
 
results
 
of
 
this
 
study
 
suggest
 
that
 
sensorimotor
 
problems
 
are
 
more
 
than
 
just
 
somatic
 
complaints;
 
they
 
are
 
inextricably
 
connected
 
to
 
cognitive
 
and
 
global
 
functioning
 
in
 
mental
 
health.
 
In
 
other
 
words,
 
the
 
authors
 
have
 
demonstrated
 
that
 
problems
 
with
 
motor
 
control
 
(sensorimotor
 
problems)
 
are
 
statistically
 
related
 
to
 
the
 
presence
 
and
 
severity
 
of
 
psychopathological
 
symptoms,
 
suggesting
 
that
 
motor
 
behavior
 
is
 
a
 
valid
 
and
 
useful
 
point
 
of
 
connection
 
in
 
the
 
network
 
of
 
mental
 
suffering.
 
  
In  tandem  with  this  functional  approach,  Moussa-Tooks  et  al.  (2024)  offer  
neurobiological
 
specificity
 
by
 
identifying
 
the
 
unique
 
neural
 
networks
 
responsible
 
for
 
Action
 
(motor
 
execution)
 
versus
 
Cognition
 
(motor
 
planning)
 
in
 
psychomotor
 
functions.
 
Although
 
Fritze
 
et
 
al.
 
highlight
 
the
 
importance
 
of
 
connectivity,
 
Moussa-Tooks
 
et
 
al.
 
highlight
 
the
 
unique
 
neural
 
networks,
 
illustrating
 
that
 
although
 
the
 
networks
 
responsible
 
for
 
“willing
 
an
 
action”
 
and
 
“performing
 
an
 
action”
 
are
 
distinguishable,
 
the
 
integrated
 
function
 
of
 
both
 
is
 
necessary
 
for
 
normative
 
functioning.
 
They
 
argue
 
that
 
clinical
 
manifestations
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
function,
 
such
 
as
 
retardation
 
or
 
agitation,
 
result
 
from
 
specific
 
dysfunctions
 
within
 
these
 
6  

=== PAGE 22 ===
 
distinguishable  yet  interrelated  networks,  thus  supporting  the  idea  that  observed  motor  
delays
 
are
 
often
 
the
 
result
 
of
 
dysfunctional
 
cognitive
 
control
 
networks.
 
  
This  theoretical  framework  provides  the  biological  foundation  for  the  study,  
supporting
 
the
 
independent
 
variable
 
that
 
internal
 
mental
 
distress
 
in
 
adults
 
manifests
 
as
 
specific
 
sensorimotor
 
alterations
 
measurable
 
through
 
hardware-abstracted
 
methods.
 
The
 
transdiagnostic
 
findings
 
of
 
Fritze
 
et
 
al.
 
(2025)
 
reinforce
 
PsyClick’s
 
strategy
 
of
 
employing
 
motor
 
dynamics
 
to
 
identify
 
distress
 
markers
 
that
 
are
 
common
 
across
 
various
 
conditions,
 
such
 
as
 
depression
 
and
 
anxiety,
 
rather
 
than
 
restricting
 
detection
 
to
 
a
 
single
 
diagnosis.
 
Additionally,
 
the
 
distinction
 
between
 
action
 
and
 
cognition
 
networks
 
validates
 
the
 
system’s
 
methodological
 
separation
 
of
 
eight
 
clinical
 
biomarkers
 
into
 
two
 
functional
 
clusters.
 
Keystroke
 
features,
 
including
 
flight
 
time,
 
dwell
 
time,
 
typing
 
velocity,
 
and
 
error
 
rate,
 
primarily
 
capture
 
the
 
cognitive
 
planning
 
network.
 
In
 
contrast,
 
cursor
 
features
 
such
 
as
 
path
 
entropy,
 
cursor
 
velocity,
 
jerk,
 
and
 
pause
 
frequency
 
primarily
 
capture
 
the
 
action
 
execution
 
network.
 
All
 
eight
 
metrics
 
are
 
normalized
 
through
 
hardware
 
abstraction
 
to
 
ensure
 
device-agnostic
 
clinical
 
validity,
 
and
 
their
 
simultaneous
 
evaluation
 
through
 
a
 
multivariate
 
statistical
 
model
 
reflects
 
the
 
integrated,
 
network-level
 
nature
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
function
 
described
 
by
 
Moussa-Tooks
 
et
 
al.
 
  
Behavioral  Biometrics  and  Digital  Phenotyping  
The  integration  of  digital  phenotyping  and  behavioral  biometrics  provides  the  
theoretical
 
basis
 
for
 
the
 
system’s
 
hardware-abstracted
 
data-collection
 
methodology.
 
Subash
 
et
 
al.
 
(2025)
 
characterize
 
behavioral
 
biometrics,
 
particularly
 
keystroke
 
and
 
mouse
 
dynamics,
 
7  

=== PAGE 23 ===
 
as  “idiosyncratic”  kinematic  patterns.  In  contrast  to  static  physiological  biometrics,  such  as  
fingerprints,
 
behavioral
 
biometrics
 
are
 
dynamic
 
and
 
reflect
 
the
 
user’s
 
current
 
neurophysiological
 
state.
 
Their
 
survey
 
indicates
 
that
 
metrics
 
including
 
flight
 
time
 
(latency),
 
dwell
 
time
 
(press
 
duration),
 
and
 
cursor
 
trajectory
 
are
 
sufficiently
 
sensitive
 
to
 
detect
 
changes
 
resulting
 
from
 
fatigue,
 
stress,
 
or
 
cognitive
 
decline,
 
while
 
remaining
 
stable
 
enough
 
to
 
identify
 
users
 
uniquely.
 
  
A  significant  limitation  identified  by  Subash  et  al.  (2025)  is  hardware  dependency:  
raw
 
interaction
 
metrics
 
vary
 
considerably
 
across
 
input
 
devices
 
due
 
to
 
differences
 
in
 
keyboard
 
mechanisms,
 
mouse
 
sensor
 
technology,
 
and
 
polling
 
rates.
 
This
 
technical
 
challenge
 
has
 
led
 
to
 
the
 
implementation
 
of
 
a
 
Hardware
 
Abstraction
 
Layer
 
(HAL)
 
in
 
PsyClick,
 
which
 
standardizes
 
device-specific
 
timing
 
values
 
through
 
jitter
 
compensation,
 
polling-rate
 
normalization,
 
and
 
device-classification
 
algorithms.
 
By
 
removing
 
hardware
 
variance
 
as
 
a
 
confounding
 
factor,
 
the
 
system
 
enables
 
“continuous,
 
passive
 
collection
 
of
 
data
 
without
 
interrupting
 
the
 
user’s
 
natural
 
workflow,”
 
providing
 
a
 
high-resolution
 
temporal
 
perspective
 
on
 
motor
 
control
 
that
 
is
 
both
 
hardware-agnostic
 
and
 
clinically
 
reliable.
 
  
This  methodology  aligns  with  the  broader  concept  of  digital  phenotyping,  as  defined  
by
 
Bufano
 
et
 
al.
 
(2023)
 
as
 
the
 
“moment-by-moment
 
quantification
 
of
 
the
 
individual-level
 
human
 
phenotype
 
in
 
situ”
 
using
 
personal
 
digital
 
devices.
 
Their
 
systematic
 
review
 
suggests
 
that
 
human-computer
 
interaction
 
(HCI)
 
logs
 
function
 
as
 
an
 
ecological
 
extension
 
of
 
the
 
central
 
nervous
 
system.
 
Consequently,
 
behavioral
 
“anomalies”
 
in
 
these
 
logs,
 
such
 
as
 
irregular
 
cursor
 
movements
 
or
 
sudden
 
decreases
 
in
 
typing
 
speed,
 
serve
 
as
 
reliable
 
digital
 
8  

=== PAGE 24 ===
 
biomarkers  that  can  accurately  predict  mental  health  episodes,  including  schizophrenia  
relapses
 
or
 
the
 
onset
 
of
 
depressive
 
episodes.
 
Digital
 
phenotyping
 
bridges
 
the
 
gap
 
between
 
observable
 
external
 
behavior
 
and
 
internal
 
clinical
 
states
 
by
 
shifting
 
assessment
 
from
 
subjective
 
self-report
 
to
 
objective
 
behavioral
 
monitoring.
 
  
This  theoretical  framework  supports  the  transformation  of  standard  peripherals,  such  
as
 
keyboards
 
and
 
mice,
 
into
 
clinical
 
sensors.
 
It
 
demonstrates
 
that
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
biometrics,
 
when
 
adequately
 
abstracted
 
from
 
hardware
 
variability,
 
serve
 
as
 
reliable
 
proxies
 
for
 
internal
 
psychomotor
 
states.
 
The
 
unobtrusive
 
design
 
is
 
supported
 
by
 
scientific
 
literature
 
indicating
 
that
 
passive
 
interaction
 
logs,
 
when
 
standardized
 
through
 
hardware
 
abstraction,
 
yield
 
objective
 
measurements
 
that
 
minimize
 
the
 
subjectivity
 
associated
 
with
 
traditional
 
self-reported
 
assessments.
 
9  

=== PAGE 25 ===
 
 
Figure  1.  Taxonomy  of  Biometric  Authentication  and  Research  Focus.  (Adaptability  of  current  keystroke  and  mouse  behavioral  biometric  systems:  A  survey  by  
Subash
 
et
 
al.
 
(2025))
   
The  framework  outlines  the  theoretical  positioning  of  the  study's  variables  and  is  
based
 
on
 
Subash
 
et
 
al.
 
(2025).
 
Unlike
 
static
 
physiological
 
characteristics,
 
Keystroke
 
Dynamics,
 
and
 
Mouse
 
Dynamics
 
are
 
classified
 
within
 
the
 
"Behavioral
 
Biometrics"
 
domain.
 
This
 
categorization
 
substantiates
 
the
 
hypothesis
 
that
 
dynamic
 
behavioral
 
patterns,
 
when
 
10  


=== PAGE 26 ===
 
normalized  via  hardware  abstraction,  can  be  leveraged  to  quantify  psychomotor  disturbances  
non-invasively.
 
Furthermore,
 
the
 
figure
 
highlights
 
"Adaptability"
 
and
 
"Features
 
Extracted"
 
as
 
principal
 
areas
 
of
 
emphasis,
 
reinforcing
 
the
 
methodological
 
focus
 
on
 
extracting
 
specific
 
kinematic
 
features,
 
including
 
latency
 
and
 
jitter,
 
to
 
adaptively
 
monitor
 
changes
 
in
 
user
 
behavior
 
over
 
time—a
 
process
 
that
 
requires
 
hardware-agnostic
 
measurement
 
to
 
maintain
 
clinical
 
validity
 
across
 
diverse
 
computing
 
environments.
 
  
Ipsative  and  Normative  Assessment  
The  statistical  reasoning  supporting  this  study  is  based  on  the  fundamental  distinction  
between
 
Normative
 
Assessment
 
and
 
Ipsative
 
Assessment.
 
According
 
to
 
Care
 
Advantage
 
(2021),
 
Normative
 
Assessment
 
evaluates
 
an
 
individual’s
 
traits
 
by
 
comparing
 
them
 
to
 
group
 
data,
 
employing
 
a
 
bell
 
curve
 
to
 
rank
 
performance
 
relative
 
to
 
a
 
population
 
standard.
 
Although
 
Rhodes
 
(2025)
 
recognizes
 
the
 
utility
 
of
 
this
 
method
 
for
 
ranking
 
or
 
recruitment,
 
he
 
argues
 
that
 
normative
 
benchmarks
 
are
 
inadequate
 
for
 
longitudinal
 
monitoring
 
because
 
they
 
do
 
not
 
measure
 
the
 
specific
 
magnitude
 
of
 
growth
 
or
 
decline,
 
or
 
the
 
“distance
 
travelled,”
 
that
 
an
 
individual
 
experiences
 
over
 
time.
 
  
In  contrast,  Care  Advantage  (2021)  defines  Ipsative  Assessment  as  a  method  that  
uses
 
an
 
individual’s
 
historical
 
performance
 
as
 
the
 
benchmark,
 
comparing
 
current
 
performance
 
to
 
previous
 
results.
 
This
 
approach
 
is
 
particularly
 
valuable
 
in
 
development
 
and
 
coaching
 
contexts,
 
where
 
tracking
 
individual
 
progress
 
is
 
essential.
 
Martínez
 
and
 
Salgado’s
 
(2021)
 
meta-analysis
 
demonstrates
 
that
 
Ipsative
 
(Forced-Choice)
 
methods
 
are
 
more
 
effective
 
than
 
normative
 
scales
 
at
 
preventing
 
faking.
 
These
 
findings
 
indicate
 
that
 
self-referential
 
11  

=== PAGE 27 ===
 
systems  are  less  vulnerable  to  social  desirability  bias,  making  them  a  more  reliable  means  of  
collecting
 
authentic
 
behavioral
 
data.
 
  
This  theoretical  distinction  is  essential  for  addressing  the  technical  challenge  of  
“User
 
Behavior
 
Evolution”
 
identified
 
by
 
Subash
 
et
 
al.
 
(2025).
 
Their
 
research
 
demonstrates
 
that
 
keystroke
 
and
 
mouse
 
dynamics
 
are
 
variable,
 
influenced
 
by
 
factors
 
such
 
as
 
fatigue,
 
stress,
 
and
 
familiarity.
 
A
 
purely
 
normative
 
system
 
may
 
fail
 
to
 
detect
 
a
 
“fast
 
typist”
 
who
 
has
 
slowed
 
to
 
“average
 
speed”
 
due
 
to
 
depression,
 
as
 
their
 
performance
 
would
 
still
 
conform
 
to
 
population
 
norms.
 
PsyClick
 
addresses
 
this
 
through
 
a
 
strictly
 
ipsative,
 
single-session
 
design:
 
during
 
the
 
Calibration
 
Phase,
 
a
 
Within-Session
 
Exponentially
 
Weighted
 
Moving
 
Average
 
(EWMA)
 
algorithm
 
builds
 
the
 
user’s
 
personal
 
behavioral
 
baseline
 
(μᵡ
 ᵣᵃ)
 
exclusively
 
from
 
their
 
own
 
early-session
 
keystrokes,
 
weighting
 
more
 
recent
 
behavior
 
higher
 
than
 
initial
 
behavior
 
to
 
account
 
for
 
early-session
 
arousal
 
or
 
anxiety.
 
Because
 
the
 
baseline
 
is
 
computed
 
and
 
consumed
 
within
 
the
 
same
 
session,
 
no
 
cross-session
 
state
 
is
 
stored,
 
and
 
each
 
assessment
 
is
 
self-contained
 
and
 
privacy-preserving.
 
This
 
approach
 
ensures
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
identifies
 
relative
 
psychomotor
 
deviation
 
from
 
the
 
individual’s
 
own
 
within-session
 
norm
 
rather
 
than
 
against
 
population
 
averages,
 
thereby
 
maximizing
 
the
 
sensitivity
 
of
 
disturbance
 
detection
 
for
 
every
 
client
 
regardless
 
of
 
their
 
absolute
 
typing
 
speed.
 
  
Multivariate  Statistical  Anomaly  Detection  
The  decision-support  engine  employs  a  multi-stage  statistical  pipeline  that  
progressively
 
answers
 
three
 
clinical
 
questions:
 
whether
 
a
 
psychomotor
 
anomaly
 
is
 
present,
 
what
 
type
 
of
 
disturbance
 
it
 
represents,
 
and
 
how
 
clinically
 
significant
 
it
 
is.
 
Each
 
stage
 
is
 
12  

=== PAGE 28 ===
 
grounded  in  established  statistical  theory  and  is  designed  to  operate  as  a  fully  transparent,  
white-box
 
system
 
in
 
which
 
every
 
output
 
is
 
traceable
 
to
 
a
 
mathematical
 
rule.
 
  
The  primary  anomaly  detection  algorithm  is  Hotelling’s  T²  statistic,  the  multivariate  
generalization
 
of
 
the
 
univariate
 
t-test
 
first
 
formalized
 
by
 
Hotelling
 
(1931)
 
and
 
subsequently
 
operationalized
 
for
 
process
 
monitoring
 
by
 
Tracy
 
et
 
al.
 
(1992)
 
and
 
Mason
 
and
 
Young
 
(2002).
 
Where
 
a
 
univariate
 
statistical
 
algorithm
 
evaluates
 
each
 
biometric
 
feature
 
independently
 
and
 
therefore
 
cannot
 
detect
 
patterns
 
that
 
emerge
 
from
 
the
 
combination
 
of
 
features,
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
evaluates
 
all
 
eight
 
clinical
 
biomarkers
 
simultaneously
 
as
 
a
 
single
 
multivariate
 
observation.
 
The
 
statistic
 
is
 
expressed
 
as
 
T²
 
=
 
n
 
·
 
(x
 
−
 
μ)
ᵀ
 
·
 
S
⁻
¹
 
·
 
(x
 
−
 
μ),
 
where
 
x
 
is
 
the
 
current
 
eight-feature
 
assessment
 
vector,
 
μ
 
is
 
the
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
mean
 
vector
 
from
 
the
 
calibration
 
phase,
 
S
⁻
¹
 
is
 
the
 
inverse
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
computed
 
via
 
the
 
Ledoit-Wolf
 
shrinkage
 
estimator
 
(Ledoit
 
&
 
Wolf,
 
2004),
 
and
 
n
 
is
 
the
 
number
 
of
 
calibration
 
windows.
 
The
 
Ledoit-Wolf
 
estimator
 
is
 
applied
 
specifically
 
to
 
address
 
matrix
 
singularity
 
under
 
small
 
calibration
 
sample
 
sizes,
 
a
 
common
 
practical
 
constraint
 
in
 
clinical
 
single-session
 
settings.
 
The
 
statistical
 
threshold
 
for
 
the
 
T²
 
score
 
is
 
derived
 
from
 
the
 
F-distribution
 
at
 
α
 
=
 
0.05,
 
producing
 
a
 
threshold
 
that
 
reflects
 
genuine
 
departure
 
from
 
the
 
session
 
baseline
 
with
 
95%
 
confidence.
 
  
The  use  of  Hotelling’s  T²  is  theoretically  motivated  by  the  neurobiological  
architecture
 
described
 
by
 
Moussa-Tooks
 
et
 
al.
 
(2024).
 
Since
 
the
 
cognitive
 
planning
 
and
 
motor
 
execution
 
networks
 
operate
 
in
 
an
 
integrated
 
fashion,
 
psychomotor
 
disturbances
 
rarely
 
manifest
 
in
 
a
 
single
 
isolated
 
feature;
 
instead,
 
they
 
produce
 
correlated
 
shifts
 
across
 
multiple
 
biomarkers
 
simultaneously.
 
A
 
multivariate
 
model
 
that
 
captures
 
inter-feature
 
covariance
 
is
 
13  

=== PAGE 29 ===
 
therefore  a  more  accurate  reflection  of  the  underlying  psychomotor  state  than  a  set  of  
independent
 
univariate
 
thresholds.
 
  
Once  a  T²  anomaly  is  detected,  Feature  Contribution  Analysis  decomposes  the  T²  
score
 
into
 
per-feature
 
contributions
 
using
 
the
 
formula
 
Cᵢ
 
=
 
(xᵢ
 
−
 
μ
ᵢ)
 
×
 
[S
⁻
¹
 
·
 
(x
 
−
 
μ)]
ᵢ
 
(Mason
 
&
 
Young,
 
2002).
 
The
 
contributions
 
are
 
aggregated
 
into
 
two
 
clinically
 
interpretable
 
composite
 
indices.
 
The
 
Psychomotor
 
Slowing
 
Index
 
(PSI)
 
pools
 
contributions
 
from
 
flight
 
time,
 
dwell
 
time,
 
and
 
pause
 
frequency.
 
When
 
elevated,
 
these
 
features
 
are
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
motor
 
retardation
 
and
 
cognitive
 
slowing
 
associated
 
with
 
depressive
 
states.
 
The
 
Psychomotor
 
Agitation
 
Index
 
(PAI)
 
pools
 
contributions
 
from
 
path
 
entropy,
 
jerk,
 
error
 
rate,
 
cursor
 
velocity,
 
and
 
typing
 
velocity.
 
When
 
these
 
features
 
are
 
elevated,
 
they
 
are
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
erratic,
 
impulsive
 
motor
 
patterns
 
associated
 
with
 
anxiety
 
and
 
agitation.
 
This
 
decomposition
 
transforms
 
a
 
single
 
aggregate
 
anomaly
 
score
 
into
 
a
 
clinically
 
directed
 
profile
 
that
 
supports
 
differential
 
characterization
 
of
 
the
 
type
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance,
 
not
 
merely
 
its
 
presence.
 
  
The  final  classification  stage  employs  Fuzzy  Logic  inference,  a  method  introduced  by  
Zadeh
 
(1965)
 
and
 
subsequently
 
applied
 
to
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support
 
systems
 
by
 
Phuong
 
and
 
Thang
 
(2007)
 
and
 
Warner
 
et
 
al.
 
(2013).
 
Standard
 
threshold
 
classification
 
is
 
binary:
 
a
 
T²
 
score
 
marginally
 
above
 
the
 
threshold
 
is
 
treated
 
identically
 
to
 
a
 
score
 
far
 
above
 
it,
 
which
 
is
 
clinically
 
unrealistic
 
because
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance
 
exists
 
on
 
a
 
continuum
 
rather
 
than
 
as
 
a
 
discrete
 
categorical
 
state.
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
resolves
 
this
 
limitation
 
by
 
converting
 
the
 
T²
 
score,
 
PSI,
 
and
 
PAI
 
into
 
graded
 
membership
 
degrees
 
across
 
clinical
 
categories:
 
Normal,
 
Borderline,
 
Concerning,
 
and
 
Severe.
 
This
 
process
 
uses
 
triangular
 
and
 
trapezoidal
 
membership
 
functions.
 
14  

=== PAGE 30 ===
 
An  inference  engine  evaluates  a  set  of  transparent  IF-THEN  clinical  rules,  and  the  resulting  
membership
 
degrees
 
are
 
defuzzified
 
via
 
dominant
 
rule
 
confidence
 
scoring.
 
It
 
computes
 
the
 
ratio
 
of
 
the
 
top-activated
 
rule’s
 
strength
 
to
 
the
 
sum
 
of
 
all
 
activated
 
rule
 
strengths
 
to
 
produce
 
a
 
crisp
 
clinical
 
category
 
and
 
a
 
numeric
 
confidence
 
score.
 
A
 
Heuristic
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
maps
 
this
 
output
 
to
 
the
 
final
 
Green,
 
Amber,
 
or
 
Red
 
clinical
 
flag
 
accompanied
 
by
 
a
 
plain-language
 
clinical
 
rationale.
 
  
This  pipeline  architecture  reflects  the  “White  Box”  requirement  of  a  clinical  Decision  
Support
 
System
 
(DSS):
 
every
 
flag
 
produced
 
by
 
PsyClick
 
is
 
traceable
 
through
 
a
 
deterministic,
 
auditable
 
sequence
 
of
 
statistical
 
operations,
 
ranging
 
from
 
EWMA
 
calibration,
 
through
 
T²
 
computation
 
and
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
decomposition,
 
to
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
rule
 
firing
 
and
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
mapping.
 
The
 
result
 
is
 
a
 
system
 
that
 
enables
 
clinical
 
assessment
 
to
 
shift
 
from
 
subjective
 
impression
 
to
 
an
 
objective,
 
multi-dimensional
 
psychomotor
 
profile.
 
Rather
 
than
 
stating
 
simply
 
that
 
“the
 
patient
 
appears
 
slow,”
 
the
 
system
 
provides
 
a
 
specific
 
clinical
 
profile:
 
“the
 
patient’s
 
T²
 
score
 
of
 
24.5
 
exceeds
 
the
 
session
 
threshold
 
of
 
16.2
 
(p
 
<
 
.05),
 
with
 
dominant
 
contribution
 
from
 
PSI
 
indices
 
consistent
 
with
 
psychomotor
 
retardation,
 
classified
 
as
 
Psychomotor
 
Retardation
 
at
 
76%
 
fuzzy
 
confidence,
 
warranting
 
clinical
 
attention.”
 
  
The  detection  pipeline  is  structured  sequentially  across  four  distinct  analytical  stages.  
First,
 
the
 
EWMA
 
Baseline
 
stage
 
establishes
 
the
 
within-session
 
personal
 
norm
 
through
 
the
 
adaptive
 
exponential
 
weighting
 
of
 
calibration
 
keystrokes.
 
Second,
 
the
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
stage
 
evaluates
 
the
 
assessment-phase
 
feature
 
vector
 
against
 
this
 
baseline
 
as
 
a
 
multivariate
 
observation,
 
producing
 
a
 
statistically
 
justified
 
anomaly
 
score.
 
Third,
 
the
 
Feature
 
15  

=== PAGE 31 ===
 
Contribution  stage  decomposes  the  T²  score  into  PSI  and  PAI  indices  to  characterize  the  type  
and
 
direction
 
of
 
the
 
disturbance.
 
Finally,
 
the
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
and
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
stage
 
translates
 
this
 
numerical
 
profile
 
into
 
a
 
graded
 
clinical
 
classification
 
and
 
a
 
clinician-readable
 
flag.
 
This
 
multi-stage
 
architecture
 
operationalizes
 
the
 
continuum
 
model
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance,
 
which
 
is
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
transdiagnostic
 
neurobiological
 
framework
 
established
 
by
 
Fritze
 
et
 
al.
 
(2025)
 
and
 
Moussa-Tooks
 
et
 
al.
 
(2024).
 
 
1.3.  Conceptual  Framework  
The  conceptual  framework  for  PsyClick  serves  as  a  dual-layered  research  and  
development
 
pipeline
 
that
 
transforms
 
short,
 
in-session
 
digital
 
interactions
 
into
 
explainable
 
psychomotor
 
indicators
 
for
 
clinical
 
use.
 
Essentially,
 
it
 
is
 
rooted
 
in
 
the
 
Psychomotor-Cognitive
 
Link,
 
which
 
posits
 
that
 
internal
 
mental
 
distress
 
can
 
physically
 
manifest
 
through
 
changes
 
in
 
motor
 
control
 
and
 
planning.
 
This
 
framework
 
is
 
structured
 
to
 
bridge
 
the
 
gap
 
between
 
subjective
 
counseling
 
observations
 
and
 
objective
 
behavioral
 
evidence
 
by
 
utilizing
 
two
 
complementary
 
Input-Process-Output
 
models:
 
a
 
Development
 
IPO,
 
which
 
documents
 
the
 
project’s
 
lifecycle,
 
and
 
a
 
System
 
IPO,
 
which
 
details
 
the
 
operational
 
runtime
 
logic
 
used
 
during
 
counseling
 
sessions.
 
16  

=== PAGE 32 ===
 
 
Figure  2.  Development  IPO  
The  figure  above  serves  as  the  blueprint  for  this  project’s  creation,  ensuring  that  the  
final
 
tool
 
is
 
grounded
 
in
 
established
 
scientific
 
literature
 
and
 
ethical
 
standards
 
shown
 
in
 
the
 
Theoretical
 
Framework.
 
 
 
Development  Inputs:  The  foundation  of  the  study  is  built  upon  Psychomotor  Theory  
and
 
Digital
 
Phenotyping,
 
which
 
validate
 
the
 
use
 
of
 
sensorimotor
 
functioning
 
as
 
a
 
proxy
 
for
 
mental
 
health.
 
These
 
inputs
 
are
 
augmented
 
by
 
stakeholder
 
requirements
 
from
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals
 
and
 
adults,
 
alongside
 
strict
 
data
 
privacy
 
policies
 
and
 
technical
 
hardware
 
specifications.
 
  
Development  Processes:  These  inputs  are  used  to  design  controlled  counseling  tasks,  
such
 
as
 
guided
 
text
 
copying
 
and
 
free-text
 
microstressor
 
responses.
 
They
 
are
 
intended
 
to
 
elicit
 
specific
 
psychomotor
 
behaviors.
 
A
 
“Pilot
 
and
 
Iteration”
 
phase
 
(where
 
the
 
researchers
 
target
 
17  


=== PAGE 33 ===
 
n  >=  100)  is  used  to  refine  the  computational  feature  library  and  establish  the  statistical  
weights
 
for
 
the
 
detection
 
engine
 
(our
 
baseline).
 
  
Development  Outputs:  The  primary  output  is  the  PsyClick  Prototype,  accompanied  
by
 
validated
 
baseline
 
statistical
 
models
 
and
 
clinician
 
interpretation
 
guides.
 
These
 
artifacts
 
enable
 
the
 
system
 
to
 
function
 
as
 
a
 
“White
 
Box”
 
decision-support
 
tool,
 
with
 
every
 
flag
 
backed
 
by
 
a
 
reproducible
 
methodology.
 
  
Going  through  the  nodes,  the  development  flow  begins  with  ethics  and  policy  
requirements,
 
ensuring
 
informed
 
consent,
 
local-only
 
data
 
processing,
 
and
 
strict
 
data
 
minimization.
 
Knowledge
 
prerequisites
 
include
 
psychomotor-cognitive
 
theory,
 
digital
 
phenotyping
 
studies,
 
behavioral
 
biometrics
 
literature,
 
and
 
statistical
 
anomaly
‑
detection
 
methods
 
like
 
Hotelling’s
 
T
2
.
 
Software
 
needs
 
include
 
a
 
high
‑
resolution
 
event
 
logger,
 
a
 
feature-extraction
 
library,
 
a
 
local
 
analytics
 
engine,
 
a
 
clinician
 
UI,
 
a
 
secure
 
local
 
datastore,
 
and
 
a
 
test
 
harness.
 
Hardware
 
requirements
 
specify
 
adequate
 
keyboard
 
and
 
mouse
 
sampling
 
rates,
 
screen-resolution
 
standards,
 
device-metadata
 
capture,
 
and
 
the
 
use
 
of
 
test
 
devices
 
for
 
variance
 
analysis.
 
System
 
design
 
then
 
formalizes
 
architecture,
 
data
 
schemas,
 
feature
 
definitions,
 
baseline
 
model
 
structures,
 
and
 
explainability
 
criteria.
 
  
Implementation  includes  creating  the  event  logger,  feature-extraction  algorithms,  
baseline
‑
computation
 
modules,
 
composite
 
scoring
 
systems,
 
and
 
clinician
‑
report
 
templates.
 
This
 
is
 
followed
 
by
 
testing
 
through
 
unit
 
and
 
integration
 
tests,
 
device-variance
 
analysis,
 
test
 
datasets,
 
and
 
automated
 
suites
 
for
 
reproducibility.
 
A
 
pilot
 
phase
 
(n>=100)
 
supports
 
18  

=== PAGE 34 ===
 
calibration,  weight  tuning,  threshold  refinement,  and  usability  improvements.  Validation  
compares
 
outputs
 
against
 
PHQ
‑
9
 
and
 
GAD
‑
7
 
scores,
 
integrates
 
clinician
 
ratings,
 
and
 
computes
 
sensitivity,
 
specificity,
 
reliability,
 
and
 
device-impact
 
metrics.
 
Documentation
 
and
 
governance
 
close
 
the
 
process
 
with
 
consent
 
forms,
 
audit
 
logs,
 
local
‑
processing
 
rules,
 
and
 
clinician
 
guidance
 
materials.
 
 
Figure  3.  System  IPO  
The  figure  above  shows  the  System  IPO  that  documents  the  live  data  flow  during  a  
counseling
 
session,
 
illustrating
 
how
 
raw
 
kinetic
 
events
 
are
 
translated
 
into
 
clinical
 
markers.
 
  
Session  Inputs:  The  system  captures  raw  hardware  input  in  the  form  of  
high-resolution,
 
timestamped
 
keystroke
 
events
 
(key
 
down/up)
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics
 
(move,
 
click,
 
scroll)
 
during
 
standardized
 
tasks.
 
It
 
also
 
incorporates
 
Self-Report
 
Anchors
 
(PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
scores)
 
and
 
Calibration
 
Data
 
to
 
establish
 
a
 
unique
 
baseline
 
for
 
each
 
adult.
 
  
19  


=== PAGE 35 ===
 
Runtime  Processes:  The  raw  interaction  data  first  passes  through  a  Hardware  
Abstraction
 
Layer
 
(HAL)
 
responsible
 
for
 
jitter
 
compensation
 
and
 
polling
 
rate
 
normalization.
 
Next
 
is
 
the
 
Calibration
 
Phase,
 
which
 
applies
 
a
 
Sliding
 
Window
 
Weighted
 
Moving
 
Average
 
(WMA)
 
to
 
smooth
 
the
 
signal.
 
It
 
then
 
utilizes
 
a
 
Within-Session
 
Exponentially
 
Weighted
 
Moving
 
Average
 
(EWMA)
 
to
 
build
 
a
 
personal
 
baseline
 
defined
 
by
 
a
 
mean
 
vector
 
(μ)
 
and
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
(S)
 
.
 
  
The  Assessment  Phase  follows.  Feature  Extraction  isolates  8  core  biomarkers  per  
window.
 
The
 
system
 
computes
  
Hotelling’s
 
T2
 
statistic
 
to
 
determine
 
if
 
an
 
anomaly
 
exists,
 
generating
 
a
 
T2
 
score
 
and
 
a
 
p-value.
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
analysis
 
then
 
evaluates
 
what
 
kind
 
of
 
disturbance
 
is
 
occurring,
 
mapping
 
the
 
data
 
to
 
the
 
Psychomotor
 
Slowing
 
Index
 
(PSI)
 
and
 
the
 
Psychomotor
 
Agitation
 
Index
 
(PAI).
 
A
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classifier
 
provides
 
graded
 
membership
 
to
 
determine
 
the
 
certainty
 
of
 
the
 
assessment.
 
Finally,
 
a
 
Heuristic
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
assigns
 
a
 
final
 
Green,
 
Amber,
 
or
 
Red
 
label.
 
  
Session  Outputs:  The  result  is  an  automated  post-session  report  that  includes  the  
Psychomotor
 
Slowing
 
Index
 
(PSI)
 
and
 
the
 
Psychomotor
 
Agitation
 
Index
 
(PAI).
 
These
 
are
 
presented
 
as
 
color-coded
 
flags
 
(Green,
 
Amber,
 
Red)
 
with
 
an
 
explainable
 
rationale
 
through
 
fuzzy
 
logic
 
certainty
 
metrics,
 
providing
 
clinicians
 
with
 
objective
 
digital
 
biomarkers
 
to
 
complement
 
their
 
professional
 
judgment.
 
  
The  Input  Phase  commences  with  the  Participant  Device,  which  functions  as  the  
endpoint
 
(keyboard,
 
mouse,
 
laptop,
 
or
 
desktop)
 
streaming
 
raw
 
interaction
 
events
 
20  

=== PAGE 36 ===
 
accompanied  by  high-resolution  timestamps  and  device  metadata.  This  data  stream  is  
supplemented
 
by
 
Session
 
Metadata,
 
which
 
provides
 
contextual
 
details
 
such
 
as
 
task
 
identifiers,
 
device
 
type,
 
sampling
 
rates,
 
and
 
start/stop
 
markers.
 
To
 
establish
 
a
 
personalized
 
baseline,
 
Calibration
 
Data
 
is
 
collected
 
through
 
brief,
 
standardized
 
tasks.
 
Finally,
 
the
 
system
 
incorporates
 
Self-Report
 
Anchors
 
in
 
the
 
form
 
of
 
validated
 
screening
 
scores
 
(PHQ-9,
 
GAD-7)
 
collected
 
pre
 
or
 
post
 
session,
 
which
 
serve
 
as
 
external
 
validation
 
anchors.
 
  
Next  is  the  Process  Phase.  It  begins  with  the  HAL  executing  jitter  compensation  and  
polling
 
rate
 
normalization
 
to
 
mitigate
 
noise
 
and
 
sampling
 
irregularities
 
from
 
varying
 
hardware.
 
The
 
Calibration
 
Phase
 
follows,
 
utilizing
 
a
 
Sliding
 
Window
 
WMA
 
to
 
smooth
 
the
 
incoming
 
signal.
 
It
 
then
 
applies
 
a
 
Within-Session
 
EWMA
 
to
 
establish
 
a
 
dynamic,
 
personalized
 
baseline
 
for
 
the
 
user.
 
  
The  Assessment  Phase  then  evaluates  the  smoothed  data  through  the  lens  of  8  
primary
 
biomarkers.
 
Feature
 
Extraction
 
isolates
 
four
 
keyboard
 
metrics
 
(Flight
 
Time,
 
Dwell
 
Time,
 
Typing
 
Velocity,
 
and
 
Error
 
Rate)
 
and
 
four
 
mouse
 
kinematics
 
(Path
 
Entropy,
 
Cursor
 
Velocity,
 
Jerk,
 
and
 
Pause
 
Frequency)
 
for
 
each
 
window.
 
  
To  compute  Jerk,  the  system  performs  successive  differentiation  of  the  cursor  
position;
 
it
 
first
 
derives
 
Cursor
 
Velocity,
 
differentiates
 
again
 
to
 
find
 
Acceleration
 
as
 
an
 
intermediate
 
state,
 
and
 
finally
 
differentiates
 
a
 
third
 
time
 
to
 
produce
 
the
 
Jerk
 
biomarker.
 
  
21  

=== PAGE 37 ===
 
To  detect  anomalies,  the  system  calculates  Hotelling’s  T2  by  comparing  these  8  
features
 
against
 
the
 
user’s
 
EWMA
 
baseline,
 
generating
 
a
 
T2
 
score
 
and
 
p-value
 
to
 
statistically
 
quantify
 
if
 
the
 
user’s
 
current
 
behavior
 
is
 
anomalous.
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
analysis
 
identifies
 
the
 
nature
 
of
 
the
 
deviation.
 
For
 
example,
 
increased
 
Flight
 
Time
 
and
 
decreased
 
Cursor
 
Velocity
 
contribute
 
to
 
the
 
Psychomotor
 
Slowing
 
Index
 
(PSI),
 
while
 
increased
 
Jerk
 
and
 
Error
 
Rates
 
contribute
 
to
 
the
 
Psychomotor
 
Agitation
 
Index
 
(PAI).
 
To
 
handle
 
behavioral
 
uncertainty,
 
a
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classifier
 
assigns
 
graded
 
membership
 
to
 
these
 
observations.
 
A
 
Heuristic
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
then
 
processes
 
these
 
fuzzy
 
outputs
 
to
 
generate
 
a
 
final
 
categorical
 
label.
 
  
Lastly,  the  Output  Phase  provides  a  comprehensive  suite  of  data-driven  artifacts  
packaged
 
into
 
a
 
Session
 
Report.
 
This
 
automated
 
PDF
 
includes
 
the
 
numeric
 
PSI
 
and
 
PAI
 
indices,
 
the
 
T2
 
p-values,
 
and
 
the
 
fuzzy
 
logic
 
certainty
 
scores.
 
Each
 
metric
 
is
 
supported
 
by
 
a
 
Feature-Level
 
Breakdown
 
that
 
details
 
raw
 
values
 
for
 
the
 
8
 
biomarkers
 
and
 
their
 
deviations
 
from
 
the
 
baseline.
 
For
 
visual
 
inspection,
 
the
 
system
 
generates
 
plots
 
and
 
charts
 
to
 
contextualize
 
temporal
 
patterns.
 
Clinician
 
attention
 
is
 
guided
 
by
 
the
 
Heuristic
 
Decision
 
Tree’s
 
color-coded
 
flag
 
(Green,
 
Amber,
 
Red),
 
which
 
serves
 
as
 
a
 
single-session
 
triage
 
indicator.
 
The
 
output
 
also
 
includes
 
a
 
concise,
 
non-diagnostic
 
clinician
 
narrative
 
to
 
facilitate
 
clinical
 
review
 
and
 
discussion,
 
alongside
 
a
 
tamper-evident
 
Audit
 
Log.

foundation

analysis

Feature  Taxonomy  and  Statistical  Decision  Logic  
The  "engine"  of  the  PsyClick  framework  relies  on  a  rigorous  taxonomy  of  Behavioral  
Biometrics.
 
By
 
treating
 
keyboard
 
and
 
mouse
 
interactions
 
as
 
an
 
"ecological
 
extension
 
of
 
the
 
central
 
nervous
 
system,"
 
the
 
system
 
extracts
 
specific
 
kinematic
 
markers
 
that
 
are
 
sensitive
 
to
 
stress
 
and
 
cognitive
 
load.
 
  
The  system  prioritizes  8  core  biomarkers  per  sliding  window.  These  are  categorized  
into
 
keyboard
 
dynamics
 
(Flight
 
Time,
 
Dwell
 
Time,
 
Typing
 
Velocity,
 
Error
 
Rate)
 
and
 
mouse
 
kinematics
 
(Path
 
Entropy,
 
Cursor
 
Velocity,
 
Jerk,
 
Pause
 
Frequency).
 
These
 
specific
 
metrics
 
quantify
 
the
 
smoothness,
 
hesitation,
 
or
 
erratic
 
nature
 
of
 
an
 
adult's
 
movements.
 
To
 
determine
 
the
 
clinical
 
significance
 
of
 
these
 
metrics,
 
PsyClick
 
applies
 
multivariate
 
anomaly
 
detection.
 
Rather
 
than
 
evaluating
 
features
 
in
 
isolation,
 
the
 
system
 
uses
  
Hotelling’s
 
T
2
 
statistic
 
to
 
measure
 
how
 
far
 
this
 
specific
 
set
 
of
 
8
 
features
 
deviates
 
from
 
the
 
user's
 
expected
 
baseline,
 
accounting
 
for
 
the
 
covariance
 
between
 
different
 
motor
 
metrics.
 
By
 
focusing
 
on
 
Cursor
 
Velocity
 
(the
 
speed
 
of
 
intent)
 
and
 
Jerk
 
(the
 
smoothness
 
of
 
execution),
 
the
 
model
 
captures
 
the
 
most
 
clinically
 
relevant
 
aspects
 
of
 
motor
 
control.
 
While
 
the
 
pipeline
 
internally
 
computes
 
acceleration
 
to
 
derive
 
jerk,
 
the
 
framework's
 
decision
 
logic
 
evaluates
 
individual
 
feature
 
contributions
 
to
 
map
 
anomalies
 
to
 
specific
 
clinical
 
presentations
 
like
 
slowing
 
or
 
agitation.
 
A
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classifier
 
processes
 
these
 
inputs
 
to
 
handle
 
the
 
inherent
 
uncertainty
 
in
 
human
 
24  

=== PAGE 40 ===
 
behavior,  assigning  graded  membership  rather  than  strict  binary  thresholds.  Finally,  a  
Heuristic
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
interprets
 
these
 
outputs
 
to
 
categorize
 
the
 
session
 
into
 
a
 
final
 
triage
 
state.
 
  
  
Dual-Analytical  Approach:  Ipsative  vs.  Normative  Assessment  
A  critical  component  of  this  framework  is  the  integration  of  Ipsative  
(Self-Referential)
 
Analysis
 
to
 
overcome
 
the
 
limitations
 
of
 
population-level
 
norms.
 
Ipsative
 
Assessment
 
uses
 
the
 
adult’s
 
own
 
historical
 
data
 
as
 
the
 
benchmark,
 
while
 
Normative
 
Assessment
 
compares
 
the
 
adult
 
to
 
the
 
general
 
population.
 
  
By  utilizing  a  Within-Session  EWMA  during  the  calibration  phase,  the  system  
dynamically
 
builds
 
a
 
personal
 
baseline
 
(μ
 
for
 
the
 
average
 
value
 
of
 
the
 
8
 
biomarkers,
 
S
 
for
 
the
 
covariance
 
matrix)
 
for
 
each
 
user.
 
This
 
ensures
 
the
 
system
 
captures
 
"distance
 
travelled"
 
(the
 
magnitude
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
change
 
within
 
the
 
session
 
specific
 
to
 
that
 
individual),
 
making
 
distress
 
detection
 
highly
 
personalized
 
and
 
less
 
susceptible
 
to
 
social
 
desirability
 
bias.
 
For
 
example,
 
a
 
naturally
 
fast
 
typist
 
may
 
slow
 
down
 
due
 
to
 
depression;
 
by
 
comparing
 
their
 
current
 
task-specific
 
metrics
 
against
 
their
 
newly
 
established
 
EWMA
 
baseline,
 
PsyClick
 
identifies
 
significant
 
internal
 
deviations
 
that
 
might
 
otherwise
 
be
 
missed.
 
 
1.4.   Statement  of  the  Problem  
25  

=== PAGE 41 ===
 
Despite  the  growing  mental  health  crisis  in  the  Philippines,  mental  health  professionals  lack  
objective,
 
real-time
 
behavioral
 
metrics
 
that
 
can
 
support
 
the
 
assessment
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
agitation
 
and
 
psychomotor
 
slowing
 
during
 
counseling
 
sessions.
 
Specifically,  this  study  seeks  to  address  the  following  research  questions:  
1.  How  can  a  hardware-abstracted  telemetry  module  be  engineered  to  capture  
synchronized
 
millisecond-level
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
events
 
while
 
minimizing
 
temporal
 
bias
 
from
 
hardware?
 
2.  How  can  a  within-session  EWMA  algorithm  be  implemented  to  construct  a  dynamic,  
personalized
 
behavioral
 
baseline
 
that
 
accounts
 
for
 
early-session
 
variables
 
like
 
initial
 
nervousness?
 
3.  How  can  a  multivariate  Anomaly  Detection  Engine  using  Hotelling’s  T
 2
 and  the  
Ledoit-Wolf
 
estimator
 
be
 
developed
 
to
 
identify
 
psychomotor
 
deviations
 
across
 
eight
 
simultaneous
 
biomarkers?
 
4.  How  can  Feature  Contribution  Analysis  and  a  Fuzzy  Logic  Classifier  be  utilized  to  
characterize
 
disturbances
 
as
 
slowing
 
(PSI)
 
or
 
agitation
 
(PAI),
 
and
 
provide
 
graded
 
clinical
 
outputs
 
supported
 
by
 
domain-segmented
 
T
2
 
analysis
 
across
 
specific
 
stressor
 
groups?
 
5.  What  is  the  convergent  validity  and  overall  software  quality  of  PsyClick  as  evaluated  
through
 
clinical
 
screening
 
instruments
 
(PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7)
 
and
 
established
 
software
 
engineering
 
frameworks
 
(ISO/IEC
 
25010)
 
among
 
participants?
 
 
 
1.5.    Objectives  of  the  Study  
26  

=== PAGE 42 ===
 
The  primary  objective  of  this  study  is  to  develop  a  clinical  decision-support  system  that  
applies
 
multivariate
 
statistical
 
anomaly
 
detection
 
on
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics
 
to
 
identify
 
psychomotor
 
agitation
 
and
 
slowing
 
among
 
participants.
 
The
 
system
 
seeks
 
to
 
enhance
 
standard
 
assessment
 
methods
 
by
 
combining
 
personal
 
baselines
 
with
 
a
 
multi-stage
 
analytical
 
pipeline,
 
thus
 
offering
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals
 
objective
 
digital
 
biomarkers.
 
Specifically,
 
the
 
study
 
aims
 
to:
 
1.  To  develop  a  secure,  hardware-abstracted  data  acquisition  module  that  records  
high-resolution
 
behavioral
 
telemetry
 
while
 
minimizing
 
temporal
 
bias
 
from
 
input
 
devices.
 
2.  To  implement  a  within-session  EWMA  algorithm  that  uses  personalized  calibration  
to
 
construct
 
a
 
dynamic
 
behavioral
 
baseline
 
that
 
accounts
 
for
 
early-session
 
variables.
 
3.  To  construct  an  anomaly  engine  utilizing  Hotelling’s  T
 2
 and  the  Ledoit-Wolf  
estimator
 
to
 
identify
 
psychomotor
 
deviations
 
across
 
eight
 
simultaneous
 
biomarkers.
 
4.  To  utilize  Feature  Contribution  Analysis  and  a  Fuzzy  Logic  Classifier  to  characterize  
disturbances
 
as
 
either
 
slowing
 
(PSI)
 
or
 
agitation
 
(PAI)
 
and
 
provide
 
graded
 
clinical
 
outputs,
 
and
 
enable
 
domain-segmented
 
T²
 
analysis
 
across
 
four
 
stressor
 
groups
 
(Time/Workload,
 
Interpersonal,
 
Academic/Performance,
 
and
 
Self-evaluation)
 
and
 
three
 
difficulty
 
levels
 
(Level
 
A–C)
 
to
 
support
 
domain-specific
 
psychomotor
 
interpretation.
 
5.  To  examine  the  system's  convergent  validity  in  relation  to  recognized  clinical  
instruments
 
(PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7)
 
and
 
evaluate
 
its
 
software
 
quality
 
(Functional
 
Suitability,
 
Performance
 
Efficiency,
 
Usability,
 
Reliability,
 
and
 
Security)
 
using
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010
 
standards
 
and
 
clinician
 
feedback.
 
27  

=== PAGE 43 ===
 
 
1.6.  Scope  and  Delimitations  
This  study  focuses  on  the  design,  development,  and  evaluation  of  PsyClick,  a  
prototype
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support
 
system
 
intended
 
for
 
use
 
in
 
controlled
 
counseling
 
or
 
assessment
 
sessions
 
in
 
clinical
 
environments.
 
The
 
system
 
records
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics
 
as
 
participants
 
complete
 
a
 
predefined
 
sequence
 
of
 
short,
 
standardized
 
tasks
 
lasting
 
approximately
 
10
 
to
 
15
 
minutes.
 
These
 
tasks,
 
which
 
include
 
guided
 
text
 
copying,
 
simple
 
stimulus–response
 
activities,
 
and
 
graduated
 
free-text
 
microstressor
 
composition,
  
are
 
selected
 
to
 
elicit
 
both
 
baseline
 
psychomotor
 
behavior
 
and
 
systematically
 
escalating
 
cognitive-emotional
 
load,
 
enabling
 
within-session
 
comparison
 
of
 
motor
 
dynamics
 
across
 
stimulus
 
difficulty
 
levels.
 
Following
 
each
 
session,
 
the
 
system
 
generates
 
a
 
report
 
detailing
 
the
 
participant’s
 
psychomotor
 
patterns
 
and
 
comparing
 
them
 
to
 
both
 
normative
 
population
 
benchmarks
 
and
 
the
 
participant’s
 
typical
 
performance.
 
The  following  table  details  the  specific  data  points  and  tasks  included  in  the  study:  
Category  Specific  Elements  Included  Rationale  
Monitored  Events  Key  down,  key  up,  mouse  
move,  mouse  click,  and  mouse  
scroll.  
To  capture  high-resolution,  
timestamped  interaction  
data.  
Keystroke  Features  Press  duration,  inter-key  
latency,  typing  speed,  and  
error  rate.  
Established  markers  for  
affective  computing  and  
behavioral  analysis.  
28  

=== PAGE 44 ===
 
Cursor  Features  Velocity,  path  entropy,  click  
hesitation,  and  pause  
frequency.  
Quantifies  motor  latency  
and  psychomotor  agitation.  
Standardized  Tasks  Guided  Text-Copying,  
Simple-Stimulus  Response,  
and  Free-Text  Microstressor  
Response  (four  thematic  
groups:  
Group  1:  Time/Workload;  
Group  2:  Interpersonal;  Group  
3:  Academic/Performance;  
Group  4:  Self-evaluation,  each  
containing  items  across  three  
graduated  difficulty  levels:  
Level  A:  routine  situational,  
Level  B:   mild  
interpersonal/workload,  Level  
C:  cognitive-evaluative;  12  
items  total).  
Designed  to  elicit  baseline  
motor  behavior  (Tasks  1–2)  
and  graduated  
emotional-cognitive  load  
during  open-ended  free-text  
composition  (Task  3),  
enabling  within-session  and  
across-level  psychomotor  
comparison  via  
EWMA-referenced  
Hotelling's  T².  The  
four-group  domain  
structure  additionally  
enables  
stressor-domain-specific  T²  
profiling  and  
clinician-interpretable  
domain  attribution  of  
psychomotor  anomalies.

=== PAGE 45 ===
 
Analysis  Model  Normative :  Project-specific  
baseline  model  
  
Individualized :  Comparison  
of  copying  vs.  active  
composition  
To  quantify  significant  
deviations  from  both  a

Table  2.  Specific  Data  Points  Included  
The  data  analyzed  in  this  study  comprise  time-stamped  keyboard  and  mouse  
interaction
 
logs
 
recorded
 
during
 
each
 
session,
 
as
 
well
 
as
 
self-reported
 
scores
 
from
 
validated
 
screening
 
instruments
 
(PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7).
 
Linguistic
 
or
 
semantic
 
analysis
 
of
 
typed
 
content
 
is
 
not
 
conducted;
 
the
 
analysis
 
is
 
restricted
 
to
 
motor
 
dynamics.
 
Normative
 
models
 
are
 
constructed
 
exclusively
 
from
 
the
 
participant
 
sample
 
and
 
are
 
applied
 
solely
 
for
 
within-study
 
comparisons,
 
not
 
for
 
clinical
 
generalization.
 
Data
 
collection
 
is
 
conducted
 
with
 
informed
 
consent
 
and
 
in
 
compliance
 
with
 
data
 
privacy
 
and
 
ethical
 
research
 
standards.
 
PsyClick  is  designed  as  a  decision-support  augmentation  tool  rather  than  a  diagnostic  
instrument,
 
providing
 
quantitative
 
behavioral
 
markers
 
to
 
inform
 
clinical
 
judgment.
 
This
 
study
 
should
 
be
 
regarded
 
as
 
a
 
pilot
 
clinical
 
validation
 
study
 
and
 
is
 
not
 
yet
 
intended
 
for
 
full
 
clinical
 
diagnostic
 
deployment
 
due
 
to
 
the
 
limited
 
clinical-session
 
sample
 
size.
 
The
 
prototype
 
is
 
hardware-dependent
 
across
 
input
 
devices
 
(standard
 
versus
 
low-resolution
 
keyboards
 
and
 
mice),
 
although
 
intra-session
 
baselines
 
are
 
used
 
to
 
mitigate
 
individual
 
skill
 
differences.
 
Participant
 
recruitment
 
is
 
limited
 
to
 
adults
 
(n
 
>
 
100,
 
aged
 
18+),
 
reflecting
 
documented
 
mental
 
health
 
needs
 
in
 
this
 
demographic
 
(Shibuya
 
et
 
al.,
 
2025).
 
Still,
 
this
 
focus
 
restricts
 
30  

=== PAGE 46 ===
 
generalizability  to  pediatric,  geriatric,  or  non-academic  populations.  Confounding  variables  
such
 
as
 
caffeine
 
intake,
 
acute
 
fatigue,
 
and
 
native
 
typing
 
proficiency
 
are
 
not
 
isolated,
 
but
 
are
 
partially

through

The

operates
 
exclusively
 
in
 
active

contexts,
 
does
 
not

or
 
natural

of
 
content,
 
and
 
provides
 
only
 
post-session

without
 
real-time

All

is
 
conducted
 
locally
 
to
 
ensure
 
privacy
 
compliance,
 
with
 
no
 
cloud
 
integration
 
or
 
longitudinal
 
tracking
 
beyond

1.7.  Significance  of  the  Study  
This  study  is  significant  as  it  introduces  an  objective,  data-driven  approach  to  
supporting
 
mental
 
health
 
assessment
 
within
 
educational
 
settings.
 
The
 
findings
 
and
 
the
 
developmental
 
system,
 
PsyClick,
 
are
 
expected
 
to
 
benefit
 
the
 
following
 
stakeholders:
 
 
Medical  Clinicians  and  Mental  Health  Professionals. .  PsyClick  serves  as  an  advanced  
clinical
 
decision
 
support
 
system
 
(CDSS)
 
that
 
augments
 
standard
 
psychiatric
 
and
 
psychological
 
evaluations.
 
By
 
capturing
 
objective,
 
continuous
 
digital
 
biomarkers
 
via
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics,
 
the
 
system
 
helps
 
clinicians
 
quantify
 
psychomotor
 
retardation
 
or
 
agitation—key
 
diagnostic
 
criteria
 
for
 
depressive
 
disorders.
 
This
 
quantitative
 
data
 
complements
 
traditional
 
clinical
 
interviews
 
and
 
self-report
 
scales,
 
enabling
 
more
 
accurate
 
diagnostic
 
assessments,
 
personalized
 
treatment
 
planning,
 
and
 
the
 
objective
 
tracking
 
of
 
treatment
 
efficacy
 
over
 
time.
 
 
31  

=== PAGE 47 ===
 
Adults.  Adults  undergoing  psychiatric  evaluation  or  psychological  therapy  benefit  from  a  
more
 
comprehensive
 
and
 
objective
 
assessment
 
process.
 
Because
 
depressive
 
symptoms
 
can
 
impair
 
an

ability
 
to
 
accurately
 
self-report
 
their
 
cognitive
 
or

states,
 
this

provides
 
a

to
 
quantify
 
psychomotor
 
symptoms.
 
This
 
facilitates
 
the

of

markers
 
and
 
ensures
 
timely

interventions
 
for
 
adults
 
who
 
may
 
struggle
 
to
 
articulate
 
their
 
distress.
 
 
Healthcare  Institutions  and  Psychiatric  Facilities.  Hospitals,  mental  health  clinics,  and

facilities
 
can
 
integrate
 
this

into
 
their

to

Utilizing

allows

manage
 
clinical

more

and

regarding

of

Mental  Health  and  HCI  Researchers.  This  study  contributes  valuable  empirical  data  to  the  
rapidly

of

By

1.8.   Definition  of  Terms  
Throughout  the  study,  the  words  described  in  this  section  will  be  used  to  make  sure  
that
 
the
 
meaning
 
is
 
clear
 
and
 
consistent.
 
The
 
definitions
 
are
 
derived
 
from
 
the
 
operationalization
 
of
 
concepts
 
in
 
this
 
study
 
and
 
are
 
intended
 
to
 
help
 
the
 
reader
 
understand
 
key
 
technical
 
and
 
conceptual
 
ideas.
 
  
Behavioral  Biometrics  –  Denotes  the  quantifiable  patterns  of  human  interaction,  such  as  
typing
 
and
 
mouse
 
movement
 
rhythms,
 
that
 
are
 
distinctive
 
to
 
an
 
individual
 
and
 
responsive
 
to
 
variations
 
in
 
cognitive
 
or
 
emotional
 
states.
 
This
 
study
 
derives
 
it
 
from
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics
 
recorded
 
during
 
controlled
 
counseling
 
activities.
 
Clinical  Decision  Support  System  –  A  system  that  provides  objective,  data-driven  
measures
 
that
 
enhance
 
judgment
 
clinically,
 
rather
 
than
 
replacing
 
it,
 
to
 
assist
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals.
 
PsyClick
 
functions
 
as
 
this
 
system
 
by
 
presenting
 
quantified
 
psychomotor
 
markers
 
to
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals.
 
Cursor  Dynamics  –  The  measurable  attributes  of  mouse  movement  behavior,  including  
patterns
 
in
 
speed,
 
path
 
efficiency,
 
pauses,
 
and
 
hesitation.
 
This
 
study
 
analyzes
 
them
 
as
 
indicators
 
of
 
motor
 
execution
 
and
 
psychomotor
 
agitation
 
or
 
impairment
 
during
 
computer-based
 
tasks.
 
Digital  Phenotyping  –  The  measurement  of  personal  behavioral  patterns  in  real-time  that  
are
 
derived
 
from
 
data
 
produced
 
by
 
digital
 
device
 
interactions.
 
This
 
study
 
describes
 
it
 
as
 
the
 
extraction
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
indicators
 
from
 
keyboard
 
and
 
mouse
 
interaction
 
data
 
gathered
 
during
 
clinical
 
sessions.
 
33  

=== PAGE 49 ===
 
EWMA  (Within-Session  Exponentially  Weighted  Moving  Average)  –  A  method  of  
adaptive
 
statistical
 
modeling
 
that,
 
in
 
the
 
calibration
 
stage,
 
establishes
 
a
 
participant’s
 
unique
 
behavioral
 
baseline.
 
To
 
eliminate
 
early
 
session
 
variables
 
like
 
nervousness
 
or
 
“settling
 
in”
 
effects,
 
it
 
gives
 
older
 
observations
 
exponentially
 
declining
 
weights.
 
Flight  Time  –  The  interval  between  the  release  of  one  key  and  the  next  key  press  of  another  
while
 
typing.
 
It
 
is
 
used
 
as
 
a
 
psychomotor
 
metric
 
indicative
 
of
 
cognitive
 
processing
 
and
 
motor
 
planning
 
latency.
 
Fuzzy  Logic  Classifier  –  A  rule-based  inference  engine  that  gives  clinical  categories  
(Green,
 
Amber,
 
Red)
 
varying
 
degrees
 
of
 
membership.
 
It
 
departs
 
from
 
binary
 
“pass/fail”
 
outcomes
 
and
 
instead
 
captures
 
the
 
continuous
 
character
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance.
 
GAD-7  (Generalized  Anxiety  Disorder-7)  –  The  severity  of  generalized  anxiety  disorder  is  
assessed
 
using
 
a
 
standardized
 
7-item
 
self-report
 
questionnaire.
 
This
 
study
 
uses
 
it
 
as
 
a
 
clinical
 
basis
 
to
 
connect
 
subjective
 
client
 
distress
 
with
 
agitation
 
scores
 
provided
 
by
 
the
 
system.
 
HAL  (Hardware  Abstraction  Layer)  –  A  software  layer  that  ensures  measurement  
independent
 
of
 
the
 
hardware
 
by
 
normalizing
 
interaction
 
metrics
 
across
 
various
 
input
 
devices.
 
To
 
prevent
 
device-specific
 
latencies
 
from
 
affecting
 
the
 
integrity
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
data,
 
it
 
applies
 
correction
 
factors
 
to
 
raw
 
timestamps.
 
Hotelling’s  T
 2
 Statistic  –  A  multivariate  statistical  measure  that  assesses  each  of  the  eight  
biometric
 
features
 
at
 
the
 
same
 
time
 
as
 
one
 
observation.
 
Univariate
 
techniques
 
may
 
miss
 
complex
 
abnormalities
 
that
 
arise
 
from
 
the
 
joint
 
behavior
 
of
 
many
 
features.
 
Ipsative  Analysis  –  An  analytical  approach  that  is  introspective  and  evaluates  an  
individual’s
 
present
 
performance
 
in
 
relation
 
to
 
their
 
personal
 
baseline
 
rather
 
than
 
against
 
a
 
34  

=== PAGE 50 ===
 
normative  population  standard.  In  a  single  session,  it  assesses  differences  in  an  adult’s  
psychomotor
 
behavior
 
across
 
numerous
 
tasks.
 
Keystroke  Dynamics  –  The  analysis  of  typing  behavior  using  timing  and  rhythm  attributes,  
including
 
the
 
key
 
press
 
time,
 
inter-key
 
latency,
 
and
 
typing
 
speed.
 
It
 
serves
 
as
 
the
 
behavioral
 
indicator
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
activity
 
in
 
this
 
study.
 
Ledoit-Wolf  Estimator  –  A  robust  covariance  estimator  that  is  employed  when  computing  
T
2
 
to
 
guarantee
 
the
 
covariance
 
matrix’s
 
invertibility
 
and
 
numerical
 
stability.
 
When
 
there
 
are
 
fewer
 
calibration
 
samples
 
than
 
features,
 
it
 
avoids
 
matrix
 
singularity
 
and
 
analytical
 
mistakes.
 
Microstressor  –  brief,  low-intensity  situational  stressor,  such  as  time  pressure,  minor  
interpersonal
 
friction,
 
or
 
self-evaluative
 
demands,
 
that
 
does
 
not
 
constitute
 
a
 
clinical
 
trauma
 
trigger
 
but
 
reliably
 
activates
 
mild
 
affective
 
and
 
cognitive
 
engagement
 
in
 
the
 
respondent.
 
In
 
this
 
study,
 
microstressor
 
items
 
used
 
in
 
Task
 
3
 
are
 
organized
 
into
 
four
 
thematic
 
groups
 
representing
 
distinct
 
stressor
 
domains
 
(Group
 
1:
 
Time/Workload;
 
Group
 
2:
 
Interpersonal;
 
Group
 
3:
 
Academic/Performance;
 
Group
 
4:
 
Self-evaluation),
 
with
 
items
 
within
 
each
 
group
 
spanning
 
three
 
graduated
 
difficulty
 
levels
 
(Level
 
A:
 
routine
 
situational;
 
Level
 
B:
 
mild
 
workload
 
and
 
interpersonal;
 
Level
 
C:
 
cognitively
 
demanding
 
self-evaluative),
 
for
 
a
 
total
 
of
 
12
 
items,
 
adapted
 
from
 
validated
 
daily
 
hassles
 
and
 
well-being
 
instruments,
 
including
 
the
 
Mainz
 
Inventory
 
of
 
Microstressors
 
(Kötter

2020).
 
Microstressor

are
 
presented
 
as
 
open-ended
 
composition

requiring
 
free-text

so
 
that
 
the
 
full

of
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
behavior
 
during
 
emotionally
 
engaged
 
writing
 
can
 
be
 
captured
 
for
 
psychomotor
 
analysis.
