Normative  Baseline  Model  –  A  statistical  reference  model  based  on  the  data  of  aggregated  
participants
 
that
 
represents
 
the
 
average
 
psychomotor
 
performance
 
of
 
the
 
study
 
population.
 
Individual
 
psychomotor

are
 
contextualized
 
within

in

PHQ-9  (Patient  Health  Questionnaire-9)  –  A  standard  9-item  test  to  screen  for,  track,  and  
assess
 
depression
 
severity.
 
To
 
check
 
the
 
accuracy
 
of
 
the
 
Psychomotor
 
Slowing
 
Index
 
(PSI),
 
this
 
study
 
uses
 
PHQ-9
 
scores
 
as
 
a
 
benchmark.
 
PSI  and  PAI  (Psychomotor  Slowing  Index  and  Psychomotor  Agitation  Index)  –  
Standardized
 
composite
 
metrics
 
derived
 
from
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
Analysis
 
that
 
quantify
 
the
 
nature
 
of
 
motor
 
deviations
 
within
 
a
 
session.
 
Psychomotor
 
Agitation
 
–
 
A
 
condition
 
marked
 
by
 
irregular
 
movement
 
patterns,
 
PSI
 
aggregates
 
contributions
 
from
 
features
 
like
 
flight
 
time,
 
dwell
 
time,
 
and
 
pause
 
frequency
 
to
 
indicate
 
motor
 
slowing.
 
PAI
 
aggregates
 
contributions
 
from
 
path
 
entropy,
 
jerk,
 
error
 
rate,
 
and
 
cursor
 
velocity
 
to
 
indicate
 
erratic
 
or
 
impulsive
 
motor
 
patterns.
 
unpredictability,  and  elevated  motor  activity.  This  study  operationalizes  psychomotor  
agitation
 
by
 
measuring
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
movement
 
metrics
 
with
 
increased
 
variability
 
and
 
inefficiency.
 
Psychomotor  Slowing  -  The  decrease  in  motor  responsiveness  and  quickness  that  is  
frequently
 
linked
 
to
 
depression
 
and
 
cognitive
 
decline.
 
Longer
 
keystroke
 
latencies,
 
slower
 
cursor
 
movement,
 
and
 
typing
 
speed
 
are
 
used
 
in
 
this
 
study
 
to
 
operationalize
 
psychomotor
 
slowing.
 
Sliding  Window  Filter  –  A  digital  signal  processing  method  that  applies  a  weighted  moving  
average
 
to
 
raw
 
cursor
 
coordinates.
 
It
 
is
 
used
 
to
 
preserve
 
clinically
 
relevant
 
low-frequency
 
hesitation
 
patterns
 
while
 
eliminating
 
high-frequency
 
hardware
 
noise
 
and
 
tremor
 
anomalies.

=== PAGE 52 ===
 
SSI  (Single-Session  Intervention)  –  A  planned  clinical  visit  intended  to  provide  evaluation  
or
 
therapeutic
 
benefit
 
in
 
a
 
single
 
session.
 
PsyClick
 
describes
 
the

capacity
 
to
 
provide
 
unbiased
 
psychomotor
 
insights
 
during
 
single

without
 
the

Standardized  Counseling  Tasks  –  Short,  predetermined  computer-based  activities  designed  
to
 
produce
 
baseline
 
and

active
 
psychomotor
 
responses.
 
These
 
tasks
 
include
 
free-text
 
writing,
 
basic
 
stimulus-response
 
tests,
 
and
 
guided
 
text
 
copying.
 
Temporal  Heatmap  –  A  visual  representation  of  cursor  dwell  duration  mapped  per

in

at

(workload,

the

Chapter  2  
REVIEW  OF  RELATED  LITERATURE  
AND  STUDIES  
 
REVIEW  OF  RELATED  LITERATURE  
LOCAL  LITERATURE  
Mental  health  stigma,  services,  and  interventions  in  the  Philippines  
A  comprehensive  review  of  the  Philippine  Mental  Health  Act  reveals  a  critical  
paradox:
 
although
 
the
 
legislation
 
theoretically
 
guarantees
 
access
 
to
 
care,
 
considerable
 
operational
 
costs
 
and
 
the
 
absence
 
of
 
evidence-based
 
interventions
 
in
 
school
 
settings
 
render
 
these
 
services
 
functionally
 
inadequate
 
and
 
largely
 
inaccessible.
 
To
 
broaden
 
access,
 
current
 
research
 
aims
 
to
 
utilize
 
innovative,
 
low-cost
 
technologies
 
that
 
address
 
systemic
 
barriers.
 
This
 
approach
 
reinforces
 
the
 
study's
 
cost-effectiveness
 
argument
 
by
 
demonstrating
 
that
 
the
 
primary
 
"solution
 
gap"
 
stems
 
not
 
from
 
physician
 
shortages,
 
but
 
from
 
the
 
lack
 
of
 
affordable,
 
expandable
 
tools
 
for
 
early
 
detection.
 
Consistent
 
with
 
the
 
principles
 
of
 
low-cost,
 
evidence-based
 
interventions,
 
the
 
proposed
 
system
 
addresses
 
the
 
urgent
 
need
 
for
 
tools
 
that
 
deliver
 
objective
 
clinical
 
value
 
without
 
relying
 
on
 
expensive
 
conventional
 
mental
 
health
 
infrastructure.
 
 
Towards  transforming  the  mental  health  services  of  the  Philippines  
Alibudbud  (2023)  identifies  a  significant  "service  gap"  in  the  Philippine  mental  
health
 
system,
 
noting
 
that
 
there
 
are
 
only
 
2,100
 
psychologists
 
and
 
psychiatrists
 
for
 
a
 
population
 
of
 
110
 
million.
 
This
 
deficit
 
is
 
additionally
 
worsened
 
by
 
the
 
concentration
 
of
 
38  

=== PAGE 54 ===
 
mental  health  services  in  tertiary  hospitals  rather  than  in  primary  community  care  settings.  
The
 
shortage
 
highlights
 
the
 
acute
 
need
 
for
 
automated,
 
scalable
 
screening
 
tools
 
such
 
as
 
PsyClick,
 
which
 
can
 
operate
 
without
 
instant
 
specialist
 
intervention
 
and
 
thereby
 
alleviate
 
the
 
burden
 
on
 
the
 
limited
 
professional
 
workforce.
 
PsyClick,
 
designed
 
as
 
a
 
standalone
 
desktop
 
application
 
for
 
use
 
in
 
school
 
counseling
 
offices,
 
directly
 
responds
 
to
 
Alibudbud’s
 
call
 
for
 
"community-based
 
interventions"
 
that
 
decentralize
 
care
 
from
 
overextended
 
medical
 
facilities.
 
Furthermore,
 
the
 
study
 
identifies
 
deeply
 
rooted
 
cultural
 
barriers,
 
as
 
mental
 
distress
 
is
 
often
 
stigmatized
 
as
 
a
 
personal
 
weakness,
 
discouraging
 
adults
 
from
 
seeking
 
professional
 
assistance.
 
PsyClick
 
addresses
 
these
 
challenges
 
by
 
providing
 
a
 
non-confrontational,
 
passive-monitoring
 
system
 
based
 
on
 
psychomotor
 
dynamics.
 
This
 
approach
 
enables
 
objective
 
screening
 
with
 
reduced
 
psychological
 
pressure
 
during
 
initial
 
interviews,
 
thereby
 
avoiding
 
much
 
of
 
the
 
cultural
 
stigma
 
and
 
"self-stigma"
 
that
 
commonly
 
hinder
 
traditional
 
help-seeking
 
behaviors
 
in
 
the
 
Filipino
 
context.
 
 
The  promise  and  perils  of  e-mental  health  in  the  Philippines:  A  review  of  the  literature  
and
 
the
 
national
 
legal
 
and
 
policy
 
framework
 
This  paper  reviews  the  current  state  of  e-mental  health,  specifically  the  use  of  
technology
 
in
 
mental
 
health
 
services
 
in
 
the
 
Philippines.
 
Although
 
the
 
field
 
is
 
expanding,
 
most
 
existing
 
solutions
 
emphasize
 
service
 
delivery,
 
such
 
as
 
tele-counseling.
 
A
 
significant
 
gap
 
remains
 
in
 
the
 
development
 
and
 
validation
 
of
 
locally
 
relevant,
 
evidence-based
 
tools,
 
particularly
 
those
 
designed
 
for
 
objective
 
assessment
 
and
 
monitoring.
 
This
 
review
 
positions
 
PsyClick
 
as
 
a
 
direct,
 
timely
 
response
 
to
 
a
 
recognized
 
need
 
in
 
the
 
Philippine
 
e-mental
 
health
 
landscape.
 
The
 
field
 
currently
 
lacks
 
tools
 
that
 
offer
 
objective
 
measurement
 
to
 
support
 
39  

=== PAGE 55 ===
 
clinicians.  A  behavioral  analysis  system  represents  the  type  of  evidence-based,  data-driven  
assessment
 
tool
 
that
 
is
 
presently
 
absent.
 
The
 
development
 
of
 
such
 
a
 
system
 
constitutes
 
a
 
crucial
 
contribution
 
to
 
advancing
 
the
 
technological
 
capacity
 
for
 
mental
 
health
 
support
 
in
 
the
 
Philippines.
 
 
FOREIGN  LITERATURE  
Machine  learning  in  mental  health  
This  article  examines  the  application  of  machine  learning  (ML)  in  mental  health,  
with
 
emphasis
 
on
 
data
 
sources
 
such
 
as
 
behavioral,
 
physiological,
 
and
 
interaction
 
data,
 
model
 
development,
 
clinical
 
interpretability
 
challenges,
 
and
 
ethical
 
considerations.
 
The
 
review
 
explores
 
how
 
digital
 
behavioral
 
signals,
 
including
 
typing
 
and
 
navigation
 
patterns,
 
can
 
be
 
analyzed
 
using
 
ML
 
to
 
facilitate
 
diagnosis,
 
screening,
 
and
 
monitoring
 
of
 
mental
 
health
 
conditions.
 
The
 
literature
 
provides
 
a
 
foundation
 
for
 
backend
 
analysis
 
engines
 
that
 
extract
 
psychomotor
 
features
 
and
 
construct
 
baseline
 
models
 
for
 
deviation
 
detection.
 
Additionally,
 
it
 
supports
 
integrating
 
machine
 
learning
 
and
 
behavioral
 
data
 
into
 
the
 
PsyClick
 
system.
 
The
 
article
 
underscores
 
the
 
need
 
to
 
develop
 
interpretable,
 
secure,
 
and
 
clinically
 
relevant
 
systems,
 
thereby
 
reinforcing
 
the
 
design
 
of
 
secure,
 
standalone
 
solutions.
 
 
Fuzzy  Logic  Based  Decision  Support  System  for  Medical  Diagnosis  
In  order  to  improve  the  precision  and  interpretability  of  medical  diagnoses,  Basvant  et  al.  
(2024)
 
investigated
 
the
 
use
 
of
 
fuzzy
 
logic,
 
particularly
 
in
 
relation
 
to
 
the
 
uncertainty
 
present
 
in
 
clinical
 
data.
 
In
 
an
 
effort
 
to
 
provide
 
a
 
more
 
human-like
 
reasoning
 
process
 
than
 
conventional
 
binary
 
logic,
 
the
 
study
 
uses
 
the
 
Mamdani
 
Fuzzy
 
Inference
 
System
 
to
 
build
 
40  

=== PAGE 56 ===
 
membership  functions  that  link  imprecise  symptoms,  such  as  different  degrees  of  distress,  to  
graded
 
categories.
 
This
 
supports
 
PsyClick’s
 
White
 
Box
 
architecture,
 
which
 
turns
 
multivariate
 
T
2
 
scores
 
and

(PSI/PAI)

“Borderline,”
 
and
 
“Severe
 
using
 
a

Additionally,

highlights
 
how

can
 
function
 
as
 
non-diagnostic

that

in

contexts
 
by
 
generating
 
deterministic
 
and
 
auditable
 
outputs.
 
 
Beyond  neural  data:  Cognitive  biometrics  and  mental  privacy  
Magee,  Ienca,  and  Farahany  (2024)  present  a  major  shift  in  the  discussion  of  
neuro-rights
 
by
 
arguing
 
that
 
the
 
current
 
legal
 
focus
 
on
 
neural
 
data
 
is
 
overly
 
narrow.
 
While
 
much
 
of
 
the
 
existing
 
literature
 
addresses
 
the
 
privacy
 
risks
 
associated
 
with
 
direct
 
brain-computer
 
interfaces
 
(BCIs),
 
these
 
authors
 
present
 
the
 
broader
 
concept
 
of
 
“cognitive
 
biometrics.”
 
They
 
note
 
that
 
contemporary
 
consumer
 
wearables,
 
such
 
as
 
VR
 
headsets,
 
fitness
 
trackers,
 
and
 
AR
 
glasses,
 
already
 
collect
 
proximate
 
data,
 
including
 
eye
 
movements,
 
heart
 
rate
 
variability,
 
and
 
facial
 
micro-expressions.
 
The
 
authors
 
assert
 
that
 
artificial
 
intelligence
 
can
 
now
 
utilize
 
these
 
metrics
 
to
 
accurately
 
infer
 
an
 
individual's
 
mental
 
state,
 
intentions,
 
or
 
emotions,
 
rendering
 
such
 
data
 
functionally
 
equivalent
 
to
 
"brain
 
data"
 
even
 
when
 
not
 
derived
 
directly
 
from
 
brain
 
sensors.
 
 
This  perspective  is  significant  as  it  exposes  a  substantial  loophole  in  current  privacy  
laws.
 
Magee
 
et
 
al.
 
(2024)
 
observe
 
that,
 
although
 
medical-grade
 
neural
 
data
 
is
 
typically
 
protected
 
by
 
stringent
 
health
 
privacy
 
regulations,
 
"cognitive
 
biometric"
 
data
 
collected
 
by
 
41  

=== PAGE 57 ===
 
technology  companies  for  entertainment  or  wellness  purposes  often  falls  into  a  legal  gray  
area.
 
Their
 
analysis
 
aligns
 
with
 
recent
 
UNESCO
 
recommendations,
 
which
 
advocate
 
for
 
a
 
comprehensive
 
framework
 
to
 
protect
 
"mental
 
privacy"
 
in
 
its
 
entirety.
 
For
 
the
 
present
 
study,
 
these
 
findings
 
indicate
 
that
 
threats
 
to
 
cognitive
 
liberty
 
are
 
not
 
limited
 
to
 
future
 
scenarios
 
involving
 
neural
 
implants,
 
but
 
are
 
already
 
present
 
in
 
the
 
everyday
 
devices
 
currently
 
in
 
use.
 
 
 Single-Session  Interventions  for  Mental  Health  Problems  and  Service  Engagement:  
Umbrella
 
Review
 
of
 
Systematic
 
Reviews
 
and
 
Meta-Analyses
 
To  evaluate  the  effectiveness  of  Single-Session  Interventions  (SSIs),  Schleider  et  al.  
(2025)
 
conducted
 
a
 
comprehensive
 
umbrella
 
review
 
that
 
synthesized
 
data
 
from
 
24
 
systematic
 
reviews
 
encompassing
 
415
 
distinct
 
clinical
 
trials.
 
The
 
study
 
reported
 
that
 
83.33%
 
of
 
the
 
reviews
 
identified
 
significant,
 
favorable
 
effects
 
of
 
SSIs
 
on
 
clinical
 
outcomes,
 
including
 
substantial
 
reductions
 
in
 
anxiety
 
and
 
depression
 
symptoms.
 
The
 
analysis
 
shows
 
that
 
organized
 
programs
 
involving
 
a
 
single,
 
intentional
 
interaction
 
(SSIs)
 
are
 
a
 
critical
 
strategy
 
for
 
addressing
 
the
 
“treatment
 
gap,”
 
especially
 
in
 
low-resource
 
settings
 
where
 
clients
 
often
 
receive
 
care
 
only
 
once.
 
This
 
evidence
 
substantiates
 
the
 
rationale
 
for
 
PsyClick
 
by
 
demonstrating
 
that
 
a
 
single,
 
high-resolution
 
assessment
 
session
 
can
 
yield
 
clinically
 
meaningful
 
results.
 
Implementing
 
an
 
objective,
 
computer-based
 
screening
 
during
 
this
 
key
 
initial
 
encounter
 
is
 
consistent
 
with
 
Schleider
 
et
 
al.’s
 
(2025)
 
recommendation
 
to
 
maximize
 
the
 
clinical
 
value
 
of
 
early
 
interactions,
 
rather
 
than
 
depending
 
on
 
multi-session
 
diagnostic
 
processes.
 
 
Exploring  depression  in  adults  over  a  decade:  a  review  of  longitudinal  studies

=== PAGE 58 ===
 
In  order  to  investigate  how  depression  symptoms  develop  in  individuals  over  time,  De  Sousa  
et
 
al.
 
(2025)
 
carried
 
out
 
an
 
extensive
 
evaluation
 
of
 
longitudinal
 
research.
 
According
 
to
 
their
 
findings,
 
depression
 
is
 
a
 
very
 
diverse
 
condition
 
with
 
a
 
wide
 
range
 
of
 
trajectories,
 
impacted
 
by
 
a
 
complex
 
interaction
 
of
 
social
 
and
 
biological
 
factors.
 
It
 
emphasizes
 
that
 
since
 
depression
 
is
 
not
 
a
 
“monolithic

there
 
is
 
a
 
pressing
 
need
 
for
 
individualized
 
healthcare
 
strategies
 
that
 
can
 
use
 
objective
 
patterns
 
to
 
detect
 
high-risk
 
clients
 
early.
 
The

also
 
highlight
 
the
 
revolutionary

of
 
wearable
 
technology
 
and

for

This

provides
 
a
 
strong
 
foundation
 
for

focus
 
on
 
the
 
general
 
adult
 
population
 
and
 
validates
 
the
 
use
 
of
 
technical,
 
hardware-abstracted
 
biometrics
 
as
 
a
 
means
 
to
 
capture
 
the
 
dynamic,
 
non-linear
 
nature
 
of
 
adult
 
depression
 
and
 
anxiety.
 
 
Adaptability  of  current  keystroke  and  mouse  behavioral  biometric  systems:  A  survey  
Subash  et  al.  (2025)  provide  an  extensive  survey  on  the  evolution  of  keystroke  and  
mouse
 
dynamics,
 
classifying
 
them
 
as
 
established
 
behavioral
 
biometrics
 
capable
 
of
 
detecting
 
complex
 
internal
 
states
 
such
 
as
 
emotion,
 
age-related
 
decline,
 
and
 
physiological
 
fatigue.
 
The
 
study
 
shows
 
that
 
these
 
modalities
 
have
 
gained
 
considerable
 
popularity
 
in
 
clinical
 
and
 
security
 
research
 
because
 
they
 
are
 
inherently
 
non-invasive
 
and
 
enable
 
continuous,
 
passive
 
data
 
collection
 
without
 
interrupting
 
the
 
user’s
 
natural
 
workflow.
 
This
 
high-frequency
 
data
 
acquisition
 
is
 
critical
 
for
 
spotting
 
subtle
 
psychomotor
 
shifts
 
that
 
traditional
 
periodic
 
assessments
 
might
 
miss.
 
This
 
research
 
provides
 
a
 
rigorous
 
technical
 
foundation
 
for
 
the
 
technical
 
variables,
 
confirming
 
that
 
unique
 
interaction
 
schemes
 
are
 
not
 
merely
 
peripheral
 
data
 
but
 
scientifically
 
validated
 
markers
 
of
 
a
 
user’s
 
internal
 
cognitive
 
and
 
motor
 
health.
 
By

framing  keystroke  and  mouse  dynamics  as  behavioral  biometrics,  the  study  strengthens  the

to

of

A  review  of  emotion  recognition  methods  from  keystroke,  mouse,  and  touchscreen  
dynamics
 
This  comprehensive  survey  examines  methods  for  recognizing  users'  emotional  states  
by
 
analyzing
 
interaction
 
dynamics,
 
including
 
keystroke
 
dynamics,
 
mouse
 
movements,
 
and

The

extracted
 
from
 
these

machine

used
 
for

and
 
the

employed
 
in

Its

to

of
 
current

and

in

It

by

features
 
and
 
appropriate

techniques,
 
ensuring

system
 
is
 
based
 
on
 
established,

and

Liu  and  Barolli  (2024)  compared  two  distinct  intelligent  system  designs  in  order  to  examine

of

to

in

This

not

Case  studies  from  the  digital  clinic:  integrating  digital  phenotyping  and  clinical  
practice
 
into
 
today’s
 
world
 
 
Rauseo-Ricupero  et  al.  (2021)  examined  the  integration  of  modern  technology  into  
mental
 
health

The
 
authors
 
provided
 
practical
 
examples
 
from
 
the

were
 
implemented
 
alongside
 
conventional
 
therapy
 
to

and

Rather
 
than
 
focusing
 
solely
 
on
 
theoretical
 
considerations,
 
the

demonstrated
 
the
 
application
 
of
 
digital

on

and

These
 
digital
 
instruments
 
enabled

to
 
gain
 
a
 
more
 
comprehensive
 
understanding
 
of
 
clients’
 
experiences
 
between
 
traditional
 
sessions
 
by
 
providing
 
additional
 
data
 
on
 
daily
 
behaviors
 
and
 
emotional
 
fluctuations
 
that
 
may
 
not
 
always
 
be
 
disclosed
 
during
 
in-person
 
consultations.
 
The
 
study
 
further
 
emphasized
 
the
 
45  

=== PAGE 61 ===
 
value  of  introducing  roles  such  as  a  “digital  navigator”  to  assist  both  clients  and  clinicians  in  
interpreting
 
technological
 
data,
 
reinforcing
 
the
 
concept
 
that
 
digital
 
tools
 
can
 
complement,
 
rather

Although

and

of

particularly
 
when

are
 
constrained,
 
as
 
observed
 
during

Digital  phenotyping  for  mental  health  based  on  data  analytics:  A  systematic  literature  
review
 
Heckler  and  colleagues  conducted  a  comprehensive  review  examining  the  application  
of
 
digital
 
phenotyping
 
in
 
mental
 
health
 
research,
 
with
 
a
 
particular
 
focus
 
on
 
data
 
analytics.
 
Rather
 
than
 
evaluating
 
new
 
technologies
 
directly,
 
the
 
authors
 
systematically
 
reviewed
 
thousands
 
of
 
studies
 
from
 
major
 
academic
 
repositories
 
and
 
identified
 
74
 
relevant
 
articles.
 
This
 
process
 
provided
 
an
 
overview
 
of
 
how
 
smartphones
 
and
 
wearable
 
devices
 
are
 
utilized
 
to
 
collect
 
behavioral
 
data
 
that
 
may
 
indicate
 
psychological
 
states.
 
The
 
findings
 
revealed
 
that
 
most
 
current
 
approaches
 
continue
 
to
 
rely
 
heavily
 
on
 
self-reported
 
mental
 
health
 
symptoms,
 
despite
 
the
 
increasing
 
use
 
of
 
passive
 
data
 
from
 
smartphones,
 
such
 
as
 
movement
 
patterns,
 
application
 
usage,
 
and
 
sleep
 
habits.
 
The
 
review
 
also
 
noted
 
that
 
traditional
 
machine
 
learning
 
methods
 
remain
 
the
 
primary
 
analytical
 
tools,
 
although
 
challenges
 
such
 
as
 
sample
 
bias
 
and
 
missing
 
data
 
limit
 
the
 
reliability
 
of
 
results.
 
Furthermore,
 
while
 
digital
 
data
 
show
 
significant
 
potential
 
for
 
understanding
 
mood,
 
anxiety,
 
and
 
stress,
 
few
 
studies
 
have
 
progressed
 
to
 
developing
 
practical
 
tools
 
for
 
client
 
support;
 
most
 
remain
 
focused
 
on
 
data
 
collection
 
and
 
analysis.
 
In
 
summary,
 
the
 
article
 
demonstrates
 
that
 
digital
 
phenotyping
 
is
 
advancing
 
rapidly
 
46  

=== PAGE 62 ===
 
but  still  requires  further  development  before  it  can  be  reliably  integrated  into  psychological  
health
 
support
 
systems.
 
 
What  is  the  difference  between  normative  and  ipsative  assessments?  
This  literature  review  explores  the  operational  distinctions  between  normative  and  
ipsative
 
evaluation
 
systems.
 
Normative
 
assessment
 
is
 
a
 
comparative
 
method
 
in
 
which
 
an
 
individual's
 
performance
 
is
 
measured
 
against
 
a
 
population
 
average,
 
or
 
the
 
Bell
 
Curve
 
(inter-individual
 
comparison),
 
making
 
it
 
primarily
 
suitable
 
for
 
selection
 
and
 
ranking.
 
In
 
contrast,
 
ipsative
 
assessment
 
is
 
a
 
self-referential
 
approach
 
that
 
compares
 
an
 
individual's
 
current
 
performance
 
with
 
their
 
historical
 
records.
 
The
 
analysis
 
argues
 
that
 
normative
 
criteria
 
are
 
often
 
inadequate
 
for
 
ongoing
 
monitoring
 
and
 
development
 
because
 
they
 
do
 
not
 
capture
 
the
 
precise
 
extent
 
of
 
individual
 
development
 
or
 
decline
 
over
 
time,
 
a
 
phenomenon
 
referred
 
to
 
as
 
the
 
"distance
 
travelled."
 
Ipsative
 
approaches,
 
by
 
contrast,
 
effectively
 
differentiate
 
individual
 
changes
 
from
 
group
 
trends.
 
Care
 
Advantage's
 
(2021)
 
distinction
 
provides
 
the
 
foundation
 
for
 
PsyClick's
 
Calibration
 
Phase.
 
Traditional
 
systems
 
may
 
overlook
 
high-functioning
 
users
 
whose
 
performance
 
declines
 
to
 
average
 
levels,
 
whereas
 
the
 
ipsative
 
approach
 
enables
 
comparison
 
against
 
the
 
user's
 
established
 
baseline
 
(μ
user).
 
This
 
method
 
prioritizes
 
the
 
detection
 
of
 
relative
 
psychomotor
 
decline
 
(ipsative
 
anomalies)
 
over
 
absolute
 
slowness,
 
thereby
 
enhancing
 
sensitivity
 
to
 
individual
 
behavioral
 
changes.
 
 
Ipsative  Assessment  –  The  Legal  Journey  
Rhodes  (2025)  demonstrates  that  Ipsative  Assessment,  which  uses  an  individual's  
previous
 
performance
 
as
 
a
 
reference
 
point
 
to
 
measure
 
growth,
 
is
 
effective
 
in
 
evaluating
 
the
 
47  

=== PAGE 63 ===
 
teaching  and  educational  efficacy  of  performance  assessments  in  legal  education.  The  study  
contends
 
that
 
traditional
 
norm-referenced
 
assessment
 
methods,
 
which
 
compare
 
students
 
to
 
one
 
another,
 
fail
 
to
 
capture
 
individual
 
growth
 
or
 
decline
 
in
 
performance
 
adequately.
 
Research
 
findings
 
indicate
 
that
 
Ipsative
 
Assessment,
 
which
 
measures
 
progress
 
relative
 
to
 
a
 
student's
 
baseline
 
performance,
 
provides
 
a
 
more
 
accurate
 
representation
 
of
 
an
 
individual's
 
capabilities
 
at
 
a
 
given
 
time
 
and
 
throughout
 
their
 
developmental
 
trajectory.
 
These
 
findings
 
support
 
the
 
decision
 
to
 
design
 
the
 
system
 
to
 
enable
 
Ipsative
 
Calibration
 
for
 
each
 
student
 
individually,
 
rather
 
than
 
imposing
 
a
 
universal
 
performance
 
standard
 
for
 
all
 
users.
 
Furthermore,
 
the
 
research
 
suggests
 
that
 
establishing
 
an
 
Ipsative
 
Baseline
 
is
 
essential
 
for
 
detecting
 
declines
 
in
 
mental
 
health.
 
For
 
example,
 
a
 
person
 
who
 
typically
 
types
 
at
 
100
 
WPM
 
would
 
experience
 
a
 
more
 
significant
 
decline
 
if
 
their
 
speed
 
drops
 
to
 
80
 
WPM
 
than
 
a
 
person
 
whose
 
usual
 
speed
 
is
 
80
 
WPM.
 
The
 
evidence
 
presented
 
supports
 
the
 
conclusion
 
that
 
comparing
 
a
 
user's
 
current
 
session
 
to
 
their
 
historical
 
Ipsative
 
Baseline
 
is
 
the
 
most
 
scientifically
 
valid
 
method
 
for
 
assessing
 
changes
 
in
 
individual
 
psychomotor
 
activity.
 
 
 
A  fuzzy  logic  model  for  stress  assessment  
In  order  to  evaluate  people’s  stress  levels  by  looking  at  their  facial  expressions,  Yağcı  and  
Kandemir
 
(2025)
 
created
 
a
 
cascade
 
model
 
that
 
uses
 
a
 
variety
 
of
 
fuzzy
 
logic
 
approaches,
 
such
 
as
 
Fuzzy
 
C-Means
 
(FCM)
 
clustering,
 
Adaptive
 
Neuro-Fuzzy
 
Inference
 
System
 
(ANFIS),
 
and
 
Fuzzy
 
Inference
 
System
 
(FIS).
 
Their
 
research
 
shows
 
that
 
stress
 
may
 
be
 
effectively
 
classified
 
into
 
“None,”
 
“Low,”
 
“Moderate,”
 
and
 
“High”
 
levels
 
by
 
comparing
 
a
 
person’s
 
current
 
state
 
to
 
a
 
known
 
neutral
 
baseline,
 
with
 
a
 
focus
 
on
 
areas
 
like
 
the
 
mouth
 
and
 
eyebrows.
 
This
 
study
 
backs
 
up
 
PsyClick’s
 
use
 
of
 
an
 
Ipsative
 
(Self-Referential)
 
Analysis,
 
which
 
detects
 
48  

=== PAGE 64 ===
 
substantial  multivariate  deviations  in  motor  control  by  creating  a  customized  behavioral  
baseline
 
during
 
a
 
calibration.
 
 
Exponential  Weight  Moving  Average  (EWMA)  Control  Chart  for  Quality  Control  of  
Crude
 
Palm
 
Oil
 
Product
 
The  Exponentially  Weighted  Moving  Average  (EWMA)  control  chart  is  an  advanced  
Statistical
 
Process
 
Control
 
(SPC)
 
technique
 
designed
 
for
 
the
 
continuous
 
monitoring
 
of
 
time-series
 
data.
 
Unlike
 
traditional
 
threshold
 
models
 
(like
 
standard
 
Z-scores
 
or
 
Shewhart
 
charts)
 
that
 
evaluate
 
individual
 
data
 
points
 
in
 
isolation,
 
the
 
EWMA
 
algorithm
 
calculates
 
a
 
weighted
 
average
 
of
 
all
 
past
 
and
 
current
 
observations.
 
It
 
applies
 
exponentially
 
decreasing
 
weights
 
to
 
older
 
data,
 
ensuring
 
that
 
the
 
most
 
recent
 
behaviors
 
heavily
 
influence
 
the
 
calculation
 
while
 
still
 
maintaining
 
historical
 
context.
 
This
 
mathematical
 
accumulation
 
makes
 
the
 
EWMA
 
chart
 
exceptionally
 
sensitive
 
to
 
detecting
 
small,
 
persistent
 
shifts
 
or
 
gradual
 
drifts
 
in
 
a
 
process
 
that
 
standard
 
variance
 
tests
 
would
 
miss.
 
This
 
literature
 
provides
 
the
 
direct
 
mathematical
 
justification
 
for
 
upgrading
 
the
 
PsyClick
 
system's
 
Analytical
 
Layer
 
from
 
a
 
purely
 
Z-score-based
 
engine
 
to
 
an
 
EWMA-integrated
 
framework.
 
By
 
implementing
 
EWMA,
 
PsyClick
 
functions
 
as
 
a
 
sophisticated
 
low-pass
 
filter:
 
it
 
smooths
 
out
 
high-frequency
 
noise
 
(like
 
natural
 
hand
 
tremors
 
or
 
hardware
 
latency)
 
while
 
accumulating
 
the
 
subtle,
 
time-series
 
signals
 
of
 
cognitive
 
distress.
 
This
 
allows
 
the
 
system
 
to
 
detect
 
relative
 
psychomotor
 
decline
 
much
 
earlier
 
and
 
more
 
accurately,
 
providing
 
a
 
highly
 
sensitive
 
mechanism
 
for
 
clinical
 
decision-support.
 
 Behavior  modeling  via  cursor  movement  data  
49  

=== PAGE 65 ===
 
This  presentation  examines  behavioral  modeling  for  detecting  health  changes,  
specifically
 
cognitive
 
decline
 
and
 
mental
 
health
 
issues,
 
using
 
cursor
 
or
 
mouse
 
movement
 
data.
 
The
 
researchers
 
found
 
that
 
individuals
 
with
 
cognitive
 
or
 
health
 
impairments
 
exhibit
 
distinct
 
cursor
 
movement
 
patterns,
 
including
 
slower
 
movement
 
(psychomotor
 
retardation),
 
less
 
direct
 
and
 
more
 
circuitous
 
paths,
 
and
 
increased
 
movement
 
latency.
 
Rather
 
than
 
analyzing
 
individual
 
movements
 
in
 
isolation,
 
the
 
researchers
 
developed
 
a
 
machine
 
learning
 
model
 
that
 
integrates
 
both
 
spatial
 
(screen
 
location)
 
and
 
temporal
 
(timing
 
and
 
duration)
 
features
 
to
 
improve
 
early-detection
 
accuracy.
 
The
 
video
 
explicitly
 
states
 
that
 
slower
 
and
 
less
 
efficient
 
mouse
 
movements
 
serve
 
as
 
markers
 
of
 
health
 
decline.
 
This
 
finding
 
demonstrates
 
that
 
cursor
 
velocity
 
and
 
path
 
entropy
 
are
 
established
 
indicators
 
of
 
cognitive
 
state.
 
Furthermore,
 
the
 
speaker
 
contends
 
that
 
integrating
 
spatial
 
and
 
temporal
 
features
 
within
 
a
 
single
 
model
 
yields
 
superior
 
results
 
compared
 
to
 
analyzing
 
them
 
separately,
 
thereby
 
supporting
 
the
 
Analysis
 
Engine's
 
use
 
of
 
a
 
complex
 
feature
 
vector
 
that
 
combines
 
speed,
 
jitter,
 
and
 
pause
 
time,
 
rather
 
than
 
relying
 
solely
 
on
 
a
 
simple
 
speed
 
test.
 
 
A  Holistic  Approach  to  Hardware  Abstraction  Layers  
The  IEEE  paper  "A  Holistic  Approach  to  Hardware  Abstraction  Layers"  (2022)  
examines
 
the
 
development
 
of
 
a
 
new
 
Hardware
 
Abstraction
 
Layer
 
(HAL)
 
for
 
the
 
US
 
Marine
 
Corps,
 
aiming
 
to
 
replace
 
aging,
 
highly
 
coupled
 
software
 
systems
 
with
 
a
 
more
 
flexible,
 
modern
 
architecture.
 
The
 
paper
 
details
 
the
 
transition
 
from
 
outdated
 
SOAP-based
 
layers
 
to
 
a
 
robust
 
HAL
 
implemented
 
in
 
C#,
 
enabling
 
seamless
 
replacement
 
of
 
hardware
 
instruments
 
without
 
requiring
 
modifications
 
to
 
the
 
underlying
 
application
 
code.
 
This
 
study
 
is
 
particularly
 
relevant
 
as
 
it
 
validates
 
the
 
significance
 
of
 
a
 
decoupled
 
system
 
design,
 
which
 
parallels
 
the
 
50  

=== PAGE 66 ===
 
architecture  employed  in  PsyClick.  By  implementing  a  HAL  to  standardize  inputs  from  
various
 
peripherals,
 
such
 
as
 
mechanical
 
and
 
membrane
 
keyboards,
 
the
 
study
 
demonstrates
 
that
 
high-resolution
 
psychomotor
 
data
 
can
 
remain
 
consistent
 
regardless
 
of
 
the
 
device
 
used.
 
These
 
findings
 
indicate
 
that
 
a
 
well-designed
 
HAL
 
is
 
critical
 
for
 
maintaining
 
data
 
correctness
 
and
 
ensuring
 
system
 
longevity
 
in
 
clinical
 
tools
 
that
 
depend
 
on
 
precise
 
hardware-level
 
interaction
 
logs.
 
 
Identify  user  features  impacting  keystroke,  mouse  and  touchscreen  dynamics  
This  study  investigated  how  personal  characteristics  influence  interactions  with  input  
devices
 
such
 
as
 
keyboards,
 
mice,
 
and
 
touchscreens,
 
and
 
how
 
these
 
factors
 
relate
 
to
 
emotion
 
recognition.
 
Rather
 
than
 
assuming
 
uniform
 
user
 
behavior,
 
the
 
researchers
 
surveyed
 
107
 
participants.
 
They
 
analyzed
 
which
 
user
 
profile
 
features—including
 
gender,
 
device
 
proficiency,
 
language
 
level,
 
hand-use
 
habits,
 
software
 
knowledge,
 
task
 
urgency,
 
and
 
current
 
emotional
 
state—significantly
 
affected
 
measurable
 
behaviors
 
such
 
as
 
typing
 
speed,
 
error
 
rate,
 
key
 
or
 
screen
 
pressure,
 
and
 
mouse
 
movement
 
or
 
clicking
 
speed.
 
The
 
findings
 
indicated
 
that
 
these
 
personal
 
traits
 
were
 
closely
 
linked
 
to
 
variations
 
in
 
device
 
interaction,
 
with
 
certain
 
features
 
exerting
 
a
 
stronger
 
influence
 
on
 
specific
 
behaviors.
 
For
 
instance,
 
greater
 
technological
 
familiarity
 
was
 
associated
 
with
 
increased
 
typing
 
and
 
mouse
 
movement
 
speed,
 
while
 
emotional
 
state
 
and
 
task
 
urgency
 
significantly
 
impacted
 
multiple
 
behavioral
 
metrics.
 
Based
 
on
 
these
 
results,
 
the
 
study
 
proposed
 
a
 
framework
 
connecting
 
individual
 
user
 
traits
 
to
 
specific
 
input-device
 
behaviors,
 
enabling
 
future
 
systems
 
to
 
enhance
 
emotion
 
detection
 
by
 
accounting
 
for
 
individual
 
differences
 
in
 
device
 
interaction.

LOCAL  STUDIES  
Navigating  the  Philippine  mental  health  system  for  the  nation's  youth:  Challenges  and  
opportunities.
 
Alibudbud  (2024)  reviewed  the  structural  challenges  facing  the  Philippine  mental  
health
 
system,
 
noting
 
a
 
severe
 
shortage
 
of
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals
 
—
 
approximately
 
1,600
 
registered
 
psychologists
 
and
 
500
 
psychiatrists
 
for
 
over
 
110
 
million
 
people
 
—
 
compounded
 
by
 
geographic
 
concentration
 
of
 
services
 
in
 
tertiary
 
hospitals
 
rather
 
than
 
community
 
or
 
school
 
settings.
 
The
 
study
 
emphasizes
 
that
 
addressing
 
these
 
inadequacies
 
requires
 
incentivizing
 
the
 
training
 
of
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals
 
and
 
integrating
 
mental
 
health
 
into
 
primary
 
care
 
settings
 
to
 
decentralize
 
access.
 
For
 
PsyClick,
 
this
 
study
 
directly
 
validates
 
the
 
deployment
 
context:
 
a
 
standalone,
 
clinician-facing
 
decision-support
 
tool
 
designed
 
for
 
school
 
counseling
 
offices,
 
where
 
no
 
psychiatrist
 
is
 
present,
 
operationalizes
 
the
 
community-based,
 
non-specialist-dependent
 
approach
 
that
 
Alibudbud
 
identifies
 
as
 
the
 
primary
 
solution
 
pathway
 
for
 
the
 
Philippine
 
mental
 
health
 
system.
 
 
Issues  and  Challenges  on  the  Mental  Health  Services  of  a  Selected  Municipality  in  
Metro
 
Manila:
 
A
 
Case
 
Study
 
This  case  study  analyzes  original  qualitative  data  to  reveal  substantial  deficiencies  in  
the
 
provision
 
of
 
quality
 
mental
 
health
 
care
 
within
 
a
 
Metro
 
Manila
 
Health
 
Office,
 
despite
 
the
 
global
 
prevalence
 
of
 
mental
 
health
 
disorders
 
as
 
a
 
leading
 
cause
 
of
 
disability.
 
The
 
study
 
aims
 
to
 
identify
 
and
 
assess
 
the
 
current
 
mental
 
health
 
services
 
available
 
in
 
a
 
specific
 
Health
 
Office.
 
Findings
 
indicate
 
that
 
the
 
primary
 
barriers
 
to
 
quality
 
mental
 
health
 
care
 
include
 
a
 
critically
 
limited
 
budget,
 
a
 
shortage
 
of
 
trained
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals
 
such
 
as
 
psychologists,
 
and
 
the
 
lack
 
of
 
implementation
 
of
 
the
 
Mental
 
Health
 
Law.
 
These
 
factors
 
collectively
 
restrict
 
52  

=== PAGE 68 ===
 
access  to  essential  services  and  disproportionately  benefit  residents  located  nearer  to  the  
National
 
Capital
 
Region
 
(NCR).
 
 
Filipino  help-seeking  for  mental  health  problems  and  associated  barriers  and  
facilitators:
 
a
 
systematic
 
review
 
A  systematic  review  of  Filipino  help-seeking  behavior  found  that  although  many  
Filipinos
 
experience
 
mental
 
health
 
problems
 
such
 
as
 
anxiety
 
and
 
depression,
 
most
 
do
 
not
 
seek
 
formal
 
psychological
 
assistance
 
from
 
professionals.
 
Instead,
 
individuals
 
often
 
confide
 
in
 
close
 
friends
 
or
 
family
 
members
 
and
 
only
 
consider
 
consulting
 
a
 
therapist
 
or
 
mental
 
health
 
specialist
 
when
 
their
 
condition
 
becomes
 
severe.
 
The
 
review
 
synthesized
 
findings
 
from
 
15
 
studies
 
conducted
 
across
 
seven
 
countries.
 
It
 
revealed
 
that
 
Filipinos
 
residing
 
in
 
the
 
Philippines
 
frequently
 
encounter
 
financial
 
obstacles
 
and
 
reduced
 
access
 
to
 
services,
 
while
 
those
 
living
 
abroad
 
face
 
additional
 
challenges,
 
including
 
a
 
lack
 
of
 
health
 
insurance,
 
language
 
barriers,
 
and
 
discrimination
 
in
 
host
 
countries.
 
Stigma
 
and
 
concern
 
about
 
judgment
 
were
 
significant
 
deterrents
 
to
 
seeking
 
help
 
across
 
all
 
groups.
 
Concerns
 
about
 
losing
 
face
 
or
 
being
 
perceived
 
as
 
weak
 
contributed
 
to
 
a
 
preference
 
for
 
self-reliance
 
over
 
professional
 
support.
 
Factors
 
that
 
increased
 
the
 
likelihood
 
of
 
pursuing
 
professional
 
assistance
 
included
 
greater
 
awareness
 
of
 
the
 
severity
 
of
 
distress,
 
encouragement
 
from
 
supportive
 
friends
 
or
 
family,
 
and
 
previous
 
positive
 
experiences
 
with
 
mental
 
health
 
services.
 
The
 
review
 
concluded
 
that
 
both
 
cultural
 
and
 
practical
 
barriers
 
substantially
 
influence
 
whether
 
Filipino
 
college
 
students
 
and
 
other
 
Filipinos
 
access
 
mental
 
health
 
care.
 
 
FOREIGN  STUDIES  
53  

=== PAGE 69 ===
 
Psychomotor  Slowing  in  Psychosis  and  Inhibitory  Repetitive  Transcranial  Magnetic  
Stimulation
 
Walther  et  al.  (2024)  conducted  a  randomized  clinical  trial  investigating  psychomotor  
slowing
 
in
 
individuals
 
with
 
psychosis.
 
They
 
found
 
that
 
inhibitory
 
repetitive
 
transcranial
 
magnetic
 
stimulation
 
(rTMS)
 
over
 
the
 
supplementary
 
motor
 
area
 
resulted
 
in
 
measurable
 
improvements
 
in
 
motor
 
behavior
 
versus
 
control
 
conditions.
 
The
 
study
 
shows
 
that
 
psychomotor
 
slowing
 
is
 
a
 
quantifiable,
 
clinically
 
significant
 
motor
 
symptom
 
in
 
psychiatric
 
populations,
 
which
 
is
 
traditionally
 
assessed
 
with
 
structured
 
motor
 
rating
 
scales.
 
By
 
establishing
 
that
 
psychomotor
 
behavior
 
can
 
be
 
systematically
 
evaluated
 
and
 
modified
 
through
 
targeted
 
interventions,
 
this
 
research
 
supports
 
the
 
view
 
that
 
motor
 
behavior
 
constitutes
 
a
 
measurable
 
dimension
 
of
 
mental
 
health
 
rather
 
than
 
merely
 
an
 
observable
 
clinical
 
sign.
 
This
 
conceptual
 
framework
 
informs
 
the
 
present
 
study,
 
which
 
seeks
 
to
 
capture
 
and
 
quantify
 
psychomotor
 
agitation
 
and
 
slowing
 
by
 
analyzing
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics
 
during
 
counseling
 
tasks.
 
While
 
Walther
 
et
 
al.
 
utilized
 
clinical
 
rating
 
scales
 
and
 
neuromodulation
 
to
 
examine
 
motor
 
alterations,
 
PsyClick
 
adopts
 
a
 
digital
 
phenotyping
 
approach
 
that
 
operationalizes
 
subtle
 
interaction
 
styles
 
as
 
proxies
 
for
 
psychomotor
 
states.
 
By
 
integrating
 
clinical
 
findings
 
on
 
the
 
significance
 
of
 
motor
 
behavior
 
with
 
digital
 
interaction
 
metrics,
 
this
 
study
 
adds
 
to
 
the
 
growing
 
body
 
of
 
evidence
 
that
 
objective,
 
precise
 
measurement
 
of
 
motor
 
signatures
 
can
 
advance
 
mental
 
health
 
assessment.
 
Actigraphically  measured  psychomotor  slowing  in  depression:  Systematic  review  and  
meta-analysis
 
Wüthrich  et  al.  (2022)  addressed  the  urgent  need  for  objective  biomarkers  in  
psychiatry
 
by
 
conducting
 
the
 
largest
 
meta-analysis
 
to
 
date,
 
examining
 
the
 
relationship
 
54  

=== PAGE 70 ===
 
between  movement  and  mood  disorders.  The  study  assessed  objective  physical  activity  
levels
 
in
 
individuals
 
with
 
Major
 
Depressive
 
Disorder
 
and
 
Bipolar
 
Disorder.
 
By
 
synthesizing
 
data
 
from
 
34
 
studies
 
with
 
over
 
1,800
 
participants,
 
the
 
researchers
 
used
 
actigraphy,
 
which
 
involves
 
medical-grade
 
wearable
 
movement
 
sensors
 
worn
 
for
 
at

24
 
hours,
 
to
 
demonstrate
 
that
 
clients
 
with
 
these
 
mood
 
disorders
 
have
 
significantly
 
lower
 
physical
 
activity
 
levels
 
than
 
healthy
 
controls.
 
The
 
findings
 
are
 
notable
 
because,
 
after
 
examining
 
moderators
 
such
 
as
 
age,
 
sex,
 
and
 
treatment
 
setting,
 
the
 
authors
 
showed
 
that
 
psychomotor
 
slowing
 
persists
 
even
 
during
 
“remitted”
 
or
 
“euthymic”
 
(stable)

states,
 
rather
 
than
 
being
 
limited
 
to
 
active
 
depressive
 
episodes.
 
This
 
provides
 
strong
 
evidence
 
that
 
objective
 
motor
 
tracking
 
is
 
an
 
effective
 
and
 
unbiased
 
tool
 
for
 
long-term
 
monitoring
 
and
 
clinical
 
staging
 
of
 
affective
 
disorders
 
in
 
real-world
 
settings.
 
 
Evaluation  of  impairment  in  fine  motor  skills  in  patients  with  major  depressive  
disorder
 
Sahin  et  al.  (2024)  employed  a  comparative  observational  methodology  to  examine  
differences
 
in

abilities
 
among
 
patients
 
with

The

that
 
depressive
 
symptoms
 
are
 
associated
 
with
 
measurable
 
impairments
 
in

and

The

a

relevant  marker  of  psychomotor  slowing  in  depression,  particularly  when  assessed  via

Deciphering  the  interplay  between  psychopathological  symptoms,  sensorimotor,  
cognitive
 
and

Fritze  et  al.  (2025)  conducted  a  comprehensive  transdiagnostic  network  analysis  to

among

The

with

shows
 
that
 
observable

closely
 
reflects
 
internal

and

as
 
a

external
 
indicator
 
of
 
psychopathology.
 
This

establishes
 
a
 
foundation
 
for
 
the

validation
 
of
 
the
 
PsyClick
 
system.
 
Fritze
 
et

(2025)
 
further

that

difficulties
 
can
 
appear
 
physically
 
as
 
changes
 
in

supporting
 
the

independent
 
variable.
 
The

nature  of  their  findings  specifically  endorses  PsyClick's  approach  to  identifying  general

Isolation  of  distinct  networks  driving  action  and  cognition  in  psychomotor  processes  
Moussa-Tooks  et  al.  (2024)  elucidate  the  neurobiological  basis  of  psychomotor  
processes
 
by
 
identifying
 
distinct
 
brain
 
networks
 
responsible
 
for
 
"action"
 
(motor
 
execution)
 
and
 
"cognition"
 
(motor
 
planning).
 
Through
 
advanced
 
neuroimaging
 
techniques,
 
the
 
researchers
 
demonstrate
 
that,
 
although
 
the
 
networks
 
governing
 
action
 
initiation
 
and
 
execution
 
collaborate
 
for
 
normal
 
functioning,
 
they
 
remain
 
anatomically
 
separate.
 
The
 
study
 
suggests
 
that
 
clinical
 
symptoms
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
dysfunction,
 
such
 
as
 
retardation
 
or
 
agitation,
 
arise
 
from
 
specific
 
abnormalities
 
within
 
those
 
interconnected
 
networks.
 
This
 
distinction
 
highlights
 
that
 
motor
 
delays
 
are
 
often
 
attributable
 
to
 
disruptions
 
in
 
the
 
brain's
 
cognitive
 
control
 
networks
 
rather
 
than
 
to
 
muscular
 
abnormalities.
 
The
 
findings
 
directly
 
support
 
the
 
methodological
 
framework
 
of
 
the
 
PsyClick
 
system,
 
particularly
 
the
 
division
 
of
 
keystroke
 
dynamics
 
into
 
discrete
 
metrics.
 
The
 
differentiation
 
between
 
action
 
and
 
cognition
 
networks
 
provides
 
a
 
theoretical
 
basis
 
for
 
using
 
Flight
 
Time
 
(delay)
 
as
 
an
 
indicator
 
of
 
the
 
cognitive
 
planning
 
network,
 
and
 
Cursor
 
Dynamics/Jitter
 
as
 
indicators
 
of
 
the
 
execution
 
network.
 
By
 
aligning
 
software-based
 
measures
 
with
 
neurobiological
 
evidence,
 
the
 
study
 
ensures
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
detects
 
specific
 
forms
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
interference
 
rather
 
than
 
general
 
slowness.
 
 
57  

=== PAGE 73 ===
 
Diagnostic  accuracy  of  keystroke  dynamics  as  digital  biomarkers  for  fine  motor  decline  
in
 
neuropsychiatric
 
disorders:
 
a
 
systematic
 
review
 
and

This  systematic  review  and  meta-analysis  evaluated  41  studies  to  assess  the  
diagnostic
 
accuracy
 
of
 
keystroke
 
dynamics
 
as
 
digital
 
biomarkers
 
for
 
neuropsychiatric
 
and
 
psychiatric
 
disorders.
 
Analysis
 
of
 
data
 
from
 
over
 
4,400
 
participants
 
demonstrated
 
high
 
pooled
 
sensitivity
 
(0.83)
 
and
 
specificity
 
(0.87)
 
in
 
identifying
 
psychomotor
 
impairment.
 
The
 
findings
 
indicate
 
that

patterns
 
derived
 
from
 
natural
 
keyboard
 
interactions
 
effectively
 
translate
 
clinical

tests
 
into
 
non-intrusive,
 
real-world
 
digital

enabling
 
the
 
identification
 
of
 
disease-induced
 
phenotypes
 
that
 
may
 
be
 
too
 
subtle
 
for
 
traditional
 
observation.

(operationalized

Computer  mouse  movements  as  an  indicator  of  work  stress:  Longitudinal

Lutz  et  al.  (2021)  conducted  a  longitudinal  observational  field  study  and  found  that

movements,

in

Velocity,  Jerk,  Pause  Frequency),  not  as  an  isolated  channel  but  as  components  of  a

A  Sliding  Window  Common  Spatial  Pattern  for  Enhancing  Motor  Imagery  
Classification
 
in
 
EEG-BCI
 
This  study  aims  to  improve  the  classification  of  brain  signals  by  employing  sliding  
window
 
techniques
 
to
 
address
 
irregularities
 
in
 
trial
 
data.
 
The
 
researchers
 
introduced
 
methods
 
such
 
as
 
SW-LCR
 
and
 
SW-Mode
 
to
 
more
 
effectively
 
capture
 
the
 
"activation
 
time"
 
within
 
a
 
sequence,
 
resulting
 
in
 
significant
 
improvements
 
in
 
accuracy
 
for
 
both
 
healthy
 
individuals
 
and
 
stroke
 
clients.
 
These
 
findings
 
are
 
particularly
 
relevant
 
to
 
the
 
PsyClick
 
system,
 
as
 
they
 
support
 
the
 
application
 
of
 
a
 
sliding-window
 
filter
 
to
 
process
 
raw
 
behavioral
 
data.
 
Similar
 
to
 
the
 
study's
 
approach
 
for
 
managing
 
signal
 
noise
 
and
 
timing
 
issues
 
in
 
brainwaves,
 
the
 
PsyClick
 
system
 
can
 
utilize
 
this
 
logic
 
to
 
filter
 
out
 
high-frequency
 
noise
 
from
 
cursor
 
movements
 
while
 
preserving
 
critical
 
hesitation
 
patterns.
 
This
 
methodology
 
enhances
 
the
 
robustness
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
analysis
 
and
 
assures
 
that
 
statistical
 
Z-scores
 
are
 
derived
 
from
 
clean,
 
meaningful
 
interaction
 
data
 
rather
 
than
 
random
 
hardware
 
artifacts.
 
 
Keystroke-Based  Cognitive  Load  Detection:  A  Large-Scale  Analysis  Across

Cohorts
 
This  study  utilized  a  large  dataset  comprising  136  million  keystrokes  from  over

Using

80%

in

between
 
61  

=== PAGE 77 ===
 
high  and  low  cognitive  load  among  adults,  a  demographic  similar  to  that  of  the  present  study.  
Key
 
metrics

inter-key

words

Notably,

Detection  of  mental  fatigue  in  the  general  population:  feasibility  study  of  keystroke  
dynamics
 
as
 
a
 
real-world
 
biomarker
 
Acien  et  al.  (2022)  investigated  the  viability  of  identifying  mental  fatigue—a  
condition
 
strongly
 
associated
 
with
 
depression
 
and
 
burnout—using
 
keystroke
 
dynamics.
 
Their

achieved
 
72-80%

in
 
assessing

levels,
 
as
 
they
 
found
 
that
 
cognitive
 
exhaustion
 
significantly
 
increased
 
flight
 
time
 
(the
 
delay
 
between

and

errors.
 
This
 
study
 
reinforces

Assessing  user  behaviour  by  mouse  movement  
The  study  contends  that  although  standard  mouse  inputs,  such  as  clicks  and  
movements,
 
are
 
functional
 
requirements
 
of

This

by

an
 
anomaly
 
detection
 
problem
 
rather
 
than
 
a
 
conventional
 
classification
 
task.
 
The
 
study
 
posits
 
that

dynamics
 
function
 
as
 
idiosyncratic

that

internal
 
states.

and

of

(flight

Fuzzy  Gaussian  Decision  Tree  
In  their  study,  Pałczyński,  Czyżewska,  and  Talaśka  (2023)  introduce  the  Fuzzy  Gaussian  
Decision
 
Tree,
 
a
 
novel
 
modification
 
of
 
the
 
traditional
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
algorithm
 
designed
 
to
 
improve
 
the
 
classification
 
of
 
continuous,
 
real-world
 
signals.
 
Unlike
 
standard
 
Decision
 
Trees
 
that
 
rely
 
on
 
arbitrary,
 
fixed
 
points
 
for
 
sharp
 
input
 
space
 
division,
 
this
 
proposed
 
model
 
utilizes
 
a
 
fuzzy
 
decision
 
process
 
and
 
Gaussian
 
distribution
 
functions
 
to
 
perform
 
a
 
proximity-based
 
division
 
of
 
the
 
solution
 
space.
 
By
 
generating
 
a
 
sparse
 
tree
 
of
 
multidimensional
 
Gaussian
 
kernels,
 
the
 
algorithm
 
clusters
 
samples
 
based
 
on
 
common
 
patterns
 
and
 
evaluates
 
input
 
similarity
 
through
 
probability
 
measurement
 
and
 
maximum
 
likelihood
 
selection.
 
The
 
researchers
 
evaluated
 
the
 
algorithm's
 
performance
 
using
 
two
 
public
 
Kaggle
 
datasets:
 
the
 
Power
 
Quality
 
Classification
 
Dataset,
 
which
 
involves
 
predicting
 
six
 
classes
 
of
 
alternating
 
current
 
distortions,
 
and
 
the
 
Fetal
 
Cardiotocography
 
Dataset.
 
Based
 
on
 
accuracy
 
and
 
minimum
 
F1
 
score
 
metrics,
 
the
 
Fuzzy
 
Gaussian
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
outperformed
 
the
 
basic
 
Decision
 
Tree,
 
K-Nearest
 
Neighbors,
 
and
 
Support
 
Vector
 
Machine
 
algorithms
 
on
 
the
 
power
 
quality
 
data,
 
while
 
maintaining
 
comparable,
 
highly
 
competitive
 
results
 
on
 
the
 
fetal
 
dataset.
 
69  

=== PAGE 85 ===
 
Ultimately,  the  study  demonstrates  that  integrating  fuzzy,  relative  associations  via  Gaussian  
kernels
 
creates
 
a
 
highly
 
efficient,
 
CPU-friendly
 
standalone
 
model
 
for
 
continuous
 
signal
 
classification.
 
 
Decision  Tree-Based  Modeling  in  Mental  Health  Early  Warning  System  for  Higher  
Education
 
Students
 
In  their  study,  Jin  (2025)  addresses  the  escalating  mental  health  challenges  among  college  
students
 
by
 
developing
 
a

health
 
early

system

by

The

utilized

(GCBA)
 
to
 
extract

and

By
 
classifying

psychological

such
 
as
 
depression,
 
anxiety,
 
and
 
interpersonal

the
 
system
 
effectively
 
flags
 
at-risk
 
individuals
 
for
 
early
 
intervention.
 
Performance

demonstrated
 
that
 
the
 
decision

outperformed
 
other

models
 
like
 
Random
 
Forest
 
and
 
LightGBM,
 
achieving
 
a
 
notable
 
92.6%
 
accuracy
 
and
 
an
 
AUC
 
of
 
0.928.
 
This
 
literature
 
is
 
highly
 
relevant
 
to
 
the
 
ongoing
 
development
 
of
 
PsyClick,
 
as
 
it
 
validates
 
the
 
efficacy
 
of
 
utilizing
 
machine
 
learning
 
classifiers
 
to
 
process
 
complex
 
psychological
 
data
 
and
 
accurately
 
detect
 
depressive
 
conditions.
 
Furthermore,

successful
 
implementation
 
of
 
an
 
early
 
warning
 
architecture
 
provides
 
a
 
robust
 
methodological
 
reference
 
for
 
structuring

tailored
 
to

monitoring

in
 
student
 
populations
 
 
SYNTHESIS  
70  

=== PAGE 86 ===
 
The  Philippine  Mental  Health  Service  Gap  
The  mental  health  care  system  in  the  Philippines  exhibits  a  significant  service  gap,  as  
legislative
 
frameworks
 
are
 
in
 
place,
 
but
 
practical
 
support
 
remains
 
largely
 
inaccessible
 
for
 
most
 
of
 
the
 
population.
 
National
 
literature
 
highlights
 
a
 
marked
 
shortage
 
of
 
mental
 
health
 
specialists,
 
with
 
only
 
about
 
2,100
 
professionals
 
available
 
for
 
a
 
population
 
of
 
110
 
million.
 
Services
 
are
 
primarily
 
concentrated
 
in
 
major
 
hospitals,
 
rather
 
than
 
being
 
distributed
 
across
 
schools
 
or
 
local
 
communities.
 
As
 
a
 
result,
 
educational
 
institutions
 
encounter
 
considerable
 
challenges
 
in
 
meeting
 
personal
 
needs,
 
since
 
guidance
 
offices
 
often
 
lack
 
sufficient
 
funding
 
and
 
personnel.
 
Additionally,  cultural  barriers  such  as  self-stigma  and  the  fear  of  being  labeled  as  
mentally
 
ill
 
deter
 
individuals
 
from
 
seeking
 
assistance,
 
even
 
when
 
experiencing
 
distress.
 
Research
 
indicates
 
that
 
Filipinos
 
frequently
 
turn
 
to
 
family
 
or
 
friends
 
before
 
seeking
 
professional
 
help,
 
typically
 
only
 
doing
 
so
 
when
 
a
 
crisis
 
escalates.
 
This
 
context
 
highlights
 
the
 
necessity
 
for
 
community-based
 
interventions
 
and
 
affordable,
 
scalable
 
tools
 
that
 
operate
 
independently
 
of
 
continuous
 
specialist
 
supervision.
 
Psychomotor  Dynamics  as  Digital  Biomarkers  
To  overcome  the  challenges  posed  by  stigma  and  subjective  self-reporting,  
researchers
 
are
 
increasingly
 
utilizing
 
digital
 
phenotyping,
 
which
 
involves
 
analyzing
 
individuals'
 
interactions
 
with
 
technology
 
to
 
assess
 
mental
 
states.
 
Studies
 
in
 
behavioral
 
biometrics
 
have
 
demonstrated
 
that
 
keystroke
 
and
 
mouse
 
dynamics
 
are
 
not
 
random
 
movements
 
but
 
rather
 
idiosyncratic
 
patterns
 
indicative
 
of
 
internal
 
cognitive
 
and
 
motor
 
health.
 
71  

=== PAGE 87 ===
 
Mental  health  conditions  such  as  depression  and  anxiety  frequently  manifest  as  
psychomotor
 
retardation,
 
characterized
 
by
 
slowed
 
movements,
 
or
 
agitation,
 
marked
 
by
 
restlessness.
 
Literature
 
indicates
 
that
 
high-definition
 
data,
 
including
 
flight
 
time
 
(the
 
interval
 
between
 
key
 
presses)
 
and
 
cursor
 
jitter,
 
can
 
effectively
 
identify
 
mental
 
fatigue
 
and
 
cognitive
 
load.
 
By
 
conceptualizing
 
these
 
communications
 
as
 
cognitive
 
biometrics,
 
systems
 
can
 
monitor
 
mental
 
health
 
unobtrusively
 
and
 
detect
 
subtle
 
changes
 
that
 
may
 
be
 
overlooked
 
in
 
traditional
 
face-to-face
 
interviews.
 
Normative  Calibration  and  Individualized  Baseline  Modeling  
 Assessment  frameworks  in  behavioral  monitoring  generally  fall  into  two  categories:  
normative
 
and
 
ipsative.
 
Normative
 
assessment
 
evaluates
 
an
 
individual’s
 
performance
 
by
 
comparing
 
it
 
against
 
a
 
population
 
average,
 
essentially
 
measuring
 
where
 
a
 
person
 
stands
 
relative
 
to
 
others.
 
While
 
useful
 
for
 
broad
 
screening
 
and
 
selection
 
purposes,
 
this
 
approach
 
presents
 
a
 
critical
 
limitation
 
in
 
mental
 
health
 
monitoring,
 
it
 
fails
 
to
 
detect
 
meaningful
 
decline
 
in
 
high-functioning
 
adults
 
whose
 
performance
 
drops
 
significantly
 
yet
 
still
 
falls
 
within
 
the
 
average
 
range.
 
Furthermore,
 
research
 
confirms
 
that
 
demographic
 
factors
 
such
 
as
 
educational
 
background
 
and
 
cultural
 
context
 
substantially
 
influence
 
cognitive
 
and
 
psychomotor
 
benchmarks,
 
rendering
 
Western
 
normative
 
standards
 
particularly
 
inappropriate
 
for
 
local
 
Filipino
 
populations
 
where
 
no
 
locally
 
validated
 
typing
 
or
 
motor
 
norms
 
currently
 
exist.
 
 Ipsative  assessment  addresses  this  gap  by  using  an  individual’s  own  historical  
performance
 
as
 
the
 
reference
 
point,
 
measuring
 
change
 
relative
 
to
 
their
 
personal
 
baseline
 
rather
 
than
 
against
 
a
 
group.
 
This
 
self-referential
 
approach
 
is
 
far
 
more
 
sensitive
 
to
 
individual
 
deterioration
 
as
 
it
 
captures
 
the
 
"distance
 
travelled"
 
from
 
a
 
person's
 
own
 
norm
 
rather
 
than
 
their
 
position
 
within
 
a
 
population
 
distribution.
 
For
 
instance,
 
an
 
adult
 
who
 
typically
 
operates
 
72  

=== PAGE 88 ===
 
at  a  high  level  of  psychomotor  efficiency  will  register  a  clinically  meaningful  deviation  even  
if
 
their
 
declined
 
performance
 
still
 
appears
 
average
 
by
 
population
 
standards.
 
By
 
establishing
 
a
 
personal
 
behavioral
 
baseline
 
during
 
a
 
calibration
 
phase,
 
the
 
system
 
prioritizes
 
relative
 
psychomotor
 
change
 
over
 
absolute
 
performance,
 
significantly
 
improving
 
the
 
sensitivity
 
of
 
early
 
detection
 
for
 
adults
 
across
 
varying
 
ability
 
levels.
 
Algorithmic  Foundation  and  Signal  Processing  
The  technical  reliability  of  detecting  psychomotor  shifts  depends  on  a  structured  data  
pipeline
 
that
 
transforms
 
raw
 
hardware
 
signals
 
into
 
clinically
 
interpretable
 
outputs.
 
Literature
 
affirms
 
that
 
implementing
 
a
 
Hardware
 
Abstraction
 
Layer
 
(HAL)
 
is
 
critical
 
for
 
decoupling
 
the
 
software
 
from
 
specific
 
peripheral
 
devices,
 
ensuring
 
consistent
 
and
 
standardized
 
timing
 
data
 
regardless
 
of
 
whether
 
a
 
mechanical
 
or
 
membrane
 
keyboard
 
is
 
used.
 
High-frequency
 
cursor
 
noise
 
can
 
be
 
reduced
 
through
 
a
 
Sliding
 
Window
 
Filter,
 
which
 
preserves
 
clinically
 
relevant
 
hesitation
 
patterns
 
while
 
eliminating
 
artifacts
 
caused
 
by
 
natural
 
hand
 
tremors
 
or
 
hardware
 
latency.
 
For  anomaly  detection  and  temporal  signal  analysis,  the  Exponentially  Weighted  
Moving
 
Average
 
(EWMA)
 
control
 
chart
 
provides
 
a
 
mathematically
 
rigorous
 
foundation.
 
Unlike
 
single-point
 
deviation
 
methods,
 
EWMA
 
applies
 
exponentially
 
decreasing
 
weights
 
to
 
historical
 
observations,
 
making
 
it
 
exceptionally
 
sensitive
 
to
 
subtle,
 
gradual
 
drifts
 
in
 
behavior
 
—
 
precisely
 
the
 
kind
 
of
 
slow-onset
 
changes
 
characteristic
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance
 
in
 
depression
 
or
 
anxiety.
 
This
 
is
 
complemented
 
by
 
the
 
Weighted
 
Moving
 
Average
 
(WMA)
 
method,
 
which
 
enables
 
accurate
 
forecasting
 
and
 
smoothing
 
of
 
behavioral
 
trends
 
over
 
time.
 
For  classification  and  decision  support,  the  literature  validates  multiple  
complementary
 
approaches.
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
—
 
particularly
 
the
 
Mamdani
 
Fuzzy
 
Inference
 
73  

=== PAGE 89 ===
 
System  —  enables  graded,  human-like  reasoning  that  maps  continuous,  imprecise  behavioral  
signals
 
into
 
interpretable
 
clinical
 
categories
 
such
 
as
 
"Normal,"
 
"Borderline,"
 
and
 
"Severe,"
 
accounting
 
for
 
individual
 
variability
 
rather
 
than
 
enforcing
 
rigid
 
binary
 
thresholds.
 
The
 
Fuzzy
 
Gaussian
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
extends
 
this
 
further
 
by
 
using
 
Gaussian
 
kernels
 
to
 
perform
 
proximity-based
 
classification
 
of
 
continuous
 
signals,
 
outperforming
 
standard
 
decision
 
trees
 
on
 
comparable
 
datasets.
 
Additionally,
 
the
 
C4.5
 
Decision
 
Tree,
 
validated
 
in
 
mental
 
health
 
early
 
warning
 
systems
 
for
 
adult
 
populations,
 
demonstrates
 
strong
 
accuracy
 
in
 
classifying
 
psychological
 
conditions
 
from
 
behavioral
 
data
 
and
 
offers
 
transparent,
 
auditable
 
decision
 
pathways.
 
The
 
Random
 
Forest
 
classifier
 
has
 
likewise
 
been
 
validated
 
across
 
multiple
 
studies
 
for
 
stress
 
and
 
psychomotor
 
detection
 
tasks,
 
providing
 
robust
 
multi-feature
 
ensemble
 
classification.
 
Together,
 
these
 
algorithms
 
constitute
 
a
 
multi-layered
 
analytical
 
architecture
 
that
 
is
 
both
 
interpretable
 
and
 
clinically
 
meaningful,
 
avoiding
 
the
 
opacity
 
of
 
"black
 
box"
 
deep
 
learning
 
models
 
in
 
favor
 
of
 
explainable,
 
evidence-based
 
decision
 
support.
 
Privacy,  Security,  and  Offline  Architecture  
With  the  transition  of  mental  health  tools  to  digital  platforms,  user  mental  privacy  has  
emerged
 
as
 
a
 
critical
 
concern.
 
Numerous
 
health
 
applications
 
have
 
faced
 
criticism
 
for
 
sharing
 
sensitive
 
data
 
with
 
third
 
parties
 
or
 
lacking
 
transparent
 
privacy
 
policies.
 
Given
 
that
 
keystroke
 
dynamics
 
are
 
as
 
distinctive
 
as
 
fingerprints,
 
they
 
are
 
increasingly
 
recognized
 
as
 
confidential
 
biometric
 
data
 
requiring
 
protection
 
equivalent
 
to
 
that
 
of
 
medical
 
records.
 
To  mitigate  these  risks,  there  is  increasing  emphasis  on  Local-First  or  Offline-First  
system
 
architectures.
 
Processing
 
data
 
locally
 
on
 
standalone
 
desktop
 
applications,
 
rather
 
than
 
transmitting
 
it
 
to
 
the
 
cloud,
 
eliminates
 
the
 
danger
 
of
 
data
 
leaks
 
and
 
irreversible
 
transmission.
 
This
 
strategy
 
not
 
only
 
ensures
 
adherence
 
to
 
data
 
ethics
 
but
 
also
 
enhances
 
reliability
 
in
 
74  

=== PAGE 90 ===
 
regions  with  unstable  internet  connectivity,  thereby  providing  a  secure  and  dependable  
environment
 
for
 
sensitive
 
personal
 
information.
 
Decision  Support  and  Holistic  Counseling  
Contemporary  clinical  literature  emphasizes  that  behavioral  data  should  serve  to  
empower
 
practitioners
 
rather
 
than
 
replace
 
human
 
judgment.
 
At
 
present,
 
many
 
health
 
interventions
 
remain
 
reactive,
 
activated
 
only
 
after
 
a
 
crisis
 
becomes
 
visible.
 
However,
 
research
 
consistently
 
shows
 
that
 
individuals
 
with
 
mild-to-moderate
 
risk
 
profiles
 
frequently
 
conceal
 
their
 
distress,
 
rendering
 
them
 
undetectable
 
through
 
conventional
 
observation
 
alone.
 
Transdiagnostic
 
evidence
 
further
 
confirms
 
that
 
psychomotor
 
impairment
 
is
 
a
 
reliable
 
cross-diagnostic
 
marker
 
spanning
 
depression,
 
anxiety,
 
and
 
broader
 
psychiatric
 
conditions
 
—
 
making
 
behavioral
 
monitoring
 
broadly
 
applicable
 
across
 
adult
 
populations
 
rather
 
than
 
limited
 
to
 
a
 
single
 
diagnosis.
 
The  deployment  of  automated  dashboards  enables  counselors  to  transition  from  
subjective
 
impressions
 
to
 
the
 
objective
 
identification
 
of
 
service
 
gaps.
 
By
 
delivering
 
behavioral
 
insights,
 
these
 
systems
 
serve
 
as
 
decision-support
 
tools
 
that
 
help
 
counselors
 
prioritize
 
individuals
 
at
 
greatest
 
risk.
 
This
 
multidisciplinary
 
approach,
 
which
 
combines
 
computer
 
science
 
and
 
psychology,
 
repositions
 
the
 
counseling
 
office
 
as
 
a
 
proactive
 
center
 
for
 
holistic
 
well-being
 
rather
 
than
 
a
 
resource
 
accessed
 
only
 
during
 
crises.
 
 
75  

=== PAGE 91 ===
 
Chapter  3  
METHODOLOGY  
3.1  Type  of  Research  
The  primary  objective  of  this  research  is  to  design  and  construct  PsyClick:  Clinical  
Decision
 
Support
 
System
 
for
 
Detecting
 
Single-Session
 
Psychomotor
 
Disturbances
 
via
 
EWMA-Based
 
Multivariate
 
Analysis
 
of
 
Keystroke
 
and
 
Cursor
 
Dynamics,
 
a
 
novel
 
software
 
artifact
 
intended
 
to
 
address
 
a
 
specific
 
gap
 
in
 
mental
 
health
 
assessment.
 
This
 
study
 
utilizes
 
a
 
Quantitative
 
Developmental
 
Research
 
approach.
 
According
 
to
 
Kumar
 
(2023),
 
developmental
 
research
 
is
 
a
 
systematic
 
process
 
focused
 
on
 
designing
 
and
 
creating
 
products
 
to
 
ensure
 
their
 
effectiveness.
 
The
 
primary
 
objective
 
of
 
this
 
research
 
is
 
to
 
design
 
and
 
construct
 
the
 
PsyClick
 
Clinical
 
Decision-Support
 
System,
 
a
 
novel
 
software
 
artifact
 
intended
 
to
 
address
 
a
 
specific
 
gap
 
in
 
mental
 
health
 
assessment.
 
The
 
research
 
team
 
is
 
developing
 
a
 
tool
 
that
 
does
 
not
 
currently
 
exist
 
in
 
this
 
context,
 
transitioning
 
from
 
theoretical
 
frameworks
 
of
 
digital
 
phenotyping
 
to
 
a
 
functional
 
prototype.
 
The  research  is  quantitative  in  nature,  as  it  relies  exclusively  on  numerical  data,  
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
foundational
 
principles
 
of
 
quantitative
 
research.
 
Stanat
 
(2025)
 
defines
 
quantitative
 
research
 
as
 
a
 
methodical
 
approach
 
to
 
data
 
collection
 
and
 
analysis
 
that
 
emphasizes
 
numerical,
 
quantifiable
 
data.
 
The
 
system
 
extracts
 
specific
 
digital
 
biomarkers
 
—
 
including
 
flight
 
time
 
(measured
 
in
 
milliseconds)
 
and
 
cursor
 
velocity
 
(measured
 
in
 
pixels
 
per
 
second).
 
The
 
evaluation
 
process
 
involves
 
analyzing
 
statistical
 
correlations
 
between
 
these
 
machine-generated
 
scores
 
and
 
numerical
 
results
 
from
 
established
 
survey
 
instruments,
 
specifically
 
the
 
PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7.
 
This
 
methodology
 
enables
 
the
 
study
 
to
 
move
 
beyond
 
subjective
 
observation
 
and
 
provide
 
objective,
 
data-driven
 
evidence.
 
76  

=== PAGE 92 ===
 
 
3.2  Research  Design  
A  descriptive  correlational  research  design  will  be  employed.  A  descriptive  
correlational
 
research
 
design
 
enables
 
the
 
identification
 
of
 
associations
 
between
 
variables
 
without
 
manipulation
 
or
 
experimental
 
intervention.
 
This
 
methodology
 
facilitates
 
the
 
observation
 
and
 
examination
 
of
 
interactions
 
among
 
two
 
or
 
more
 
variables
 
within
 
their
 
natural
 
environments
 
(Barooah,
 
2025).
 
The  correlational  component  is  essential  for  system  validation.  The  study  seeks  to  
determine
 
the
 
relationship
 
between
 
two
 
primary
 
variables:
 
the
 
system-calculated
 
scores
 
for
 
psychomotor
 
deviation
 
(the
 
software's
 
T²
 
outputs)
 
and
 
the
 
results
 
of
 
recognized
 
clinical
 
screening
 
instruments
 
(PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7).
 
Employing
 
a
 
correlational
 
design
 
allows
 
for
 
a
 
mathematical
 
demonstration
 
of
 
the
 
software's
 
concurrent
 
validity
 
—
 
its
 
accuracy
 
in
 
detecting
 
distress
 
relative
 
to
 
established
 
self-report
 
measures
 
—
 
without
 
requiring
 
experimental
 
manipulation
 
of
 
the
 
participants'
 
mental
 
states.
 
 
3.3  Project  Design  and  System  Architecture  
3.3.1  Activity  /  Sequence  Diagram  
 
77  

=== PAGE 93 ===
 
Figure  4.  Activity  Diagram  
The  operational  logic  of  PsyClick  is  structured  into  two  primary  phases:  Calibration  
and
 
Intake,
 
and
 
the
 
Active
 
Assessment
 
Engine.
 
This
 
workflow
 
is
 
designed
 
to
 
deliver
 
objective,
 
in-session
 
psychomotor
 
data
 
that
 
the
 
attending
 
mental
 
health
 
professional
 
can
 
use
 
to
 
verify
 
or
 
validate
 
their
 
independent
 
clinical
 
observations
 
of
 
the
 
patient's
 
behavioral
 
state.
 
 
The  workflow  commences  with  Clinician  Authentication,  during  which  the  mental  
health
 
professional
 
logs
 
in
 
to
 
access
 
secure
 
local
 
records.
 
After
 
authentication,
 
the
 
system
 
determines
 
whether
 
the
 
session
 
pertains
 
to
 
a
 
new
 
or
 
an
 
existing
 
patient.
 
For
 
new
 
patients,
 
the
 
Patient
 
Intake
 
and
 
Consent
 
process
 
is
 
completed.
 
For
 
existing
 
clients,
 
prior
 
session
 
records
 
78  


=== PAGE 94 ===
 
are  retrieved  for  contextual  reference.  In  both  cases,  the  Calibration  Phase  is  initiated  at  the  
start
 
of
 
every
 
session,
 
as
 
PsyClick
 
operates
 
on
 
a
 
strictly
 
within-session
 
ipsative
 
architecture:
 
no
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
is
 
carried
 
over
 
from
 
a
 
previous
 
session,
 
and
 
the
 
EWMA
 
state
 
is
 
held
 
exclusively
 
in
 
session
 
memory
 
and
 
discarded
 
upon
 
session
 
termination.
 
In
 
this
 
phase,
 
the
 
adult
 
client
 
performs
 
neutral
 
typing
 
and
 
mouse
 
navigation
 
tasks.
 
The
 
Baseline
 
Processing
 
unit
 
constructs
 
the
 
patient’s
 
personal
 
behavioral
 
baseline
 
—
 
mean
 
vector
 
(μ)
 
and
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
(S)
 
—
 
across
 
eight
 
psychomotor
 
biomarkers
 
using
 
the
 
Within-Session
 
Exponentially
 
Weighted
 
Moving
 
Average
 
(EWMA)
 
algorithm,
 
storing
 
these
 
values
 
in
 
session
 
memory
 
to
 
establish
 
the
 
individualized
 
norm
 
against
 
which
 
subsequent
 
assessment-phase
 
deviations
 
are
 
measured.
 
 
After  the  baseline  is  established,  the  system  initiates  the  Active  Assessment  Phase.  In  
this
 
phase,
 
the
 
Dynamics
 
Logger
 
continuously
 
records
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
events
 
in
 
the
 
background
 
as
 
the
 
patient
 
completes
 
the
 
designated
 
assessment
 
tasks
 
—
 
including
 
guided
 
text-copying,
 
stimulus-response
 
activities,
 
and
 
the
 
primary
 
clinical
 
stimulus:
 
graduated
 
free-text
 
microstressor
 
composition
 
prompts
 
organized
 
across
 
four
 
stressor
 
domains
 
(Time/Workload,
 
Interpersonal,
 
Academic/Performance,
 
and
 
Self-Evaluation)
 
at
 
three
 
difficulty
 
levels
 
(A,
 
B,
 
and
 
C).
 
The
 
Feature
 
Extractor
 
processes
 
these
 
raw
 
timestamped
 
events
 
into
 
millisecond-level
 
clinical
 
metrics.
 
The
 
Analytical
 
Engine
 
compares
 
these
 
real-time
 
features
 
against
 
the
 
stored
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
using
 
a
 
multi-stage
 
statistical
 
pipeline,
 
computing
 
Hotelling's
 
T²
 
statistic
 
to
 
evaluate
 
all
 
eight
 
extracted
 
psychomotor
 
biomarkers
 
simultaneously
 
as
 
a
 
single
 
multivariate
 
observation,
 
quantifying
 
the
 
degree
 
of
 
behavioral
 
deviation
 
from
 
the
 
patient's
 
own
 
within-session
 
norm.
 
79  

=== PAGE 95 ===
 
 
Feature  Contribution  Analysis  is  subsequently  applied  to  decompose  the  T²  score  into  
directional
 
indices,
 
identifying
 
whether
 
the
 
detected
 
anomaly
 
reflects
 
psychomotor
 
slowing
 
(Psychomotor
 
Slowing
 
Index,
 
PSI)
 
or
 
psychomotor
 
agitation
 
(Psychomotor
 
Agitation
 
Index,
 
PAI).
 
A
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classifier
 
then
 
assigns
 
graded
 
membership
 
degrees
 
to
 
clinical
 
categories,
 
producing
 
confidence-weighted
 
classifications
 
that
 
reflect
 
the
 
continuous
 
nature
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance.
 
A
 
Heuristic
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
maps
 
these
 
outputs
 
to
 
a
 
final
 
clinical
 
flag:
 
a
 
Green
 
(Normal
 
Baseline)
 
flag
 
is
 
assigned
 
if
 
the
 
T²
 
score
 
falls
 
within
 
the
 
statistically
 
normal
 
range;
 
an
 
Amber
 
(Unusual
 
Deviation)
 
flag
 
is
 
triggered
 
for
 
moderate
 
statistical
 
deviation;
 
and
 
a
 
Red
 
(Extreme
 
Deviation)
 
flag
 
is
 
generated
 
for
 
significant
 
psychomotor
 
anomaly
 
warranting
 
clinical
 
attention.
 
The
 
process
 
concludes
 
with
 
the
 
Generate
 
Clinical
 
Report
 
function,
 
which
 
presents
 
the
 
attending
 
mental
 
health
 
professional
 
with
 
a
 
dashboard
 
of
 
T²
 
scores,
 
PSI/PAI
 
indices,
 
Fuzzy
 
confidence
 
scores,
 
domain-segmented
 
stressor
 
profiles,
 
per-question
 
behavioral
 
snapshots,
 
hover-word
 
reading
 
latency
 
data,
 
and
 
a
 
plain-language
 
clinical
 
rationale
 
—
 
providing
 
objective
 
behavioral
 
evidence
 
that
 
the
 
clinician
 
may
 
use
 
to
 
verify,
 
contextualize,
 
or
 
validate
 
their
 
own
 
observational
 
impressions
 
of
 
the
 
client’s
 
psychomotor
 
state
 
during
 
that
 
session.
 
The
 
clinician
 
may
 
then
 
reset
 
the
 
session
 
for
 
further
 
assessment
 
or
 
end
 
the
 
process
 
to
 
finalize
 
the
 
local
 
record.
