=== PAGE 96 ===
 
3.3.2  Use  Case  Diagram  
 
Figure  5.  Use-Case  Diagram  
The  PsyClick  Use-Case  Diagram  illustrates  the  functional  interactions  between  the  
system's
 
human
 
actors
 
and
 
its
 
automated
 
backend
 
processes.
 
The
 
system
 
involves
 
three
 
human
 
actors
 
and
 
one
 
automated
 
system
 
actor.
 
The
 
Mental
 
Health
 
Professional
 
(Clinician)
 
acts
 
as
 
the
 
primary
 
clinical
 
administrator
 
and
 
end-recipient
 
of
 
objective
 
psychomotor
 
evidence
 
for
 
in-session
 
decision
 
support.
 
The
 
Adult
 
Patient
 
provides
 
raw
 
behavioral
 
biomarkers
 
through
 
natural
 
computer
 
interaction
 
during
 
the
 
administered
 
session.
 
The
 
Normative
 
Tester
 
is
 
a
 
third
 
human
 
role:
 
a
 
research
 
participant
 
who
 
completes
 
the
 
same
 
session
 
protocol
 
specifically
 
to
 
contribute
 
behavioral
 
data
 
to
 
the
 
aggregate
 
normative
 
baseline,
 
without
 
a
 
clinical
 
purpose
 
—
 
their
 
data
 
is
 
stored
 
in
 
the
 
normative_sessions
 
and
 
normative_stats
 
database
 
tables
 
and
 
used
 
to
 
compute
 
population-level
 
reference
 
statistics.
 
The
 
PsyClick
 
System
 
acts
 
as
 
the
 
automated
 
backend
 
actor
 
representing
 
the
 
detection
 
and
 
81  


=== PAGE 97 ===
 
analysis  engine.  The  internal  use  cases  are  organized  into  distinct  operational  clusters  to  
reflect
 
the
 
system's
 
"Security
 
by
 
Design"
 
and
 
"Ipsative
 
Assessment"
 
philosophy.
 
 
The  procedure  starts  with  the  Session  Initialization  cluster,  where  the  Mental  Health  
Professional
 
initiates
 
a
 
Login
 
that
 
strictly
 
«includes»

to

sensitive

This

Patient

«includes»
 
the

Signing
 
of
 
Digital
 
Consent
 
to
 
ensure
 
compliance
 
with

Privacy
 
Act
 
(RA

Once

Patient
 
moves
 
into
 
the

cluster
 
to

that

Both

«include»

Keystrokes
 
and
 
Cursor
 
Dynamics
 
without

the

natural

Following  data  collection,  the  Behavioral  Analysis  Engine  cluster  is  triggered.  The  
system

Extraction
 
to

(including

Ipsative
 
Analysis

«includes»

of
 
Hotelling's
 
T²
 
against
 
the

within-session
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
and
 
domain-segmented
 
T²
 
profiling
 
across
 
the
 
four
 
microstressor
 
groups;
 
if

accesses  the  View  Clinical  Dashboard  to  review  objective  psychomotor  findings  alongside

3.3.3  Class  Diagram  
 
Figure  6.  Class  Diagram  
The  PsyClick  Class  Diagram  outlines  the  system's  static  structure  and  object-oriented

The
 
SessionManager
 
oversees
 
the
 
full
 
assessment
 
lifecycle
 
—
 
from
 
clinician
 
83

authentication  and  patient  intake  to  session  termination  —  and  launches  the  
ScreeningModule
 
to
 
collect
 
self-report
 
anchors
 
(PHQ-9
 
and

that
 
serve
 
as

for

During  each  session,  the  EventLogger  records  high-frequency  keystroke  and  cursor

into

The

The  AnomalyEngine  centralizes  baseline  modeling  and  the  full  detection  pipeline.  It  
implements

algorithm
 
to

(μ)

(S)

Using

Hotelling's
 
T²
 
via

Ledoit-Wolf
 
covariance
 
estimator
 
to
 
detect
 
multivariate

anomalies,
 
and

computation
 
—
 
grouping
 
assessment
 
windows
 
by
 
stressor
 
domain
 
and

level
 
to
 
produce
 
a

interpretable

of
 
which
 
microstressor
 
categories
 
activated
 
the

deviation.
 
Feature

Analysis
 
decomposes

(PSI)

flags.  The  ReportGenerator  then  compiles  these  outputs  —  including  T²  scores,  PSI/PAI

3.3.3  System  Architecture  
 
Figure  7  .  System  Architecture  
86  


=== PAGE 102 ===
 
Figure  7  illustrates  the  Layered  System  Architecture  of  PsyClick,  which  is  designed  
to
 
ensure
 
modularity,
 
high-resolution
 
data
 
fidelity,
 
and
 
strict
 
adherence
 
to
 
the
 
“Local-First”
 
privacy
 
standard.
 
The
 
architecture
 
is
 
organized
 
into
 
four
 
distinct
 
layers:
 
the
 
Presentation
 
Layer,
 
Application
 
Logic
 
Layer,
 
Data
 
Persistence
 
Layer,
 
and
 
Infrastructure
 
Layer.
 
This
 
hierarchical
 
structure
 
ensures
 
that
 
high-frequency
 
data-capture
 
processes
 
remain
 
decoupled
 
from
 
the
 
user
 
interface,
 
preventing
 
system
 
latency
 
from
 
compromising
 
the
 
accuracy
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
timestamps.
 
1.  Presentation  Layer  (UI):  
This
 
top-level
 
layer
 
serves
 
as
 
the
 
interface
 
for
 
the
 
two
 
primary
 
actors:
 
the
 
Mental
 
Health
 
Professional
 
and
 
the
 
Adult
 
Patient.
 
Built
 
using
 
React
 
18
 
and
 
Electron
 
29,
 
it
 
provides
 
two
 
distinct
 
views.
 
The
 
Clinician
 
Dashboard
 
provides
 
secure
 
login,
 
patient
 
profile
 
management,
 
and
 
full
 
visualization
 
of
 
the
 
session's
 
clinical
 
outputs
 
—
 
including
 
T²
 
scores
 
with
 
their
 
corresponding
 
F-distribution
 
p-values,
 
PSI/PAI
 
indices,
 
color-coded
 
clinical
 
flags
 
(Green,
 
Amber,
 
Red),
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
confidence
 
scores,
 
domain-segmented
 
stressor
 
profiles
 
across
 
the
 
four
 
microstressor
 
groups
 
and
 
three
 
difficulty
 
levels,
 
and
 
per-question
 
flight
 
time
 
and
 
response
 
timing
 
data
 
—
 
to
 
support
 
in-session
 
clinical
 
decision-making
 
and
 
the
 
verification
 
of
 
the
 
clinician's
 
independent
 
observational
 
impressions
 
of
 
the
 
patient's
 
psychomotor
 
state.
 
The
 
Patient
 
Assessment
 
View
 
provides
 
a
 
controlled,
 
distraction-free
 
interface
 
for
 
the
 
adult
 
patient
 
to
 
complete
 
the
 
standardized
 
three-task
 
sequence:
 
Task
 
1
 
(guided
 
text-copying),
 
Task
 
2
 
(simple
 
stimulus-response),
 
and
 
Task
 
3
 
(graduated
 
free-text
 
microstressor
 
composition
 
prompts
 
across
 
four
 
thematic
 
domains
 
and
 
three
 
difficulty
 
levels).
 
This
 
layer
 
handles
 
87  

=== PAGE 103 ===
 
all  user  inputs  and  renders  all  visual  feedback  but  performs  no  computational  or  
statistical
 
logic
 
—
 
all
 
processing
 
is
 
fully
 
delegated
 
to
 
the
 
Application
 
Logic
 
Layer.
 
2.  Application  Logic  Layer  (Backend):  
This
 
layer
 
orchestrates
 
the
 
data
 
processing
 
pipeline.
 
It
 
contains
 
four
 
integrated
 
modules:
 
2  Event  Listener:  Uses  the  pynput  library  in  combination  with  the  Win32  API  
(pywin32)
 
on
 
a
 
dedicated
 
background
 
thread
 
to
 
capture
 
raw
 
hardware
 
interrupts
 
—
 
key-down,
 
key-up,
 
mouse-move,
 
mouse-click,
 
and
 
mouse-scroll
 
events
 
—
 
with
 
millisecond-precision
 
timestamps.
 
Running
 
asynchronously
 
and
 
independently
 
of
 
the
 
UI
 
rendering
 
cycle,
 
this
 
module
 
ensures
 
that
 
no
 
interaction
 
event
 
is
 
dropped
 
or
 
delayed
 
due
 
to
 
interface
 
redraws,
 
preserving
 
the
 
temporal
 
integrity
 
of
 
the
 
psychomotor
 
data
 
stream.
 
3  Hardware  Abstraction  Layer  (HAL):  This  normalizes  interaction  metrics  
across
 
diverse
 
input
 
devices
 
to
 
ensure
 
hardware-agnostic
 
measurement.
 
The
 
HAL
 
auto-detects
 
the
 
keyboard
 
type
 
(mechanical
 
or
 
membrane),
 
measures
 
the
 
device's
 
actual
 
polling
 
rate,
 
and
 
applies
 
a
 
grid-alignment
 
Jitter
 
Compensation
 
algorithm
 
adapted
 
from
 
Cervin
 
et
 
al.
 
(2004):
 
t_norm
 
=
 
t_raw
 
−
 
(t_raw
 
mod
 
P_target)
 
+
 
δ_
latency,
 
where
 
t_raw
 
is
 
the
 
raw
 
OS
 
timestamp,
 
P_target
 
is
 
the
 
target
 
polling
 
period,
 
and
 
δ_
latency
 
is
 
the
 
average
 
hardware
 
latency
 
offset
 
measured
 
during
 
the
 
calibration
 
phase.
 
This
 
formula
 
snaps
 
noisy
 
timestamps
 
to
 
a
 
standardized
 
temporal
 
grid,
 
neutralizing
 
device-induced
 
timing
 
artifacts
 
before
 
any
 
feature
 
extraction
 
or
 
statistical
 
analysis
 
is
 
applied.
 
Device-specific
 
88  

=== PAGE 104 ===
 
correction  factors  are  stored  in  the  Device  Profile  database  for  reference  and  
audit
 
purposes.
 
4  Feature  Extraction  Engine:  This  parses  the  HAL-normalized  event  stream  
and
 
the
 
Sliding
 
Window-filtered
 
cursor
 
coordinates
 
to
 
compute
 
the
 
eight
 
core
 
psychomotor
 
biomarkers
 
per
 
sliding
 
window:
 
four
 
keystroke
 
metrics
 
—
 
Flight
 
Time
 
(inter-key
 
latency
 
in
 
ms),
 
Dwell
 
Time
 
(key-press
 
duration
 
in
 
ms),
 
Typing
 
Velocity
 
(characters
 
per
 
second),
 
and
 
Error
 
Rate
 
(proportion
 
of
 
backspace
 
events
 
relative
 
to
 
total
 
keystrokes,
 
0–1)
 
—
 
and
 
four
 
cursor
 
kinematics
 
—
 
Path
 
Entropy
 
(Shannon
 
entropy
 
of
 
discretized
 
trajectory
 
angles
 
in
 
bits),
 
Cursor
 
Velocity
 
(mean
 
tangential
 
velocity
 
in
 
pixels
 
per
 
second),
 
Jerk
 
(third
 
derivative
 
of
 
cursor
 
position
 
in
 
px/s³,
 
derived
 
via
 
successive
 
differentiation
 
through
 
Acceleration
 
as
 
an
 
intermediate
 
state),
 
and
 
Pause
 
Frequency
 
(count
 
of
 
movement
 
cessations
 
exceeding
 
500
 
ms
 
per
 
minute).
 
Prior
 
to
 
cursor
 
feature
 
computation,
 
the
 
module
 
applies
 
a
 
Sliding
 
Window
 
Filter
 
using
 
a
 
5-sample
 
Gaussian-approximate
 
weighted
 
kernel
 
[0.1,
 
0.2,
 
0.4,
 
0.2,
 
0.1]
 
that
 
attenuates
 
high-frequency
 
hardware
 
noise
 
above
 
8
 
Hz
 
while
 
preserving
 
clinically
 
relevant
 
low-frequency
 
hesitation
 
patterns
 
below
 
2
 
Hz.
 
For
 
Task
 
3
 
windows,
 
each
 
extracted
 
feature
 
vector
 
x
 
is
 
tagged
 
at
 
capture
 
time
 
with
 
a
 
domain
 
context
 
object
 
specifying
 
the
 
task
 
ID,
 
item
 
ID
 
(e.g.,
 
'C2'),
 
group
 
ID
 
(1–4),
 
difficulty
 
level
 
('A',
 
'B',
 
or
 
'C'),
 
and
 
domain
 
label
 
(e.g.,
 
'interpersonal'),
 
enabling
 
post-session
 
domain-segmented
 
T²
 
profiling
 
without
 
requiring
 
real-time
 
pipeline
 
segmentation.
 
89  

=== PAGE 105 ===
 
5  Statistical  Analysis  Engine:  During  the  Calibration  Phase,  this  module  
applies
 
the
 
Within-Session
 
EWMA
 
algorithm
 
(λ
 
=
 
0.2)
 
to
 
construct
 
an
 
adaptive
 
personalized
 
baseline
 
across
 
all
 
eight
 
features,
 
producing
 
a
 
mean
 
vector
 
μ
 
and
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
S
 
held
 
in
 
session
 
memory.
 
During
 
the
 
Assessment
 
Phase,
 
it
 
computes
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
—
 
T²
 
=
 
n
 
·
 
(x
 
−
 
μ_
ewma)ᵀ
 
·
 
S
⁻
¹
 
·
 
(x
 
−
 
μ_
ewma)
 
—
 
using
 
a
 
Ledoit-Wolf
 
shrinkage
 
estimator
 
operating
 
in
 
correlation
 
space
 
(α
 
=
 
0.15
 
when
 
n
 
<
 
5p;
 
α
 
=
 
0.05
 
when
 
n
 
≥
 
5p),
 
evaluated
 
against
 
an
 
F-distribution
 
threshold
 
at
 
α
 
=
 
0.05
 
using
 
the
 
Tracy
 
et
 
al.
 
(1992)
 
formula
 
T²_threshold
 
=
 
[p(n−1)/(n−p)]
 
×
 
F(
α;
 
p,
 
n−p).
 
When
 
n
 
≤
 
p
 
(fewer
 
calibration
 
windows
 
than
 
features,
 
i.e.,
 
n
 
≤
 
8),
 
the
 
F-distribution
 
degrees
 
of
 
freedom
 
are
 
undefined;
 
the
 
system
 
falls
 
back
 
to
 
the
 
chi-squared
 
critical
 
value
 
χ²(0.95;
 
p=8)
 
≈
 
15.51,
 
which
 
is
 
the
 
large-sample
 
limiting
 
distribution
 
of
 
T²
 
under
 
multivariate
 
normality
 
and
 
remains
 
valid
 
when
 
Ledoit-Wolf
 
shrinkage
 
guarantees
 
matrix
 
invertibility.
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
Analysis
 
decomposes
 
each
 
flagged
 
T²
 
score
 
into
 
the
 
Psychomotor
 
Slowing
 
Index
 
(PSI),
 
aggregating
 
contributions
 
from
 
Flight
 
Time,
 
Dwell
 
Time,
 
and
 
Pause
 
Frequency,
 
and
 
the
 
Psychomotor
 
Agitation
 
Index
 
(PAI),
 
aggregating
 
contributions
 
from
 
Path
 
Entropy,
 
Jerk,
 
Error
 
Rate,
 
Cursor
 
Velocity,
 
and
 
Typing
 
Velocity.
 
A
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classifier
 
fuzzifies
 
the
 
T²,
 
PSI,
 
and
 
PAI
 
values
 
into
 
graded
 
clinical
 
memberships
 
(Normal,
 
Borderline,
 
Concerning,
 
Severe)
 
and
 
evaluates
 
seven
 
priority-ordered
 
IF-THEN
 
rules
 
via
 
a
 
min-conjunction
 
inference
 
architecture;
 
the
 
dominant
 
rule’s
 
firing
 
strength
 
relative
 
to
 
total
 
strength
 
across
 
all
 
rules
 
yields
 
the
 
crisp
 
confidence
 
score.
 
A
 
Heuristic
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
maps
 
this
 
output
 
90  

=== PAGE 106 ===
 
to  the  final  clinical  flag:  Green  when  T²  ratio  ≤  1.0  (within  threshold);  Amber  
when
 
borderline-class
 
rule
 
strength
 
exceeds
 
severe-class
 
rule
 
strength;
 
Red

severe-class
 
rule
 
strength
 
equals

exceeds

strength.
 
Post-session,

performs
 
domain-segmented

by

windows

and

producing
 
per-question
 
behavioral
 
snapshots
 
and
 
level-resolved

averages.
 
-  Data  Persistence  Layer  (Storage):  
 
A
 
local
 
SQLite
 
database
 
is
 
maintained
 
as

The

μ
 
and

matrix

held
 
exclusively
 
in

fully

upon

ensuring
 
no

data
 
persists
 
beyond

-  Infrastructure  Layer  (Hardware):  
 
The
 
physical
 
hardware
 
environment
 
serves
 
as
 
the
 
foundational

layer.
 
A
 
multi-core
 
processor
 
(Intel
 
i3/Ryzen
 
3

supports
 
real-time
 
parallel
 
execution
 
of
 
the
 
background

and
 
the

pipeline,
 
which
 
achieves
 
O(N)
 
total
 
session
 
complexity
 
due
 
to
 
the
 
O(1)
 
per-event
 
cost
 
of
 
both
 
the
 
EWMA
 
update
 
and
 
the
 
T²
 
computation.
 
A
 
high-precision
 
optical
 
mouse
 
and
 
standard

serve

the

sensor
 
inputs,
 
with
 
their

The
 
system
 
operates
 
on
 
a
 
91  

=== PAGE 107 ===
 
Windows  64-bit  environment,  leveraging  OS-level  drivers  via  pynput  and  the  Win32  
API
 
to

3.4  Hardware  and  Software  Specifications  
3.4.1  Hardware  Specification  
The  hardware  selection  for  PsyClick  is  motivated  by  the  need  for  
high-precision,
 
millisecond-level
 
data
 
acquisition.
 
To
 
accurately
 
operationalize
 
the
 
Psychomotor-Cognitive
 
Link,
 
the
 
system
 
must
 
minimize
 
input
 
lag
 
and
 
processing
 
latency,
 
ensuring
 
that
 
“noise”
 
is
 
not
 
mistaken
 
for
 
clinical
 
discomfort.

RAM  8  GB  DDR4  16  GB  DDR4  or  higher  
Storage  5  GB  10  GB

High-precision  optical  mouse  (1600+  DPI),  Mechanical  keyboard  with  1000Hz  polling  rate  
GPU  Integrated  Graphics  Card  NVIDIA  GTX  1650  /  RTX  3050  (or  equivalent)  for  visualization  rendering  
Display  1366×768  resolution  1920×1080  resolution  or  higher

3.5  Method  in  Developing  Software  
3.5.1  Agile  Process  /  Scrum  
 
Figure   8.  Agile  Scrum  Process  Diagram  (Atlassian,  n.d.)  
The  researchers  implemented  the  Agile  Scrum  Framework  to  manage  the  
software
 
development
 
lifecycle,
 
prioritizing
 
iterative
 
progress,
 
clinical
 
accuracy,
 
and
 
continuous
 
stakeholder
 
feedback.
 
This
 
methodology
 
ensured
 
the
 
system
 
remained
 
adaptable
 
to
 
technical
 
refinements
 
in
 
behavioral
 
feature
 
extraction.
 
The
 
development
 
was
 
organized
 
into
 
four
 
iterative
 
phases.
 
 
  
The  Requirements  Planning  phase  involved  extensive  collaboration  with  
stakeholders
 
to
 
establish
 
and
 
prioritize
 
the
 
project’s
 
Product
 
Backlog.
 
The
 
primary
 
focus
 
was
 
on
 
critical
 
technical
 
functionalities,
 
including
 
the
 
PHQ-9/GAD-7
 
screening
 
module,
 
millisecond-precision
 
keystroke
 
logging,
 
Hardware
 
Abstraction
 
Layer
 
(HAL)
 
for
 
device
 
normalization,
 
and
 
real-time
 
multivariate
 
anomaly
 
detection
 
with
 
95  


=== PAGE 111 ===
 
visualization  capabilities.  By  isolating  these  core  features  early,  the  team  ensured  that  
non-essential
 
UI/UX
 
elements
 
did
 
not
 
detract
 
from
 
the
 
system’s
 
primary
 
clinical
 
objectives.
 
 
During  the  User  Design  phase,  the  researchers  prioritized  speed  and

These

In  the  Construction  Phase,  development  was  structured  into  two-week

This

3.5.2  Algorithm  
The  PsyClick  system  uses  a  Multi-Stage  Statistical  Anomaly  Detection  
Pipeline
 
that
 
identifies
 
psychomotor
 
disturbances
 
by
 
modeling
 
behavioral
 
data
 
using
 
multivariate
 
probability
 
distributions
 
and
 
graded
 
fuzzy
 
inference.
 
This
 
approach
 
assumes
 
that
 
psychomotor
 
distress
 
is
 
distinct
 
from
 
an
 
individual’s
 
typical
 
motor
 
rhythm
 
and
 
can
 
be
 
identified
 
as
 
a
 
statistical
 
outlier
 
across
 
the
 
full
 
feature
 
space.
 
Unlike
 
single-metric
 
approaches,
 
PsyClick
 
evaluates
 
all
 
eight
 
biometric
 
features
 
simultaneously,
 
accounts
 
for
 
the
 
natural
 
correlations
 
between
 
them,
 
and
 
produces
 
a
 
clinically
 
interpretable,
 
graded
 
output
 
rather
 
than
 
a
 
binary
 
flag.
 
1  Hardware  Abstraction  Layer  (HAL)  Algorithm  
To  ensure  hardware-agnostic  measurement,  PsyClick  implements  a  
Jitter
 
Compensation
 
algorithm
 
as
 
the
 
foundational
 
preprocessing
 
stage.
 
This
 
97  

=== PAGE 113 ===
 
approach  operationalizes  the  findings  of  Killourhy  and  Maxion  (2009),  who  
demonstrated
 
that
 
operating
 
system
 
clock
 
resolution
 
introduces
 
“quantization
 
noise”
 
that
 
degrades
 
behavioral
 
analysis.
 
Furthermore,
 
they
 
established
 
that
 
varying
 
sampling
 
rates
 
across
 
devices
 
create
 
inconsistencies
 
in
 
dwell-time
 
measurement.
 
Without
 
this
 
normalization,
 
downstream
 
statistical
 
algorithms
 
would
 
measure
 
hardware
 
variation
 
rather
 
than
 
human
 
psychomotor
 
behavior.
 
  
To  mitigate  these  hardware-dependent  variables,  the  system  applies  a  
grid-alignment
 
formula
 
adapted
 
from
 
real-time
 
jitter
 
compensation
 
techniques
 
(Cervin
 
et
 
al.,
 
2004):
 
t
norm
 =  t
raw
 
-  (t
raw
 
 (
mod
 P
target
))  +  δ
latency
 
Where:  
1.  t
norm
 :  The  standardized  timestamp  passed  to  the  analysis  engine  
2.  t
raw
:  The  raw  timestamp  received  from  the  Operating  System  
3.  P
target
 :  The  target  polling  period  
4.  (mod)  :  The  modulo  operator,  isolating  the  jitter  variance  
5.  δ
latency
 
:  The  average  hardware  latency  offset  derived  during  the  calibration  
phase
 
 
The  HAL  auto-detects  keyboard  type  (mechanical/membrane),  
measures
 
actual
 
polling
 
rate,
 
and
 
stores
 
device-specific
 
correction
 
factors
 
that
 
compensate
 
for
 
debounce
 
delays
 
and
 
polling
 
rate
 
differences.
 
This
 
98  

=== PAGE 114 ===
 
aligns  noisy  timestamps  to  a  standardized  temporal  grid,  neutralizing  
hardware-induced
 
irregularities.
 
For
 
example,
 
with
 
a
 
1
 
ms
 
target
 
polling
 
period,
 
a
 
raw
 
event
 
arriving
 
at
 
10,005.7
 
ms
 
yields
 
a
 
remainder
 
of
 
0.7
 
ms
 
(10,005.7
 
mod
 
1).
 
Subtracting
 
this
 
jitter
 
snaps
 
the
 
timestamp
 
to
 
10,005
 
ms,
 
effectively
 
removing
 
the
 
sub-millisecond
 
quantization
 
noise
 
while
 
preserving
 
the
 
event’s
 
chronological
 
integrity.
 
 
2  Sliding  Window  Filter  Algorithm  
To  distinguish  between  clinically  meaningful  psychomotor  patterns  
and
 
natural
 
tremor
 
artifacts,
 
PsyClick
 
applies
 
a
 
Weighted
 
Moving
 
Average
 
(WMA)
 
via
 
a
 
Sliding
 
Window.
 
This
 
functions
 
as
 
a
 
Low-Pass
 
Filter,
 
attenuating
 
high-frequency
 
noise
 
(>8
 
Hz)
 
while
 
preserving
 
low-frequency
 
hesitation
 
patterns
 
(<2
 
Hz),
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
biomechanical
 
signal
 
bandwidths
 
defined
 
by
 
Winter
 
(2009).
 
 
 
The  mathematical  definition  for  a  window  size  N  at  time  t  follows  the  
discrete
 
linear
 
convolution
 
model
 
described
 
by
 
Smith
 
(1997):
 
X
filterd
(t)  =   
𝑖
=
0
𝑁 − 1
∑
𝑤
𝑖
⋅
𝑋
(
𝑡
−
𝑖
)
𝑖
=
0
𝑁
−
1
∑
𝑤
𝑖 
Where:  
-  X
filterd
(t)  :  The  smoothed  output  value  at  the  current  timestamp,  
stripped
 
of
 
high-frequency
 
hardware
 
jitter.
 
99  

=== PAGE 115 ===
 
-   :  The  raw  historical  input  data  from  previous  timestamps  𝑋 ( 𝑡 − 𝑖 )
within  the  current  window.  
-  
 
:
 
The
 
specific
 
weight
 
assigned
 
to
 
each
 
data
 
point
 
in
 
the
 
sequence.
 𝑤
𝑖
-   :  The  summation  of  the  weighted  inputs,  divided  by  the  total  sum  of  ∑
weights,  to  normalize  the  result  and  maintain  scale.  
 
In  this  implementation,  w  represents  the  weighted  kernel  [0.1,  0.2,  0.4,  0.2,  
0.1].
 
This
 
specific
 
5-sample
 
distribution
 
is
 
a
 
Gaussian-approximate
 
kernel.
 
By
 
assigning
 
the
 
highest
 
weight
 
(0.4)
 
to
 
the
 
central
 
(current)
 
data
 
point
 
and
 
progressively
 
lower
 
weights
 
(0.2,
 
0.1)
 
to
 
neighboring
 
points,
 
the
 
algorithm
 
achieves
 
effective
 
noise
 
reduction
 
without
 
introducing
 
the
 
significant
 
"lag"
 
or
 
sluggishness
 
associated
 
with
 
simple
 
moving
 
averages,
 
which
 
weight
 
all
 
data
 
points
 
equally
 
 
3  Within-Session  EWMA  Baseline  Algorithm  
Unlike  static  calibration  approaches  that  compute  a  fixed  mean  and  
standard
 
deviation
 
from
 
early-session
 
data,
 
PsyClick
 
implements
 
an
 
Exponentially
 
Weighted
 
Moving
 
Average
 
(EWMA)
 
to
 
build
 
an
 
adaptive
 
within-session
 
baseline.
 
The
 
EWMA
 
algorithm,
 
widely
 
applied
 
in
 
statistical
 
process
 
control
 
and
 
behavioral
 
monitoring
 
(Montgomery,
 
2009),
 
assigns
 
exponentially
 
decreasing
 
weights
 
to
 
older
 
observations,
 
ensuring
 
that
 
the
 
baseline
 
reflects
 
the
 
user’s
 
most
 
recent
 
behavioral
 
rhythm
 
rather
 
than
 
being
 
100  

=== PAGE 116 ===
 
permanently  skewed  by  early-session  variance  such  as  initial  nervousness  or  
unfamiliarity
 
with
 
the
 
task.
 
 
The  EWMA  baseline  is  constructed  during  the  calibration  phase  and  
used
 
throughout
 
the
 
assessment
 
phase
 
within
 
the
 
same
 
session.
 
The
 
update
 
equations
 
are:
 
μ_ ewma
t
 =  λ  ·  x
t
 +  (1  −  λ)  ·  μ_ ewma
t−1
 
S_ ewma
t
 =  λ  ·  (x
t
 −  μ_ ewma
t
)(x
t
 −  μ_ ewma
t
)ᵀ  +  (1  −  λ)  ·  
S_
ewma
t−1
  Where:  
‒  μ_ ewma
t
 :  The  EWMA  mean  vector  at  time  t,  representing  the  
adaptive
 
baseline
 
across
 
all
 
p
 
features
 
‒  S_ ewma
t
 :  The  EWMA  covariance  matrix  at  time  t  
‒  x
t
 :  The  current  feature  vector  extracted  from  the  active  
keystroke/cursor
 
window
 
‒  λ  :  The  smoothing  parameter,  set  to  0.2  
This  implementation  ensures  that  the  “normal”  reference  range  is  unique  to  
the
 
individual
 
and
 
reflects
 
their
 
behavioral
 
state
 
at
 
the
 
time
 
of
 
the
 
session,
 
preventing
 
naturally
 
fast
 
or
 
slow
 
typists
 
from
 
being
 
incorrectly
 
flagged
 
as
 
anomalies.
 
Because
 
EWMA
 
operates
 
within
 
a
 
single
 
session,
 
its
 
state
 
is
 
maintained
 
in
 
memory
 
and
 
does
 
not
 
require
 
cross-session
 
database
 
storage.
 
4  Multi-Feature  Extraction  with  Domain  Tagging  
101  

=== PAGE 117 ===
 
Before  statistical  analysis  is  applied,  the  FeatureExtractor  uses  a  
data-parsing
 
algorithm
 
to
 
convert
 
raw
 
hardware
 
signals
 
into
 
eight
 
meaningful
 
biomarkers
 
and
 
attach
 
contextual
 
metadata
 
to
 
each
 
feature
 
window.
 
For
 
Task
 
3
 
windows,
 
each
 
extracted
 
feature
 
vector
 
x
 
is
 
tagged
 
with
 
a
 
domain
 
context
 
object
 
at
 
capture
 
time:
 
1.  task_id:  Integer  identifier  for  the  current  assessment  phase  
(corresponding
 
to
 
the
 
Emotional
 
Task
 
microstressor
 
session)
 
2.  item_id:  String  identifier  for  the  specific  prompt  (e.g.,  'A1',  'B3',  'C4')  
3.  group_id:  Integer  (1–4)  corresponding  to  the  stressor  domain  group  
4.  level:  String  ('A',  'B',  or  'C')  corresponding  to  the  difficulty  level  of  the  
current
 
item
 
5.  domain_label:  String  descriptor  (e.g.,  'time_workload',  
'interpersonal',
 
'academic_performance',
 
'self_evaluation')
 
This  metadata  is  stored  alongside  the  feature  vector  in  the  session  log  
and
 
is
 
used
 
post-session
 
by
 
the
 
domain-segmented
 
T²
 
computation
 
step.
 
It
 
does
 
not
 
affect
 
the
 
real-time
 
T²
 
computation,
 
which
 
continues
 
to
 
operate
 
on
 
individual
 
windows
 
against
 
the
 
EWMA
 
baseline.
 
The
 
domain
 
context
 
enables
 
post-session
 
grouping
 
and
 
level-resolved
 
analysis
 
without
 
requiring
 
real-time
 
segmentation
 
of
 
the
 
statistical
 
pipeline.
 
Keystroke  Processing:  
-  Flight  Time:  Latency  between  key  release  and  next  key  press  (cognitive  
planning
 
metric)
 
-  Dwell  Time:  Duration  a  key  is  held  down  (motor  execution  stability)  
102  

=== PAGE 118 ===
 
-  Typing  Velocity:  Characters  per  second  (overall  motor  throughput)  
-  Error  Rate:  Proportion  of  keystrokes  that  are  backspace  corrections,  range  
0–1
 
(self-monitoring
 
indicator)
 
Mouse  Path  Analysis  (post-filtering):  
-  Path  Entropy:  Shannon  entropy  of  discretized  trajectory  angles  (irregularity  
measure)
 
-  Cursor  Velocity:  Mean  tangential  velocity  in  pixels  per  second  
-  Jerk:  Third  derivative  of  cursor  position  (smoothness  measure)  
-  Pause  Frequency:  Count  of  movement  cessations  greater  than  500  ms  
This  extraction  process  is  essential  for  transforming  disorganized  
hardware
 
events
 
into
 
the
 
structured
 
clinical
 
markers
 
required
 
for
 
multivariate
 
anomaly
 
detection.
 
Together,
 
these
 
eight
 
features
 
constitute
 
the
 
feature
 
vector
 
x
 
fed
 
into
 
Hotelling's
 
T²
 
engine.
 
All
 
eight
 
features
 
must
 
be
 
accounted
 
for
 
in
 
the
 
PSI
 
and
 
PAI
 
decomposition
 
described
 
in
 
Algorithm
 
6.
 
 
5  Hotelling’s  T²  Multivariate  Anomaly  Detection  
The  core  anomaly  detection  algorithm  of  PsyClick  is  Hotelling’s  T²  
statistic,
 
the
 
multivariate
 
generalization
 
of
 
the
 
Individual’s
 
t-test
 
introduced
 
by
 
Harold
 
Hotelling
 
(1931)
 
and
 
widely
 
adopted
 
in
 
multivariate
 
statistical
 
process
 
control
 
(MSPC)
 
for
 
clinical
 
and
 
behavioral
 
monitoring
 
applications
 
(Tracy
 
et
 
al.,
 
1992;
 
Mason
 
&
 
Young,
 
2002).
 
Where
 
univariate
 
approaches
 
evaluate
 
each
 
biometric
 
feature
 
independently
 
and
 
fail
 
to
 
detect
 
anomalies
 
that
 
emerge
 
from
 
the
 
joint
 
behavior
 
of
 
multiple
 
features,
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
103  

=== PAGE 119 ===
 
evaluates  all  eight  extracted  features  simultaneously  as  a  single  multivariate  
observation.
 
This
 
is
 
clinically
 
critical:
 
a
 
person
 
experiencing
 
psychomotor
 
slowing
 
may
 
show
 
only
 
marginal
 
individual
 
deviations
 
in
 
Flight
 
Time,
 
Dwell
 
Time,
 
and
 
Pause
 
Frequency,
 
none
 
of
 
which
 
independently
 
exceed
 
a
 
univariate
 
threshold,
 
but
 
whose
 
combined
 
deviation
 
is
 
statistically
 
significant
 
when
 
evaluated
 
jointly.
 
The  T²  statistic  is  computed  as:  
T²  =  n  ·  (x  −  μ_ ewma )ᵀ  ·  S ⁻ ¹  ·  (x  −  μ_ ewma )  
Where:  
-  x  :  The  current  assessment-phase  feature  vector  (p  ×  1,  where  p  =  8)  
-  μ_ ewma  :  The  EWMA  baseline  mean  vector  established  during  the  
calibration
 
phase
 
-  S ⁻ ¹  :  The  inverse  of  the  covariance  matrix,  estimated  via  the  
Ledoit-Wolf
 
shrinkage
 
estimator
 
-  n  :  The  number  of  observations  in  the  current  assessment  window  
To  ensure  numerical  stability  when  the  number  of  calibration  windows  
is
 
small
 
relative
 
to
 
the
 
number
 
of
 
features,
 
PsyClick
 
employs
 
the
 
Ledoit-Wolf
 
shrinkage
 
estimator
 
(Ledoit
 
&
 
Wolf,
 
2004)
 
rather
 
than
 
the
 
raw
 
sample
 
covariance
 
matrix.
 
The
 
Ledoit-Wolf
 
estimator
 
applies
 
analytical
 
shrinkage
 
toward
 
a
 
well-conditioned
 
target,
 
guaranteeing
 
invertibility
 
of
 
the
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
and
 
producing
 
a
 
more
 
accurate
 
precision
 
matrix
 
S
⁻
¹
 
under
 
small-sample
 
conditions.
 
When
 
the
 
number
 
of
 
calibration
 
windows
 
exceeds
 
40
 
(5p)
.
 
The
 
Ledoit-Wolf
 
estimator
 
operates
 
throughout
 
the
 
session
 
in
 
104  

=== PAGE 120 ===
 
correlation  space,  with  shrinkage  intensity  α  =  0.15  when  n  <  5p  and  α  =  0.05  
when
 
n
 
≥
 
5p,
 
ensuring
 
numerical
 
stability
 
and
 
bounded
 
condition
 
number
 
regardless
 
of
 
feature
 
scale
 
differences
 
across
 
the
 
eight
 
biomarkers.
 
 
 The  T²  score  is  evaluated  against  a  statistically  derived  threshold  
based
 
on
 
the
 
F-distribution
 
(Tracy
 
et
 
al.,
 
1992):
 
T²_ threshold  =  [p(n  −  1)  /  (n  −  p)]  ·  F( α;  p,  n  −  p)  
Where:  
-  p  :  The  number  of  features  (8)  
-  n  :  The  number  of  calibration  windows  
-  F( α;  p,  n−p)  :  The  critical  value  of  the  F-distribution  at  significance  
level
 
α
 
=
 
0.05
 
 
This  threshold  is  mathematically  derived  rather  than  arbitrarily  chosen,  
meaning
 
that
 
sessions
 
with
 
T²
 
scores
 
exceeding
 
the
 
threshold
 
can
 
be
 
stated
 
to
 
deviate
 
significantly
 
from
 
the
 
user’s
 
normal
 
behavioral
 
distribution
 
at
 
the
 
95%
 
confidence
 
level.
 
This
 
provides
 
a
 
rigorous
 
statistical
 
justification
 
for
 
clinical
 
flagging.
 
 
6  Feature  Contribution  Analysis  (PSI  /  PAI)  
Hotelling’s  T²  identifies  that  a  session  is  anomalous  but  does  not  
specify
 
the
 
nature
 
of
 
the
 
disturbance.
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
Analysis
 
decomposes
 
the
 
T²
 
score
 
to
 
determine
 
which
 
features
 
drove
 
the
 
anomaly
 
and
 
105  

=== PAGE 121 ===
 
in  which  direction  (Mason  &  Young,  2002).  The  contribution  of  each  feature  i  
to
 
the
 
total
 
T²
 
score
 
is
 
computed
 
as:
 
C
i
 =  (x
i
 −  μ
i
)  ×  [S ⁻ ¹  ·  (x  −  μ)]
i
 
The  eight  features  are  grouped  into  two  clinically  meaningful  clusters:  
-  Psychomotor  Slowing  Index  (PSI):  Aggregates  contributions  from  
Flight
 
Time,
 
Dwell
 
Time,
 
and
 
Pause
 
Frequency.
 
Elevated
 
PSI
 
indicates
 
slowing
 
of
 
cognitive
 
planning
 
and
 
motor
 
execution,
 
consistent
 
with
 
psychomotor
 
retardation
 
patterns
 
associated
 
with
 
depression
 
and
 
anxiety-related
 
inhibition.
 
-  Psychomotor  Agitation  Index  (PAI):  Aggregates  contributions  from  
Path
 
Entropy,
 
Jerk,
 
Error
 
Rate,
 
Cursor
 
Velocity,
 
and
 
Typing
 
Velocity.
 
Elevated
 
PAI
 
indicates
 
erratic,
 
impulsive
 
movement
 
and
 
disrupted
 
motor
 
throughput,
 
consistent
 
with
 
psychomotor
 
agitation
 
associated
 
with
 
anxiety
 
and
 
hyperarousal
 
states.
 
This  decomposition  transforms  the  T²  scalar  output  into  a  directional  
clinical
 
profile,
 
enabling
 
the
 
system
 
to
 
distinguish
 
between
 
clinically
 
opposite
 
disturbance
 
patterns
 
and
 
provide
 
the
 
counselor
 
with
 
actionable
 
characterization
 
rather
 
than
 
a
 
binary
 
flag.
 
 
7  Fuzzy  Logic  Classification  
To  address  the  inherent  limitation  of  hard-threshold  classification,  
where
 
clinically
 
equivalent
 
sessions
 
on
 
either
 
side
 
of
 
a
 
boundary
 
receive
 
opposite
 
labels,
 
PsyClick
 
employs
 
a
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classifier
 
based
 
on
 
the
 
106  

=== PAGE 122 ===
 
theoretical  framework  established  by  Zadeh  (1965)  and  applied  extensively  in  
clinical
 
decision
 
support
 
systems
 
(Phuong
 
&
 
Thang,
 
2007).
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
assigns
 
degrees
 
of
 
membership
 
to
 
each
 
clinical
 
category
 
rather
 
than
 
forcing
 
binary
 
classification,
 
reflecting
 
the
 
clinically
 
realistic
 
understanding
 
that
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance
 
exists
 
on
 
a
 
continuum
 
rather
 
than
 
as
 
a
 
discrete
 
state.
 
The  classification  proceeds  in  three  steps:  
1.  Fuzzification:  The  T²  score,  PSI,  and  PAI  are  each  converted  into  
membership
 
degrees
 
across
 
linguistic
 
categories
 
(Low,
 
Moderate,
 
High)
 
using
 
triangular
 
(trimf)
 
and
 
trapezoidal
 
(trapmf)
 
membership
 
functions.
 
2.  Rule  Evaluation:  A  set  of  clinically  derived  IF-THEN  rules  is  
evaluated
 
by
 
the
 
inference
 
engine,
 
listed
 
in
 
priority
 
order.
 
Rule
 
priority
 
is
 
necessary
 
because
 
a
 
patient
 
in
 
a
 
mixed-state
 
episode,
 
exhibiting
 
concurrent
 
psychomotor
 
slowing
 
and
 
agitation
 
within
 
the
 
same
 
assessment
 
window,
  
must
 
be
 
classified
 
as
 
a
 
distinct
 
and
 
more
 
severe
 
clinical
 
state
 
rather
 
than
 
having
 
the
 
two
 
opposing
 
signals
 
averaged
 
or
 
cancelled.
 
The
 
AND
 
operator
 
applies
 
the
 
minimum
 
t-norm,
 
so
 
all
 
conditions
 
in
 
a
 
rule
 
must
 
be
 
substantially
 
satisfied
 
for
 
it
 
to
 
fire.
 
The
 
rules
 
are:
 
4.1  IF  T²  is  High  AND  PSI  is  High  AND  PAI  is  High  →  Mixed  
Disturbance,
 
Severe
 
(Rule
 
1
 
—
 
highest
 
priority)
 
4.2  IF  T²  is  High  AND  PSI  is  High  AND  PAI  is  Low  →  
Psychomotor
 
Retardation,
 
Severe
 
107  

=== PAGE 123 ===
 
4.3  IF  T²  is  High  AND  PAI  is  High  AND  PSI  is  Low  →  
Psychomotor
 
Agitation,
 
Severe
 
4.4  IF  T²  is  Moderate  AND  PSI  is  Moderate  AND  PAI  is  
Moderate
 
→
 
Mixed
 
Disturbance,
 
Borderline
 
4.5  IF  T²  is  Moderate  AND  PAI  is  Moderate  →  Psychomotor  
Agitation,
 
Borderline
 
4.6  IF  T²  is  Moderate  AND  PSI  is  Moderate  →  Psychomotor  
Retardation,
 
Borderline
 
4.7  IF  T²  is  Low  →  Normal  Behavior  
These  rules  were  designed  by  the  researchers  based  on  the  
clinical
 
disturbance
 
profiles
 
established
 
in
 
the
 
Theoretical
 
Framework
 
(Moussa-Tooks
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024;
 
Fritze
 
et
 
al.,
 
2025),
 
the
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
methodology
 
of
 
Mason
 
and
 
Young
 
(2002),
 
and
 
the
 
Mamdani
 
inference
 
architecture
 
(Mamdani
 
&
 
Assilian,
 
1975).
 
The
 
mixed-state
 
priority
 
rule
 
reflects
 
documented
 
clinical
 
presentations
 
of
 
concurrent
 
psychomotor
 
retardation
 
and
 
agitation
 
(Swann
 
et
 
al.,
 
2013).
 
3.  Defuzzification:  The  fuzzy  output  is  converted  to  a  crisp  clinical  
category
 
and
 
a
 
numerical
 
confidence
 
score
 
via
 
dominant-rule
 
confidence
 
scoring:
 
the
 
winning
 
rule’s
 
firing
 
strength
 
is
 
divided
 
by
 
the
 
total
 
firing
 
strength
 
across
 
all
 
seven
 
rules,
 
producing
 
an
 
interpretable
 
output
 
such
 
as
 
“Psychomotor
 
Retardation,
 
Moderate
 
Concern
 
(68%
 
confidence).”
 
108  

=== PAGE 124 ===
 
The  Fuzzy  Logic  Classifier  is  implemented  as  a  custom  module  in  
NumPy
 
and
 
SciPy,
 
using
 
triangular
 
(trimf)
 
and
 
trapezoidal
 
(trapmf)
 
membership
 
functions
 
and
 
a
 
min-conjunction
 
(Mamdani-style)
 
inference
 
engine,
 
without
 
reliance
 
on
 
any
 
external
 
fuzzy
 
logic
 
library.
 
Every
 
decision
 
is
 
traceable
 
to
 
a
 
readable
 
IF-THEN
 
rule,
 
maintaining
 
the
 
white-box,
 
explainable
 
character
 
of
 
the
 
system
 
appropriate
 
for
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support
 
under
 
SE/DSS
 
classification.
 
 
8  Decision  Rule  Mapping  (Thresholding)  
The  final  stage  of  the  algorithm  is  a  Heuristic  Decision  Tree  that  maps  
the
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
output
 
to
 
the
 
final
 
clinical
 
flag
 
and
 
generates
 
a
 
plain-language
 
rationale
 
for
 
clinician
 
interpretation:
 
1.  Green  (Normal):  T²  score  within  the  session  baseline  threshold  (T²  
ratio
 
≤
 
1.0)
 
–
 
behavior
 
is
 
statistically
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
within-session
 
baseline.
 
Always
 
assigned
 
Green
 
regardless
 
of
 
fuzzy
 
rule
 
dominance.
 
2.  Amber  (Unusual):  T²  score  is  above  the  threshold  but  below  the  
1.5×
 
multiplier,
 
OR
 
borderline-class
 
rule
 
firing
 
strength
 
(max
 
of
 
Rules
 
4,
 
5,
 
6)
 
exceeds
 
severe-class
 
rule
 
firing
 
strength
 
—
 
moderate
 
statistical
 
shift
 
warranting
 
clinical
 
attention
 
at
 
next
 
appointment.
 
3.  Red  (Extreme):  T²  score  significantly  exceeds  the  1.5×  threshold  
multiplier
 
AND
 
severe-class
 
rule
 
firing
 
strength
 
(max
 
of
 
Rules
 
1,
 
2,
 
3)
 
equals
 
or
 
exceeds
 
borderline-class
 
rule
 
firing
 
strength,
 
indicating
 
significant
 
psychomotor
 
anomaly.
 
This
 
flag
 
is
 
an
 
escalation,
 
not
 
a
 
cancellation:
 
109  

=== PAGE 125 ===
 
concurrent  high-PSI  and  high-PAI  (Rule  1  —  Mixed  Disturbance,  Severe)  or  
a
 
dominant
 
single-index
 
severe
 
presentation
 
(Rule
 
2:
 
Psychomotor
 
Retardation;
 
Rule
 
3:
 
Psychomotor
 
Agitation)
 
all
 
route
 
to
 
Red.
 
The
 
plain-language
 
rationale
 
explicitly
 
names
 
the
 
dominant
 
disturbance
 
pattern,
 
identifies
 
the
 
driving
 
features
 
for
 
each
 
index,
 
and
 
defers
 
diagnostic
 
interpretation
 
to
 
the
 
attending
 
clinician.
 
Clinical
 
intervention
 
is
 
recommended.
 
 
Each  classification  includes  a  T²  score,  F-distribution  p-value  
equivalent,
 
PSI/PAI
 
breakdown,
 
Fuzzy
 
confidence
 
score,
 
and
 
a
 
plain-language
 
rationale
 
specifying
 
which
 
features
 
drove
 
the
 
anomaly
 
and
 
in
 
which
 
clinical
 
direction,
 
providing
 
the
 
counselor
 
with
 
a
 
complete,
 
interpretable
 
basis
 
for
 
clinical
 
judgment.
 
 
9  Computational  Complexity  Analysis  
A  critical  practical  requirement  for  a  clinical  decision  support  system  
operating
 
on
 
consumer-grade
 
hardware
 
is
 
computational
 
tractability.
 
Every
 
inference
 
step
 
must
 
complete
 
within
 
the
 
constraints
 
of
 
a
 
real-time
 
clinical
 
session
 
without
 
specialized
 
hardware.
 
This
 
subsection
 
provides
 
a
 
formal
 
analysis
 
of
 
the
 
time
 
and
 
space
 
complexity
 
of
 
the
 
PsyClick
 
detection
 
pipeline,
 
demonstrating
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
is
 
designed
 
for
 
efficient,
 
low-resource
 
execution.
 
Time  Complexity  of  EWMA  Baseline  Update:  O(1)  per  event  
110  

=== PAGE 126 ===
 
The  EWMA  update  at  each  calibration  window  t  requires  the  
execution
 
of
 
exactly
 
two
 
fixed-structure
 
equations
 
regardless
 
of
 
how
 
many
 
prior
 
windows
 
have
 
been
 
processed:
 
μ_ ewma   =  λ  ·  x   +  (1  −  λ)  ·  μ_ ewma  ₋₁  
S_ewma   =  λ  ·  (x   −  μ_ ewma )(x   −  μ_ ewma )ᵀ  +  (1  −  λ)  ·  S_ewma  ₋₁  
Each  equation  performs  only  element-wise  scalar  multiplication  and  
addition
 
on
 
fixed-length
 
vectors
 
and
 
matrices
 
of
 
dimension
 
F
 
=
 
8.
 
Formally,
 
for
 
a
 
feature
 
vector
 
x 
 
∈
 
ℝ
ᴼ
 
and
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
S
 
∈
 
ℝ
ᴼ×ᴼ:
 
1.  The  mean  update  involves  F  =  8  multiplications  and  F  =  8  additions:  
O(F)
 
=
 
O(8)
 
=
 
O(1).
 
2.  The  covariance  update  involves  computing  an  outer  product  (x   −  
μ)(
x 
 
−
 
μ)
ᵀ,
 
which
 
requires
 
F²
 
=
 
64
 
operations,
 
followed
 
by
 
element-wise
 
scaling:
 
O(F²)
 
=
 
O(64)
 
=
 
O(1).
 
 
Because  F  =  8  is  a  fixed  constant  defined  by  the  system’s  feature  set  
and
 
does
 
not
 
scale
 
with
 
any
 
variable
 
input,
 
both
 
operations
 
are
 
O(1)
 
with
 
respect
 
to
 
the
 
session
 
length.
 
The
 
EWMA
 
update
 
does
 
not
 
accumulate,
 
store,
 
or
 
reprocess
 
historical
 
windows:
 
it
 
discards
 
the
 
previous
 
state
 
and
 
replaces
 
it
 
with
 
the
 
newly
 
weighted
 
value
 
in
 
a
 
single
 
pass.
 
There
 
is
 
no
 
recurrence
 
over
 
prior
 
observations,
 
no
 
growing
 
data
 
structure,
 
and
 
no
 
batch
 
computation.
 
This
 
is
 
the
 
defining
 
efficiency
 
property
 
of
 
the
 
EWMA:
 
it
 
achieves
 
recency-weighting
 
of
 
an
 
unbounded
 
time
 
series
 
at
 
constant
 
computational
 
cost
 
per
 
event.
 
111  

=== PAGE 127 ===
 
 
Time  Complexity  over  a  Full  Session  of  N  Events:  O(N)  
Let  N  denote  the  total  number  of  feature  windows  processed  during  a  
session
 
(calibration
 
+
 
assessment).
 
Since
 
each
 
EWMA
 
update
 
is
 
O(1),
 
and
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
computation
 
for
 
each
 
assessment
 
window
 
is
 
also
 
O(F²)
 
=
 
O(1)
 
for
 
fixed
 
F,
 
the
 
total
 
computational
 
cost
 
across
 
all
 
N
 
windows
 
is:
 
T_session  =  N  ×  O(1)  =  O(N)  
This  is  the  minimum  possible  complexity  for  any  algorithm  that  must  
process
 
each
 
event
 
at
 
least
 
once.
 
The
 
pipeline
 
scales
 
linearly
 
with
 
session
 
length
 
and
 
does
 
not
 
exhibit
 
superlinear
 
growth,
 
even
 
as
 
the
 
session
 
spans
 
multiple
 
task
 
phases.
 
For
 
a
 
typical
 
10–15
 
minute
 
session
 
producing
 
approximately
 
200–500
 
feature
 
windows,
 
the
 
total
 
computational
 
burden
 
remains
 
negligible
 
on
 
the
 
minimum-specification
 
hardware
 
defined
 
in
 
Section
 
3.4.1.
 
Space  Complexity:  O(F)  
The  EWMA  algorithm  requires  storing  only  two  objects  in  session  
memory
 
at
 
any
 
given
 
time:
 
the
 
current
 
mean
 
vector
 
μ_
ewma 
 
∈
 
ℝ
ᴼ
 
and
 
the
 
current
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
S_ewma 
 
∈
 
ℝ
ᴼ×ᴼ.
 
No
 
historical
 
feature
 
vectors,
 
no
 
ring
 
buffers,
 
and
 
no
 
growing
 
arrays
 
are
 
maintained.
 
The
 
complete
 
memory
 
footprint
 
of
 
the
 
baseline
 
model
 
is
 
therefore:
 
M_baseline  =  ||μ||  +  ||S||  =  F  +  F²  =  8  +  64  =  72  scalar  values  
For  F  =  8  features,  this  is  a  constant  72  floating-point  values  
regardless
 
of
 
session
 
length
 
N.
 
The
 
space
 
complexity
 
is
 
therefore
 
O(F²)
 
=
 
112  

=== PAGE 128 ===
 
O(64)  =  O(1)  with  respect  to  N,  and  O(F)  with  respect  to  the  feature  
dimensionality.
 
In
 
practical
 
terms,
 
the
 
entire
 
baseline
 
model
 
occupies
 
less
 
than
 
600
 
bytes
 
of
 
memory
 
at
 
64-bit
 
precision,
 
a
 
negligible
 
footprint
 
that
 
does
 
not
 
grow
 
during
 
the
 
session
 
and
 
is
 
fully
 
discarded
 
upon
 
session
 
termination.
 
This  property  is  particularly  significant  for  PsyClick’s  privacy  
architecture:
 
because
 
no
 
historical
 
sequence
 
is
 
stored,
 
there
 
is
 
no
 
risk
 
of
 
residual
 
behavioral
 
data
 
persisting
 
in
 
memory
 
beyond
 
the
 
session
 
boundary.
 
The
 
O(F)
 
space
 
complexity
 
is
 
therefore
 
both
 
a
 
computational
 
advantage
 
and
 
a
 
privacy
 
guarantee,
 
satisfying
 
the
 
“Local-First”
 
design
 
principle
 
without
 
any
 
additional
 
data
 
sanitization
 
procedures.
 
3.6   Method  in  Evaluating  the  Study  
3.6.1  Software  Evaluation  (ISO)  
 
Figure  9.  Software  Product  Quality  -  Reference  from  
https://iso25000.com/index.php/en/iso-25000-standards/iso-25010 
To  ensure  the  quality,  operational  effectiveness,  and  clinical  utility  of  the  developed  
system,
 
a
 
rigorous
 
evaluation
 
process
 
will
 
be
 
implemented
 
in
 
accordance
 
with
 
established
 
113  


=== PAGE 129 ===
 
software  engineering  standards.  The  primary  framework  for  this  evaluation  is  ISO/IEC  
25010:
 
Software
 
Product
 
Quality.
 
Domain
 
experts
 
will
 
apply
 
this
 
comprehensive
 
model
 
to
 
assess
 
the
 
system.
 
Rather
 
than
 
using
 
all
 
eight
 
characteristics,
 
only
 
those
 
most
 
relevant
 
to
 
the
 
system’s
 
nature
 
and
 
intended
 
purpose
 
will
 
be
 
selected
 
to
 
ensure
 
a
 
focused,
 
appropriate
 
evaluation.
 
The
 
primary
 
characteristics
 
considered
 
are
 
Functional
 
Suitability,
 
Performance
 
Efficiency,
 
Usability,
 
Reliability,
 
and
 
Security.
 
The  technical  assessment  will  be  facilitated  via  Microsoft  Teams,  following  a  coordinated  
timeline
 
established
 
between
 
the
 
proponents
 
and
 
the
 
invited
 
experts.
 
This
 
evaluation
 
phase
 
begins
 
immediately
 
after
 
either
 
a
 
live
 
demonstration
 
of
 
the
 
software
 
or
 
after
 
evaluators
 
conduct
 
independent
 
hands-on
 
testing
 
of
 
the
 
application
 
on
 
their
 
local
 
devices.
 
During
 
this
 
period,
 
the
 
experts
 
are
 
encouraged
 
to
 
interact
 
with
 
the
 
proponents
 
to
 
seek
 
technical
 
clarifications,
 
provide
 
professional
 
insights,
 
and
 
offer
 
critical
 
feedback
 
on
 
the
 
system’s
 
performance.
 
The
 
process
 
reaches
 
its
 
conclusion
 
when
 
the
 
professionals
 
complete
 
a
 
structured
 
survey,
 
which
 
serves
 
to
 
measure
 
the
 
system’s
 
adherence
 
to
 
the
 
specific
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010
 
based
 
on
 
the
 
following
 
qualities:
 
Functional  Suitability.   This  is  prioritized  because  the  system’s  core  purpose  is  to  
compute
 
psychomotor
 
indicators
 
from
 
interaction
 
data
 
correctly.
 
The
 
evaluation
 
checks
 
whether
 
the
 
feature
 
extraction,
 
T²
 
anomaly
 
detection,
 
and
 
classification
 
processes
 
produce
 
accurate
 
and
 
meaningful
 
outputs.
 
If
 
the
 
system
 
fails
 
here,
 
the
 
rest
 
of
 
the
 
evaluation
 
becomes
 
irrelevant.
 
Performance  Efficiency.  This  is  included  because  the  system  needs  to  capture  
high-resolution
 
keystore
 
and
 
cursor
 
data
 
in
 
real
 
time.
 
The
 
study
 
examines
 
response
 
114  

=== PAGE 130 ===
 
time,  resource  usage,  and  stability  while  processing  interaction  streams  during  
counseling
 
tasks.
 
This
 
ensures
 
analysis
 
proceeds
 
without
 
delays
 
that
 
could
 
affect
 
usability
 
or
 
data
 
integrity.
 
Usability.   This  is  considered  essential  because  the  primary  users  are  mental  health  
professionals
 
who
 
may
 
not
 
have
 
technical
 
training.
 
The
 
evaluation
 
focuses
 
on
 
how
 
easily
 
users
 
can
 
navigate
 
the
 
interface,
 
interpret
 
visual
 
outputs,
 
and
 
understand
 
the
 
generated
 
reports.
 
This
 
ensures
 
the
 
system
 
supports
 
decision-making
 
rather
 
than
 
creating
 
confusion.
 
Reliability.  This  is  selected  to  verify  that  the  system  consistently  produces  stable  
results
 
when
 
used
 
repeatedly.
 
Since
 
the
 
tool
 
is
 
used
 
across
 
different
 
sessions
 
and
 
device
 
conditions,
 
testing
 
ensures
 
it
 
does
 
not
 
crash,
 
lose
 
data,
 
or
 
generate
 
inconsistent
 
classifications
 
when
 
exposed
 
to
 
varying
 
input
 
behavior.
 
Security.  This  is  included  because  the  system  handles  sensitive  behavioral  and  
screening
 
data.
 
The
 
evaluation
 
checks
 
whether
 
local
 
processing,
 
protected
 
storage,
 
and
 
controlled
 
access
 
mechanisms
 
safeguard
 
user
 
information
 
and
 
maintain
 
confidentiality
 
standards
 
expected
 
in
 
mental
 
health
 
contexts.
 
3.7  Testing  Method  
Black  Box  and  White  Box  Testing  methodologies  are  employed  to  evaluate  the  
PsyClick
 
system
 
among
 
study
 
participants.
 
Black
 
Box
 
Testing
 
assesses
 
only
 
the
 
external
 
behavior
 
of
 
the
 
system,
 
without
 
consideration
 
of
 
its
 
internal
 
mechanisms.
 
The
 
testers
 
for
 
this
 
phase
 
are
 
the
 
intended
 
end-users,
 
specifically
 
Mental
 
Health
 
Professionals
 
and
 
Adults.
 
These
 
115  

=== PAGE 131 ===
 
individuals  are  not  required  to  possess  knowledge  of  Python  programming  or  the  statistical  
algorithms
 
that
 
underpin
 
the
 
system’s
 
operation.
 
 
White  Box  Testing  examines  the  system’s  internal  logic  by  evaluating  components  
such
 
as
 
the
 
Hardware
 
Abstraction
 
Layer
 
(HAL),
 
Within-Session
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
construction,
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
computation,
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
Analysis,
 
and
 
the
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classification
 
engine.
 
Researchers
 
and
 
software
 
developers
 
with
 
relevant
 
expertise
 
will
 
serve
 
as
 
testers
 
for
 
this
 
method,
 
assessing
 
code-level
 
processes
 
including
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
estimation,
 
T²
 
scoring,
 
fuzzy
 
membership
 
function
 
evaluation,
 
and
 
sliding-window
 
filters.
 
Following
 
testing,
 
a
 
Likert-scale
 
survey
 
will
 
be
 
distributed
 
to
 
testers
 
to
 
evaluate
 
the
 
system,
 
with
 
survey
 
statements
 
aligned
 
with
 
ISO-25010
 
standards.
 
 
3.7.1  Alpha  and  Beta  Testing  
Alpha  Testing  (Gray  Box  -  Matrix  Testing)  
Gray  box  testing,  a  variant  of  alpha  testing  that  integrates  elements  of  both  
white
 
box
 
and
 
black
 
box
 
methodologies,
 
is
 
utilized
 
to
 
evaluate
 
the
 
PsyClick
 
system.
 
This
 
approach
 
provides
 
testers
 
with
 
restricted
 
access
 
to
 
the
 
system’s
 
source
 
code,
 
statistical
 
logic,
 
and
 
design
 
documentation,
 
while
 
preserving
 
a
 
functional
 
testing
 
perspective.
 
The
 
gray-box
 
testing
 
technique
 
used
 
in
 
this
 
study
 
is
 
Matrix
 
Testing.
 
Matrix  Testing  -  Matrix  Testing  identifies  defects  within  individual  system  
components
 
by
 
assessing
 
various
 
combinations
 
of
 
inputs,
 
task
 
conditions,
 
and
 
baseline
 
states.
 
This
 
process
 
verifies
 
whether
 
each
 
component
 
functions
 
116  

=== PAGE 132 ===
 
correctly  under  diverse  psychomotor  patterns  and  statistical  scenarios.  In  the  
context
 
of
 
PsyClick,
 
matrix
 
testing
 
addresses
 
the
 
following
 
aspects:
 
1.  Correct  logging  of  keystroke  and  cursor  events  during  standardized  
counseling
 
tasks.
 
2.  Accurate  application  of  HAL  correction  factors  across  different  
keyboard
 
types
 
(mechanical
 
vs.
 
membrane)
 
and
 
polling
 
rates.
 
3.  Proper  execution  of  the  sliding  window  filter,  with  validation  of  
noise-reduction
 
effectiveness.
 
4.  Accurate  computation  of  psychomotor  features  such  as  flight  time,  
dwell
 
time,
 
cursor
 
velocity,
 
and
 
path
 
entropy.
 
5.  Correct  construction  of  the  within-session  EWMA  baseline,  including  
proper
 
mean
 
vector
 
and
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
updates
 
across
 
calibration
 
windows.
 
6.  Accurate  computation  of  Hotelling’s  T²  scores  using  the  Ledoit-Wolf  
covariance
 
estimator
 
and
 
proper
 
application
 
of
 
the
 
F-distribution
 
threshold.
 
7.  Correct  decomposition  of  T²  scores  into  PSI  and  PAI  feature  
contribution
 
indices.
 
8.  Proper  evaluation  of  Fuzzy  Logic  membership  functions  and  
IF-THEN
 
rule
 
firing
 
for
 
all
 
clinical
 
scenarios,
 
including
 
the
 
Mixed
 
Disturbance
 
pathway
 
where
 
both
 
PSI
 
and
 
PAI
 
simultaneously
 
exceed
 
the
 
Severe
 
threshold.
 
117  

=== PAGE 133 ===
 
9.  Consistent  mapping  of  Fuzzy  outputs  through  the  Heuristic  Decision  
Tree
 
into
 
Green
 
(Normal),
 
Amber
 
(Unusual),
 
Red
 
(Extreme),
 
and
 
Red
 
—
 
Mixed
 
Disturbance
 
psychomotor
 
states.
 
10.  Accurate  generation  of  the  self-contained  HTML  session  report  with  
T²
 
scores,
 
flags,
 
and
 
per-question
 
flight
 
time
 
and
 
hover-word
 
data.
 
 
This  approach  ensures  that  the  system’s  psychomotor  anomaly  
detection
 
logic
 
remains
 
stable,
 
reproducible,
 
and
 
interpretable
 
across
 
all
 
valid
 
operational
 
conditions.
 
 
 Beta  Testing  (Black  Box  -  Functional  Testing)  
Black-box  testing  is  used  during  the  beta  testing  phase  to  evaluate  the  
functionality,
 
usability,
 
and
 
performance
 
of
 
PsyClick
 
without
 
requiring
 
testers
 
to
 
know
 
its
 
internal
 
implementation.
 
The
 
specific
 
type
 
of
 
black-box
 
testing
 
used
 
in
 
this
 
study
 
is
 
Functional
 
Testing,
 
which
 
follows
 
these
 
steps:
 
2.  Tester  Recruitment:  Selection  of  mental  health  professionals  and  young  
adults
 
who
 
represent
 
the
 
system’s
 
target
 
users.
 
3.  Preparation:  Setup  of  testing  devices  (with  varied  hardware  configurations),  
standardized
 
counseling
 
tasks,
 
and
 
patient
 
profiles.
 
4.  Testing  Proper:  Execution  of  system  functions,  including  baseline  
calibration,
 
task
 
completion,
 
psychomotor
 
analysis,
 
visualization
 
review,
 
and
 
report
 
generation.
 
118  

=== PAGE 134 ===
 
5.  Feedback  Collection:  Gathering  feedback  regarding  clarity  of  psychomotor  
indicators
 
(T²
 
scores,
 
PSI/PAI
 
indices,
 
Fuzzy
 
confidence
 
scores),
 
interpretability
 
of
 
the
 
HTML
 
session
 
report
 
(flags,
 
rationales,
 
per-question
 
flight
 
time
 
data),
 
and
 
system
 
responsiveness.
 
6.  Clinical  Concordance  Evaluation:  Before  viewing  the  PsyClick  report,  the  
attending
 
mental
 
health
 
professional
 
independently
 
records
 
their
 
observational
 
clinical
 
impression
 
of
 
the
 
participant’s
 
psychomotor
 
state
 
during
 
that
 
session
 
using
 
a
 
structured
 
Clinical
 
Observation
 
Rating
 
Form
 
(CORF),
 
rating
 
the
 
session
 
as
 
Normal,
 
Mildly
 
Unusual,
 
Moderately
 
Concerning,
 
or
 
Severely
 
Concerning,
 
and
 
noting
 
whether
 
slowing,
 
agitation,
 
a
 
mixed
 
presentation,
 
or
 
no
 
disturbance
 
was
 
observed.
 
The
 
system
 
report
 
is
 
then
 
revealed,
 
and
 
the
 
clinician
 
completes
 
a
 
Concordance
 
Assessment
 
Form
 
(CAF)
 
indicating
 
whether
 
the
 
system’s
 
flag
 
and
 
disturbance
 
characterization
 
agree
 
with
 
their
 
independent
 
rating.
 
Across
 
all
 
beta
 
sessions,
 
Cohen’s
 
κ
 
is
 
computed
 
to
 
quantify
 
system-clinician
 
agreement,
 
with
 
a
 
minimum
 
acceptable
 
concordance
 
threshold
 
of
 
κ
 
≥
 
0.60
 
(Substantial
 
Agreement,
 
Landis
 
&
 
Koch,
 
1977).
 
This
 
step
 
validates
 
whether
 
the
 
system’s
 
Red,
 
Amber,
 
and
 
Green
 
flags,
 
and
 
the
 
Red
 
Mixed
 
Disturbance
 
flag
 
for
 
concurrent
 
high-PSI
 
and
 
high-PAI
 
sessions,
 
reflect
 
genuine
 
clinical
 
signal
 
rather
 
than
 
technical
 
output
 
alone.
 
 
7.  Feedback-Based  Changes:  Refinement  of  interface  layout,  reporting  format,  
visualization
 
parameters,
 
and
 
sensitivity
 
thresholds
 
based
 
on
 
tester
 
feedback
 
and
 
concordance
 
outcomes.
 
119  

=== PAGE 135 ===
 
3.7.2  White  Box  Testing  Procedure   
White  Box  Testing  involves  two  primary  steps:  understanding  the  source  code  
and
 
conducting
 
test
 
cases.
 
In
 
this
 
process,
 
the
 
researchers
 
examine
 
the
 
functionality
 
of
 
the
 
pynput
 
loggers
 
(KeyLogger,
 
MouseLogger),
 
the
 
HardwareAbstractionLayer,
 
the
 
within-session
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
engine,
 
the
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
computation
 
module,
 
the
 
custom
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classifier,
 
while
 
also
 
supplementing
 
Black-Box
 
Testing.
 
This
 
approach
 
is
 
necessary
 
because
 
resource
 
constraints
 
prevent
 
the
 
creation
 
of
 
exhaustive
 
test
 
cases.
 
 
Understanding  the  Source  Code:  
●  A  group  of  individuals  with  expertise  in  Python,  multivariate  statistics,  and  
fuzzy
 
logic
 
systems
 
will
 
be
 
assembled
 
to
 
evaluate
 
the
 
system’s
 
internal
 
workings
 
and
 
structure.
 
●  Each  tester  will  receive  access  to  the  system  backend,  including  the  Hardware  
Abstraction
 
Layer
 
(HAL),
 
Sliding
 
Window
 
Filter,
 
Within-Session
 
EWMA
 
module,
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
engine,
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
Analysis
 
module,
 
and
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classifier.
 
●  The  researchers  will  facilitate  a  discussion  of  the  source  code  to  ensure  that  
testers
 
understand
 
the
 
Ledoit-Wolf
 
covariance
 
estimation,
 
T²
 
threshold
 
derivation
 
from
 
the
 
F-distribution,
 
PSI/PAI
 
decomposition,
 
and
 
Fuzzy
 
membership
 
function
 
design.
 
●  Testers  will  be  instructed  to  focus  on  the  internal  functions,  logic,  and  
structure
 
of
 
the
 
biometric
 
data
 
processing
 
pipeline.
 
120  

=== PAGE 136 ===
 
●  The  testers  will  be  given  a  task  to  learn  and  understand  the  code  to  find  logic  
errors,
 
ensuring
 
that
 
raw
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
interaction
 
data
 
are
 
correctly
 
logged,
 
normalized
 
via
 
HAL,
 
filtered
 
for
 
noise
 
reduction,
 
processed
 
through
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
construction,
 
and
 
scored
 
accurately
 
by
 
the
 
T²
 
engine.
 
 
Conducting  Test  Cases   
●  The  testers  will  run  the  test  cases  for  the  system’s  source  code,  specifically  
verifying
 
the
 
logic
 
for
 
Flight
 
Time
 
and
 
Path
 
Entropy
 
calculations,
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
updates,
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
computation,
 
and
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
rule
 
evaluation.
 
●  The  researchers  will  run  the  system  following  the  test  cases  developed  by  the  
testers
 
to
 
confirm
 
that
 
T²
 
scores
 
exceeding
 
the
 
F-distribution
 
threshold
 
correctly
 
trigger
 
an
 
“Amber”
 
or
 
“Red”
 
flag
 
via
 
the
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classifier
 
and
 
Heuristic
 
Decision
 
Tree,
 
and
 
that
 
the
 
decision
 
rules
 
map
 
to
 
clinically
 
interpretable
 
states
 
with
 
accurate
 
PSI/PAI
 
characterization.
 
●  The  testers  will  answer  the  feedback  form  to  confirm  the  system’s  statistical  
integrity.
 
 
White-Box  testing  verifies  that  raw  keystroke  and  cursor  interaction  data  are  
correctly
 
logged,
 
normalized
 
via
 
HAL,
 
filtered
 
for
 
noise
 
reduction,
 
transformed
 
into
 
psychomotor
 
features,
 
processed
 
through
 
Within-Session
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
construction,
 
evaluated
 
by
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
against
 
the
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
using
 
the
 
Ledoit-Wolf
 
covariance
 
estimator,
 
decomposed
 
into
 
PSI/PAI
 
contributions,
 
classified
 
121  

=== PAGE 137 ===
 
by  the  Fuzzy  Logic  engine,  and  mapped  to  a  final  clinical  flag  by  the  Heuristic  
Decision
 
Tree.
 
Special
 
attention
 
is
 
given
 
to
 
ensuring
 
that
 
the
 
F-distribution
 
threshold
 
is
 
computed
 
accurately
 
and
 
that
 
the
 
Fuzzy
 
classification
 
rules
 
consistently
 
map
 
multivariate
 
deviations
 
into
 
clinically
 
interpretable,
 
graded
 
states.
 
 
The  following  pseudocode  represents  the  standardized  White  Box  test  case  
used
 
to
 
verify
 
psychomotor
 
anomaly
 
detection:

=== PAGE 139 ===
 
PAI  =  sum(C[PathEntropy],  C[Jerk],  C[ErrorRate],  C[CursorVelocity],

borderline_strength:
