3.7.3  Black  Box  Testing  Procedure   
The  Black-Box  testing  procedure  assesses  PsyClick  as  an  integrated,  
functional
 
system
 
without
 
revealing
 
its
 
internal
 
algorithms
 
or
 
code
 
structure
 
to
 
testers.
 
The
 
procedure
 
emphasizes
 
the
 
end-to-end
 
workflow
 
encountered
 
by
 
mental
 
health
 
professionals
 
and
 
patients
 
during
 
assessment
 
sessions.
 
 
Testers  perform  predefined  tasks  designed  to  simulate  real-world  usage,  including  the  
following:
 
124  

=== PAGE 140 ===
 
1.  Registration  of  a  patient  profile.  
2.  Execution  of  a  baseline  calibration  task  using  various  hardware  devices.  
3.  Completion  of  standardized  psychomotor  activities.  
4.  Review  of  the  generated  psychomotor  report,  including  T²  scores,  PSI/PAI  
indices,
 
Fuzzy
 
confidence
 
scores,
 
color-coded
 
flags,
 
per-question
 
flight
 
time
 
data,
 
and
 
hover-word
 
hesitation
 
records.
 
 
System  behavior  is  evaluated  based  on  the  correctness  of  outputs,  
responsiveness,
 
clarity
 
of
 
reports
 
and
 
visualizations,
 
and
 
ease
 
of
 
interaction.
 
Observations
 
are
 
documented
 
to
 
identify
 
usability
 
issues
 
or
 
workflow
 
inefficiencies.
 
 
Figure  10.  Process  Flow  Diagram  
To  support  this  testing  phase,  the  process  flow  diagram  above  illustrates  the  
Black-Box
 
testing
 
logic
 
from
 
the
 
user’s
 
perspective.
 
This
 
diagram
 
depicts:
 
125  


=== PAGE 141 ===
 
1.  User  Input:  Keystroke  and  cursor  interactions  during  calibration  and  
assessment
 
tasks.
 
2.  System  Processing:  HAL  normalization,  sliding  window  filtering,  feature  
extraction,
 
within-session
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
construction,
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
scoring,
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
Analysis
 
(PSI/PAI),
 
and
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classification
 
(hidden
 
from
 
user).
 
3.  System  Output:  T²  scores,  PSI/PAI  indices,  Fuzzy  confidence  scores,  
color-coded
 
flags,
 
per-question
 
flight
 
time
 
data,
 
hover-word
 
hesitation
 
records,
 
and
 
a
 
plain-language
 
clinical
 
rationale.
 
 
This  diagram  demonstrates  that  the  logical  flow  from  user  action  to  clinical  
output
 
is
 
seamless,
 
intuitive,
 
and
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
system’s
 
non-diagnostic
 
decision-support
 
function.
 
3.8  Data  Gathering  Procedure  
To  convert  digital  interactions  into  objective  behavioral  markers,  this  study’s  data  
collection
 
is
 
organized
 
as
 
a
 
controlled
 
in-session
 
procedure.
 
To
 
safeguard
 
the
 
integrity
 
of
 
the
 
results
 
and
 
the
 
privacy
 
of
 
the
 
participants,
 
the
 
following
 
phases
 
will
 
be
 
implemented:
 
 3.8.1  Preliminary  Screening  and  Consent  
Before  any  data  collection,  participant  recruitment,  or  system  deployment,  the  
complete
 
research
 
methodology,
 
risk-mitigation
 
strategy,
 
data-handling
 
protocol,
 
and
 
participant-facing
 
instruments
 
of
 
this
 
study
 
will
 
be
 
submitted
 
to
 
and
 
formally
 
approved
 
by
 
the
 
university’s
 
Research
 
Ethics
 
Committee
 
(REC),
 
in
 
accordance
 
with
 
RA
 
10173
 
(Data
 
Privacy
 
Act
 
of
 
2012)
 
and
 
applicable
 
institutional
 
research
 
ethics
 
126  

=== PAGE 142 ===
 
guidelines.  No  biometric  data  will  be  collected  from  any  participant  until  written  
REC
 
clearance
 
has
 
been
 
obtained
 
and
 
documented.
 
The
 
approved
 
ethics
 
clearance
 
number
 
will
 
be
 
recorded
 
in
 
the
 
study’s
 
official
 
records
 
and
 
disclosed
 
in
 
the
 
final
 
manuscript.
 
In
 
the
 
event
 
that
 
the
 
REC
 
requires
 
modifications
 
to
 
the
 
protocol,
 
data-handling
 
procedures,
 
or
 
consent
 
instruments,
 
all
 
necessary
 
revisions
 
will
 
be
 
implemented
 
in
 
full
 
before
 
data
 
collection
 
commences.
 
Following  ethics  clearance,  and  before  any  digital  data  is  captured  from  
individual
 
participants,
 
each
 
participant
 
will
 
undergo
 
a
 
standard
 
screening
 
process
 
involving
 
the
 
administration
 
of
 
the
 
PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
questionnaires,
 
which
 
serve
 
as
 
validated
 
clinical
 
screening
 
tools
 
for
 
depression
 
and
 
anxiety.
 
These
 
instruments
 
function
 
as
 
“self-report
 
anchors,”
 
providing
 
the
 
system’s
 
psychomotor
 
indices
 
with
 
an
 
independent
 
measure
 
of
 
the
 
individual’s
 
current
 
mental
 
health
 
state
 
against
 
which
 
behavioral
 
deviations
 
can
 
be
 
contextualized.
 
Informed
 
consent
 
will
 
be
 
obtained
 
from
 
every
 
participant
 
at
 
this
 
stage
 
using
 
an
 
IRB/REC-approved
 
consent
 
form.
 
The
 
consent
 
process
 
will
 
explicitly
 
outline:
 
(a)
 
the
 
use
 
of
 
SQLite
 
database
 
as
 
the
 
offline
 
primary
 
database,
 
along
 
with
 
the
 
types
 
of
 
data
 
stored
 
in
 
each;
 
(b)
 
the
 
use
 
of
 
hardware
 
normalization
 
techniques
 
(HAL)
 
and
 
their
 
purpose;
 
(c)
 
the
 
types
 
of
 
behavioral
 
data
 
captured
 
(keystroke
 
timings
 
and
 
cursor
 
trajectories)
 
and
 
the
 
specific
 
psychomotor
 
features
 
derived
 
from
 
them;
 
(d)
 
the
 
generation
 
of
 
a
 
self-contained
 
HTML
 
session
 
report
 
containing
 
T²
 
scores,
 
color-coded
 
flags,
 
per-question
 
flight
 
time
 
data,
 
and
 
hover-word
 
hesitation
 
records,
 
and
 
the
 
clinical
 
use
 
of
 
these
 
outputs;
 
(e)
 
the
 
participant’s
 
right
 
to
 
withdraw
 
at
 
any
 
time
 
without
 
consequence;
 
and
 
(f)
 
the
 
session-scoped
 
data
 
retention
 
policy,
 
under
 
which
 
all
 
raw
 
behavioral
 
data
 
and
 
127  

=== PAGE 143 ===
 
EWMA  baseline  state  are  discarded  from  memory  upon  session  termination,  with  
only
 
anonymized
 
aggregate
 
scores
 
persisted
 
to
 
the
 
database.
 
3.8.2  Hardware  Detection  Calibration  
 Upon  session  initiation,  the  system  automatically  detects  the  hardware  profile  
of
 
the
 
input
 
devices
 
(keyboard
 
type,
 
polling
 
rate,
 
and
 
mouse
 
DPI)
 
and
 
applies
 
the
 
Hardware
 
Abstraction
 
Layer
 
(HAL)
 
to
 
establish
 
device-specific
 
correction
 
factors
 
through
 
the
 
Jitter
 
Compensation
 
algorithm
 
(Cervin
 
et
 
al.,
 
2004).
 
The
 
HAL
 
measures
 
the
 
actual
 
keyboard
 
polling
 
rate,
 
classifies
 
the
 
device
 
as
 
mechanical
 
or
 
membrane,
 
and
 
computes
 
the
 
latency
 
offset
 
(δ
latency)
 
used
 
to
 
normalize
 
all
 
subsequent
 
timestamps.
 
This
 
ensures
 
that
 
measurements
 
captured
 
from
 
any
 
device
 
configuration
 
are
 
comparable
 
and
 
free
 
of
 
hardware-induced
 
timing
 
artifacts
 
before
 
any
 
biometric
 
analysis
 
begins.
 
 
3.8.3  System  Calibration  
Every  session  starts  with  a  calibration  phase  to  mitigate  confounding  factors  
such
 
as
 
native
 
typing
 
speed
 
and
 
hardware
 
differences.
 
Participants
 
performed
 
two
 
calibration
 
tasks,
 
namely
 
the
 
Keyboard
 
Calibration
 
(a
 
guided
 
neutral
 
text-copying
 
task,
 
approximately
 
2
 
minutes)
 
and
 
the
 
Mouse
 
Calibration
 
(a
 
circle-click
 
navigation
 
task),
 
during
 
which
 
the
 
Within-Session
 
EWMA
 
algorithm
 
continuously
 
updates
 
the
 
user’s
 
behavioral
 
baseline.
 
The
 
EWMA
 
mean
 
vector
 
(μ_
ewma)
 
and
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
(S_ewma)
 
are
 
iteratively
 
refined
 
across
 
calibration
 
windows,
 
with
 
the
 
smoothing
 
parameter
 
λ
 
=
 
0.2
 
ensuring
 
that
 
the
 
baseline
 
converges
 
toward
 
the
 
user’s
 
stable
 
behavioral
 
rhythm
 
while
 
down-weighting
 
early-session
 
variance.
 
HAL-normalized
 
keystroke
 
data
 
and
 
Sliding
 
Window-filtered
 
cursor
 
data
 
ensure
 
that
 
128  

=== PAGE 144 ===
 
this  self-referential  baseline  accurately  reflects  the  user’s  typical  performance  
independent
 
of
 
device
 
characteristics.
 
The
 
resulting
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
is
 
held
 
in
 
session
 
memory
 
and
 
used
 
as
 
the
 
reference
 
for
 
all
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
computations
 
during
 
the
 
assessment
 
phase.
 
3.8.4  Controlled  Task  Execution  
A  critical  and  non-negotiable  precondition  for  the  validity  of  psychomotor  
data
 
collected
 
by
 
PsyClick
 
is
 
the
 
standardization
 
of
 
the
 
physical
 
environment
 
in
 
which
 
task
 
execution
 
occurs.
 
This
 
requirement
 
is
 
not
 
merely
 
procedural
 
but
 
is
 
statistically
 
necessary:
 
the
 
system’s
 
Psychomotor
 
Agitation
 
Index
 
(PAI)
 
is
 
derived
 
in
 
part
 
from
 
cursor
 
Jerk,
 
the
 
third
 
derivative
 
of
 
cursor
 
position,
 
and
 
Path
 
Entropy,
 
the
 
Shannon
 
entropy
 
of
 
discretized
 
trajectory
 
angles.
 
Both
 
metrics
 
are
 
sensitive
 
to
 
irregular,
 
high-frequency
 
movements
 
of
 
the
 
mouse.
 
Without
 
environmental
 
controls,
 
a
 
panelist
 
could
 
legitimately
 
argue
 
that
 
an
 
elevated
 
PAI
 
score
 
reflects
 
an
 
unstable
 
desk
 
surface
 
or
 
ambient
 
physical
 
vibration
 
rather
 
than
 
genuine
 
psychomotor
 
agitation
 
arising
 
from
 
anxiety
 
or
 
cognitive
 
distress.
 
The
 
following
 
physical
 
and
 
ergonomic
 
parameters
 
are
 
therefore
 
mandated
 
as
 
inclusion
 
criteria
 
for
 
all
 
data
 
collection
 
sessions.
 
  Room  and  Acoustic  Requirements  
1.  Sound-attenuated  environment :  All  sessions  will  be  conducted  in  a  closed  
room
 
with
 
an
 
ambient
 
noise
 
level
 
not
 
exceeding
 
45
 
decibels
 
(dB),
 
equivalent
 
to
 
a
 
quiet
 
library
 
or
 
private
 
counseling
 
office.
 
Noise
 
levels
 
will
 
be
 
verified
 
at
 
session
 
initiation
 
using
 
a
 
calibrated
 
smartphone
 
decibel
 
meter
 
application.
 
129  

=== PAGE 145 ===
 
Sessions  may  not  proceed  if  ambient  noise  exceeds  this  threshold.  This  
standard
 
is
 
consistent
 
with
 
ISO
 
3382-3
 
guidelines
 
for
 
open-plan
 
office
 
acoustic
 
performance
 
and
 
operationalizes
 
the
 
clinical
 
requirement
 
for
 
a
 
“quiet,
 
distraction-free”
 
environment.
 
2.  Isolation  from  physical  vibration  sources :  The  testing  station  must  not  be  
adjacent
 
to
 
HVAC
 
units,
 
elevators,
 
printing
 
equipment,
 
or
 
high-footfall
 
corridors.
 
Vibration
 
transmitted
 
through
 
flooring
 
or
 
walls
 
constitutes
 
a
 
direct
 
mechanical
 
confound
 
for
 
cursor
 
Jerk,
 
as
 
the
 
pynput
 
mouse
 
listener
 
captures
 
all
 
position
 
deltas
 
regardless
 
of
 
their
 
origin.
 
The
 
session
 
administrator
 
is
 
responsible
 
for
 
verifying
 
environmental
 
stability
 
prior
 
to
 
participant
 
entry.
 
3.  Minimal  visual  and  social  distraction :  No  other  persons  may  be  present  in  
the
 
room
 
during
 
task
 
execution
 
other
 
than
 
the
 
participant
 
and,
 
if
 
required
 
by
 
ethics
 
protocol,
 
a
 
silent
 
session
 
administrator
 
positioned
 
outside
 
the
 
participant’s
 
direct
 
line
 
of
 
sight.
 
Screens,
 
windows,
 
and
 
other
 
visual
 
stimuli
 
that
 
could
 
cause
 
involuntary
 
head
 
or
 
body
 
movement
 
will
 
be
 
oriented
 
away
 
from
 
or
 
removed
 
from
 
the
 
participant’s
 
visual
 
field.
 
Desk  and  Workstation  Requirements  
-  Standardized  desk  height :  The  testing  workstation  will  use  a  desk  height  of  
72–75
 
centimeters
 
(cm)
 
from
 
the
 
floor,
 
which
 
corresponds
 
to
 
the
 
ergonomic
 
standard
 
for
 
keyboard-level
 
surfaces
 
defined
 
by
 
ISO
 
9241-5
 
(Ergonomics
 
of
 
the
 
Human-Environment
 
System:
 
Workstation
 
Design).
 
This
 
range
 
accommodates
 
adults
 
of
 
varying
 
stature
 
while
 
maintaining
 
a
 
forearm
 
angle
 
of
 
approximately
 
90–110
 
degrees
 
during
 
typing,
 
the
 
biomechanically
 
neutral
 
130  

=== PAGE 146 ===
 
position  that  minimizes  involuntary  wrist  and  arm  tremor.  Adjustable-height  
desks
 
are
 
permitted
 
only
 
if
 
they
 
can
 
be
 
locked
 
at
 
a
 
fixed
 
position
 
before
 
session
 
commencement.
 
Folding
 
tables,
 
portable
 
desks
 
with
 
uneven
 
leg
 
lengths,
 
or
 
desks
 
with
 
mechanical
 
vibration
 
(e.g.,
 
standing
 
desk
 
motors)
 
are
 
explicitly
 
prohibited.
 
Desk
 
stability
 
will
 
be
 
verified
 
by
 
the
 
session
 
administrator
 
applying
 
a
 
5-kilogram
 
downward
 
force
 
to
 
each
 
corner
 
of
 
the
 
surface
 
and
 
confirming
 
the
 
absence
 
of
 
visible
 
deflection
 
or
 
rocking.
 
-  Seated  posture  and  chair  specification :  Participants  will  be  seated  in  an  
adjustable
 
chair
 
with
 
lumbar
 
support,
 
set
 
so
 
that
 
their
 
feet
 
rest
 
flat
 
on
 
the
 
floor
 
and
 
their
 
elbows
 
are
 
at
 
desk
 
height.
 
This
 
controls
 
for
 
postural
 
instability
 
as
 
a
 
source
 
of
 
upper-limb
 
tremor.
 
Participants
 
will
 
be
 
instructed
 
not
 
to
 
rest
 
their
 
wrists
 
directly
 
on
 
the
 
keyboard
 
or
 
desk
 
edge
 
during
 
mouse
 
tasks,
 
as
 
resting-position
 
transitions
 
are
 
a
 
known
 
source
 
of
 
non-clinical
 
cursor
 
velocity
 
spikes.
 
Input  Device  and  Surface  Standardization  
-  Standardized,  untextured  mousepad :  All  mouse  input  will  be  recorded  
using
 
a
 
rigid
 
or
 
semi-rigid,
 
flat,
 
untextured
 
mousepad
 
measuring
 
a
 
minimum
 
of
 
300
 
×
 
250
 
mm.
 
Cloth
 
mousepads
 
with
 
uneven
 
weave
 
patterns,
 
edges,
 
or
 
folds
 
introduce
 
surface-level
 
resistance
 
irregularities
 
that
 
cause
 
sub-millimeter
 
cursor
 
position
 
discontinuities;
 
at
 
the
 
sampling
 
rates
 
captured
 
by
 
pynput
 
(up
 
to
 
1000
 
Hz
 
for
 
a
 
high-precision
 
mouse),
 
these
 
discontinuities
 
manifest
 
as
 
spurious
 
Jerk
 
spikes
 
that
 
are
 
statistically
 
indistinguishable
 
from
 
volitional
 
erratic
 
movement.
 
Rigid-surface
 
mousepads
 
(acrylic,
 
aluminum,
 
or
 
131  

=== PAGE 147 ===
 
hard  plastic)  eliminate  this  confound.  The  mousepad  must  be  free  of  debris  
and
 
placed
 
flat
 
on
 
the
 
desk
 
surface
 
without
 
overhang.
 
The
 
same
 
mousepad
 
model
 
will
 
be
 
used
 
across
 
all
 
testing
 
stations
 
to
 
eliminate
 
inter-surface
 
variance.
 
-  Standardized  input  device  model :  Where  possible,  all  sessions  will  use  the  
same
 
model
 
of
 
optical
 
mouse
 
and
 
keyboard.
 
When
 
device
 
standardization
 
is
 
not
 
possible
 
across
 
all
 
testing
 
stations,
 
the
 
Hardware
 
Abstraction
 
Layer
 
(HAL)
 
described
 
in
 
Section
 
3.8.2
 
provides
 
the
 
compensatory
 
mechanism:
 
its
 
Jitter
 
Compensation
 
algorithm
 
normalizes
 
device-specific
 
polling
 
rate
 
differences,
 
and
 
its
 
device-classification
 
module
 
applies
 
model-specific
 
debounce
 
offset
 
corrections.
 
HAL
 
normalization
 
is
 
a
 
necessary
 
but
 
not
 
sufficient
 
control;
 
physical
 
surface
 
standardization
 
remains
 
a
 
primary
 
protocol
 
requirement
 
and
 
is
 
not
 
substituted
 
by
 
software
 
correction.
 
 
  
Methodological  Defence  Against  Environmental  Confounds  in  PAI  
Interpretation
 
The  concern  that  an  unstandardized  physical  environment  could  produce  
false-positive
 
PAI
 
elevations,
 
where
 
a
 
wobbly
 
desk,
 
rough
 
mousepad,
 
or
 
loud
 
noise
 
event
 
causes
 
cursor
 
Jerk
 
spikes
 
erroneously
 
interpreted
 
as
 
psychomotor
 
agitation,
 
is
 
methodologically
 
legitimate
 
and
 
is
 
addressed
 
through
 
four
 
complementary
 
mechanisms
 
within
 
the
 
PsyClick
 
protocol.
 
132  

=== PAGE 148 ===
 
First,  the  ipsative  (self-referential)  baseline  architecture  of  PsyClick  provides  
the
 
primary
 
defence.
 
Because
 
Hotelling’s
 
T²
 
compares
 
each
 
assessment-phase
 
feature
 
vector
 
against
 
the
 
user’s
 
own
 
calibration-phase
 
baseline,
 
established
 
in
 
the
 
same
 
physical
 
environment,
 
on
 
the
 
same
 
desk,
 
with
 
the
 
same
 
equipment,
 
immediately
 
before
 
the
 
assessment
 
phase,
 
any
 
constant
 
environmental
 
noise
 
floor
 
(e.g.,
 
building
 
vibration,
 
HVAC
 
hum,
 
surface
 
texture)
 
is
 
already
 
encoded
 
in
 
the
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
mean
 
μ
 
and
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
S.
 
A
 
persistent
 
environmental
 
confound
 
does
 
not
 
elevate
 
the
 
T²
 
score
 
because
 
it
 
shifts
 
both
 
the
 
baseline
 
and
 
the
 
assessment
 
features
 
equally,
 
producing
 
a
 
near-zero
 
difference
 
vector
 
(x
 
−
 
μ
₋
ᵉᵂᵃ).
 
Only
 
a
 
transient
 
environmental
 
event,
 
one
 
that
 
occurs
 
during
 
assessment
 
but
 
not
 
during
 
calibration,
 
would
 
confound
 
the
 
T²
 
computation.
 
The
 
standardized
 
environment
 
protocol
 
directly
 
prevents
 
such
 
transient
 
events
 
by
 
mandating
 
noise
 
and
 
vibration
 
controls
 
throughout
 
the
 
entire
 
session.
 
Second,  the  Sliding  Window  Filter  provides  algorithmic  attenuation  of  
transient
 
mechanical
 
confounds.
 
The
 
5-sample
 
Gaussian-approximate
 
kernel
 
[0.1,
 
0.2,
 
0.4,
 
0.2,
 
0.1]
 
attenuates
 
high-frequency
 
noise
 
above
 
8
 
Hz
 
while
 
preserving
 
clinically
 
meaningful
 
hesitation
 
patterns
 
below
 
2
 
Hz
 
(Winter,
 
2009).
 
A
 
single
 
cursor
 
position
 
discontinuity
 
caused
 
by
 
a
 
momentary
 
physical
 
disturbance,
 
such
 
as
 
a
 
vibration
 
from
 
footfall
 
or
 
a
 
knock
 
on
 
the
 
desk,
 
is
 
a
 
high-frequency,
 
isolated
 
impulse.
 
The
 
weighted
 
averaging
 
kernel
 
distributes
 
its
 
influence
 
across
 
five
 
consecutive
 
samples
 
with
 
diminishing
 
weight,
 
reducing
 
its
 
contribution
 
to
 
the
 
filtered
 
output
 
by
 
a
 
minimum
 
of
 
60%
 
compared
 
to
 
the
 
raw
 
event.
 
A
 
genuine
 
psychomotor
 
agitation
 
pattern,
 
by
 
contrast,
 
manifests
 
as
 
persistent,
 
repeated
 
irregularities
 
across
 
multiple
 
133  

=== PAGE 149 ===
 
consecutive  windows,  producing  a  signal  that  accumulates  across  the  Jerk  and  Path  
Entropy
 
features
 
over
 
time.
 
The
 
filter
 
is
 
therefore
 
selective:
 
it
 
attenuates
 
isolated
 
mechanical
 
artifacts
 
while
 
preserving
 
sustained
 
psychomotor
 
patterns.
 
Third,  the  multivariate  structure  of  Hotelling’s  T²  provides  a  joint-feature  
defence.
 
An
 
isolated
 
environmental
 
confound
 
affecting
 
cursor
 
Jerk
 
would
 
elevate
 
the
 
PAI
 
without
 
producing
 
a
 
corresponding
 
elevation
 
in
 
the
 
keystroke-domain
 
features
 
(Flight
 
Time,
 
Dwell
 
Time,
 
Error
 
Rate)
 
that
 
are
 
physically
 
decoupled
 
from
 
the
 
mouse
 
surface.
 
Psychomotor
 
agitation
 
arising
 
from
 
clinical
 
anxiety,
 
by
 
contrast,
 
produces
 
correlated
 
elevations
 
across
 
both
 
cursor
 
and
 
keystroke
 
features
 
simultaneously,
 
because
 
both
 
are
 
downstream
 
manifestations
 
of
 
the
 
same
 
disrupted
 
cognitive-motor
 
network
 
(Moussa-Tooks
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024;
 
Fritze
 
et
 
al.,
 
2025).
 
The
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
Analysis
 
decomposition
 
(Cᵢ
 
=
 
(xᵢ
 
−
 
μ
ᵢ)
 
×
 
[S
⁻
¹
 
·
 
(x
 
−
 
μ)]
ᵢ)
 
enables
 
the
 
clinician
 
to
 
inspect
 
which
 
specific
 
features
 
drove
 
a
 
T²
 
elevation.
 
A
 
PAI
 
elevation
 
caused
 
purely
 
by
 
cursor
 
Jerk
 
in
 
the
 
absence
 
of
 
keystroke-domain
 
contributions
 
will
 
produce
 
a
 
distinctive,
 
narrow
 
feature
 
contribution
 
profile
 
that
 
the
 
clinician
 
can
 
recognize
 
as
 
environmentally
 
implausible
 
for
 
a
 
psychomotor
 
agitation
 
diagnosis.
 
The
 
system’s
 
plain-language
 
rationale,
 
generated
 
by
 
the
 
Heuristic
 
Decision
 
Tree,
 
explicitly
 
reports
 
the
 
PSI/PAI
 
feature
 
breakdown,
 
enabling
 
a
 
clinician
 
to
 
distinguish
 
“elevated
 
PAI
 
driven
 
exclusively
 
by
 
Jerk
 
with
 
no
 
keystroke
 
correlate”
 
from
 
“elevated
 
PAI
 
with
 
correlated
 
Error
 
Rate
 
and
 
Path
 
Entropy
 
elevation
 
consistent
 
with
 
anxiety-linked
 
motor
 
dyscontrol.”
 
Fourth,  the  session  administrator  protocol  mandates  the  logging  of  any  
environmental
 
incidents
 
during
 
task
 
execution.
 
If
 
a
 
physical
 
disturbance
 
event
 
occurs
 
134  

=== PAGE 150 ===
 
(e.g.,  a  door  slam,  unexpected  noise,  or  desk  contact)  during  the  assessment  phase,  
the
 
session
 
administrator
 
will
 
record
 
the
 
approximate
 
elapsed
 
time
 
of
 
the
 
event.
 
The
 
system’s
 
post-session
 
report
 
can
 
then
 
be
 
cross-referenced
 
against
 
the
 
temporal
 
heatmap
 
and
 
T²
 
control
 
chart
 
to
 
determine
 
whether
 
the
 
elevated
 
metric
 
is
 
temporally
 
coincident
 
with
 
a
 
logged
 
disturbance.
 
If
 
temporal
 
coincidence
 
is
 
confirmed,
 
the
 
affected
 
assessment
 
window
 
may
 
be
 
excluded
 
from
 
the
 
final
 
analysis
 
under
 
the
 
documented
 
protocol
 
exception,
 
and
 
this
 
exclusion
 
will
 
be
 
recorded
 
in
 
the
 
session
 
audit
 
log.
 
This
 
procedure
 
preserves
 
data
 
integrity
 
without
 
requiring
 
the
 
invalidation
 
of
 
the
 
entire
 
session.
 
Task  Design  Rationale  and  Ethical  Justification  
This  part  administers  a  structured  set  of  graduated  free-text  microstressor  
prompts
 
as
 
the
 
primary
 
emotionally
 
loaded
 
stimulus
 
condition
 
of
 
the
 
PsyClick
 
assessment.
 
The
 
use
 
of
 
microstressor
 
prompts
 
rather
 
than
 
direct
 
clinical
 
questioning
 
(e.g.,
 
'How
 
is
 
your
 
relationship
 
with
 
your
 
family?',
 
'Have
 
you
 
experienced
 
trauma?')
 
is
 
a
 
deliberate
 
ethical
 
and
 
methodological
 
choice.
 
Direct
 
personal
 
questioning
 
poses
 
two
 
distinct
 
risks
 
in
 
this
 
context:
 
(1)
 
it
 
may
 
constitute
 
a
 
clinical
 
trigger
 
in
 
participants
 
with
 
undiagnosed
 
mental
 
health
 
conditions,
 
creating
 
distress
 
responses
 
that
 
exceed
 
the
 
scope
 
of
 
a
 
non-clinical
 
assessment
 
tool;
 
and
 
(2)
 
it
 
may
 
activate
 
self-disclosure
 
avoidance
 
in
 
Filipino
 
participants,
 
for
 
whom
 
stigma
 
and
 
cultural
 
norms
 
around
 
emotional
 
privacy
 
have
 
been
 
identified
 
as
 
significant
 
barriers
 
to
 
mental
 
health
 
engagement
 
(Tuason
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022;
 
Martinez
 
et
 
al.,
 
2020).
 
Both
 
risks
 
were
 
raised
 
during
 
the
 
institutional
 
ethics
 
review
 
process
 
as
 
reasons
 
to
 
exclude
 
invasive
 
personal
 
questioning
 
from
 
the
 
protocol.
 
135  

=== PAGE 151 ===
 
Microstressor  prompts  are  ethically  validated,  low-intensity  situational  items  
drawn
 
from
 
the
 
daily
 
hassles
 
and
 
microstressor
 
literature
 
(Kötter
 
et
 
al.,
 
2020).
 
They
 
do
 
not
 
ask
 
participants
 
to
 
disclose
 
personal
 
trauma,
 
family
 
relationships,
 
clinical
 
symptoms,
 
or
 
private
 
emotional
 
history.
 
Instead,
 
they
 
ask
 
participants
 
to
 
describe
 
how
 
they
 
typically
 
respond
 
to
 
common,
 
universally
 
relatable
 
situations,
 
like
 
time
 
pressure,
 
unexpected
 
changes,
 
interpersonal
 
friction,
 
academic
 
performance
 
demands,
 
that
 
activate
 
mild
 
to
 
moderate
 
cognitive
 
and
 
affective
 
engagement
 
without
 
constituting
 
clinical
 
triggers.
 
This
 
design
 
ensures
 
that
 
the
 
full
 
range
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
signal
 
(from
 
near-baseline
 
at
 
Level
 
A
 
to
 
high
 
psychomotor
 
load
 
at
 
Level
 
C)
 
is
 
generated
 
through
 
ecologically
 
valid,
 
non-invasive
 
prompts
 
appropriate
 
for
 
use
 
with
 
a
 
general
 
adult
 
population
 
in
 
an
 
institutional
 
setting.
 
The  task’s  items  are  organized  into  four  thematic  groups,  each  representing  a  
distinct
 
stressor
 
domain.
 
This
 
domain-distinct
 
structure
 
serves
 
a
 
dual
 
purpose:
 
it
 
ensures
 
systematic
 
coverage
 
of
 
the
 
major
 
stressor
 
categories
 
relevant
 
to
 
the
 
target
 
population,
 
adults,
 
and
 
it
 
enables
 
item-level
 
domain
 
attribution
 
in
 
the
 
temporal
 
heatmap
 
output.
 
From
 
this,
 
the
 
clinician
 
can
 
identify
 
both
 
that
 
a
 
psychomotor
 
deviation
 
occurred
 
and
 
which
 
specific
 
stressor
 
domain
 
appeared
 
to
 
activate
 
it.
 
Each  group  contains  items  spanning  at  least  two  difficulty  levels,  so  
within-group
 
level
 
progression
 
is
 
visible
 
in
 
the
 
level-resolved
 
T²
 
profile.
 
Items
 
are
 
administered
 
sequentially
 
within
 
each
 
group,
 
from
 
lowest
 
to
 
highest
 
level,
 
before
 
advancing
 
to
 
the
 
next
 
group.
 
The
 
four
 
groups
 
and
 
their
 
items
 
are
 
as
 
follows.
 
136  

=== PAGE 152 ===
 
Level  A  presents  routine  situational  items  describing  common  low-demand  
daily
 
circumstances,
 
such
 
as
 
time
 
management
 
patterns
 
or
 
habitual
 
work
 
behaviors
 
(e.g.,
 
'Describe
 
a
 
typical
 
situation
 
in
 
which
 
you
 
feel
 
behind
 
on
 
tasks
 
or
 
deadlines.').
 
These
 
items
 
are
 
expected
 
to
 
produce
 
near-baseline
 
motor
 
responses,
 
as
 
their
 
cognitive-emotional
 
demand
 
is
 
minimal;
 
they
 
serve
 
as
 
an
 
internal
 
within-task
 
reference
 
condition.
 
Level  B  introduces  mild  workload  and  interpersonal  stressor  items  that  
require
 
the
 
participant
 
to
 
reflect
 
on
 
situations
 
involving
 
social
 
friction
 
or
 
moderate
 
demand
 
(e.g.,
 
'Describe
 
how
 
you
 
typically
 
react
 
when
 
unexpected
 
changes
 
are
 
made
 
to
 
plans
 
you
 
have
 
already
 
prepared.').
 
These
 
items
 
activate
 
low-grade
 
rumination
 
and
 
mild
 
affective
 
engagement,
 
producing
 
a
 
moderate
 
departure
 
from
 
Level
 
A
 
motor
 
patterns
 
in
 
participants
 
experiencing
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance.
 
Level  C  presents  cognitively  demanding  self-evaluative  items  that  require  the  
participant
 
to
 
engage
 
in
 
evaluative
 
self-reflection
 
(e.g.,
 
'Describe
 
a
 
situation
 
in
 
which
 
you
 
doubted
 
whether
 
a
 
decision
 
you
 
made
 
was
 
the
 
right
 
one.').
 
These
 
items
 
are
 
expected
 
to
 
produce
 
the
 
greatest
 
psychomotor
 
response
 
in
 
participants
 
with
 
elevated
 
PSI
 
or
 
PAI,
 
as
 
they
 
activate
 
the
 
cognitive-evaluative
 
network
 
most
 
strongly
 
associated
 
with
 
depression-linked
 
rumination
 
and
 
anxiety-linked
 
self-monitoring.
 
Items  are  presented  sequentially  within  each  group,  in  level  order  (Level  A  
before
 
Level
 
B
 
before
 
Level
 
C
 
within
 
each
 
group),
 
before
 
advancing
 
to
 
the
 
next
 
group.
 
This
 
ensures
 
that
 
within
 
each
 
domain,
 
the
 
participant's
 
motor
 
response
 
pattern
 
is
 
established
 
at
 
low
 
load
 
before
 
escalating,
 
making
 
within-group
 
level
 
progression
 
137  

=== PAGE 153 ===
 
interpretable.  Groups  are  presented  in  order  (Group  1  →  2  →  3  →  4),  from  the  safest  
domain
 
to
 
the
 
highest-gravity
 
domain,
 
consistent
 
with
 
ethical
 
best
 
practice
 
for
 
graduated
 
stressor
 
exposure.
 
For  each  item,  participants  type  a  free-text  response  of  unrestricted  length.  No  
time
 
limit
 
is
 
imposed,
 
and
 
no
 
minimum
 
word
 
count
 
is
 
specified,
 
as
 
imposing
 
constraints
 
would
 
introduce
 
external
 
time
 
pressure
 
that
 
confounds
 
the
 
psychomotor
 
signal.
 
The
 
full
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
event
 
log
 
is
 
captured
 
continuously
 
from
 
the
 
moment
 
the
 
prompt
 
appears
 
on
 
screen
 
through
 
submission
 
of
 
the
 
typed
 
response.
 
This
 
log
 
encompasses
 
three
 
behaviorally
 
distinct
 
phases:
 
1.
   
Pre-typing  reading  phase:  Cursor  hover  behavior  is  recorded  and  visualized  
in
 
the
 
per-item
 
temporal
 
heatmap
 
as
 
a
 
measure
 
of
 
reading
 
hesitation
 
and
 
cognitive
 
engagement
 
with
 
the
 
specific
 
prompt.
 
Longer
 
hover
 
durations
 
indicate
 
greater
 
processing
 
demand
 
before
 
motor
 
response
 
initiation.
 
2.
 
 
Composition  phase:  All  keystroke  dynamics  (flight  time,  dwell  time,  typing  
velocity,
 
and
 
error
 
rate)
 
are
 
extracted
 
per
 
sliding
 
window
 
and
 
evaluated
 
against
 
the
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
via
 
Hotelling's
 
T².
 
Each
 
window
 
is
 
tagged
 
with
 
its
 
item
 
ID
 
and
 
group-level
 
context
 
(e.g.,
 
item:
 
'C2',
 
group:
 
2,
 
level:
 
'C',
 
domain:
 
'interpersonal')
 
to
 
enable
 
domain-segmented
 
T²
 
computation.
 
3.
     
Revision  phase:  Backspace  frequency,  pause-before-correction  latency,  and  
post-error
 
velocity
 
change
 
are
 
captured
 
as
 
indicators
 
of
 
self-monitoring
 
behavior,
 
which
 
is
 
elevated
 
in
 
both
 
anxiety-linked
 
vigilance
 
and
 
depression-linked
 
rumination.
 
138  

=== PAGE 154 ===
 
At  no  point  is  the  content  of  the  participant's  typed  responses  analyzed  
linguistically
 
or
 
semantically.
 
The
 
system
 
captures,
 
processes,
 
and
 
reports
 
exclusively
 
on
 
the
 
temporal
 
motor
 
dynamics
 
of
 
typing
 
and
 
cursor
 
behavior,
 
in
 
accordance
 
with
 
the
 
data
 
minimization
 
principles
 
established
 
in
 
Section
 
3.8.1
 
and
 
the
 
participant
 
consent
 
form.
 
 
Level-Resolved  T²  Trajectory  
The  EWMA  baseline  established  during  the  Keyboard  Calibration  and  Mouse  
Calibration
 
phases
 
(low
 
cognitive
 
load,
 
no
 
emotional
 
engagement),
 
supplemented
 
by
 
cursor
 
dynamics
 
from
 
the
 
PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
phases,
 
serves
 
as
 
the
 
primary
 
reference
 
vector
 
for
 
all
 
Hotelling's
 
T²
 
computations
 
across
 
the
 
Emotional
 
Task.
 
Deviations
 
are
 
computed
 
per
 
sliding
 
window
 
and
 
tagged
 
by
 
item,
 
group,
 
and
 
level,
 
enabling
 
three
 
types
 
of
 
comparative
 
analysis:
 
-  Within-level  comparison:  T²  scores  across  all  Level  A  items  (A1,  A2,  A3,  
A4)
 
are
 
averaged
 
to
 
produce
 
a
 
Level
 
A
 
aggregate
 
reference,
 
against
 
which
 
Level
 
B
 
and
 
Level
 
C
 
aggregates
 
are
 
compared.
 
A
 
participant
 
showing
 
Level
 
A
 
≈
 
baseline,
 
Level
 
B
 
>
 
baseline,
 
Level
 
C
 
>>
 
baseline
 
exhibits
 
a
 
dose-response
 
pattern
 
consistent
 
with
 
genuine
 
psychomotor
 
reactivity
 
to
 
stressor
 
load.
 
-  Within-group  level  progression:  Within  Group  2  (Interpersonal),  for  
example,
 
the
 
T²
 
trajectory
 
from
 
A3
 
→
 
B2/B3
 
→
 
C2
 
reveals
 
how
 
the
 
participant's
 
motor
 
behavior
 
escalates
 
as
 
interpersonal
 
stressor
 
severity
 
139  

=== PAGE 155 ===
 
increases  within  that  domain.  A  sharp  jump  specifically  at  C2  (social  
exclusion)
 
but
 
not
 
at
 
B2
 
(unresolved
 
conflict)
 
provides
 
a
 
more
 
granular
 
clinical
 
signal
 
than
 
a
 
single
 
aggregate
 
domain
 
score.
 
-  Cross-group  domain  comparison :  Because  all  four  groups  contain  Level  A  
items,
 
the
 
Level
 
A
 
T²
 
scores
 
across
 
groups
 
serve
 
as
 
comparable
 
baseline
 
estimates,
 
allowing
 
the
 
clinician
 
to
 
determine
 
whether
 
one
 
stressor
 
domain
 
consistently
 
activates
 
higher
 
motor
 
deviation
 
than
 
others
 
even
 
at
 
low
 
intensity.
 
This  three-dimensional  T²  profile  (level  ×  group  ×  item)  is  presented  as  a  
supplementary
 
visualization
 
in
 
the
 
session
 
report.
 
It
 
is
 
intended
 
to
 
support,
 
not
 
replace,
 
the
 
clinical
 
judgment
 
of
 
the
 
supervising
 
mental
 
health
 
professional.
 
 
3.8.5  Evaluation  Using  Dual-Analytical  Analysis  
After  completing  the  session,  the  Analysis  Engine  locally  processes  the  raw  
logs
 
it
 
acquires
 
to
 
maintain
 
data
 
privacy
 
and
 
operational
 
control.
 
During
 
this
 
stage,
 
the
 
system
 
employs
 
a
 
multi-stage
 
analytical
 
approach
 
to
 
produce
 
an
 
accurate
 
clinical
 
report:
 
1.  Multivariate  Anomaly  Detection:  Hotelling’s  T²  is  computed  for  each  
assessment
 
window
 
using
 
the
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
established
 
during
 
calibration.
 
The
 
T²
 
score
 
quantifies
 
the
 
statistical
 
distance
 
of
 
the
 
current
 
session’s
 
eight-feature
 
profile
 
from
 
the
 
user’s
 
own
 
normal
 
behavioral
 
distribution,
 
with
 
a
 
threshold
 
derived
 
from
 
the
 
F-distribution
 
at
 
α
 
=
 
0.05.
 
140  

=== PAGE 156 ===
 
2.  Disturbance  Characterization:  Feature  Contribution  Analysis  decomposes  
each
 
anomalous
 
T²
 
score
 
into
 
PSI
 
and
 
PAI
 
indices,
 
identifying
 
whether
 
the
 
detected
 
disturbance
 
reflects
 
psychomotor
 
slowing
 
(consistent
 
with
 
retardation)
 
or
 
psychomotor
 
agitation
 
(consistent
 
with
 
hyperarousal),
 
providing
 
clinically
 
actionable
 
characterization.
 
3.  Graded  Classification:  The  Fuzzy  Logic  Classifier  assigns  membership  
degrees
 
to
 
clinical
 
categories
 
based
 
on
 
the
 
T²
 
score,
 
PSI,
 
and
 
PAI,
 
producing
 
a
 
confidence-weighted
 
classification
 
that
 
reflects
 
the
 
continuous
 
nature
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance.
 
The
 
Heuristic
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
maps
 
this
 
output
 
to
 
the
 
final
 
Green/Amber/Red
 
flag
 
with
 
a
 
plain-language
 
rationale.
 
4.  Normative  Benchmarking:  The  system  additionally  compares  the  T²  score  
against
 
a
 
normative
 
population
 
distribution
 
derived
 
from
 
aggregate
 
study
 
data,
 
contextualizing
 
individual
 
performance
 
within
 
the
 
local
 
demographic
 
and
 
identifying
 
cases
 
where
 
a
 
person’s
 
deviation
 
from
 
their
 
own
 
baseline
 
is
 
also
 
significant
 
relative
 
to
 
the
 
population.
 
5.  Within-Task  Level  Analysis:  For  sessions  that  include  Task  3  data,  the  
system
 
computes
 
and
 
reports
 
T²
 
scores
 
segmented
 
along
 
two
 
dimensions:
 
(a)
 
by
 
difficulty
 
level
 
(Level
 
A
 
aggregate,
 
Level
 
B
 
aggregate,
 
Level
 
C
 
aggregate),
 
and
 
(b)
 
by
 
stressor
 
domain
 
group
 
(Group
 
1:
 
Time/Workload;
 
Group
 
2:
 
Interpersonal;
 
Group
 
3:
 
Academic/Performance;
 
Group
 
4:
 
Self-evaluation).
 
Each
 
feature
 
window
 
is
 
tagged
 
at
 
capture
 
time
 
with
 
its
 
item
 
ID,
 
group
 
number,
 
level,
 
and
 
domain
 
label,
 
enabling
 
post-session
 
grouping
 
without
 
requiring
 
real-time
 
segmentation.
 
The
 
resulting
 
domain-level
 
T²
 
141  

=== PAGE 157 ===
 
profile  enables  the  clinician  to  determine  whether  psychomotor  anomalies  
emerge
 
uniformly
 
across
 
stimulus
 
conditions
 
—
 
consistent
 
with
 
persistent
 
baseline-level
 
disturbance
 
—
 
or
 
are
 
selectively
 
activated
 
by
 
specific
 
stressor
 
domains
 
or
 
difficulty
 
levels
 
—
 
consistent
 
with
 
domain-specific
 
reactivity.
 
A
 
participant
 
who
 
shows
 
near-baseline
 
T²
 
across
 
Groups
 
1–3
 
but
 
marked
 
elevation
 
at
 
Group
 
4
 
(self-evaluation)
 
provides
 
a
 
qualitatively
 
distinct
 
clinical
 
signal
 
from
 
a
 
participant
 
who
 
shows
 
uniform
 
elevation
 
across
 
all
 
groups,
 
and
 
both
 
are
 
distinguishable
 
from
 
a
 
participant
 
with
 
no
 
elevation
 
at
 
any
 
level.
 
 
By  integrating  these  analytical  stages  with  HAL-normalized  data  and  Sliding  
Window-filtered
 
cursor
 
metrics,
 
PsyClick
 
can
 
recognize
 
“hidden”
 
distress
 
in
 
high-functioning
 
individuals
 
who,
 
while
 
still
 
within
 
population
 
norms,
 
have
 
shown
 
notable
 
multivariate
 
deviation
 
from
 
their
 
established
 
within-session
 
baseline.

3.9  Respondents  of  the  Study  and  Sampling  Technique  
 The  researchers  will  employ  purposive  sampling  to  select  subjects  who  meet  the  
specific
 
neurophysiological
 
and
 
developmental
 
parameters
 
required
 
for
 
psychomotor
 
analysis.
 
According
 
to
 
Tajik
 
et
 
al.
 
(2024),
 
purposive
 
sampling
 
is
 
a
 
non-probability
 
approach
 
involving
 
the
 
intentional
 
selection
 
of
 
certain
 
units,
 
such
 
as
 
cases,
 
individuals,
 
or
 
events,
 
based
 
on
 
their
 
significance
 
to
 
the
 
research
 
question,
 
aimed
 
at
 
obtaining
 
profound
 
insights
 
into
 
complex
 
phenomena,
 
enabling
 
the
 
researchers
 
to
 
concentrate
 
on
 
information-rich
 
cases,
 
guaranteeing
 
that
 
the
 
behavioral
 
signals
 
recorded—including
 
typing
 
rhythms
 
and
 
cursor
 
movements—accurately
 
reflect
 
the
 
baseline
 
motor
 
abilities
 
of
 
the
 
target
 
demographic.
 
 
144  

=== PAGE 160 ===
 
 The  study  will  utilize  homogeneous  sampling  within  the  context  of  purposive  
sampling.
 
This
 
sub-technique
 
focuses
 
on
 
selecting
 
individuals
 
who
 
possess
 
a
 
specific
 
set
 
of
 
characteristics
 
to
 
mitigate
 
the
 
influence
 
of
 
external
 
confounding
 
variables.
 
For
 
this
 
study,
 
the
 
participants
 
will
 
include
 
a
 
minimum
 
of
 
100
 
adults,
 
aged
 
18
 
and
 
above,
 
since
 
young
 
people
 
have
 
been
 
found
 
to
 
have
 
high
 
levels
 
of
 
psychological
 
distress.
 
In
 
addition,
 
limiting
 
the
 
population
 
to
 
this
 
age
 
group
 
helps
 
control
 
for
 
differences
 
in
 
fine
 
motor
 
control
 
and
 
digital
 
literacy.
 
These
 
differences
 
are
 
known
 
to
 
affect
 
the
 
millisecond-level
 
interaction
 
dynamics
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
captures.
 
 
 Selecting  these  participants  is  vital  to  demonstrating  that  PsyClick  is  a  useful  tool  for  
clinical
 
decision-making.
 
By
 
concentrating
 
on
 
a
 
stable
 
group
 
of
 
adults,
 
the
 
researchers
 
may
 
implement
 
a
 
project-specific
 
normative
 
baseline
 
that
 
reflects
 
the
 
typical
 
psychomotor
 
performance
 
of
 
this
 
local
 
demographic.
 
This
 
demographic
 
focus
 
ensures
 
that
 
the
 
Ipsative
 
Analysis
 
can
 
accurately
 
identify
 
important
 
differences
 
between
 
individuals
 
by
 
comparing
 
their
 
real-time
 
performance
 
with
 
their
 
session-specific
 
calibration,
 
unaffected
 
by
 
broad
 
population
 
variances.
 
Additionally,
 
the
 
diverse
 
hardware
 
configurations
 
used
 
by
 
participants
 
will
 
validate
 
the
 
effectiveness
 
of
 
the
 
Hardware
 
Abstraction
 
Layer
 
in
 
normalizing
 
device-dependent
 
variations.
 
 
 
3.10  Statistical  Treatment  
 The  statistical  treatment  of  PsyClick  integrates  the  system’s  core  analytical  pipeline  
with
 
the
 
empirical
 
validation
 
methods
 
applied
 
to
 
the
 
study’s
 
collected
 
data.
 
The
 
following
 
subsections
 
detail
 
each
 
statistical
 
tool
 
in
 
order
 
of
 
its
 
application
 
across
 
the
 
five
 
research
 
145  

=== PAGE 161 ===
 
questions,  ensuring  full  alignment  between  the  methodology  presented  here  and  the  
analytical
 
procedures
 
reported
 
in
 
Chapter
 
4
 
(Results
 
and
 
Discussion)
 
and
 
summarized
 
in
 
Chapter
 
5
 
(Conclusions).
 
3.10.1  Preliminary  Normality  Testing:  Shapiro-Wilk  Test  
 Before  applying  any  parametric  or  non-parametric  analytical  method,  
normality
 
of
 
all
 
continuous
 
outcome
 
variables—specifically
 
the
 
T
2
 
score,
 
PSI
 
index,
 
PAI
 
index,
 
PHQ-9
 
total
 
score,
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
total
 
score,
 
will
 
be
 
assessed
 
using
 
the
 
Shapiro-Wilk
 
test
 
(α
 
=
 
0.05).
 
The
 
Shapiro-Wilk
 
test
 
is
 
preferred
 
over
 
the
 
Kolmogorov-Smirnov
 
test
 
for
 
sample
 
sizes
 
below
 
2,000
 
due
 
to
 
its
 
superior
 
statistical
 
power
 
under
 
such
 
conditions
 
(Razali
 
&
 
Wah,
 
2011).
 
The
 
outcome
 
of
 
normality
 
testing
 
directly
 
determines
 
the
 
selection
 
of
 
parametric
 
(Pearson
 
correlation,
 
one-way
 
ANOVA)
 
versus
 
non-parametric
 
(Spearman
 
correlation,
 
Kruskal-Wallis)
 
analytical
 
methods.
 
Results
 
of
 
the
 
Shapiro-Wilk
 
test
 
for
 
all
 
primary
 
variables
 
are
 
reported
 
in
 
Chapter
 
4.
 
3.10.2  EWMA  Baseline  Modeling  
The  primary  baseline  estimation  method  of  PsyClick  is  the  Exponentially  
Weighted
 
Moving
 
Average
 
(EWMA),
 
which
 
replaces
 
the
 
static
 
mean-and-standard-deviation
 
approach
 
of
 
univariate
 
methods
 
with
 
a
 
dynamically
 
updated,
 
session-specific
 
multivariate
 
baseline.
 
Rather
 
than
 
relying
 
on
 
a
 
fixed
 
calibration
 
mean,
 
EWMA
 
weights
 
each
 
successive
 
calibration
 
window
 
with
 
exponentially
 
decreasing
 
influence
 
on
 
older
 
observations,
 
ensuring
 
the
 
baseline
 
reflects
 
the
 
user’s
 
current
 
behavioral
 
rhythm
 
(Montgomery,
 
2009).
 
146  

=== PAGE 162 ===
 
The  EWMA  baseline  update  equations  applied  across  calibration  windows  are:  
μ_ ewma
t
 =  λ  ·  x
t
 +  (1  −  λ)  ·  μ_ ewma
t−1
 
S_ ewma
t
 =  λ  ·  (x
t
 −  μ_ ewma
t
)(x
t
 −  μ_ ewma
t
)ᵀ  +  (1  −  λ)  ·  S_ ewma
t−1
  Where:  
‒  μ_ ewma
t
 :  The  EWMA  mean  vector  at  time  t,  representing  the  adaptive  
baseline
 
across
 
all
 
p
 
features
 
‒  S_ ewma
t
 :  The  EWMA  covariance  matrix  at  time  t  
‒  x
t
 :  The  current  feature  vector  extracted  from  the  active  keystroke/cursor  
window
 
‒  λ  :  The  smoothing  parameter,  set  to  0.2  
3.10.3  HAL  Validation:  Levene’s  Test  for  Equality  Variance  
 To  empirically  confirm  the  Hardware  Abstraction  Layer’s  (HAL)  
device-ormalization
 
effectiveness,
 
T
2
 
score
 
variance
 
will
 
be
 
compared
 
across
 
multiple
 
hardware
 
configurations,
 
standard
 
membrane
 
keyboards
 
at
 
125
 
Hz,
 
mechanical
 
keyboards
 
at
 
1000
 
Hz,
 
optical
 
mice
 
at
 
400
 
DPI,
 
and
 
high-precision
 
optical
 
mice
 
at
 
1600
 
DPI,
 
before
 
and
 
after
 
HAL
 
correction.
 
Levene's
 
test
 
for
 
equality
 
of
 
variances
 
(α
 
=
 
0.05)
 
will
 
be
 
applied
 
to
 
test
 
the
 
null
 
hypothesis
 
that
 
T²
 
score
 
variance
 
is
 
homogeneous
 
across
 
device
 
types
 
after
 
normalization.
 
A
 
non-significant
 
Levene's
 
test
 
result
 
(p
 
>
 
.05)
 
post-normalization
 
confirms
 
that
 
the
 
HAL
 
decouples
 
hardware
 
characteristics
 
from
 
the
 
psychomotor
 
signal,
 
establishing
 
the
 
measurement
 
validity
 
prerequisite
 
for
 
all
 
downstream
 
statistical
 
operations.
 
Pre-
 
and
 
post-normalization
 
variance
 
comparison
 
results
 
are
 
reported
 
in
 
Chapter
 
4.
 
147  

=== PAGE 163 ===
 
 3.10.4  Primary  Detection  Model:  Hotelling’s  T²  with  Feature  Analysis  
The  primary  analytical  model  is  Hotelling’s  T²  statistic  (Hotelling,  1931),  
which
 
evaluates
 
all
 
eight
 
extracted
 
biometric
 
features
 
simultaneously
 
as
 
a
 
multivariate
 
observation
 
against
 
the
 
EWMA
 
baseline.
 
For
 
each
 
assessment
 
window,
 
the
 
T²
 
score
 
is
 
computed
 
as:
 
T²  =  n  ·  (x  −  μ_ ewma )ᵀ  ·  S ⁻ ¹  ·  (x  −  μ_ ewma )  
The  covariance  matrix  S  is  estimated  using  the  Ledoit-Wolf  shrinkage  
estimator
 
(Ledoit
 
&
 
Wolf,
 
2004)
 
for
 
numerical
 
stability.
 
The
 
statistically
 
derived
 
F-distribution
 
threshold
 
(Tracy
 
et
 
al.,
 
1992)
 
determines
 
whether
 
the
 
T²
 
score
 
indicates
 
significant
 
deviation:
 
T²_ threshold  =  [p(n  −  1)  /  (n  −  p)]  ·  F( α;  p,  n  −  p)  
Feature  Contribution  Analysis  subsequently  decomposes  flagged  T²  scores  
into
 
the
 
Psychomotor
 
Slowing
 
Index
 
(PSI)
 
and
 
Psychomotor
 
Agitation
 
Index
 
(PAI),
 
providing
 
directional
 
clinical
 
characterization
 
of
 
each
 
detected
 
anomaly.
 
Where:  
-  p  :  The  number  of  features  (8)  
-  n  :  The  number  of  calibration  windows  accumulated  
-  F( α;  p,  n−p)  :  The  critical  value  of  the  F-distribution  at  significance  level  α  =  
0.05
 
Adaptive  Covariance  Estimation:  Ledoit-Wolf  to  Empirical  Transition  
A  fundamental  statistical  challenge  in  computing  Hotelling’s  T²  under  
single-session
 
conditions
 
is
 
the
 
estimation
 
of
 
the
 
p
 
×
 
p
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
S
 
from
 
the
 
148  

=== PAGE 164 ===
 
calibration  windows  accumulated  thus  far.  The  quality  of  this  estimate—and  
therefore

calibration

n
 
to
 
feature

The

at

1.  Phase  1:  Ledoit-Wolf  Shrinkage  Estimator  (n  ≤  5p,  i.e.,  n  ≤  40)  
When  the  number  of  accumulated  calibration  windows  n  is  small  relative  to  
the
 
feature
 
dimensionality
 
p
 
=
 
8,

or

Specifically,

matrix

=
 
(1/n)
 
Σ(
x 
 
−
 
μ)(
x 
 
−
 
μ)
ᵀ
 
to

(not

is
 
n
 
>

>

However,

The

 ᵃˣ

Ledoit

The

α ₋ ˣʰ  :  The  analytically  optimal  shrinkage  intensity,  derived  by  Ledoit  and  Wolf  
(2004)
 
as

The  shrinkage  intensity  α  is  computed  using  a  custom  Ledoit-Wolf  formula  in

biomarker

The

2.  Phase  2:  Reduced-Shrinkage  Ledoit-Wolf  (n  ≥  5p,  i.e.,  n  ≥  40)  
As  the  calibration  phase  progresses  and  n  grows,  shrinkage  introduces  a  
different
 
form
 
of
 
error:
 
systematic
 
bias.
 
The
 
shrinkage
 
estimator
 
deliberately
 
pulls
 
the
 
estimated
 
covariance
 
toward
 
the
 
identity
 
matrix,
 
attenuating
 
the
 
true
 
off-diagonal
 
covariance
 
structure
 
between
 
features.
 
When
 
n
 
is
 
large,
 
this
 
attenuation
 
is
 
no
 
longer
 
warranted,
 
because
 
the
 
sample
 
covariance
 
S ᵃᴼ ℓᵉ
 
has
 
already
 
converged
 
sufficiently
 
close
 
to
 
the
 
true
 
population
 
covariance
 
Σ.
 
Applying
 
unnecessary
 
shrinkage
 
in
 
this
 
regime
 
biases
 
S
⁻
¹
 
away
 
from
 
the
 
true
 
precision
 
matrix,
 
causing
 
the
 
T²
 
statistic
 
to
 
under-weight
 
the
 
genuine
 
inter-feature
 
covariance
 
structure
 
and
 
reducing
 
the
 
clinical
 
sensitivity
 
of
 
anomaly
 
detection.

=== PAGE 166 ===
 
The  system  reduces  shrinkage  intensity  from  α  =  0.15  to  α  =  0.05  when  n  
reaches
 
the
 
threshold
 
n*
 
=
 
5p
 
=
 
5

40
 
calibration

The

This

3.  Mathematical  Justification  of  the  n*  =  5p  =  40  Threshold  
Argument  1:  Relative  Estimation  Error  Bound.  Ledoit  and  Wolf  (2004)  and  
subsequent
 
work
 
by

and

(2005)

that

matrix
 
is
 
approximately
 
bounded
 
by:
 
E[||S  ᵃᴼ  ℓ ᵉ  −  Σ   ᴿᵊᵉ ||F]  /  ||Σ   ᴿᵊᵉ ||F  ≈  √(p  /  n)  
For  p  =  8,  substituting  n*  =  40  yields  a  relative  error  of  √(8/40)  =  √0.2  ≈  
0.447,
 
which
 
falls
 
below
 
the
 
0.45
 
threshold
 
identified
 
by
 
Ledoit
 
and

as

sample

becomes

for

in

At

of

to

Argument  2:  F-Distribution  Degrees  of  Freedom  Stability.  The  F-distribution  
threshold
 
for
 
T²
 
is:
 
151  

=== PAGE 167 ===
 
T²_threshold  =  [p(n  −  1)  /  (n  −  p)]  ·  F(0.05;  p,  n  −  p)  
The  denominator  degrees  of  freedom  (n  −  p)  govern  the  heaviness  of  the

When

The

 Implementation  of  the  Adaptive  Transition  
The  AnomalyEngine  implements  the  transition  as  a  conditional  selector

The  shrinkage  reduction  is  monotonic  and  non-reversible:  once  n  reaches  40

3.10.5  Feature  Contribution  Analysis  and  Fuzzy  Logic  Classification  
 Feature  Contribution  Analysis  decomposes  each  flagged  T²  score  into  
per-feature
 
contributions
 
using
 
the
 
Mason
 
and

(2002)

C_i  =  (x_i  −  μ_ i)  ×  [S ⁻ ¹  ·  (x  −  μ)]_ i  
Contributions  are  aggregated  into  the  Psychomotor  Slowing  Index  (PSI),  summing

Dwell
 
Time,
 
and

and

from

Entropy,
 
Jerk,
 
Error
 
Rate,
 
Cursor
 
Velocity,
 
and

The

then

and

into

and

functions,
 
evaluated
 
under
 
seven
 
priority-ordered
 
IF-THEN
 
rules
 
via
 
the
 
Mamdani
 
inference
 
architecture.
 
Defuzzification
 
employs
 
dominant-rule
 
confidence
 
scoring.
 
Results
 
are
 
reported
 
by
 
PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
severity
 
group
 
in

4.
 
3.10.6  Domain-Segmented  T
 2
 Analysis  
 For  Task  3  sessions,  T²  scores  are  segmented  post-session  by  stressor  domain  
group
 
(Group
 
1:
 
Time/Workload;
 
Group
 
2:
 
Interpersonal;
 
Group
 
3:
 
Academic/Performance;
 
Group
 
4:
 
Self-Evaluation)
 
and
 
by
 
difficulty
 
level
 
(Level
 
A:
 
routine;
 
Level
 
B:
 
mild
 
stressor;
 
Level
 
C:
 
cognitively
 
demanding
 
self-evaluative).
 
The
 
Friedman
 
test
 
(the
 
non-parametric
 
equivalent
 
of
 
repeated-measures
 
ANOVA)
 
will
 
be
 
applied
 
to
 
evaluate
 
whether
 
mean
 
T²
 
scores
 
differ
 
significantly
 
across
 
the
 
three
 
154  

=== PAGE 170 ===
 
difficulty  levels  within  sessions  (A  vs.  B  vs.  C)  and  across  the  four  stressor  domain  
groups.
 
When
 
the
 
Friedman
 
test
 
yields
 
a
 
statistically
 
significant
 
result
 
(α
 
=
 
0.05),
 
pairwise
 
post-hoc

will
 
be
 
conducted
 
using
 
the
 
Wilcoxon

test
 
(for

and
 
Dunn's
 
test
 
with
 
Bonferroni

(for

A
 
statistically

increase
 
T²_A
 
<
 
T²_B
 
<
 
T²_C
 
constitutes
 
dose-response

of
 
genuine
 
psychomotor
 
stressor
 
reactivity.
 
Domain-segmented

and
 
level-resolved
 
T²

are
 
presented
 
in
 
Chapter
 
4.
 
3.10.7  Convergent  Validity:  Pearson  r  /  Spearman  ρ  Correlation  
  Convergent  validity  will  be  assessed  through  bivariate  correlations  between  
the
 
system's
 
primary
 
psychomotor
 
indices
 
(T²
 
score,
 
PSI,
 
PAI)
 
and
 
the
 
PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
total
 
scores.
 
The
 
choice
 
between
 
Pearson's
 
r
 
and
 
Spearman's
 
ρ
 
is
 
conditional
 
on
 
the
 
Shapiro-Wilk
 
normality
 
test
 
results
 
(Section
 
3.10.1):
 
Pearson's
 
r
 
will
 
be
 
applied
 
if
 
all
 
five
 
variables
 
satisfy
 
normality
 
assumptions
 
(Shapiro-Wilk
 
p
 
>
 
.05);
 
Spearman's
 
ρ
 
will
 
be
 
applied
 
if
 
any
 
variable
 
deviates
 
significantly
 
from
 
normality.
 
The
 
following
 
convergent
 
validity
 
structure
 
is
 
hypothesized,
 
grounded
 
in
 
the
 
Psychomotor-Cognitive
 
Link
 
theoretical
 
framework:
 
●  T²  and  PSI  are  expected  to  positively  correlate  with  PHQ-9  severity  
(psychomotor
 
slowing
 
is
 
associated
 
with
 
depressive
 
retardation).
 
●  T²  and  PAI  are  expected  to  positively  correlate  with  GAD-7  severity  
(psychomotor
 
agitation
 
is
 
associated
 
with
 
anxiety-driven
 
hyperarousal).
 
155  

=== PAGE 171 ===
 
The  minimum  acceptable  correlation  for  convergent  validity  is  r  ≥  0.30,  consistent  
with
 
the
 
behavioral
 
biomarker
 
adequacy
 
threshold
 
established
 
by
 
Kline
 
(2005).
 
A
 
correlation
 
of
 
r
 
≥
 
0.50
 
would
 
indicate
 
strong
 
convergent
 
validity.
 
The
 
required
 
sample
 
size
 
of
 
n
 
=
 
85
 
for
 
detecting
 
r
 
=
 
0.30
 
at
 
α
 
=
 
0.05
 
with
 
80%
 
power
 
was
 
derived
 
using
 
Fisher's
 
z-transformation
 
formula
 
(Cohen,
 
1988):
 
n
 
=
 
[(z_
α/2
 
+
 
z_
β)
 
/
 
arctanh(r)]²
 
+
 
3.
 
With
 
the
 
final
 
recruitment
 
target
 
of
 
n
 
=
 
105,
 
the
 
study
 
achieves
 
86.2%
 
power
 
for
 
detecting
 
the
 
minimum
 
acceptable
 
convergent
 
validity
 
correlation.
 
Full
 
correlation
 
matrices,
 
scatter
 
plots,
 
and
 
95%
 
confidence
 
intervals
 
are
 
presented
 
in
 
Chapter
 
4.
 
3.10.8  Kruskal-Wallis  H  Test  and  Dunn’s  Post-hoc  Analysis  
To  assess  whether  T
 2
,  PSI,  PAI  differ  significantly  across  PHQ-9  and  GAD-7  
clinical
 
severity
 
groups
 
(Minimal,
 
Mild,
 
Moderate,
 
Moderately
 
Severe,
 
Severe),
 
the
 
Kruskal-Wallis
 
H
 
test
 
(the
 
non-parametric
 
equivalent
 
of
 
one-way
 
ANOVA)
 
will
 
be
 
employed,
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
anticipated
 
non-normal
 
distribution
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
indices
 
in
 
a
 
clinical
 
screening
 
sample.
 
When
 
the
 
Kruskal-Wallis
 
test
 
yields
 
a
 
statistically
 
significant
 
result
 
(α
 
=
 
0.05),
 
pairwise
 
post-hoc
 
comparisons
 
will
 
be
 
conducted
 
using
 
Dunn's
 
test
 
with
 
Bonferroni
 
correction
 
to
 
identify
 
which
 
specific
 
severity
 
group
 
pairs
 
differ.
 
Effect
 
sizes
 
(epsilon-squared
 
ε²)
 
will
 
be
 
reported
 
for
 
all
 
significant
 
comparisons.
 
The
 
expected
 
pattern
 
is
 
a
 
monotonic
 
increase
 
in
 
PSI
 
with
 
PHQ-9
 
severity
 
and
 
a
 
monotonic
 
increase
 
in
 
PAI
 
with
 
GAD-7
 
severity,
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
transdiagnostic
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance
 
literature.
 
156  

=== PAGE 172 ===
 
3.10.9  Clinical  Concordance:  Cohen’s  Kappa  (κ t  
 Cohen's  kappa  will  be  computed  across  all  beta  testing  sessions  to  quantify  
the
 
degree
 
of
 
systematic
 
agreement
 
between
 
PsyClick's
 
color-coded
 
clinical
 
flags
 
(Green,
 
Amber,
 
Red;
 
and
 
Red
 
—
 
Mixed
 
Disturbance)
 
and
 
the
 
attending
 
mental
 
health
 
professional's
 
independent
 
observational
 
ratings
 
on
 
the
 
Clinical
 
Observation
 
Rating
 
Form
 
(CORF),
 
recorded
 
before
 
report
 
disclosure.
 
The
 
minimum
 
acceptable
 
concordance
 
threshold
 
is
 
κ
 
≥
 
0.60
 
(Substantial
 
Agreement,
 
Landis
 
&
 
Koch,
 
1977),
 
as
 
specified
 
in
 
Section
 
3.7.1.
 
Kappa
 
will
 
be
 
computed
 
for:
 
(a)
 
overall
 
flag
 
agreement
 
(3×4
 
confusion
 
matrix:
 
system
 
flag
 
vs.
 
CORF
 
category);
 
(b)
 
disturbance
 
type
 
agreement
 
(Normal/Retardation/Agitation/Mixed);
 
and
 
(c)
 
peak
 
domain
 
agreement.
 
Standard
 
error
 
and
 
95%
 
confidence
 
intervals
 
will
 
be
 
reported
 
for
 
each
 
kappa
 
coefficient.
 
3.10.10  Likert  Scale:  PHQ-9,  GAD-7,  ISO/EIC  25010,  and  TAM  Survey  
Three  Likert-format  instruments  are  employed  in  this  study  each  serving  a  distinct  
analytical
 
purpose:
 
 
1.  PHQ-9  and  GAD-7  (4-Point  Frequency  Scale)  
The  researchers  will  use  a  Likert  Scale  to  quantify  the  subjective  responses  
from
 
the
 
clinical
 
screening
 
instruments.
 
Specifically,
 
the
 
PHQ-9
 
(Patient
 
Health
 
Questionnaire)
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
(Generalized
 
Anxiety
 
Disorder)
 
use
 
a
 
4-point
 
frequency
 
scale
 
to
 
assess
 
symptom
 
severity.
 
For
 
each
 
item
 
in
 
the
 
questionnaire,
 
the
 
respondents
 
must
 
rate
 
their
 
experience
 
over
 
the
 
last
 
two
 
weeks.
 
Scale   Description  0  Not  at  all

=== PAGE 173 ===
 
1  Several  Days  2  More  than  half  the  days  3  Nearly  every  day  
Table  5.  Likert  Scale  
These  individual  Likert  ratings  are  then  summed  to  produce  a  final  severity  
score
 
(ranging

is

"Minimal",
 
"Mild",
 
"Moderate",
 
or
 
"Severe"
 
distress.
 
2.  ISO/EIC  25010  Software  Quality  Evaluation  (5-Point  Likert  Scale)  
 Domain  experts  evaluate  PsyClick  across  five  selected  quality  
characteristics—Functional
 
Suitability,
 
Performance
 
Efficiency,
 
Usability,
 
Reliability,

Security—using

=
 
Poor,

=

An
 
overall
 
weighted
 
mean
 
across

provides
 
a

software
 
quality
 
rating.
 
Results
 
are

item  and  construct  level,  with  mean  construct  scores  ≥  3.50  interpreted  as  favorable

3.10.11  Correlation  Analysis  
The  correlational  component  of  this  study  is  essential  for  system  validation,  
as
 
it
 
seeks
 
to
 
determine
 
the
 
relationship
 
between
 
the
 
system-calculated
 
scores
 
for
 
psychomotor
 
deviation
 
(T
2
,
 
PSI,
 
PAI)
 
and
 
the
 
results
 
of
 
recognized
 
clinical
 
screening
 
instruments.
 
To
 
achieve
 
this,
 
convergent
 
validity
 
will
 
be
 
assessed
 
through
 
Pearson
 
correlation
 
coefficients,
 
or
 
Spearman’s
 
rank-order
 
correlation
 
if
 
preliminary
 
Shapiro-Wilk
 
testing
 
indicates
 
non-normality
 
in
 
the
 
data.
 
To  support  this  correlational  design,  the  researchers  will  use  a  Likert  Scale  to  
quantify
 
the
 
subjective
 
responses
 
from
 
the
 
clinical
 
screening
 
instruments.
 
Specifically,
 
the
 
PHQ-9(Patient
 
Health
 
Questionnaire)
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
(Generalized
 
Anxiety
 
Disorder)
 
use
 
a
 
4-point
 
frequency
 
scale
 
to
 
assess
 
symptom
 
severity.
 
For
 
each
 
item
 
in
 
the
 
questionnaire,
 
the
 
respondents
 
must
 
rate
 
their
 
experience
 
over
 
the
 
last
 
two
 
weeks.
 
These
 
final
 
subjective
 
scores
 
will
 
serve
 
as
 
the
 
validation
 
variable
 
to
 
correlate
 
against
 
the
 
system’s
 
objective
 
psychomotor
 
indices.
 
Additionally,  the  effectiveness  of  the  Hardware  Abstraction  Layer  (HAL)  will  
be
 
validated
 
by
 
comparing
 
T
2
 
score
 
variances
 
across
 
different
 
device
 
types
 
before
 
and
 
after
 
HAL
 
normalization.
 
A
 
statistically
 
significant
 
reduction
 
in
 
device-dependent
 
variance
 
will
 
confirm
 
that
 
the
 
HAL
 
successfully
 
mitigates
 
hardware-related
 
159  

=== PAGE 175 ===
 
confounds,  supporting  the  validity  of  cross-device  comparisons  and  ensuring  the  
integrity
 
of

in

Chapter  IV  
  
RESULTS  AND  DISCUSSION  
This  chapter  presents  the  analysis  and  interpretation  of  data  gathered  during  the  
development
 
and
 
evaluation
 
of
 
PsyClick:
 
Clinical
 
Decision
 
Support
 
System
 
for
 
Detecting
 
Single-Session
 
Psychomotor
 
Disturbances
 
via
 
EWMA-Based
 
Multivariate
 
Analysis
 
of
 
Keystroke
 
and
 
Cursor
 
Dynamics.
 
It
 
covers
 
the
 
results
 
of
 
the
 
multi-stage
 
detection
 
pipeline
 
implementation,
 
the
 
development
 
and
 
testing
 
process,
 
convergent
 
validity
 
analysis
 
with
 
PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
clinical
 
screening
 
instruments,
 
clinical
 
concordance
 
evaluation
 
using
 
the
 
Clinical
 
Observation
 
Rating
 
Form
 
(CORF)
 
and
 
Concordance
 
Assessment
 
Form
 
(CAF),
 
and
 
expert
 
software
 
quality
 
evaluation
 
guided
 
by
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010.
 
Each
 
sub-section
 
directly
 
addresses
 
one
 
of
 
the
 
five
 
research
 
questions
 
as
 
stated
 
in
 
Chapter
 
1,
 
presenting
 
the
 
system's
 
technical
 
outputs
 
alongside
 
the
 
empirical
 
validation
 
evidence
 
gathered
 
from
 
the
 
participant
 
sample.
 
4.1.  How  can  a  hardware-abstracted  telemetry  module  be  engineered  to  capture  
synchronized
 
millisecond-level
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
events
 
while
 
minimizing
 
temporal
 
bias
 
from
 
hardware?
 
The  first  research  objective  required  the  design  and  implementation  of  a  data  
acquisition
 
subsystem
 
capable
 
of
 
delivering
 
hardware-agnostic,
 
millisecond-precision
 
psychomotor
 
timestamps
 
regardless
 
of
 
the
 
input
 
device
 
used
 
during
 
a
 
clinical
 
session.
 
This
 
requirement
 
is
 
not
 
procedural
 
but
 
statistically
 
foundational:
 
Killourhy
 
and
 
Maxion
 
(2009)
 
demonstrated
 
that
 
raw
 
OS
 
timestamps
 
from
 
keyboard
 
polling
 
are
 
contaminated
 
by
 
161  

=== PAGE 177 ===
 
quantization  noise,  and  that  devices  operating  at  different  polling  rates  produce  
systematically
 
incomparable
 
dwell-time
 
distributions.
 
Without
 
normalization
 
at
 
the
 
hardware
 
level,
 
any
 
downstream
 
statistical
 
computation
 
—
 
including
 
the
 
EWMA
 
baseline,
 
the
 
Hotelling's
 
T²
 
score,
 
and
 
the
 
PSI
 
and
 
PAI
 
indices
 
—
 
measures
 
device-specific
 
variance
 
rather
 
than
 
human
 
psychomotor
 
behavior,
 
rendering
 
cross-session
 
and
 
cross-device
 
comparisons
 
invalid.
 
4.1.1.  Hardware  Abstraction  Layer  (HAL)  Implementation  
The  Hardware  Abstraction  Layer  (HAL)  was  implemented  in  the  
`dynamics_logger.py`
 
module
 
as
 
the
 
foundational
 
preprocessing
 
stage.
 
The
 
HAL
 
intercepts
 
events
 
from
 
the
 
background
 
listener
 
and
 
applies
 
a
 
grid-alignment
 
Jitter
 
Compensation
 
algorithm
 
adapted
 
from
 
Cervin
 
et
 
al.
 
(2004).
 
The
 
implemented
 
formula
 
is:
 
t_norm  =  t_raw  −  (t_raw  mod  P_target)  +  δ_ latency  
where  t_raw  is  the  raw  OS  timestamp  delivered  by  the  pynput  listener,  P_target  is  the  
standardized
 
target
 
polling
 
period
 
(1
 
ms),
 
and
 
δ
latency
 
is
 
the
 
average
 
hardware
 
latency
 
offset
 
computed
 
across
 
calibration-phase
 
events.
 
The
 
modulo
 
operation
 
isolates
 
the
 
fractional
 
deviation
 
of
 
the
 
raw
 
timestamp
 
from
 
the
 
nearest
 
polling
 
grid
 
boundary.
 
Subtracting
 
this
 
remainder
 
snaps
 
the
 
timestamp
 
to
 
the
 
standardized
 
grid,
 
removing
 
quantization
 
noise
 
while
 
preserving
 
the
 
event's
 
chronological
 
integrity.
 
The
 
δ_
latency
 
term
 
corrects
 
for
 
any
 
systematic,
 
device-specific
 
latency
 
introduced
 
by
 
the
 
keyboard's
 
debounce
 
circuit
 
or
 
OS
 
driver
 
stack.
 
For
 
mechanical
 
keyboards,
 
an
 
additional
 
debounce
 
correction
 
of
 
−2.3
 
ms
 
is
 
applied
 
to
 
compensate
 
for
 
the
 
earlier-than-expected
 
event
 
registration
 
that
 
results
 
from
 
mechanical
 
switch
 
actuation
 
dynamics.
 
All
 
device-specific
 
correction
 
factors
 
are
 
stored
 
in
 
162  

=== PAGE 178 ===
 
the  Device  Profile  table  within  the  database,  fulfilling  the  audit  requirements  of  the  system’s  
privacy
 
architecture.
 
 
Figure  11.  HAL  Normalization  Diagram  
The  Hardware  Abstraction  Layer  was  implemented  in  dynamics_logger.py  as  the  
HardwareAbstractionLayer
 
class.
 
It
 
applies
 
the
 
grid-alignment
 
Jitter
 
Compensation
 
formula
 
t_norm
 
=
 
t_raw
 
−
 
(t_raw
 
mod
 
P_target)
 
+
 
δ_
latency,
 
where
 
P_target
 
=
 
1
 
ms
 
(1000
 
Hz
 
standardized
 
grid).
 
The
 
HAL
 
auto-detects
 
keyboard
 
type
 
(mechanical
 
or
 
membrane),
 
measures
 
actual
 
polling
 
rate,
 
and
 
stores
 
device-specific
 
correction
 
factors
 
in
 
the
 
Device
 
Profile
 
database
 
table.
 
For
 
mechanical
 
keyboards,
 
an
 
additional
 
debounce
 
correction
 
of
 
−2.3
 
ms
 
is
 
applied
 
to
 
compensate
 
for
 
early-event
 
registration
 
from
 
mechanical
 
switch
 
actuation
 
dynamics.

4.1.2.  Sliding  Window  Filter  Implementation  
The  Sliding  Window  Filter  was  applied  as  the  second  preprocessing  stage,  operating  
on
 
cursor
 
coordinate
 
data
 
before
 
any
 
mouse
 
feature
 
is
 
computed.
 
The
 
module
 
applies
 
a
 
5-sample
 
Gaussian-approximate
 
weighted
 
kernel
 
as
 
a
 
discrete
 
weighted
 
moving
 
average:
 
X_filtered(t)  =  Σ( w_i  ·  X(t−i))  /  Σ w_i,    where  w  =  [0.1,  0.2,  0.4,  0.2,  0.1]  
Consistent  with  the  biomechanical  signal  bandwidth  definitions  established  by  
Winter
 
(2009),
 
this
 
kernel
 
attenuates
 
high-frequency
 
noise
 
above
 
8
 
Hz
 
while
 
preserving
 
sub-2
 
Hz
 
movement
 
hesitation
 
patterns.
 
The
 
clinical
 
rationale
 
for
 
this
 
specific
 
frequency
 
boundary
 
is
 
that
 
voluntary
 
motor
 
control
 
signals
 
—
 
the
 
hesitations,
 
direction
 
changes,
 
and
 
pauses
 
that
 
constitute
 
clinically
 
meaningful
 
cursor
 
behavior
 
—
 
occupy
 
the
 
sub-2
 
Hz
 
band,
 
while
 
hardware
 
jitter,
 
natural
 
tremor,
 
and
 
mechanical
 
surface
 
artifacts
 
concentrate
 
above
 
8
 
Hz.
 
By
 
assigning
 
the
 
highest
 
weight
 
(0.4)
 
to
 
the
 
current
 
sample
 
and
 
progressively
 
lower
 
weights
 
to
 
neighboring
 
samples,
 
the
 
kernel
 
achieves
 
this
 
band-selective
 
attenuation
 
without
 
introducing
 
the
 
phase
 
lag
 
characteristic
 
of
 
simple
 
equal-weight
 
moving
 
averages.
 
The
 
result
 
164  

=== PAGE 180 ===
 
is  that  Path  Entropy,  Cursor  Velocity,  Jerk,  and  Pause  Frequency  are  computed  on  a  
noise-attenuated
 
signal
 
in
 
which
 
clinically
 
meaningful
 
hesitation
 
patterns
 
are
 
preserved,
 
while
 
spurious
 
jerk
 
spikes
 
from
 
surface
 
artifacts
 
or
 
desk
 
vibration
 
are
 
suppressed.
 
The  5-sample  Gaussian-approximate  weighted  kernel  was  applied  via  
numpy.convolve()
 
with
 
mode='same'.
 
This
 
attenuates
 
high-frequency
 
noise
 
above
 
8
 
Hz
 
while
 
preserving
 
clinically
 
meaningful
 
sub-2
 
Hz
 
hesitation
 
patterns
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
biomechanical
 
signal
 
bandwidths
 
defined
 
by
 
Winter
 
(2009).
 
The
 
boundary
 
weights
 
are
 
normalized
 
to
 
prevent
 
edge
 
artifacts
 
in
 
short
 
cursor
 
traces.
 
This
 
filter
 
is
 
applied
 
exclusively
 
to
 
mouse
 
x,
 
y
 
coordinates;
 
keyboard
 
events
 
are
 
not
 
smoothed.

hardware

the

signal
 
and

valid
 
cross-device

of
 
clinical

Figure  12.  Timestamp  Distribution  Pre-  and  Post-HAL   
To  validate  the  effectiveness  of  the  Hardware  Abstraction  Layer  (HAL),  we  
compared
 
T²
 
score
 
variance
 
across
 
different
 
hardware
 
configurations
 
before
 
and
 
after
