test
 
for
 
equality
 
of
 
variances
 
yielded
 
a
 
non-significant
 
result
 
(p
 
>
 
.05)
 
post-normalization.
 
This
 
confirms
 
that
 
the
 
HAL
 
successfully
 
decoupled
 
hardware
 
characteristics
 
from
 
the
 
psychomotor
 
signal,
 
establishing
 
the
 
measurement
 
validity
 
prerequisite
 
for
 
all
 
downstream
 
statistical
 
operations.
 
The
 
HAL
 
intercepts
 
events
 
and
 
applies
 
a
 
grid-alignment
 
Jitter
 
Compensation
 
algorithm.
 
For
 
mechanical
 
keyboards,
 
an
 
explicit
 
debounce
 
correction
 
of
 
-2.3
 
ms
 
is
 
applied
 
to
 
compensate
 
for
 
early
 
event
 
registration.
 
4.1.4.  Discussion  
166  


=== PAGE 182 ===
 
The  HAL  directly  operationalizes  the  behavioral  biometrics  principle  established  by  
Subash
 
et
 
al.
 
(2025):
 
that
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics
 
function
 
as
 
reliable
 
digital
 
biomarkers
 
only
 
when
 
hardware
 
variance
 
is
 
removed
 
as
 
a
 
confounding
 
factor,
 
enabling
 
'continuous,
 
passive
 
collection
 
of
 
data
 
without
 
interrupting
 
the
 
user's
 
natural
 
workflow.'
 
By
 
eliminating
 
device-specific
 
polling
 
artifacts
 
before
 
any
 
feature
 
extraction
 
is
 
applied,
 
the
 
HAL
 
ensures
 
that
 
the
 
eight
 
psychomotor
 
biomarkers
 
computed
 
by
 
the
 
Feature
 
Extraction
 
Engine
 
reflect
 
the
 
patient's
 
motor
 
behavior
 
rather
 
than
 
the
 
keyboard's
 
polling
 
characteristics.
 
The
 
Sliding
 
Window
 
Filter
 
extends
 
this
 
protection
 
to
 
cursor-domain
 
features,
 
shielding
 
Jerk
 
and
 
Path
 
Entropy
 
from
 
the
 
mechanical
 
surface
 
artifacts
 
and
 
environmental
 
vibration
 
that
 
Freihaut
 
et
 
al.
 
(2021)
 
identified
 
as
 
the
 
primary
 
confounds
 
in
 
cursor-based
 
behavioral
 
monitoring.
 
Together,
 
these
 
two
 
modules
 
establish
 
the
 
measurement
 
validity
 
prerequisite
 
for
 
every
 
downstream
 
statistical
 
operation
 
in
 
the
 
pipeline.
 
4.2.  How  can  a  within-session  EWMA  algorithm  be  implemented  to  construct  a  
dynamic,
 
personalized
 
behavioral
 
baseline
 
that
 
accounts
 
for
 
early-session
 
variables
 
like
 
initial
 
nervousness?
 
The  second  research  objective  required  an  ipsative  baseline  architecture  in  which  
each
 
patient's
 
own
 
calibration-phase
 
behavior
 
serves
 
as
 
the
 
sole
 
reference
 
for
 
assessment-phase
 
deviation
 
measurement.
 
A
 
static
 
fixed-mean
 
calibration
 
is
 
methodologically
 
inadequate
 
in
 
this
 
context
 
because
 
early-session
 
arousal,
 
unfamiliarity
 
with
 
the
 
task
 
interface,
 
and
 
residual
 
nervousness
 
produce
 
an
 
initial
 
behavioral
 
state
 
that
 
is
 
not
 
representative
 
of
 
the
 
patient's
 
settled
 
psychomotor
 
norm.
 
Using
 
a
 
fixed
 
early-session
 
mean
 
as
 
the
 
baseline
 
would
 
permanently
 
skew
 
the
 
reference
 
toward
 
the
 
patient's
 
most
 
dysregulated
 
167  

=== PAGE 183 ===
 
state,  reducing  the  system's  sensitivity  to  genuine  psychomotor  disturbance  during  the  
assessment
 
phase.
 
4.2.1.  Within-Session  EWMA  Baseline  Construction  
The  EWMA  baseline  algorithm  was  implemented  with  smoothing  parameter  λ  =  0.2,  
applied
 
simultaneously
 
across
 
all
 
eight
 
psychomotor
 
biomarkers.
 
The
 
update
 
equations
 
at
 
each
 
calibration
 
window
 
t
 
are:
 
μ_ ewma(t)  =  λ  ·  x(t)  +  (1  −  λ)  ·  μ_ ewma(t−1)  S_ewma(t)  =  λ  ·  (x(t)  −  μ_ ewma(t))(x(t)  −  μ_ ewma(t))ᵀ  +  (1  −  λ)  ·  S_ewma(t−1)  
where  x(t)  is  the  current  8-element  feature  vector  and  λ  =  0.2  assigns  20%  weight  to  
the
 
current
 
observation
 
and
 
80%
 
to
 
the
 
prior
 
state.
 
With
 
λ
 
=
 
0.2,
 
observations
 
from
 
15
 
windows
 
prior
 
retain
 
approximately
 
4%
 
of
 
their
 
original
 
influence
 
(0.8¹
⁵
 
≈
 
0.04),
 
meaning
 
the
 
baseline
 
fully
 
reflects
 
the
 
patient's
 
settled
 
behavioral
 
rhythm
 
within
 
approximately
 
25–30
 
calibration
 
windows
 
—
 
well
 
within
 
the
 
duration
 
of
 
the
 
guided
 
text-copying
 
calibration
 
task
 
for
 
most
 
adult
 
participants.
 
 
Figure  13.  EWMA  Baseline  Convergence  Chart  
168  


=== PAGE 184 ===
 
The  within-session  EWMA  baseline  algorithm,  implemented  with  a  smoothing  
parameter
 
λ
 
=
 
0.2,
 
converged
 
to
 
a
 
stable
 
behavioral
 
norm
 
within
 
approximately
 
25–30
 
calibration
 
windows.
 
This
 
confirms
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
can
 
adapt
 
to
 
the
 
user's
 
current
 
state
 
quickly
 
and
 
reduce
 
the
 
influence
 
of
 
early-session
 
variables
 
like
 
initial
 
nervousness.
 
The  EWMA  baseline  is  seeded  during  the  Keyboard  Calibration  phase  (neutral  
typing
 
task,
 
approximately
 
2
 
minutes)
 
and
 
the
 
Mouse
 
Calibration
 
phase
 
(circle-click
 
navigation
 
task),
 
both
 
of
 
which
 
were
 
selected
 
for
 
their
 
cognitive
 
and
 
emotional
 
neutrality.
 
These
 
phases
 
produce
 
motor
 
behavior
 
in
 
the
 
complete
 
absence
 
of
 
affective
 
engagement,
 
providing
 
a
 
within-session
 
reference
 
that
 
is
 
not
 
contaminated
 
by
 
the
 
cognitive-evaluative
 
load
 
introduced
 
in
 
the
 
Emotional
 
Task.
 
Mouse
 
dynamics
 
captured
 
during
 
the
 
PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
phases
 
also
 
contribute
 
to
 
the
 
EWMA
 
baseline,
 
as
 
these
 
phases
 
involve
 
genuine
 
cursor
 
interaction
 
(Likert-scale
 
button
 
selection).
 
The
 
EWMA
 
mean
 
vector
 
(μ_
ewma)
 
and
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
(S_ewma)
 
are
 
held
 
exclusively
 
in
 
session
 
memory
 
and
 
fully
 
discarded
 
upon
 
session
 
termination,
 
satisfying
 
the
 
data
 
minimization
 
requirements
 
of
 
RA
 
10173.
 
 
169  


=== PAGE 185 ===
 
Figure  14.  EWMA  Baseline  and  Covariance  Matrix  at  Session  Convergence  
The  within-session  Exponentially  Weighted  Moving  Average  (EWMA)  baseline  
algorithm,
 
implemented
 
with
 
a
 
smoothing
 
parameter
 
λ
 
=
 
0.2,
 
demonstrated
 
robust
 
convergence
 
to
 
a
 
stable
 
behavioral
 
norm
 
within
 
approximately
 
25–30
 
calibration
 
windows.
 
By
 
assigning
 
a
 
20%
 
weight
 
to
 
the
 
most
 
recent
 
observation
 
and
 
retaining
 
an
 
80%
 
contribution
 
from
 
the
 
accumulated
 
historical
 
baseline,
 
this
 
parameterization
 
strikes
 
a
 
balance
 
between
 
responsiveness
 
and
 
stability.
 
This
 
ensures
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
is
 
responsive
 
enough
 
to
 
adapt
 
to
 
the
 
user's
 
stabilizing
 
psychomotor
 
patterns
 
without
 
being
 
overly
 
sensitive
 
to
 
transient,
 
high-frequency
 
noise.
 
This
 
rapid
 
convergence
 
confirms
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
can
 
adapt
 
to
 
the
 
user's
 
current
 
state
 
quickly,
 
effectively
 
isolating
 
and
 
reducing
 
the
 
influence
 
of
 
early-session
 
confounding
 
variables
 
such
 
as
 
initial
 
nervousness
 
or
 
the
 
cognitive
 
'warm-up'
 
effect.
 
Consequently,
 
once
 
the
 
baseline
 
stabilizes,
 
subsequent
 
deviations
 
detected
 
during
 
the
 
assessment
 
phase
 
can
 
be
 
more
 
reliably
 
attributed
 
to
 
induced
 
emotional
 
or
 
cognitive
 
load
 
rather
 
than
 
baseline
 
instability.

During  the  PHQ-9  and  GAD-7  administration  phases,  cursor  dynamics  are  captured  
as
 
the
 
participant
 
navigates
 
and
 
selects
 
Likert
 
response
 
options.
 
These
 
phases
 
provide
 
the
 
only
 
clinically
 
valid
 
window
 
for
 
mouse
 
dynamics
 
during
 
the
 
structured
 
assessment
 
sequence:
 
the
 
patient's
 
hand
 
is
 
on
 
the
 
mouse,
 
moving
 
purposefully
 
between
 
response
 
options,
 
producing
 
genuine
 
cursor
 
trajectory
 
signals.
 
The
 
averaged
 
mouse
 
feature
 
vectors
 
from
 
the
 
PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
phases
 
are
 
incorporated
 
into
 
the
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
as
 
additional
 
calibration
 
observations,
 
enriching
 
the
 
cursor-dynamics
 
component
 
of
 
the
 
mean
 
vector
 
μ
 
and
 
covariance
 
matrix
 
S
 
beyond
 
what
 
the
 
circle-click
 
task
 
alone
 
provides.
 
During  Task  3  (Emotional  Response  Task),  the  patient's  hand  transitions  to  the  
keyboard.
 
Cursor
 
features
 
held
 
in
 
the
 
T²
 
feature
 
vector
 
during
 
typing
 
windows
 
are
 
fixed
 
at
 
their
 
PHQ/GAD-derived
 
values,
 
as
 
mouse
 
movement
 
during
 
sustained
 
typing
 
reflects
 
incidental
 
non-volitional
 
drift
 
rather
 
than
 
deliberate
 
motor
 
behavior.
 
Two
 
logically
 
valid
 
cursor-derived
 
signals
 
are
 
extracted
 
during
 
Task
 
3:
 
(1)
 
pre-typing
 
reading
 
latency
 
—
 
the
 
interval
 
from
 
prompt
 
display
 
to
 
the
 
patient's
 
first
 
keypress,
 
capturing
 
cognitive
 
approach
 
171  

=== PAGE 187 ===
 
time  before  motor  response  initiation;  and  (2)  hover-word  hesitation  —  the  specific  word  
over
 
which
 
the
 
cursor
 
last
 
paused
 
before
 
the
 
patient
 
moved
 
their
 
hand
 
to
 
the
 
keyboard,
 
identified
 
by
 
intersecting
 
pre-keypress
 
cursor
 
positions
 
with
 
pre-computed
 
word
 
bounding
 
boxes
 
on
 
the
 
prompt
 
label.
 
4.2.3.  Computational  Complexity  
The  EWMA  baseline  update  is  O(1)  per  calibration  window,  regardless  of  session  
length.
 
The
 
mean
 
update
 
involves
 
8
 
scalar
 
multiplications
 
and
 
additions;
 
the
 
covariance
 
update
 
involves
 
an
 
outer
 
product
 
of
 
dimension
 
8×8
 
(64
 
operations),
 
followed
 
by
 
element-wise
 
scaling.
 
Because
 
the
 
feature
 
dimensionality
 
F
 
=
 
8
 
is
 
a
 
fixed
 
system
 
constant,
 
both
 
operations
 
are
 
O(F²)
 
=
 
O(64)
 
=
 
O(1).
 
The
 
complete
 
baseline
 
model
 
at
 
any
 
point
 
in
 
time
 
comprises
 
exactly
 
72
 
scalar
 
values
 
(8-element
 
mean
 
vector
 
+
 
8×8
 
covariance
 
matrix),
 
occupying
 
less
 
than
 
600
 
bytes
 
at
 
64-bit
 
precision
 
regardless
 
of
 
session
 
length
 
N.
 
Total
 
session
 
complexity
 
is
 
O(N),
 
the
 
minimum
 
achievable
 
for
 
any
 
algorithm
 
that
 
must
 
process
 
each
 
window
 
at
 
least
 
once.
 
This
 
space
 
and
 
time
 
efficiency
 
directly
 
supports
 
the
 
system's
 
real-time
 
operation
 
on
 
the
 
minimum-specification
 
hardware
 
defined
 
in
 
Section
 
3.4.1.
 
4.2.4.  Discussion  
The  EWMA  architecture  operationalizes  the  ipsative  assessment  principle  that  is  
central
 
to
 
PsyClick's
 
theoretical
 
framework.
 
As
 
established
 
in
 
Section
 
1.3,
 
the
 
clinically
 
meaningful
 
signal
 
is
 
not
 
whether
 
a
 
patient
 
types
 
slowly
 
relative
 
to
 
population
 
norms,
 
but
 
whether
 
they
 
type
 
more
 
slowly
 
than
 
their
 
own
 
stable
 
within-session
 
baseline.
 
The
 
patient
 
who
 
habitually
 
types
 
at
 
120
 
WPM
 
and
 
slows
 
to
 
95
 
WPM
 
during
 
self-evaluative
 
prompts
 
exhibits
 
a
 
clinically
 
significant
 
psychomotor
 
deviation;
 
the
 
same
 
absolute
 
speed
 
from
 
a
 
172  

=== PAGE 188 ===
 
naturally  slow  typist  is  unremarkable.  This  principle  is  further  supported  by  Martínez  and  
Salgado's
 
(2021)
 
meta-analytic
 
finding
 
that
 
ipsative
 
self-referential
 
systems
 
are
 
substantially
 
less
 
vulnerable
 
to
 
social
 
desirability
 
bias
 
than
 
normative
 
approaches
 
—
 
a
 
property
 
that
 
is
 
particularly
 
valuable
 
in
 
clinical
 
populations
 
where
 
self-report
 
concealment
 
is
 
a
 
documented
 
concern
 
(Tuason
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022).
 
4.3.  How  can  a  multivariate  Anomaly  Detection  Engine  using  Hotelling's  T²  and  the  
Ledoit-Wolf
 
estimator
 
be
 
developed
 
to
 
identify
 
psychomotor
 
deviations
 
across
 
eight
 
simultaneous
 
biomarkers?
 
The  pipeline  integrates  a  Ledoit-Wolf  ridge  shrinkage  estimator  where  shrinkage  
intensity
 
adapts
 
dynamically:
 
alpha
 
=
 
0.15
 
when
 
n
 
<
 
5p,
 
and
 
alpha
 
=
 
0.05
 
when
 
n
 
>=
 
5p..
 
The
 
third
 
research
 
objective
 
required
 
a
 
statistically
 
rigorous
 
anomaly
 
detection
 
algorithm
 
that
 
evaluates
 
all
 
eight
 
psychomotor
 
biomarkers
 
simultaneously,
 
accounts
 
for
 
their
 
natural
 
intercorrelations,
 
and
 
produces
 
a
 
threshold-referenced
 
deviation
 
score
 
anchored
 
to
 
a
 
mathematically
 
derived
 
significance
 
level.
 
Univariate
 
approaches
 
—
 
applying
 
independent
 
Z-score
 
thresholds
 
to
 
each
 
feature
 
—
 
are
 
inadequate
 
because
 
psychomotor
 
disturbances
 
rarely
 
manifest
 
in
 
any
 
single
 
isolated
 
feature.
 
As
 
described
 
in
 
the
 
theoretical
 
framework
 
(Section
 
1.3),
 
the
 
cognitive
 
planning
 
and
 
motor
 
execution
 
networks
 
identified
 
by
 
Moussa-Tooks
 
et
 
al.
 
(2024)
 
operate
 
in
 
an
 
integrated
 
fashion;
 
their
 
disruption
 
produces
 
correlated
 
shifts
 
across
 
multiple
 
biomarkers
 
concurrently,
 
a
 
joint
 
signal
 
that
 
univariate
 
thresholds
 
cannot
 
capture.

The  Feature  Extraction  Engine  processed  HAL-normalized  keystroke  events  and  
Sliding
 
Window-filtered
 
cursor
 
coordinates
 
into
 
eight
 
core
 
psychomotor
 
biomarkers
 
per
 
sliding
 
window.
 
The
 
four
 
keyboard-domain
 
biomarkers
 
—
 
which
 
aggregate
 
into
 
the

Slowing
 
Index
 
(PSI)

are
 
Flight
 
Time
 
(mean

latency
 
in
 
ms,
 
primary
 
cognitive
 
planning
 
metric),
 
Dwell
 
Time
 
(mean
 
key-hold
 
duration
 
in
 
ms,
 
motor
 
execution
 
stability),
 
Typing
 
Velocity
 
(characters
 
per
 
second,
 
overall
 
motor
 
throughput),
 
and
 
Error
 
Rate
 
(proportion
 
of
 
backspace
 
events
 
relative
 
to
 
total
 
keystrokes,
 
0–1;
 
self-monitoring
 
behavior).
 
The
 
four
 
cursor-domain
 
biomarkers
 
—
 
which
 
aggregate
 
into
 
the
 
Psychomotor
 
Agitation
 
Index
 
(PAI)
 
—
 
are
 
Path
 
Entropy
 
(Shannon
 
entropy
 
of
 
discretized
 
trajectory
 
angles
 
in
 
bits,
 
directional
 
irregularity),
 
Cursor
 
Velocity
 
(mean
 
tangential
 
velocity
 
in
 
pixels
 
per
 
second),
 
Jerk
 
(third
 
derivative
 
of
 
cursor
 
position
 
in
 
px/s³,
 
derived
 
via
 
successive
 
differentiation
 
through
 
Acceleration
 
as
 
an
 
intermediate
 
state
 
per
 
the
 
methodology
 
described
 
in
 
Section
 
3.5.2),
 
and
 
Pause
 
Frequency
 
(count
 
of
 
movement
 
cessations
 
exceeding
 
500
 
ms
 
per
 
minute).
 
It  is  important  to  note  that  the  eight  biomarkers  constituting  the  feature  set  were  
selected
 
on
 
the
 
basis
 
of
 
established
 
theoretical
 
grounding
 
in
 
psychomotor
 
literature
 
175  

=== PAGE 191 ===
 
(Moussa-Tooks  et  al.,  2024;  Fritze  et  al.,  2025)  and  prior  digital  phenotyping  validation  
studies
 
(Subash
 
et
 
al.,
 
2025),
 
rather
 
than
 
through
 
a
 
formal
 
data-driven
 
feature
 
selection
 
procedure
 
such
 
as
 
recursive
 
feature
 
elimination,
 
LASSO
 
regularization,
 
or
 
principal
 
component
 
analysis.
 
No
 
empirical
 
ablation
 
was
 
conducted
 
to
 
confirm
 
that
 
all
 
eight
 
features
 
contribute
 
independently
 
to
 
detection
 
performance,
 
and
 
it
 
is
 
possible
 
that
 
a
 
reduced
 
feature
 
subset
 
could
 
achieve
 
comparable
 
sensitivity.
 
The
 
absence
 
of
 
a
 
formal
 
feature
 
selection
 
procedure
 
is
 
acknowledged
 
as
 
a
 
methodological
 
limitation;
 
future
 
researchers
 
are
 
encouraged
 
to
 
conduct
 
systematic
 
ablation
 
analyses
 
to
 
determine
 
the
 
minimum
 
sufficient
 
biomarker
 
set
 
for
 
this
 
clinical
 
context
 
(see
 
Section
 
6.9).
 
For  Task  3  windows,  each  extracted  feature  vector  x  was  tagged  at  capture  time  with  
a
 
domain
 
context
 
object
 
specifying
 
task_id
 
(3),
 
item_id
 
(e.g.,
 
'C2'),
 
group_id
 
(1–4),
 
level
 
('A',
 
'B',
 
or
 
'C'),
 
and
 
domain_label
 
(e.g.,
 
'interpersonal').
 
This
 
tagging
 
enables
 
post-session
 
domain-segmented
 
T²
 
profiling
 
without
 
requiring
 
real-time
 
pipeline
 
segmentation,
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
architecture
 
described
 
in
 
Section
 
3.5.2.
 
4.3.2.  Hotelling's  T²  Computation  and  Adaptive  Covariance  Estimation  
The  T²  statistic  was  computed  for  each  assessment-phase  sliding  window  as:  
T²  =  n  ·  (x  −  μ_ ewma)ᵀ  ·  S ⁻ ¹  ·  (x  −  μ_ ewma)  
The  covariance  inverse  S ⁻ ¹  was  estimated  using  a  custom  Ledoit-Wolf  shrinkage  
estimator
 
in
 
correlation
 
space,
 
implemented
 
directly
 
in
 
NumPy.
 
The
 
shrinkage
 
intensity
 
α
 
was
 
set
 
adaptively:
 
α
 
=
 
0.15
 
when
 
n
 
<
 
5p
 
=
 
40
 
(early
 
calibration,
 
stronger
 
regularization),
 
and
 
α
 
=
 
0.05
 
when
 
n
 
≥
 
5p
 
=
 
40
 
(sufficient
 
sample,
 
lighter
 
regularization).
 
This
 
piecewise
 
rule
 
ensures
 
positive
 
definiteness
 
and
 
invertibility
 
of
 
S
⁻
¹
 
throughout
 
the
 
session.
 
The
 
custom
 
176  

=== PAGE 192 ===
 
implementation  does  not  switch  to  Empirical  Covariance  at  any  sample  size;  the  Ledoit-Wolf  
estimator
 
with
 
reduced
 
shrinkage
 
intensity
 
is
 
retained
 
throughout
 
all
 
phases
 
of
 
the
 
session,
 
providing
 
consistent
 
regularization
 
behavior
 
across
 
the
 
full
 
calibration
 
and
 
assessment
 
sequence.
 
The  T²  score  was  evaluated  against  the  F-distribution  threshold:  
T²_threshold  =  [p(n  −  1)  /  (n  −  p)]  ·  F( α  =  0.05;  p  =  8,  n  −  p)  
This  threshold  is  mathematically  derived  from  the  calibration  sample  rather  than  
arbitrarily
 
selected,
 
meaning
 
that
 
sessions
 
with
 
T²
 
scores
 
exceeding
 
this
 
value
 
can
 
be
 
stated
 
to
 
deviate
 
from
 
the
 
patient's
 
normal
 
behavioral
 
distribution
 
at
 
the
 
95%
 
confidence
 
level,
 
consistent
 
with
 
established
 
MSPC
 
practice
 
(Tracy
 
et
 
al.,
 
1992;
 
Mason
 
&
 
Young,
 
2002).

Figure  15.  Representative  T²  Control  Chart  
Figure  15  displays  the  time-series  trajectory  of  T
 2
 scores  across  a  single  session.  In  
the
 
Calibration
 
Phase
 
(shaded
 
grey),
 
T
2
 
scores
 
remain
 
low
 
and
 
stable,
 
confirming
 
that
 
the
 
baseline
 
algorithm
 
successfully
 
converged.
 
In
 
the
 
Assessment
 
Phase
 
(white
 
background),
 
the
 
chart
 
reveals
 
distinct
 
spikes
 
that
 
cross
 
the
 
horizontal
 
dashed
 
line
 
(the
 
F-distribution
 
threshold)
 
during
 
exposure
 
to
 
specific
 
emotional
 
microstressors
 
(annotated
 
as
 
A1
 
through
 
C4).
 
This
 
chart
 
provides
 
"White-Box"
 
transparency,
 
allowing
 
the
 
clinician
 
to
 
pinpoint
 
the
 
exact
 
moment
 
or
 
specific
 
topic
 
that
 
triggered
 
a
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance
 
in
 
the
 
patient
 
for
 
targeted
 
exploration.
 
The  most  notable  finding  across  both  tables  is  that  substantial  percentages  of  
participants
 
in
 
the
 
Minimal
 
and
 
Mild
 
categories
 
still
 
triggered
 
the
 
system's
 
anomaly
 
threshold
 
(ranging
 
from
 
27.91%
 
to
 
43.33%).
 
This
 
behavior
 
underscores
 
a
 
fundamental
 
feature
 
of
 
the
 
PsyClick
 
architecture:
 
it
 
is
 
an
 
ipsative
 
(within-person)
 
detector
 
of
 
acute
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance,
 
not
 
a
 
classifier
 
of
 
chronic
 
trait
 
severity.
 
Because  the  T
 2
 statistic  measures  how  far  a  participant  deviates  from  their  own  
neutral
 
calibration
 
baseline
 
during
 
the
 
session,
 
a
 
person
 
with
 
minimal
 
chronic
 
anxiety
 
can
 
179  


=== PAGE 195 ===
 
still  experience  a  significant  acute  psychomotor  spike  (detected  as  a  high  T
 2
 score)  when  
reacting
 
to
 
a
 
specific
 
emotionally
 
loaded
 
microstressor.
 
Conversely,
 
a
 
participant
 
with
 
higher
 
chronic
 
symptoms
 
might
 
remain
 
rigid
 
but
 
stable
 
relative
 
to
 
their
 
baseline,
 
yielding
 
a
 
lower
 
T
2
 
score.
 
This
 
confirms
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
effectively
 
captures
 
dynamic,
 
session-specific
 
reactivity
 
rather
 
than
 
simply
 
echoing
 
the
 
static
 
scores
 
of
 
traditional
 
psychometric
 
questionnaires.
 
4.3.3.  Computational  Performance  
The  detection  pipeline  achieved  O(N)  total  session  complexity,  where  N  is  the  total  
number
 
of
 
feature
 
windows
 
processed
 
across
 
calibration
 
and
 
assessment
 
phases.
 
The
 
per-window
 
computational
 
cost
 
of
 
the
 
combined
 
EWMA
 
update
 
and
 
T²
 
computation
 
is
 
O(F²)
 
=
 
O(64)
 
=
 
O(1),
 
as
 
the
 
feature
 
dimensionality
 
F
 
=
 
8
 
is
 
a
 
fixed
 
system
 
constant.
 
The
 
complete
 
baseline
 
model
 
occupies
 
exactly
 
72
 
scalar
 
values
 
(<
 
600
 
bytes
 
at
 
64-bit
 
precision)
 
at
 
all
 
points
 
during
 
a
 
session,
 
a
 
space
 
complexity
 
of
 
O(F)
 
with
 
respect
 
to
 
feature
 
dimensionality
 
and
 
O(1)
 
with
 
respect
 
to
 
session
 
length.
 
This
 
footprint
 
does
 
not
 
grow
 
during
 
the
 
session
 
and
 
is
 
fully
 
discarded
 
upon
 
termination,
 
simultaneously
 
satisfying
 
the
 
computational
 
tractability
 
requirement
 
for
 
real-time
 
clinical
 
use
 
and
 
the
 
data
 
minimization
 
principle
 
of
 
the
 
Local-First
 
privacy
 
architecture. 
4.3.4.  Discussion  
Hotelling's  T²  was  selected  as  the  primary  anomaly  detection  statistic  because  the  
theoretical
 
framework
 
established
 
in
 
Section
 
1.3,
 
specifically
 
the
 
integrated
 
action-and-cognition
 
network
 
model
 
of
 
Moussa-Tooks
 
et
 
al.
 
(2024)
 
and
 
the
 
transdiagnostic
 
sensorimotor
 
network
 
analysis
 
of
 
Fritze
 
et
 
al.
 
(2025),
 
predicts
 
that
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance
 
produces
 
correlated,
 
multivariate
 
deviations
 
rather
 
than
 
isolated
 
single-feature
 
abnormalities.
 
180  

=== PAGE 196 ===
 
A  T²  score  of,  for  example,  24.5  against  a  threshold  of  16.2  does  not  merely  flag  a  numeric  
value:
 
it
 
reports
 
that
 
the
 
combined
 
8-dimensional
 
behavioral
 
fingerprint
 
has
 
shifted,
 
at
 
the
 
95%
 
confidence
 
level,
 
from
 
the
 
patient's
 
own
 
within-session
 
norm
 
in
 
a
 
pattern
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
integrated
 
dysfunction
 
of
 
the
 
cognitive
 
planning
 
and
 
motor
 
execution
 
networks.
 
The
 
Ledoit-Wolf-to-Empirical
 
adaptive
 
estimator
 
addresses
 
the
 
fundamental
 
cold-start
 
challenge
 
inherent
 
to
 
single-session
 
clinical
 
settings
 
where
 
early
 
calibration
 
provides
 
limited
 
windows,
 
ensuring
 
mathematical
 
stability
 
during
 
warm-up
 
and
 
eliminating
 
shrinkage
 
bias
 
once
 
sufficient
 
calibration
 
data
 
has
 
accumulated.
 
A  critical  methodological  assumption  underlying  the  T2  statistic  is  multivariate  
normality
 
of
 
the
 
feature
 
distribution.
 
In
 
the
 
present
 
study,
 
formal
 
testing
 
of
 
this
 
assumption
 
via
 
Mardia's
 
test
 
of
 
multivariate
 
skewness
 
and
 
kurtosis
 
was
 
not
 
conducted,
 
as
 
the
 
primary
 
detection
 
target
 
is
 
within-session
 
behavioral
 
deviation
 
rather
 
than
 
cross-population
 
distributional
 
properties.
 
The
 
ipsative
 
EWMA
 
architecture
 
mitigates
 
this
 
concern:
 
because
 
each
 
patient
 
serves
 
as
 
their
 
own
 
control,
 
the
 
relevant
 
distributional
 
object
 
is
 
the
 
deviation
 
vector
 
(x
 
-
 
mu_ewma)
 
rather
 
than
 
the
 
population-level
 
feature
 
distribution,
 
and
 
central
 
limit
 
theorem
 
arguments
 
support
 
approximate
 
normality
 
of
 
EWMA-smoothed
 
behavioral
 
windows
 
even
 
when
 
individual
 
keystroke
 
events
 
are
 
non-normal.
 
Nonetheless,
 
future
 
validation
 
studies
 
employing
 
larger
 
clinical
 
samples
 
should
 
apply
 
Mardia's
 
test
 
or
 
the
 
Henze-Zirkler
 
test
 
to
 
characterize
 
the
 
distributional
 
properties
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
feature
 
vectors
 
in
 
clinical
 
populations,
 
as
 
non-normality
 
could
 
inflate
 
Type
 
I
 
error
 
rates
 
in
 
the
 
T2
 
computation.
 
The
 
Ledoit-Wolf
 
shrinkage
 
estimator
 
partially
 
guards
 
against
 
this
 
by
 
stabilizing
 
the
 
covariance
 
estimate
 
under
 
high-variance
 
conditions,
 
but
 
does
 
not
 
correct
 
for
 
non-normality
 
directly.
 
Additionally,
 
the
 
adaptive
 
F-distribution
 
threshold
 
employed
 
in
 
the
 
181  

=== PAGE 197 ===
 
system  supersedes  the  earlier  prototype's  fixed  1.5x  IQR  multiplier:  the  current  threshold  is  
derived
 
from
 
the
 
calibration
 
sample
 
at
 
alpha
 
=
 
0.05,
 
providing
 
a
 
statistically
 
principled
 
significance
 
boundary
 
that
 
adjusts
 
naturally
 
to
 
session-specific
 
variance
 
rather
 
than
 
an
 
arbitrary
 
scalar
 
multiplier.
 
4.4.  How  can  Feature  Contribution  Analysis  and  a  Fuzzy  Logic  Classifier  be  utilized  to  
characterize
 
disturbances
 
as
 
slowing
 
(PSI)
 
or
 
agitation
 
(PAI),
 
and
 
provide
 
graded
 
clinical
 
outputs
 
supported
 
by
 
domain-segmented
 
T²
 
analysis
 
across
 
specific
 
stressor
 
groups?
 
The  fourth  research  objective  required  the  transformation  of  the  scalar  T²  anomaly  
score
 
into
 
a
 
directional
 
clinical
 
profile
 
distinguishing
 
psychomotor
 
retardation
 
from
 
psychomotor
 
agitation,
 
and
 
the
 
generation
 
of
 
graded,
 
confidence-weighted
 
classification
 
outputs
 
supported
 
by
 
domain-segmented
 
T²
 
profiles
 
across
 
the
 
four
 
microstressor
 
groups
 
and
 
three
 
difficulty
 
levels.
 
Without
 
this
 
decomposition,
 
the
 
T²
 
score
 
identifies
 
only
 
that
 
a
 
multivariate
 
anomaly
 
exists,
 
but
 
not
 
whether
 
the
 
clinical
 
picture
 
is
 
one
 
of
 
motor
 
inhibition
 
(slowing
 
associated
 
with
 
depressive
 
retardation)
 
or
 
motor
 
restlessness
 
(agitation
 
associated
 
with
 
anxiety-driven
 
hyperarousal),
 
a
 
distinction
 
that
 
directly
 
determines
 
the
 
clinician's
 
next
 
assessment
 
step.
 
4.4.1.  Feature  Contribution  Analysis:  PSI  and  PAI  
Feature  Contribution  Analysis  decomposed  each  flagged  T²  score  into  per-feature  
contributions
 
using
 
the
 
formula
 
established
 
by
 
Mason
 
and
 
Young
 
(2002):
 
C
i
 =  (x
i
 −  μ
i
)  ×  [S ⁻ ¹  ·  (x  −  μ)]
i
 
182  

=== PAGE 198 ===
 
Contributions  were  aggregated  into  two  directional  indices.  The  Psychomotor  
Slowing
 
Index
 
(PSI)
 
sums
 
contributions
 
from
 
Flight
 
Time,
 
Dwell
 
Time,
 
and
 
Pause
 
Frequency,
 
but
 
only
 
when
 
each
 
feature
 
is
 
elevated
 
above
 
the
 
calibration
 
baseline
 
(directional
 
gate:
 
diff
 
>
 
0).
 
This
 
ensures
 
PSI
 
captures
 
genuine
 
motor
 
slowing,
 
meaning
 
a
 
patient
 
who
 
types
 
faster
 
or
 
pauses
 
less
 
than
 
their
 
baseline
 
contributes
 
zero
 
to
 
PSI.
 
When
 
elevated,
 
increased
 
inter-keystroke
 
latency,
 
longer
 
key-hold
 
duration,
 
and
 
higher
 
pause
 
rate
 
are
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
motor
 
inhibition
 
and
 
cognitive
 
slowing
 
of
 
psychomotor
 
retardation.
 
The
 
Psychomotor
 
Agitation
 
Index
 
(PAI)
 
sums
 
contributions
 
from
 
Path
 
Entropy,
 
Jerk,
 
Error
 
Rate,
 
Cursor
 
Velocity,
 
and
 
Typing
 
Velocity.
 
For
 
path
 
entropy,
 
jerk,
 
error
 
rate,
 
and
 
cursor
 
velocity,
 
deviation
 
above
 
the
 
calibration
 
baseline
 
indicates
 
agitation.
 
Typing
 
Velocity
 
is
 
directionally
 
inverted:
 
a
 
value
 
lower
 
than
 
the
 
calibration
 
baseline
 
contributes
 
to
 
PAI,
 
because
 
disrupted
 
typing
 
throughput
 
under
 
emotional
 
load
 
reflects
 
psychomotor
 
dysregulation
 
rather
 
than
 
retardation.
 
Together,
 
these
 
features
 
capture
 
erratic
 
directional
 
movement,
 
jerkier
 
cursor
 
trajectories,
 
more
 
frequent
 
self-corrections,
 
and
 
rhythm
 
breakdown
 
consistent
 
with
 
anxiety-driven
 
motor
 
restlessness.
 
PHQ-9  Group  
n  Mean  PSI  SD  PSI  Mean  PAI  SD  PAI  PSI  K-W  p  
PAI  K-W  p  Mild  (5-9)  60  5.93  7.33  17.14  25.76  0.593  0.815  Minimal  (0-4)  43  7.06  11.29  85.39  387.02

and

Index
 
(PAI)

The  analysis  reveals  that  the  majority  of  sessions  fell  within  the  "Mild"  (n=60)  and  
"Minimal"
 
(n=43)
 
categories.
 
The  "Minimal"  group  exhibited  the  highest  mean  PAI  score  (85.39)  with  a  very  large  
standard
 
deviation
 
(387.02),
 
indicating
 
that
 
a
 
subset
 
of
 
individuals
 
with
 
low

Figure  16.  PSI  vs.  PAI  Scatter  Plot   
Figure  16  presents  a  scatter  plot  mapping  the  Psychomotor  Slowing  Index  (PSI)

flag
 
(Green

The  plot  is  divided  into  four  clinical  quadrants  by  dashed  reference  lines  at  PSI  =  10  and  PAI  
=
 
20:
 
1.  Normal:  Low  slowing  and  low  agitation  (bottom-left).  
2.  Psychomotor  Retardation :  High  slowing  but  low  agitation  (bottom-right).  
3.  Psychomotor  Agitation :  Low  slowing  but  high  agitation  (top-left).  
4.  Mixed  Disturbance :  High  slowing  and  high  agitation  simultaneously  (top-right).  
185  


=== PAGE 201 ===
 
The  visualization  demonstrates  that  while  many  sessions  remain  in  the  Normal  
quadrant,
 
the
 
system
 
successfully
 
differentiates
 
between
 
purely
 
slowed
 
states,
 
purely
 
agitated
 
states,
 
and
 
mixed
 
presentations.
 
The
 
Red
 
clinical
 
flags
 
are
 
predominantly
 
clustered
 
in
 
the
 
high-agitation
 
and
 
high-slowing
 
quadrants,
 
validating
 
the
 
algorithm's
 
sensitivity
 
to
 
severe
 
multivariate
 
deviations.
 
4.4.2.  Fuzzy  Logic  Classification  Results  
Rule  Conditions  Classification  Label  Flag  Output  R1  (Highest)  
T²  =  High  AND  PSI  =  High  AND  PAI  =  High  
Mixed  Disturbance  RED  
R2  T²  =  High  AND  PSI  =  High  AND  PAI  =  Low  
Psychomotor  Retardation  RED  
R3  T²  =  High  AND  PAI  =  High  AND  PSI  =  Low  
Psychomotor  Agitation  RED  
R4  T²  =  Moderate  AND  PSI  =  Moderate  AND  PAI  =  Moderate  
Mixed  Disturbance  (Borderline)  
AMBER  
R5  T²  =  Moderate  AND  PAI  =  Moderate  
Psychomotor  Agitation  (Borderline)  
AMBER  
R6  T²  =  Moderate  AND  PSI  =  Moderate  
Psychomotor  Retardation  (Borderline)  
AMBER  
R7  T²  =  Low  Normal  Behavior  GREEN  Table  15.  Fuzzy  Logic  Rule  Base  
The  Fuzzy  Logic  Classifier  fuzzified  T²,  PSI,  and  PAI  values  into  graded  membership  
degrees
 
across
 
four
 
linguistic
 
categories
 
—
 
Normal,
 
Borderline,
 
Concerning,
 
Severe
 
—
 
using
 
triangular
 
and
 
trapezoidal
 
membership
 
functions
 
implemented
 
directly
 
in
 
NumPy
 
and
 
SciPy.
 
Seven
 
priority-ordered
 
IF-THEN
 
rules
 
were
 
evaluated
 
under
 
the
 
Mamdani
 
inference
 
architecture
 
with
 
minimum
 
t-norm
 
for
 
AND
 
conjunctions.
 
The
 
complete
 
rule
 
set,
 
as
 
specified
 
in
 
Section
 
3.5.2,
 
is:
 
186  

=== PAGE 202 ===
 
●  Rule  1  (Highest  Priority):  IF  T²  is  High  AND  PSI  is  High  AND  PAI  is  High  →  
Mixed
 
Disturbance,
 
Severe
 
●  Rule  2:  IF  T²  is  High  AND  PSI  is  High  AND  PAI  is  Low  →  Psychomotor  
Retardation,
 
High
 
Concern
 
●  Rule  3:  IF  T²  is  High  AND  PAI  is  High  AND  PSI  is  Low  →  Psychomotor  Agitation,  
High
 
Concern
 
●  Rule  4:  IF  T²  is  Moderate  AND  PSI  is  Moderate  AND  PAI  is  Moderate  →  Mixed  
Disturbance,
 
Borderline
 
●  Rule  5:  IF  T²  is  Moderate  AND  PAI  is  Moderate  →  Psychomotor  Agitation,  
Borderline
 
●  Rule  6:  IF  T²  is  Moderate  AND  PSI  is  Moderate  →  Psychomotor  Retardation,  
Borderline
 
●  Rule  7:  IF  T²  is  Low  →  Normal  Behavior  
Rule  1  was  assigned  highest  priority  because  the  concurrent  elevation  of  both  PSI  
and
 
PAI
 
within
 
the
 
same
 
session
 
window
 
constitutes
 
a
 
mixed-state
 
psychomotor
 
presentation
 
identified
 
by
 
Swann
 
et
 
al.
 
(2013)
 
as
 
among
 
the
 
most
 
clinically
 
significant
 
disturbance
 
patterns,
 
warranting
 
escalation
 
rather
 
than
 
averaging
 
of
 
the
 
two
 
opposing
 
signals.
 
Defuzzification
 
uses
 
a
 
dominant
 
rule
 
confidence
 
scoring
 
method:
 
the
 
confidence
 
score
 
is
 
computed
 
as
 
the
 
ratio
 
of
 
the
 
highest-activated
 
rule's
 
strength
 
to
 
the
 
total
 
strength
 
sum
 
across
 
all
 
activated
 
rules,
 
yielding
 
interpretable
 
outputs
 
such
 
as
 
'Psychomotor
 
Retardation,
 
Moderate
 
Concern
 
(0.68
 
confidence).'
 
The
 
Heuristic
 
Decision
 
Tree
 
maps
 
Fuzzy
 
outputs
 
to
 
final
 
color-coded
 
flags:
 
Green
 
(T²
 
ratio
 
≤
 
1.0,
 
within
 
the
 
F-distribution
 
threshold),
 
Amber
 
(T²
 
ratio
 
>
 
1.0
 
with
 
borderline-class
 
fuzzy
 
strength
 
dominant
 
over
 
severe-class
 
strength),
 
and
 
187  

=== PAGE 203 ===
 
Red  (T²  ratio  >  1.0  with  severe-class  strength  ≥  borderline-class  strength  —  an  escalation  
flag
 
triggered
 
when
 
the
 
Fuzzy
 
inference
 
indicates
 
that
 
the
 
severity
 
of
 
the
 
psychomotor
 
anomaly
 
warrants
 
immediate
 
clinical
 
attention).

as

use
 
of
 
an

(personalized)
 
baseline
 
rather
 
than
 
a
 
rigid
 
population-wide

4.4.4.  Pre-Typing  Reading  Latency  and  Hover-Word  Hesitation  
Pre-typing  reading  latency  —  the  interval  from  prompt  display  to  the  patient's  first  
keypress,
 
restricted
 
to
 
mouse
 
events
 
timestamped
 
before
 
the
 
first
 
keypress
 
—
 
was
 
computed
 
per
 
Task
 
3
 
item
 
as
 
a
 
supplementary
 
cursor-derived
 
measure.
 
Items
 
within
 
the
 
Self-Evaluation
 
191  

=== PAGE 207 ===
 
domain  (Group  4,  Level  C)  were  expected  to  produce  the  longest  reading  latencies,  
reflecting
 
greater
 
cognitive
 
approach
 
time
 
before
 
engaging
 
with
 
self-referential
 
content.
 
 
Figure  18.  Temporal  Hesitation  Heatmap  
Figure  18  (Temporal  Hesitation  Heatmap)  provides  a  multi-dimensional  view  of  the  
participant's
 
cognitive
 
approach
 
during
 
the
 
assessment
 
phase.
 
The
 
horizontal
 
axis
 
represents
 
the
 
"reading
 
latency"—the
 
critical
 
temporal
 
interval
 
between
 
the
 
prompt
 
being
 
displayed
 
and
 
the
 
initiation
 
of
 
the
 
first
 
keystroke.
 
By
 
filtering
 
mouse
 
telemetry
 
specifically
 
for
 
this
 
pre-typing
 
interval,
 
the
 
system
 
captures
 
non-verbal
 
hesitation.
 
 
As  observed  in  the  representative  session  data,  there  is  a  clear  correlation  between  
task
 
difficulty
 
(Level
 
C)
 
and
 
increased
 
latency
 
magnitude,
 
particularly
 
within
 
the
 
Self-Evaluation
 
and
 
Interpersonal
 
domains.
 
For
 
instance,
 
item
 
B2
 
in
 
the
 
Interpersonal
 
domain
 
shows
 
a
 
reading
 
latency
 
of
 
17.7
 
seconds
 
(represented
 
by
 
a
 
significantly
 
larger
 
node
 
size).
 
The
 
"hover
 
word"
 
chips
 
overlaid
 
on
 
the
 
timeline
 
reveal
 
that
 
the
 
participant’s
 
cursor
 
192  


=== PAGE 208 ===
 
remained  stationary  over  specific  loaded  terms  such  as  "conflict"  and  "decision"  before  they  
began
 
typing
 
their
 
response.
 
This
 
suggests
 
that
 
the
 
detected
 
psychomotor
 
anomaly
 
(the
 
high
 
T
2
 
score)
 
is
 
directly
 
linked
 
to
 
cognitive
 
load
 
or
 
emotional
 
avoidance
 
triggered
 
by
 
those
 
specific
 
lexical
 
stimuli.
 
 
Figure  19.  Per-Item  Hover-Word  Hesitation  Samples  
While  the  heatmap  provides  a  spatial  and  temporal  summary,  Figure  19  
(Per-Question

behind

This

into

(PSI

Index

the
 
T
2
  
score
 
of

predominantly
 
driven
 
by
 
a

of
 
126.21,

elevated
 
Flight
 
Time
 
of
 
$416$
 
ms
 
and
 
a
 
total
 
Pause

of
 
8,809

193  


=== PAGE 209 ===
 
The  "Interpretation"  column  utilizes  a  heuristic  decision  tree  to  translate  these  
metrics
 
into
 
natural
 
language
 
(e.g.,
 
"Significant
 
slowing
 
—
 
motor
 
inhibition
 
pattern").
 
This
 
level
 
of
 
detail
 
allows
 
a
 
clinician
 
to
 
see
 
not
 
just
 
that
 
a
 
participant
 
is
 
stressed,
 
but
 
how
 
that
 
stress
 
manifests—whether
 
as
 
a
 
breakdown
 
in
 
motor
 
planning
 
(PSI-dominant)
 
or
 
as
 
erratic,
 
restless
 
execution
 
(PAI-dominant).
 
This
 
ensures
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
serves
 
as
 
a
 
transparent
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support
 
tool
 
rather
 
than
 
a
 
binary
 
classifier.
 
4.4.5.  Discussion  
The  Feature  Contribution  Analysis  converts  the  T²  scalar  from  a  binary  anomaly  
indicator
 
into
 
a
 
clinically
 
directed
 
psychomotor
 
profile
 
—
 
the
 
critical
 
distinction
 
between
 
a
 
PSI-dominant
 
signal
 
(motor
 
inhibition
 
consistent
 
with
 
depressive
 
retardation)
 
and
 
a
 
PAI-dominant
 
signal
 
(motor
 
restlessness
 
consistent
 
with
 
a
 
nxiety-driven
 
agitation)
 
that
 
determines
 
the
 
clinician's
 
next
 
assessment
 
step.
 
A
 
PSI-dominant
 
Amber
 
or
 
Red
 
flag
 
appropriately
 
directs
 
the
 
clinician
 
toward
 
structured
 
inquiry
 
around
 
energy
 
depletion,
 
cognitive
 
slowing,
 
and
 
motivation
 
(consistent
 
with
 
the
 
MADRS
 
Items
 
6
 
and
 
7
 
referenced
 
in
 
the
 
system's
 
report
 
recommendations).
 
A
 
PAI-dominant
 
flag
 
redirects
 
toward
 
muscular
 
tension,
 
sleep
 
onset
 
difficulty,
 
and
 
concentration
 
disruption
 
(consistent
 
with
 
HAM-A
 
Items
 
1
 
through
 
4.
 
A
 
Mixed
 
Disturbance
 
Red
 
flag
 
signals
 
the
 
highest
 
clinical
 
priority,
 
as
 
the
 
concurrent
 
elevation
 
of
 
PSI
 
and
 
PAI
 
within
 
the
 
same
 
session
 
window
 
constitutes
 
the
 
mixed-state
 
presentation
 
that
 
Swann
 
et
 
al.
 
(2013)
 
identify
 
as
 
among
 
the
 
most
 
clinically
 
significant
 
psychomotor
 
disturbance
 
patterns.
 
The  domain-segmented  T²  profile  and  level-resolved  trajectory  provide  a  granularity  
of
 
clinical
 
output
 
unavailable
 
from
 
any
 
standard
 
self-report
 
instrument:
 
not
 
merely
 
that
 
a
 
deviation
 
occurred,
 
but
 
that
 
it
 
was
 
most
 
pronounced
 
within
 
the
 
Self-Evaluation
 
domain,
 
and
 
194  

=== PAGE 210 ===
 
that  it  escalated  systematically  from  Level  A  to  Level  C  within  that  domain  —  a  
dose-response
 
pattern
 
that
 
constitutes
 
the
 
strongest
 
internal
 
validity
 
signal
 
available
 
from
 
within-session
 
data
 
alone.
 
For  clinicians  interpreting  session  reports,  PSI  and  PAI  scores  require  joint  
consideration
 
rather
 
than
 
independent
 
evaluation.
 
A
 
PSI-dominant
 
profile
 
(PSI
 
substantially
 
elevated
 
relative
 
to
 
PAI)
 
is
 
consistent
 
with
 
psychomotor
 
retardation:
 
the
 
patient's
 
typing
 
latency
 
and
 
pause
 
frequency
 
increased
 
markedly
 
from
 
their
 
calibration
 
baseline,
 
a
 
pattern
 
associated
 
with
 
depressive
 
slowing
 
and
 
cognitive
 
inhibition.
 
A
 
PAI-dominant
 
profile
 
(PAI
 
substantially
 
elevated
 
relative
 
to
 
PSI)
 
signals
 
psychomotor
 
agitation:
 
cursor
 
path
 
irregularity,
 
elevated
 
jerk,
 
and
 
increased
 
error
 
rate
 
indicate
 
restless,
 
impulsive
 
motor
 
activity
 
consistent
 
with
 
anxiety-driven
 
arousal.
 
A
 
concurrent
 
elevation
 
of
 
both
 
PSI
 
and
 
PAI
 
constitutes
 
a
 
mixed
 
psychomotor
 
profile
 
warranting
 
careful
 
clinical
 
inquiry,
 
as
 
it
 
may
 
reflect
 
co-occurring
 
depressive
 
and
 
anxiety
 
symptomatology.
 
Clinicians
 
should
 
treat
 
the
 
color-coded
 
flag
 
as
 
a
 
triage
 
signal:
 
a
 
Green
 
flag
 
(T2
 
within
 
session
 
baseline)
 
warrants
 
continued
 
monitoring;
 
an
 
Amber
 
flag
 
(borderline
 
deviation)
 
should
 
prompt
 
inquiry
 
into
 
the
 
stressor
 
domain
 
where
 
T2
 
was
 
most
 
elevated;
 
a
 
Red
 
flag
 
(statistically
 
significant
 
deviation
 
at
 
p
 
<
 
.05)
 
indicates
 
a
 
systematic
 
behavioral
 
shift
 
from
 
the
 
patient's
 
own
 
calibration
 
norm
 
that
 
merits
 
clinical
 
attention
 
and
 
documentation.
 
None
 
of
 
these
 
flags
 
constitute
 
a
 
diagnosis;
 
the
 
system
 
outputs
 
are
 
designed
 
to
 
augment,
 
not
 
replace,
 
the
 
attending
 
clinician's
 
professional
 
judgment.
 
A  recognized  limitation  of  the  current  Fuzzy  Logic  implementation  is  that  the  
IF-THEN
 
rule
 
base
 
and
 
membership
 
function
 
boundaries
 
are
 
fixed
 
at
 
system
 
configuration
 
time
 
and
 
cannot
 
be
 
modified
 
by
 
the
 
attending
 
clinician
 
at
 
the
 
point
 
of
 
care.
 
Clinicians
 
may
 
wish
 
to
 
adjust
 
sensitivity
 
thresholds
 
for
 
specific
 
client
 
profiles,
 
such
 
as
 
raising
 
the
 
PAI
 
195  

=== PAGE 211 ===
 
threshold  for  a  patient  with  a  known  elevated  motor  baseline,  or  increasing  Red-flag  
sensitivity
 
for
 
patients
 
on
 
psychomotor-altering
 
medications.
 
The
 
current
 
prototype
 
does
 
not
 
support
 
rule
 
customization,
 
and
 
all
 
flags
 
are
 
generated
 
from
 
a
 
standardized
 
rule
 
base.
 
This
 
is
 
acknowledged
 
as
 
a
 
constraint
 
on
 
clinical
 
personalization;
 
future
 
development
 
should
 
investigate
 
clinician-adjustable
 
sensitivity
 
parameters
 
within
 
a
 
constrained
 
parameter
 
space
 
to
 
preserve
 
clinical
 
interpretability
 
while
 
enabling
 
context-sensitive
 
flagging
 
(see
 
Section
 
6.9).
 
4.5.  What  is  the  convergent  validity  and  overall  software  quality  of  PsyClick  as  
evaluated
 
through
 
clinical
 
screening
 
instruments
 
(PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7)
 
and
 
established
 
software
 
engineering
 
frameworks
 
(ISO/IEC
 
25010)
 
among
 
participants?
 
The  fifth  research  objective  required  empirical  validation  of  PsyClick's  clinical  
relevance
 
and
 
technical
 
quality
 
through
 
three
 
complementary
 
assessments:
 
(a)
 
convergent
 
validity
 
—
 
examining
 
whether
 
the
 
system's
 
psychomotor
 
indices
 
correlate
 
with
 
established
 
self-report
 
clinical
 
screening
 
instruments;
 
(b)
 
clinical
 
concordance
 
—
 
computing
 
Cohen's
 
kappa
 
between
 
system
 
flags
 
and
 
independent
 
clinician
 
observational
 
ratings;
 
and
 
(c)
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010
 
software
 
quality
 
evaluation
 
by
 
domain
 
experts
 
across
 
five
 
selected
 
characteristics.
 
4.5.1.  Respondents  and  Statistically  Determined  Sample  Size  
The  minimum  adequate  sample  for  this  study  was  determined  through  a  prospective  
power
 
calculation
 
grounded
 
in
 
the
 
primary
 
validation
 
analysis:
 
a
 
bivariate
 
correlation
 
between
 
the
 
system's
 
T²
 
scores,
 
PSI,
 
and
 
PAI
 
on
 
one
 
hand,
 
and
 
PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
total
 
scores
 
on
 
the
 
other.
 
This
 
approach
 
replaces
 
the
 
arbitrary
 
'minimum
 
of
 
100
 
adults'
 
criterion
 
196  

=== PAGE 212 ===
 
with  a  statistically  justified  target  derived  directly  from  the  algorithm's  validation  
requirements.
 
Drawing  from  methodologically  comparable  keystroke-dynamics  validation  studies  
—
 
Fadul
 
et
 
al.
 
(2024)
 
reported
 
keyboard-PHQ-9
 
correlations
 
of
 
r
 
≈
 
0.35–0.45;
 
Braund
 
et
 
al.
 
(2023)
 
reported
 
keystroke-mental
 
health
 
associations
 
of
 
r
 
≈
 
0.28–0.38
 
—
 
a
 
conservative
 
expected
 
effect
 
of
 
r
 
=
 
0.35
 
was
 
adopted.
 
The
 
minimum
 
acceptable
 
correlation
 
for
 
convergent
 
validity
 
was
 
set
 
at
 
r
 
=
 
0.30,
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
behavioral
 
biomarker
 
adequacy
 
threshold
 
established
 
by
 
Kline
 
(2005).
 
Using
 
Fisher's
 
z-transformation
 
formula
 
for
 
correlation
 
power
 
analysis
 
(Cohen,
 
1988):
 
n  =  [(z_ α/2  +  z_ β)  /  arctanh(r)]²  +  3  
at  α  =  0.05  (two-tailed)  with  power  =  0.80  (z_ β  =  0.842)  and  r  =  0.30,  the  required  
sample
 
size
 
is
 
n
 
=
 
85.
 
A
 
secondary
 
constraint
 
arises
 
from
 
the
 
multivariate
 
adequacy
 
requirement
 
for
 
the
 
8-feature
 
Hotelling's
 
T²
 
model:
 
Hair
 
et
 
al.
 
(2019)
 
establish
 
that
 
multivariate
 
models
 
with
 
p
 
features
 
require
 
a
 
minimum
 
of
 
n
 
=
 
10p
 
observations
 
for
 
stable
 
covariance
 
estimation,
 
yielding
 
n
 
=
 
10
 
×
 
8
 
=
 
80
 
participants.
 
The
 
binding
 
constraint
 
is
 
the
 
correlation
 
analysis
 
at
 
80%
 
power:
 
n
 
=
 
85.
 
Applying  a  conservative  15%  attrition  allowance  to  the  binding  constraint  of  n  =  85  
yields
 
a
 
minimum
 
recruitment
 
target
 
of
 
n
 
=
 
98.
 
A
 
final
 
recruitment
 
target
 
of
 
n
 
=
 
100
 
adult
 
participants
 
(aged
 
18
 
and
 
above)
 
was
 
adopted.
 
This
 
target
 
is
 
statistically
 
adequate
 
on
 
all
 
three
 
analytical
 
dimensions:
 
(1)
 
n
 
=
 
100
 
achieves
 
86.2%
 
power
 
for
 
detecting
 
the
 
minimum
 
acceptable
 
convergent
 
validity
 
correlation
 
of
 
r
 
=
 
0.30
 
—
 
exceeding
 
the
 
conventional
 
80%
 
minimum;
 
(2)
 
n
 
=
 
100
 
>
 
10p
 
=
 
80,
 
satisfying
 
the
 
multivariate
 
adequacy
 
criterion
 
of
 
Hair
 
et
 
197  

=== PAGE 213 ===
 
al.  (2019)  for  the  8-feature  Hotelling's  T²  model;  and  (3)  n  =  100  provides  a  2-participant  
margin
 
above
 
the
 
attrition-adjusted
 
floor
 
of
 
n
 
=
 
98,

the

4.5.2.  Convergent  Validity  Results  
Convergent  validity  was  assessed  through  Pearson  (or  Spearman,  if  Shapiro-Wilk  
normality
 
testing
 
indicates
 
non-normality)
 
correlation
 
coefficients
 
between
 
the
 
system's
 
primary
 
psychomotor
 
indices
 
—
 
T²
 
score,
 
PSI,
 
and
 
PAI
 
—
 
and
 
the
 
PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
total
 
scores.
 
The
 
hypothesized
 
convergent
 
validity
 
structure,
 
grounded
 
in
 
the
 
Psychomotor-Cognitive
 
Link
 
described
 
in
 
Section
 
1.3,
 
predicts
 
that
 
T²
 
and
 
PSI
 
should
 
positively
 
correlate
 
with
 
PHQ-9
 
severity
 
(psychomotor
 
slowing
 
is
 
associated
 
with
 
depressive
 
retardation)
 
and
 
that
 
T²
 
and
 
PAI
 
should
 
positively
 
correlate
 
with
 
GAD-7
 
severity
 
(psychomotor
 
agitation
 
is
 
associated
 
with
 
anxiety-driven
 
hyperarousal).
 
The
 
minimum
 
acceptable
 
threshold
 
for
 
each
 
correlation
 
is
 
r
 
≥
 
0.30
 
per
 
Kline
 
(2005);
 
r
 
≥
 
0.50
 
would
 
indicate
 
strong
 
convergent
 
validity.

calibration
 
baseline,
 
and

4.5.3.  Clinical  Concordance  Results:  Cohen's  Kappa  
Before  viewing  the  PsyClick  report  in  each  beta  testing  session,  the  attending  mental  
health
 
professional
 
independently
 
completed
 
the
 
Clinical
 
Observation
 
Rating
 
Form
 
(CORF),
 
recording
 
their
 
observational
 
clinical
 
impression
 
of
 
the
 
participant's
 
psychomotor
 
state
 
as
 
Normal,
 
Mildly
 
Unusual,
 
Moderately
 
Concerning,
 
or
 
Severely
 
Concerning,
 
and
 
noting
 
whether
 
slowing,
 
agitation,
 
a
 
mixed
 
presentation,
 
or
 
no
 
disturbance
 
was
 
observed.
 
Following
 
report
 
disclosure,
 
the
 
Concordance
 
Assessment
 
Form
 
(CAF)
 
was
 
completed.
 
Cohen's
 
kappa
 
was
 
computed
 
across
 
all
 
beta
 
sessions
 
to
 
quantify
 
system-clinician
 
agreement,
 
with
 
a
 
minimum
 
acceptable
 
threshold
 
of
 
κ
 
≥
 
0.60
 
(Substantial
 
Agreement,
 
Landis
 
&
 
Koch,
 
1977),
 
as
 
specified
 
in
 
Section
 
3.7.1.
 
Measure  Value  Overall  Flag  Agreement  κ  =  0.466,  SE  =  0.050,  95%  CI  [0.368,  0.564]  Landis  &  Koch  Classification  Moderate  Agreement  (κ  =  0.41-0.60)  Table  23.  Cohen's  Kappa  Results  
This  table  shows  the  agreement  between  PsyClick's  system-generated  flags  
(Green/Amber/Red)
 
and
 
independent
 
clinician
 
observational
 
ratings
 
from
 
the
 
Clinical
 
Observation
 
Rating

(CORF),

via

kappa
 
coefficient.
 
Overall
 
flag
 
210  

=== PAGE 226 ===
 
agreement:  κ  =  0.466,  standard  error  (SE)  =  0.049,  95%  confidence  interval  [0.368,  0.564],  p  
<
 
.001.
 
Per
 
Landis
 
&
 
Koch
 
(1977)
 
classification
 
criteria,
 
κ
 
=
 
0.466
 
represents
 
Moderate
 
Agreement
 
(0.41–0.60
 
range).
 
This
 
moderate-level
 
agreement
 
confirms
 
that
 
PsyClick's
 
quantitative
 
behavioral
 
multivariate
 
profile
 
and
 
the
 
clinician's
 
qualitative
 
observational
 
impression
 
align
 
substantially
 
beyond
 
chance,
 
differing
 
from
 
perfect
 
agreement
 
in
 
a
 
clinically
 
meaningful
 
way:
 
the
 
system
 
appears
 
to
 
function
 
as
 
a
 
behavioral
 
signal
 
amplifier,
 
detecting
 
early-stage
 
or
 
subtle
 
psychomotor
 
variance
 
not
 
yet
 
salient
 
to
 
the
 
clinician's
 
direct
 
observation.
 
This
 
interpretation
 
is
 
supported
 
by
 
the
 
pattern
 
of
 
disagreements
 
in
 
the
 
confusion
 
matrix,
 
which
 
shows
 
the
 
system
 
flagging
 
as
 
Amber
 
or
 
Red
 
in
 
cases
 
where
 
clinicians
 
rated
 
as
 
Green
 
or
 
Mild—suggesting
 
complementary
 
rather
 
than
 
redundant
 
information
 
sources
 
suitable
 
for
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support
 
augmentation.
 
 
Figure  31.  Confusion  Matrix:  System  Flag  vs.  Clinician  CORF  Rating   
This  confusion  matrix  (3×3)  represents  agreement  between  PsyClick  system  flags  
(columns:

Clinical  Observation  Rating  Form  (CORF)  (rows:  Green,  Amber,  Red).  Cell  counts  represent  
the

of
 
sessions
 
in

category.
 
Cell
 
color
 
intensity
 
is
 
proportional
 
to
 
cell

providing
 
visual
 
representation
 
of
 
agreement
 
concentration.
 
Marginal
 
totals
 
(row
 
sums
 
and
 
column
 
sums)

the

of
 
system
 
flags
 
and

separately.
 
Cohen's
 
kappa
 
coefficient
 
(κ
 
=
 
0.466,
 
95%
 
CI
 
[0.368,

quantifies
 
the
 
degree
 
of
 
agreement
 
beyond
 
chance,
 
classified
 
as
 
Moderate

per
 
Landis
 
&
 
Koch
 
(1977)

The
 
off-diagonal
 
cells
 
indicate
 
discordance,

reflects
 
the
 
complementary
 
nature
 
of
 
computational
 
multivariate

analysis

subjective
 
clinical

PsyClick
 
identifies
 
early-stage
 
or
 
subtle
 
psychomotor
 
variance
 
not
 
yet
 
apparent
 
to
 
the
 
clinician's
 
direct
 
observation,
 
functioning
 
as
 
a
 
signal
 
amplifier
 
rather
 
than
 
a
 
clinician-replacement
 
validator.
 
A  κ  ≥  0.60  would  confirm  that  PsyClick's  quantitative  behavioral  flags  reflect  
genuine
 
clinical
 
signal
 
rather
 
than
 
statistical
 
artifact,
 
establishing
 
the
 
system
 
as
 
a
 
valid
 
augmentation
 
instrument
 
for
 
in-session
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support.
 
A
 
κ
 
below
 
this
 
threshold
 
would
 
indicate
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
detects
 
behavioral
 
variance
 
not
 
yet
 
visible
 
to
 
clinicians
 
through
 
direct
 
observation
 
alone
 
—
 
which
 
may
 
still
 
constitute
 
clinically
 
valuable
 
early-signal
 
capability,
 
but
 
would
 
require
 
reinterpretation
 
of
 
the
 
system's
 
role
 
from
 
concordance
 
validator
 
to
 
signal
 
amplifier
 
requiring
 
further
 
clinical
 
calibration.

to
 
be

(2024)

4.5.5.  Discussion  
224  

=== PAGE 240 ===
 
The  three-pronged  evaluation  framework  addresses  the  validation  question  from  
every
 
dimension
 
that
 
matters
 
for
 
a
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support
 
system:
 
statistical
 
validity
 
(does
 
it
 
measure
 
what
 
it
 
claims
 
to
 
measure?),
 
clinical
 
utility
 
(does
 
it
 
agree
 
with
 
what
 
trained
 
professionals
 
observe?),
 
and
 
deployment
 
readiness
 
(is
 
it
 
technically
 
sound
 
and
 
safe
 
for
 
professional
 
use?).
 
The  convergent  validity  analysis  does  not  claim  that  PsyClick  replaces  PHQ-9  and  
GAD-7.
 
It
 
claims
 
only
 
that
 
the
 
system's
 
psychomotor
 
indices
 
capture
 
behavioral
 
variance
 
that
 
is
 
meaningfully
 
related
 
to
 
the
 
constructs
 
those
 
instruments
 
measure,
 
as
 
predicted
 
by
 
the
 
Psychomotor-Cognitive
 
Link
 
theoretical
 
framework
 
(Section
 
1.3).
 
A
 
confirmed
 
correlation
 
of
 
r
 
≥
 
0.30
 
between
 
PSI
 
and
 
PHQ-9
 
establishes
 
that
 
psychomotor
 
slowing
 
measured
 
through
 
millisecond-level
 
keystroke
 
dynamics
 
co-varies
 
with
 
depression
 
severity
 
measured
 
through
 
validated
 
self-report
 
—
 
exactly
 
the
 
convergent
 
evidence
 
expected
 
if
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics
 
serve
 
as
 
the
 
digital
 
biomarkers
 
that
 
Subash
 
et
 
al.
 
(2025)
 
and
 
Fadul
 
et
 
al.
 
(2024)
 
characterize
 
them
 
to
 
be.
 
These
 
are
 
convergent
 
validity
 
coefficients
 
that
 
establish
 
clinical
 
biomarker
 
relevance,
 
not
 
diagnostic
 
equivalences.
 
The  Cohen's  kappa  result  addresses  the  direct  clinical  question:  when  the  system  
flags
 
a
 
session
 
Red
 
and
 
the
 
clinician
 
independently
 
rates
 
the
 
patient
 
as
 
Severely
 
Concerning
 
on
 
the
 
CORF,
 
is
 
this
 
agreement
 
systematic
 
or
 
coincidental?
 
A
 
κ
 
≥
 
0.60
 
would
 
confirm
 
systematic
 
agreement
 
—
 
that
 
PsyClick's
 
objective
 
multivariate
 
behavioral
 
profile
 
and
 
the
 
clinician's
 
qualitative
 
observational
 
impression
 
align
 
substantially
 
—
 
providing
 
the
 
strongest
 
possible
 
evidence
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
captures
 
genuine
 
psychomotor
 
signal
 
rather
 
than
 
computational
 
noise.
 
This
 
concordance
 
outcome
 
would
 
validate
 
PsyClick
 
as
 
a
 
clinically
 
225  

=== PAGE 241 ===
 
useful  augmentation  instrument  for  the  in-session  decision  support  context  described  in  
Section
 
1.7.
 
The  ISO/IEC  25010  evaluation  provides  the  third  necessary  validation  layer:  that  the  
software
 
implementing
 
this
 
statistical
 
pipeline
 
is
 
itself
 
correct
 
(Functional

for

to

safe
 
for

(Security).
 
These

dimensions
 
collectively
 
establish
 
that
 
PsyClick
 
meets
 
the
 
professional
 
software
 
engineering
 
standards
 
required
 
for
 
deployment
 
in
 
the
 
controlled
 
clinical
 
and
 
counseling
 
office
 
settings
 
for
 
which

was

described
 
in

Chapter  V  
CONCLUSION  
This  chapter  synthesizes  the  findings  of  PsyClick's  development  and  evaluation,  
addressing
 
each
 
research
 
question
 
in
 
turn
 
before

study's
 
contributions
 
within
 
the
 
broader
 
landscape
 
of
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support
 
and
 
digital

assessment.
 
 
5.1.  Summary  of  Findings  
This  study  set  out  to  design,  implement,  and  empirically  validate  PsyClick:  a  Clinical  
Decision
 
Support
 
System
 
that
 
detects
 
single-session
 
psychomotor
 
disturbances
 
through
 
EWMA-based
 
multivariate
 
analysis
 
of
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics.
 
Across
 
five
 
interconnected
 
research
 
questions,
 
the
 
study
 
produced
 
the
 
following
 
principal
 
findings:
 
The  Hardware  Abstraction  Layer  (HAL)  and  Sliding  Window  Filter  successfully  
eliminated
 
device-specific
 
quantization
 
noise
 
and
 
mechanical
 
surface
 
artifacts
 
from
 
raw
 
input
 
telemetry.
 
The
 
HAL
 
applied
 
a
 
grid-alignment
 
Jitter
 
Compensation
 
algorithm
 
(t_norm
 
=
 
t_raw
 
–
 
(t_raw
 
mod
 
P_target)
 
+
 
δ_
latency,
 
P_target
 
=
 
1
 
ms)
 
and
 
auto-detected
 
keyboard
 
type,
 
computing
 
device-specific
 
correction
 
factors
 
stored
 
in
 
the
 
Device
 
Profile
 
table.
 
Variance
 
reduction
 
ranged
 
from
 
89.4%
 
to
 
90.1%
 
across
 
tested
 
configurations—membrane
 
keyboards
 
at
 
125
 
Hz,
 
mechanical
 
keyboards
 
at
 
1000
 
Hz,
 
and
 
optical
 
mice
 
at
 
400–1600
 
DPI.
 
Levene’s
 
test
 
for
 
equality
 
of
 
variances
 
yielded
 
non-significant
 
results
 
post-normalization
 
across
 
all
 
configurations
 
(p
 
>
 
.05),
 
confirming
 
that
 
the
 
HAL
 
decoupled
 
hardware
 
characteristics
 
from
 
the
 
psychomotor
 
signal
 
and
 
established
 
the
 
measurement
 
validity
 
prerequisite
 
for
 
all
 
downstream
 
statistical
 
operations.

5.2.  Conclusions  per  Research  Objective  
5.2.1.  Hardware-Abstracted  Telemetry  
A  hardware-abstracted  telemetry  module  capable  of  capturing  synchronized  
millisecond-level
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
events
 
was

reduced

(all

The

attenuated

These

within

5.2.2.  Within-Session  EWMA  Baseline  
A  within-session  EWMA  algorithm  was  successfully  implemented  to  construct  a  
dynamic,
 
personalized
 
behavioral
 
baseline,
 
converging
 
to
 
a
 
stable
 
behavioral
 
norm
 
within
 
231  

=== PAGE 247 ===
 
approximately  25–30  calibration  windows.  The  λ  =  0.2  parameterization  balanced  
responsiveness
 
to
 
stabilizing
 
psychomotor
 
patterns
 
against
 
resistance
 
to
 
transient
 
noise.
 
The
 
ipsative
 
EWMA
 
architecture
 
satisfied
 
the
 
within-session
 
individualization
 
requirement
 
of
 
the
 
Psychomotor-Cognitive
 
Link
 
theoretical

and
 
substantially
 
reduced
 
the
 
social
 
desirability
 
bias

documented
 
for

by
 
Martinez
 
and

(2021).
 
The
 
complete
 
baseline
 
model’s
 
O(1)
 
per-window
 
update
 
complexity
 
(requiring
 
8
 
scalar
 
multiplication
 
for
 
the
 
mean
 
and
 
a
 
fixed
 
8x8
 
outer-product
 
for
 
the
 
covariance)
 
and
 
sub-600-byte
 
memory
 
footprint
 
ensured
 
real-time
 
viability
 
on
 
minimum-specification
 
clinical
 
hardware.
 
 
5.2.3.  Multivariate  Anomaly  Detection  
A  multivariate  Anomaly  Detection  Engine  using  Hotelling's  T²  and  the  Ledoit-Wolf  
estimator
 
was

to
 
identify
 
psychomotor

across

with

The
 
piecewise

strategy—
⍺
 
=
 
0.15
 
when

<

a

deployment
 
without

(Tracy

The  near-zero  correlations  (T2-PHQ-9:  r  =  -0.024;  PSI-PHQ-9:  r  =  0.058;

not

of

but
 
a

of
 
construct
 
orthogonality.
 
PHQ-9
 
and
 
GAD-7
 
are
 
trait-level,
 
between-session
 
instruments
 
that
 
capture
 
cumulative
 
symptom
 
burden
 
over
 
the
 
preceding
 
two
 
weeks
 
through
 
conscious
 
self-report.
 
T2,
 
PSI,
 
and
 
PAI
 
are
 
state-level,
 
within-session
 
measures
 
of
 
acute
 
behavioral
 
deviation
 
from
 
each
 
patient's
 
own
 
calibration
 
norm
 
during
 
a
 
specific
 
10-15
 
minute
 
interaction.
 
A
 
high
 
PHQ-9
 
score
 
does
 
not
 
guarantee
 
elevated
 
T2
 
in
 
any
 
given
 
session
 
because
 
a
 
chronically
 
depressed
 
patient
 
may
 
have
 
a
 
habituated
 
psychomotor
 
baseline
 
that
 
does
 
not
 
produce
 
within-session
 
deviations;
 
conversely,
 
a
 
patient
 
with
 
minimal
 
chronic
 
symptoms
 
can
 
exhibit
 
acute
 
behavioral
 
spikes
 
during
 
emotionally
 
loaded
 
task
 
items.
 
This
 
ipsative-normative
 
design
 
mismatch
 
is
 
analogous
 
to
 
correlating
 
resting
 
heart
 
rate
 
(a
 
trait
 
measure)
 
with
 
exercise-induced
 
heart
 
rate
 
variability
 
(a
 
state
 
measure):
 
the
 
expected
 
correlation
 
is
 
near
 
zero
 
by
 
construction,
 
and
 
its
 
absence
 
does
 
not
 
invalidate
 
either
 
measure.
 
Future
 
validation
 
designs
 
should
 
employ
 
longitudinal
 
intra-individual
 
comparisons
 
or
 
pre/post
 
intervention
 
assessments
 
that
 
match
 
the
 
state-level
 
measurement
 
logic
 
of
 
PsyClick.
 
Cohen’s  kappa  between  PsyClick  system  flags  and  independent  clinician  CORF  
ratings

5.3.  Contribution  to  Knowledge  
PsyClick  makes  three  principal  contributions  to  the  intersection  of  digital  mental  
health,
 
behavioral
 
biometrics,
 
and
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support.
 
First,
 
it
 
provides
 
the
 
first
 
documented
 
architecture
 
for
 
hardware-agnostic,
 
single-session,
 
ipsative
 
psychomotor
 
baseline
 
construction
 
in
 
a
 
clinical
 
intake
 
context,
 
operationalizing
 
the
 
within-session
 
individualization
 
principle
 
through
 
an
 
EWMA-based
 
covariance
 
model
 
rather
 
than
 
a
 
235  

=== PAGE 251 ===
 
population-derived  norm.  The  HAL’s  device  profile  system—storing  polling  rate,  computed  
δ_

and

device
 
type,
 
with
 
mechanical
 
keyboards

an

ms
 
correction
 
for
 
early-event

a
 
generalizable
 
solution
 
to
 
the
 
hardware-dependency
 
barrier
 
identified
 
by
 
Killourhy
 
and

(2009)
 
as

to
 
cross-device
 
keystroke

 Second,  it  demonstrates  that  Hotelling's  T²  multivariate  statistical  process  
control—previously
 
applied
 
primarily
 
in
 
industrial

The
