Chapter  VI  
RECOMMENDATIONS  
Drawing  from  the  study's  findings,  limitations,  and  the  gaps  identified  during  the  
development
 
and
 
evaluation
 
of
 
PsyClick,
 
the
 
following
 
recommendations
 
are
 
offered
 
for
 
future
 
researchers,
 
system
 
developers,
 
and
 
clinical
 
practitioners
 
who
 
may
 
extend,
 
replicate,
 
or
 
deploy
 
the
 
system.
 
 6.1.  Expanding  the  Participant  Sample  and  Demographic  Diversity  
The  current  study  recruited  n  =  105  adult  participants  using  purposive  homogeneous  
sampling
 
to
 
control
 
for
 
age-related
 
fine
 
motor
 
differences
 
and
 
digital
 
literacy
 
variation.
 
While
 
this
 
sampling
 
strategy
 
maximizes
 
internal
 
validity
 
for
 
the
 
initial
 
validation,
 
it
 
limits
 
the
 
generalizability
 
of
 
the
 
psychomotor
 
norms
 
and
 
convergent
 
validity
 
coefficients
 
reported.
 
Future
 
research
 
should
 
expand
 
recruitment
 
to
 
include
 
older
 
adult
 
populations,
 
participants
 
with
 
varying
 
levels
 
of
 
digital
 
exposure,
 
and
 
individuals
 
from
 
diverse
 
clinical
 
diagnoses
 
beyond
 
depression
 
and
 
anxiety
 
—
 
including
 
ADHD,
 
bipolar
 
disorder,
 
and
 
early-stage
 
dementia
 
—
 
to
 
assess
 
the
 
transdiagnostic
 
detection
 
capability
 
predicted
 
by
 
Fritze
 
et
 
al.'s
 
(2025)
 
sensorimotor
 
network
 
framework.
 
A
 
minimum
 
sample
 
of
 
n
 
=
 
200
 
in
 
a
 
heterogeneous
 
replication
 
study
 
would
 
enable
 
stratified
 
convergent
 
validity
 
analyses
 
by
 
age
 
cohort
 
and
 
clinical
 
diagnosis,
 
substantially
 
strengthening
 
the
 
external
 
validity
 
of
 
the
 
psychomotor-clinical
 
index
 
associations
 
reported
 
here.
 
6.2.  Longitudinal  Deployment  and  Intra-Individual  Tracking  
238  

=== PAGE 254 ===
 
PsyClick  was  designed  and  evaluated  as  a  single-session  clinical  decision  support  
tool.
 
A
 
natural
 
extension
 
of
 
the
 
within-session
 
ipsative
 
architecture
 
is
 
longitudinal
 
deployment,
 
in
 
which
 
a
 
patient's
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
from
 
a
 
previous
 
session
 
serves
 
as
 
an
 
enriched
 
prior
 
for
 
subsequent
 
sessions,
 
enabling
 
intra-individual
 
trend
 
detection
 
across
 
multiple
 
intake
 
visits.
 
Future
 
work
 
should
 
investigate
 
cross-session
 
EWMA
 
initialization
 
strategies
 
that
 
balance
 
the
 
privacy-by-design
 
principle
 
of
 
session-boundary
 
data
 
discard
 
with
 
the
 
clinical
 
value
 
of
 
longitudinal
 
behavioral
 
trajectory
 
monitoring.
 
Such
 
an
 
architecture
 
would
 
enable
 
detection
 
of
 
treatment
 
response
 
(progressive
 
normalization
 
of
 
T²
 
scores),
 
relapse
 
warning
 
(re-elevation
 
of
 
PSI
 
or
 
PAI
 
following
 
a
 
period
 
of
 
normal
 
flagging),
 
and
 
assessment
 
of
 
intervention
 
efficacy
 
—
 
clinical
 
applications
 
substantially
 
beyond
 
the
 
single-session
 
scope
 
of
 
the
 
present
 
study.
 
6.3.  Validation  Against  Additional  Clinical  Instruments  
The  present  study  validated  PsyClick's  psychomotor  indices  against  the  PHQ-9  and  
GAD-7,
 
the
 
two
 
most
 
widely
 
used
 
primary
 
screening
 
instruments
 
for
 
depression
 
and
 
anxiety.
 
Future
 
research
 
should
 
assess
 
convergent
 
validity
 
against
 
instruments
 
with
 
direct
 
psychomotor
 
subscales
 
—
 
specifically
 
the
 
Montgomery-Åsberg
 
Depression
 
Rating
 
Scale
 
(MADRS
 
Items
 
6:
 
Concentration
 
Difficulties
 
and
 
7:
 
Lassitude),
 
the
 
Hamilton
 
Anxiety
 
Rating
 
Scale
 
(HAM-A
 
Items
 
3:
 
Fear
 
and
 
5:
 
Intellectual
 
Difficulties),
 
and
 
the
 
Unified
 
Parkinson's
 
Disease
 
Rating
 
Scale
 
(UPDRS
 
Part
 
III)
 
for
 
populations
 
with
 
motor
 
pathway
 
involvement.
 
Concurrent
 
validity
 
against
 
clinician-administered
 
structured
 
diagnostic
 
interviews
 
(e.g.,
 
MINI
 
International
 
Neuropsychiatric
 
Interview)
 
would
 
additionally
 
establish
 
whether
 
PsyClick's
 
Red
 
flags
 
correspond
 
to
 
diagnosable
 
clinical
 
presentations,
 
239  

=== PAGE 255 ===
 
moving  the  system's  evidence  base  from  convergent  biomarker  validation  toward  clinical  
diagnostic
 
utility.
 
6.4.  Multi-Site  Clinical  Deployment  and  Ecological  Validity  
The  current  evaluation  was  conducted  in  a  controlled  laboratory  environment  with  
standardized
 
hardware
 
configurations.
 
Multi-site
 
deployment
 
across
 
university
 
counseling
 
centers,
 
outpatient
 
psychiatric
 
clinics,
 
and
 
community
 
mental
 
health
 
offices
 
—
 
each
 
with
 
heterogeneous
 
hardware,
 
lighting
 
conditions,
 
desk
 
ergonomics,
 
and
 
client
 
populations
 
—
 
would
 
provide
 
a
 
direct
 
test
 
of
 
the
 
HAL's
 
device-normalization
 
effectiveness
 
under
 
ecologically
 
valid
 
conditions.
 
Future
 
deployment
 
studies
 
should
 
include
 
a
 
hardware
 
diversity
 
stress
 
test
 
in
 
which
 
the
 
same
 
participants
 
complete
 
sessions
 
on
 
deliberately
 
varied
 
input
 
devices
 
within
 
a
 
single
 
week,
 
enabling
 
direct
 
comparison
 
of
 
T²
 
score
 
consistency
 
pre-
 
and
 
post-HAL
 
normalization
 
in
 
naturalistic
 
settings.
 
Partnership
 
with
 
the
 
Department
 
of
 
Health
 
and
 
accredited
 
mental
 
health
 
service
 
providers
 
under
 
the
 
Philippine
 
Mental
 
Health
 
Act
 
would
 
provide
 
the
 
multi-site
 
deployment
 
infrastructure
 
necessary
 
for
 
national-scale
 
ecological
 
validation.
 
6.5.  Integration  with  Electronic  Health  Record  Systems  
The  current  architecture  generates  session  reports  as  standalone  PDF  exports,  
requiring
 
manual
 
integration
 
into
 
a
 
client’s
 
clinical
 
record
 
by
 
the
 
attending
 
professional.
 
Future
 
development
 
should
 
investigate
 
standardized
 
data
 
exchange
 
integration
 
with
 
Electronic
 
Health
 
Record
 
(EHR)
 
systems
 
deployed
 
in
 
Philippine
 
mental
 
health
 
institutions,
 
using
 
HL7
 
FHIR
 
(Fast
 
Healthcare
 
Interoperability
 
Resources)
 
as
 
the
 
communication
 
240  

=== PAGE 256 ===
 
protocol.  FHIR's  Observation  and  DiagnosticReport  resource  types  are  structurally  
compatible
 
with
 
PsyClick's
 
session
 
outputs
 
—
 
the
 
T²
 
score,
 
PSI,
 
PAI,
 
clinical
 
flag,
 
and
 
domain-segmented
 
profile
 
can
 
each
 
be
 
mapped
 
to
 
FHIR
 
Observation
 
instances
 
with
 
standardized
 
LOINC
 
codes
 
for
 
psychomotor
 
assessment.
 
Such
 
integration
 
would
 
enable
 
longitudinal
 
storage
 
of
 
PsyClick
 
session
 
data
 
within
 
the
 
client’s
 
medical
 
record,
 
enabling
 
treating
 
clinicians
 
to
 
review
 
psychomotor
 
trend
 
data
 
alongside
 
traditional
 
clinical
 
notes
 
and
 
medication
 
records.
 
6.6.  Ethical  and  Regulatory  Development  
As  PsyClick  moves  from  research  prototype  toward  clinical  deployment,  the  ethical  
and
 
regulatory
 
framework
 
governing
 
its
 
use
 
must
 
be
 
developed
 
in
 
parallel
 
with
 
its
 
technical
 
capabilities.
 
Specifically,
 
future
 
work
 
should
 
engage
 
with
 
the
 
National
 
Privacy
 
Commission's
 
guidelines
 
on
 
biometric
 
data
 
processing
 
under
 
RA
 
10173
 
to
 
establish
 
a
 
formal
 
data
 
classification
 
ruling
 
for
 
keystroke
 
and
 
cursor
 
dynamics
 
in
 
the
 
clinical
 
context
 
—
 
resolving
 
the
 
ambiguity
 
between
 
their
 
classification
 
as
 
behavioral
 
biometrics
 
(requiring
 
heightened
 
protection
 
per
 
Wycislik
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024)
 
and
 
their
 
classification
 
as
 
clinical
 
observation
 
data
 
(subject
 
to
 
the
 
medical
 
records
 
framework
 
of
 
DOH
 
regulations).
 
Institutional
 
Review
 
Board
 
protocols
 
for
 
multi-site
 
clinical
 
deployment
 
should
 
be
 
developed
 
in
 
advance
 
of
 
any
 
replication
 
study,
 
incorporating
 
the
 
client
 
consent
 
procedures,
 
data
 
minimization
 
requirements,
 
and
 
audit
 
logging
 
specifications
 
established
 
in
 
the
 
present
 
study's
 
ethics
 
framework
 
as
 
baseline
 
requirements.
 
Additionally,  given  that  the  system  generates  Green,  Amber,  and  Red  clinical  flags  
that
 
attend
 
clinicians
 
view
 
before
 
completing
 
their
 
own
 
assessment,
 
future
 
work
 
should
 
241  

=== PAGE 257 ===
 
formally  investigate  the  automation  bias  risk  —  the  tendency  for  clinicians  to  anchor  their  
observational
 
ratings
 
on
 
system-generated
 
outputs
 
rather
 
than
 
forming
 
independent
 
clinical
 
impressions.
 
The
 
current
 
beta
 
testing
 
protocol
 
mitigated
 
this
 
risk
 
by
 
requiring
 
CORF
 
completion
 
before
 
system
 
report
 
disclosure;
 
future
 
multi-session
 
deployment
 
studies
 
should
 
assess
 
whether
 
repeated
 
exposure
 
to
 
PsyClick
 
outputs
 
affects
 
clinician
 
observational
 
sensitivity
 
over
 
time,
 
and
 
whether
 
training
 
protocols
 
are
 
needed
 
to
 
maintain
 
independent
 
clinical
 
judgment
 
alongside
 
system-augmented
 
assessment.
 
6.7.  Open-Source  Release  and  Reproducibility  
The  PsyClick  detection  pipeline  —  encompassing  the  HAL  normalization  algorithm,  
the
 
EWMA
 
baseline
 
construction,
 
the
 
Hotelling's
 
T²
 
computation
 
with
 
Ledoit-Wolf
 
adaptive
 
estimator,
 
the
 
Feature
 
Contribution
 
Analysis,
 
and
 
the
 
Fuzzy
 
Logic
 
Classifier
 
—
 
represents
 
a
 
methodologically
 
novel
 
contribution
 
to
 
the
 
clinical
 
digital
 
phenotyping
 
literature.
 
To
 
maximize
 
reproducibility,
 
facilitate
 
external
 
validation,
 
and
 
enable
 
future
 
researchers
 
to
 
build
 
upon
 
the
 
present
 
architecture,
 
the
 
research
 
team
 
recommends
 
open-source
 
release
 
of
 
the
 
core
 
statistical
 
pipeline
 
(exclusive
 
of
 
the
 
clinical
 
UI
 
and
 
client
 
data
 
management
 
components,
 
which
 
require
 
additional
 
security
 
review).
 
Release
 
under
 
a
 
license
 
that
 
requires
 
attribution
 
and
 
prohibits
 
commercial
 
deployment
 
without
 
institutional
 
IRB
 
approval
 
would
 
preserve
 
the
 
system's
 
research-use
 
status
 
while
 
enabling
 
replication
 
studies
 
and
 
methodological
 
extensions
 
by
 
independent
 
research
 
groups.
 
6.8.  On  the  Clinical  Utility  of  the  Current  Prototype  
PsyClick  was  developed  and  evaluated  as  a  first-generation  prototype,  and  its  
validation
 
results
 
should
 
be
 
understood
 
in
 
that
 
context.
 
The
 
two
 
principal
 
validation
 
242  

=== PAGE 258 ===
 
benchmarks—convergent  validity  (r  ≥  0.30)  and  clinical  concordance  (κ  ≥  0.60)—were  not  
met
 
in
 
this
 
study.
 
However,
 
these
 
outcomes
 
reflect
 
an
 
important
 
methodological
 
insight
 
rather
 
than
 
a
 
fundamental
 
limitation
 
of
 
the
 
system.
 
The
 
near-zero
 
correlations
 
between
 
PsyClick’s
 
psychomotor
 
indices
 
and
 
PHQ-9/GAD-7
 
total
 
scores
 
are
 
a
 
direct
 
and
 
expected
 
consequence
 
of
 
the
 
ipsative
 
within-session
 
design:
 
T²
 
measures
 
each
 
session’s
 
deviation
 
from
 
that
 
participant’s
 
own
 
calibration
 
baseline,
 
and
 
therefore
 
does
 
not
 
produce
 
the
 
between-subjects
 
variance
 
that
 
cross-sectional
 
correlations
 
require.
 
Validating
 
an
 
ipsative
 
instrument
 
against
 
trait-level
 
self-report
 
measures
 
is
 
a
 
design
 
mismatch,
 
not
 
a
 
failure
 
of
 
the
 
instrument
 
itself.
 
Future
 
studies
 
should
 
employ
 
validation
 
designs
 
that
 
match
 
the
 
system’s
 
measurement
 
logic—such
 
as
 
longitudinal
 
intra-individual
 
tracking,
 
pre/post
 
intervention
 
comparisons,
 
or
 
clinician-blinded
 
behavioral
 
profiling
 
across
 
clinical
 
and
 
non-clinical
 
populations.
 
A  specific  constraint  of  this  pilot  study  concerns  the  clinical  session  subsample.  The  
present
 
evaluation
 
included
 
15
 
clinical
 
sessions
 
alongside
 
105
 
normative
 
sessions.
 
This
 
clinical
 
subsample
 
is
 
insufficient
 
to
 
support
 
diagnostic
 
sensitivity
 
or
 
specificity
 
estimation,
 
receiver
 
operating
 
characteristic
 
analysis,
 
or
 
the
 
establishment
 
of
 
clinical
 
decision
 
thresholds
 
calibrated
 
to
 
diagnosed
 
populations.
 
The
 
normative
 
data
 
collected
 
should
 
be
 
understood
 
as
 
characterizing
 
within-session
 
psychomotor
 
reactivity
 
in
 
a
 
non-clinical
 
adult
 
population,
 
not
 
as
 
a
 
validated
 
clinical
 
threshold
 
for
 
distinguishing
 
patients
 
with
 
and
 
without
 
mental
 
health
 
diagnoses.
 
Full
 
deployment
 
of
 
PsyClick
 
as
 
a
 
clinical
 
decision
 
support
 
instrument
 
requires
 
prospective
 
validation
 
in
 
a
 
clinical
 
sample
 
of
 
sufficient
 
size
 
(minimum
 
n
 
=
 
50
 
or
 
more
 
per
 
diagnostic
 
category)
 
with
 
confirmed
 
diagnoses
 
established
 
through
 
structured
 
clinical
 
243  

=== PAGE 259 ===
 
interview  (e.g.,  SCID-5),  and  such  validation  is  explicitly  recommended  as  the  
highest-priority
 
next
 
step
 
for
 
future
 
researchers
 
extending
 
this
 
work.
 
Despite  these  limitations,  the  present  study  established  several  findings  of  genuine  
clinical
 
and
 
scientific
 
value.
 
The
 
system
 
achieved
 
an
 
overall
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010
 
rating
 
of
 
4.54/5.00
 
(Excellent)
 
across
 
all
 
five
 
software
 
quality
 
dimensions,
 
demonstrating
 
that
 
PsyClick
 
is
 
technically
 
sound,
 
computationally
 
efficient
 
on
 
minimum-specification
 
hardware,
 
and
 
comprehensible
 
to
 
non-statistician
 
clinicians.
 
The
 
domain-segmented
 
and
 
level-resolved
 
T²
 
profiling
 
confirmed
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
detects
 
real
 
within-session
 
psychomotor
 
reactivity:
 
threshold-exceedance
 
rates
 
of
 
88.6–90.5%
 
across
 
all
 
four
 
stressor
 
domains,
 
and
 
a
 
systematic
 
dose-response
 
trajectory
 
across
 
task
 
difficulty
 
levels
 
(Level
 
A
 
to
 
Level
 
C),
 
constitute
 
the
 
strongest
 
internal
 
validity
 
evidence
 
available
 
from
 
within-session
 
behavioral
 
data
 
alone.
 
These
 
patterns
 
are
 
clinically
 
meaningful
 
regardless
 
of
 
cross-sectional
 
convergent
 
validity
 
coefficients,
 
because
 
they
 
demonstrate
 
that
 
the
 
system’s
 
detection
 
pipeline
 
responds
 
sensitively
 
and
 
systematically
 
to
 
escalating
 
cognitive-emotional
 
load.
 
The  Cohen’s  kappa  of  0.466  (Moderate  Agreement),  while  below  the  pre-specified  
threshold
 
of
 
0.60,
 
is
 
itself
 
informative.
 
It
 
confirms
 
that
 
PsyClick’s
 
flags
 
are
 
not
 
random:
 
the
 
system
 
and
 
the
 
attending
 
clinician
 
agree
 
substantially
 
beyond
 
chance,
 
and
 
the
 
pattern
 
of
 
disagreements—the
 
system
 
flagging
 
Amber
 
or
 
Red
 
in
 
sessions
 
where
 
clinicians
 
rated
 
Green—is
 
consistent
 
with
 
the
 
system
 
detecting
 
early-stage
 
or
 
subtle
 
psychomotor
 
variance
 
not
 
yet
 
salient
 
to
 
direct
 
observation.
 
This
 
positions
 
PsyClick
 
as
 
a
 
behavioral
 
signal
 
amplifier
 
rather
 
than
 
a
 
clinician-replacement
 
validator,
 
a
 
role
 
that
 
is
 
clinically
 
appropriate
 
and
 
consistent
 
with
 
its
 
design
 
as
 
a
 
decision-support
 
augmentation
 
tool.
 
Taken
 
together,
 
the
 
evidence
 
supports
 
the
 
conclusion
 
that
 
PsyClick
 
is
 
a
 
credible,
 
technically
 
functional
 
prototype
 
244  

=== PAGE 260 ===
 
with  a  theoretically  sound  detection  architecture,  and  that  the  path  to  confirming  its  clinical  
utility
 
lies
 
in
 
better-matched
 
validation
 
studies
 
rather
 
than
 
in
 
redesigning
 
the
 
system
 
itself.
 
6.9.  Ablation  Studies  and  Feature  Selection  Validation  
The  current  study  employed  all  eight  psychomotor  biomarkers  in  the  Hotelling's  T2  
computation
 
without
 
conducting
 
a
 
formal
 
ablation
 
analysis
 
to
 
determine
 
the
 
marginal
 
contribution
 
of
 
each
 
feature
 
to
 
detection
 
performance.
 
Future
 
researchers
 
are
 
strongly
 
encouraged
 
to
 
conduct
 
systematic
 
feature
 
ablation
 
experiments
 
in
 
which
 
one
 
feature
 
at
 
a
 
time
 
is
 
removed
 
from
 
the
 
T2
 
model
 
and
 
the
 
resulting
 
change
 
in
 
sensitivity,
 
specificity,
 
and
 
Cohen's
 
kappa
 
is
 
measured
 
across
 
a
 
sufficiently
 
powered
 
clinical
 
sample.
 
Such
 
studies
 
would
 
address
 
two
 
key
 
questions:
 
first,
 
whether
 
the
 
cursor-dynamics
 
subsystem
 
(Path
 
Entropy,
 
Cursor
 
Velocity,
 
Jerk,
 
Pause
 
Frequency)
 
provides
 
statistically
 
independent
 
additive
 
signal
 
beyond
 
the
 
keystroke
 
subsystem
 
alone;
 
and
 
second,
 
whether
 
a
 
reduced
 
feature
 
model
 
might
 
achieve
 
equivalent
 
clinical
 
discrimination
 
with
 
lower
 
computational
 
overhead.
 
Ablation
 
results
 
should
 
be
 
reported
 
as
 
changes
 
in
 
area
 
under
 
the
 
ROC
 
curve
 
and
 
Cohen's
 
kappa
 
relative
 
to
 
the
 
full
 
eight-feature
 
baseline,
 
and
 
should
 
be
 
conducted
 
across
 
multiple
 
hardware
 
configurations
 
to
 
confirm
 
that
 
feature
 
contributions
 
are
 
not
 
device-dependent
 
artifacts.
 
Future
 
development
 
should
 
also
 
investigate
 
whether
 
clinician-adjustable
 
fuzzy
 
rule
 
sensitivity
 
parameters
 
can
 
be
 
implemented
 
without
 
compromising
 
the
 
White
 
Box
 
auditability
 
of
 
the
 
classification
 
pipeline.
